# Phase 1 — Stale-risk matrix and confirmed causes

Baseline evidence captured 2026-10-29 UTC; post-fix evidence mapped through 2026-11-05 UTC. This phase distinguishes observed failures from source-level risk and paths that have not yet been exercised.

This is the baseline stale-risk matrix. Post-fix evidence is appended below and in `00-discovery.md`; existing cells remain historical unless a row explicitly says retested.

## Matrix coverage and highlights

Current snapshot (Delta 986, 2026-11-05): the route matrix has 758 rows—681 mutating HTTP routes, five GET routes with state-changing side effects, 71 scheduled invocations, and one model-create writer. Actor-screen cells have 28 PASS / 8 PARTIAL / 722 NOT_VERIFIED; other-viewer detail has 16 PASS / 1 PARTIAL / 3 NOT_APPLICABLE / 738 NOT_VERIFIED; list cells have 24 PASS / 3 PARTIAL / 3 NOT_APPLICABLE / 728 NOT_VERIFIED; buttons/actions have 19 PASS / 10 PARTIAL / 4 NOT_APPLICABLE / 725 NOT_VERIFIED; related records and badges each have 2 PASS / 3 NOT_APPLICABLE / 753 NOT_VERIFIED; dashboards have 1 PASS / 2 PARTIAL / 1 NOT_APPLICABLE / 754 NOT_VERIFIED; approval inbox has 7 PASS / 1 PARTIAL / 4 NOT_APPLICABLE / 746 NOT_VERIFIED. Counts were parsed directly from the current CSV. Delta 985 added the missing scheduler move route after the runtime route-contract caught it; its source observer/outbox path is mapped. Delta 986 marks only its actor screen PARTIAL after a mocked-API component regression proved that the returned move is rendered while the refetch remains pending; live API and peer UI evidence remain NOT_VERIFIED. Delta 966 adds PARTIAL actor/list/inbox evidence for a mocked Finance profile-update approval reconciliation; its server response removes the completed row while a list refetch is held pending. Delta 965 adds PARTIAL actor/action evidence for a mocked Bill Payment approval race regression. Delta 972 changes the Purchase Request approval list cell from PASS to PARTIAL after an isolated response-to-render sample at 4.616 s; the peer detail sample also exceeded the 2 s target. These do not establish server endpoint behavior or real WebSocket delivery. Delta 932's Maintenance completion buttons/actions cell is based on API 409 and desktop/tablet component refetch/action-revocation checks, not peer browser or WebSocket evidence. Decision Center accept also has partial actor/action evidence. Coverage remains sparse relative to the inventory.

At the Delta 700 snapshot, [`01-stale-risk-matrix.csv`](01-stale-risk-matrix.csv) contained 680 mutating HTTP-route rows, five GET routes with state-changing read side effects, and 71 scheduled-invocation rows (756 total). It has separate columns for the acting user's screen, other viewers' detail, lists, server-provided actions/buttons, linked records, badges, dashboards, and approval inbox. The evidence column points to a reproduced scenario or states why a cell remains `NOT_VERIFIED`. This is a route-level coverage ledger, not proof that each route has been exercised.

The route inventory is still not a complete state-machine transition inventory: one endpoint can represent several state transitions, and the matrix has one row per mutating route, state-changing read route, or scheduled invocation. The Delta 700 snapshot had 756 data rows: 680 mutating HTTP routes, five GET routes with state-changing RFQ side effects, and 71 scheduled invocations. The initial regenerated CSV did not carry forward most historical UI outcomes; this was corrected by reconciling historical scenarios to their exact route rows. Counts parsed after Delta 700 (2026-11-03): actor screen 26 PASS / 3 PARTIAL / 727 NOT_VERIFIED; other-viewer detail 16 PASS / 1 PARTIAL / 3 NOT_APPLICABLE / 736 NOT_VERIFIED; lists 22 PASS / 3 NOT_APPLICABLE / 731 NOT_VERIFIED; buttons/actions 19 PASS / 3 PARTIAL / 3 NOT_APPLICABLE / 731 NOT_VERIFIED; related records 2 PASS / 3 NOT_APPLICABLE / 751 NOT_VERIFIED; badges 2 PASS / 3 NOT_APPLICABLE / 751 NOT_VERIFIED; dashboards 1 PASS / 2 PARTIAL / 1 NOT_APPLICABLE / 752 NOT_VERIFIED; approval inbox 7 PASS / 3 NOT_APPLICABLE / 746 NOT_VERIFIED. The final/intermediate PR-approval, PR-cancellation-list, approval-board, Overtime two-context E2E, Supplier Listing approval, and the Stock Adjustment create/approval scenarios are included in those counts. The latest Finance→VP approval-board sample rendered in 892 ms after the actor response; this is a single sample, not a p95. Final-approval detail/actions remain `PARTIAL` because earlier samples exceeded the target and mixed samples do not establish a stable p95. Deltas 548–550 add Payroll Adjustment approval and rejection samples, including post-change server-action rendering: actor list, HR detail, list, action visibility, and approval inbox are `PASS`; related records, numeric badge counts, and dashboards remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The latest rejection board refetch began 1,087 ms after the actor response; this is one response-to-refetch-start sample, not commit-to-UI or p95. Supplier Listing's post-change vendor-list refetch sample is 523 ms after the actor response; detail and other side effects remain unverified. Two Stock Adjustment three-context approval runs refreshed the requester row 2,053 and 2,141 ms after the actor response and observed board refetch responses at 1,915 and 1,632 ms. The list samples exceed two seconds; neither series is commit-to-UI or p95, so the target remains unproven. All 756 route rows at that snapshot had a `Source wiring:` trace. That is source evidence, not proof of event delivery or correct UI behavior. These totals do not prove every domain-state transition, role or side effect. The transition summaries below remain narrative evidence, with per-route details in the CSV.

The supplemental [`00-explicit-state-transitions.csv`](00-explicit-state-transitions.csv) initially contained 113 allowed edges from the 15 transition-map constants found by a repository-wide `api/app` source scan. Subsequent source-trace deltas expanded it with direct assignments, derived statuses, lifecycle service branches, async handoffs, scheduled jobs, initialization paths and related-record state changes. The count includes nine employee terminal edges guarded by clearance even though they are added in `EmployeeStateMachine::transitionAfterClearance()` rather than stored in its constant, and three AssetTransfer edges explicitly unrouted by the module. Complaint operations are expanded from the service's operation-to-allowed-current-status map. The current source-only inventory contains 596 transition/initialization rows after delta 604 added Employee initial-state and parent NCR effectiveness aggregate edges; all 83 enum-backed state/status fields are source-mapped or explicitly dispositioned (aggregate-derived NCR effectiveness and reference-only civil status). This is still not a complete project transition inventory: non-enum state fields, imports, asynchronous mutations, and every related-record side effect still require coverage. Runtime evidence remains partial; source references alone do not prove exact edge invocation.

The Delta 711–718 source reconciliation resolved all 116 rows that had `route_edge_mapping=CANDIDATE_ENDPOINTS_ONLY` across HR, CRM, Quality, Return Management, Production, Accounting, Loans, and Maintenance. Each now identifies an exact route, scheduled/ledger caller, or an explicit defined-but-unreachable disposition (nine Employee edges). This improves source traceability only; it does not prove those exact edges or the corresponding UI surfaces at runtime. The route-level matrix remains 756 rows and its runtime outcome counts are unchanged. Delta 719 also confirmed that the dev Compose stack was running and the public hostname and Vite client both returned HTTP 200; this is a point-in-time serving check, not an all-user delivery proof.

The model-cast supplement [`00-status-enum-catalog.csv`](00-status-enum-catalog.csv) contains 83 enum-backed state/status fields across 72 models. After delta 604, all 83 are source-mapped or explicitly dispositioned, and all 16 formerly unmapped writer-coverage cells now name source candidates or a documented exception; mapped fields may still contain grouped branches whose exact runtime state pairs have not been exercised. One parent NCR effectiveness field is aggregate-derived outside the action guard, and one civil-status classification is not a workflow. These numbers expose uncovered enum-backed state definitions; plain strings, flags, and workflow fields under other names are outside this cast scan.

The focused [`00-direct-status-write-paths.csv`](00-direct-status-write-paths.csv) began with 27 P2P PR/PO direct status-write paths and now contains 192 source-traced writer groups across Purchase Request, Purchase Order, PurchaseOrderResponse, RFQ quotes/invitations, SupplierOrderDispatch, SupplierItemListing, SalesOrderResponse, PriceRequest, CustomerComplaint NCR handoff, StockAdjustment, StockCountSession, StockMovement, PayrollPeriod lifecycle/bank-file/GL handoffs, PayrollAdjustment apply/release, B2B delivery schedules, Asset lifecycle/status, Dashboard KPI snapshots, and the previously listed production, quality and other module writers, including documented migration/demo initializers. Its actor/detail/list/action/related/badge/dashboard/inbox cells retain exact historical evidence where available (including selected PR/PO and RFQ scenarios); the rest remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The critical-stock auto-PO producer gap was fixed in delta 373 and its focused regression verified exactly one PO chain/outbox refresh; cross-role PO list/detail delivery remains `NOT_VERIFIED`. Cache-generation invalidation exists separately. Row counts describe source groups, not completed behavioral coverage.

The final-approval retest used four isolated browser contexts against the local dev deployment: Purchasing created/submitted a ₱60,000 PR, Finance advanced it to the VP step, System Administrator held the detail open, and VP approved with its socket deliberately disabled. After the request-coalescing change, the peer's private record subscription acknowledged and three `chain.refresh` frames were observed. Its authorized detail GET returned 200; without manual navigation, the peer rendered Approved and removed its stale Cancel action 1.726 s after the actor API response. The post-action GET began 113 ms after the response and completed in 1.452 s. The actor rendered Approved and removed Approve within the test's 2 s assertion bound. The latest run recorded three successful peer detail reads (and an aborted stale request), compared with eight responses in an earlier run. An earlier 3.508 s sample and a 3.628 s assertion failure show that this improvement has not established a stable p95. Read-only outbox inspection from the prior successful record found three final-state `ChainStepAdvanced` rows, all published on the first attempt; the conversion workflow later reached `manual_required`. This verifies one final PR approval across two roles, not latency distribution, list/badge/dashboard fan-out, or universal PR behavior. See the 2026-11-02 follow-up in `00-discovery.md` and `spa/e2e-real/purchase-request-final-approval-realtime.spec.ts`.

All other route rows remain unverified until covered by module migration tests. That includes direct create/edit/delete, bulk actions, transition endpoints, import/export side effects, jobs, scheduled work and webhook paths; those async entrypoints are cross-indexed in `00-async-entrypoints.csv`.

## Live infrastructure finding — local scheduler stopped cache maintenance and outbox recovery

**Impact:** broad operational risk to scheduled recovery and cache hygiene; scheduler resilience is now fixed in the dev override, while the underlying scheduled-task failure remains a separate functional investigation.

On 2026-11-02, `ogami-scheduler` was found exited with code 1 and no restart policy, with its last exit on 2026-10-29. The configured shell loop used `set -e` around `schedule:run-fail-fast`; container logs show `ar:run-dunning` returned exit code 1, which terminated the process. This prevented recurring `cache:audit`, `cache:prune-unbounded`, and `outbox:dispatch` recovery tasks from running, even though Horizon and Reverb were separately available. The cause inside `ar:run-dunning` is not established and is tracked in `FINDINGS.md` for separate investigation.

`.dev-runtime/docker-compose.override.yml` now logs a failed tick and continues after 60 seconds, with Docker `restart: unless-stopped`. Compose configuration validation passed; only the scheduler was recreated. It remained running with the new restart policy, and its startup tick reported `outbox:dispatch --limit=100` complete. This is runtime/operational evidence, not proof that all scheduled transitions have correct event coverage or that a stopped worker's missed events were replayed. `horizon:status` reports running. The metadata-only cache scan found 185 entries, all with TTL and none over 1 MiB; it did not inspect values or identify semantic staleness.

The audit also ruled out one candidate cause at the event-class boundary: all 116 scanned broadcast event classes explicitly implement both `ShouldBroadcast` and `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`, all use the dedicated broadcasts trait, and none uses `ShouldBroadcastNow`. Laravel's `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit` contract defers event listener invocation through the transaction manager. This does not rule out omitted mutation dispatches, dispatch-before-write call ordering outside transactions, missed channel consumers, failed broadcasts, or stale authorized API responses.

The shared Action Center task mutation was a source-confirmed freshness gap: `ActionCenterTaskService::apply()` changed task state that hides or assigns an item for every role, but only touched the badge cache generation and staged no broadcast for other open viewers. It now records one content-free `BadgesChanged` outbox event in the same transaction for the entire bulk action. The central signal map refetches active Action Center, badge, approval and dashboard queries. A follow-up source trace found a cache/event ordering window: outbox dispatch and cache-generation rotation are both after-commit callbacks, but `BadgeService::touch()` was called only after the transaction returned, after `OutboxService::record()` had already registered queue dispatch. A fast worker could broadcast before the badge/dashboard snapshots were retired. The service now registers cache rotation inside the transaction before registering outbox dispatch; Laravel's transaction manager executes callbacks in registration order. Empty task batches no longer rotate the global generations. The focused queue-push ordering regression now passes (1 test / 4 assertions), verifying that both generation values have advanced before a queued outbox dispatch can be observed by a worker. This verifies callback ordering in the test environment, not live delivery or the original race under a live concurrent worker. The route's UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED` without a multi-user UI observation. The page-owned 60-second refresh was removed; server-provided time boundaries now schedule one-shot refetches, with provider catch-up reserved for degraded delivery (see root cause 152).

An additional Purchasing source audit compared the PR and PO budget-acknowledgment services and found that the PO service persisted the acknowledgment without staging a chain refresh. `PurchaseOrderApprovalChainTest::test_budget_acknowledgement_stages_one_chain_refresh_with_the_record_update` failed before the change with zero outbox rows and passes after the service locks/rechecks the record and stages one refresh inside the transaction. The full affected approval-chain file passed 8 tests / 18 assertions and the mutation-ownership file passed 10 / 60. This source/test finding is not one of the ten browser-verified transitions above; UI freshness for the actor and other viewers remains unverified.

## Confirmed causes, ranked by scope

### 1. No complete event subscription/invalidation coverage (broad, reproduced; selective fix verified)

The baseline SPA had page-owned Echo hooks and no central provider or entity-to-query invalidation map. The reproduced Purchase Request detail view at `spa/src/pages/purchasing/purchase-requests/detail.tsx` did not subscribe to `chain.purchase_request.{id}`; the active record stayed pending until manual reload. The page now mounts the shared `useChainProgress` hook, and the later two-context cancellation test confirms detail refetch and stale-button removal for that transition. One inventory stock-adjustment approval also refreshed two other-role stock lists via the shared inventory invalidation hook. Subsequent work has extended the shared provider and invalidation map across many modules. The current 757-row route/side-effect matrix still has only the explicitly counted PASS/PARTIAL cells above; most route-level UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`, so project-wide behavior is not demonstrated.

Delta 800 confirmed another source-level subscriber/invalidation gap: the chain-bottleneck API includes a viewer's active delegated roles, and `ApprovalDelegationChanged` already targets the affected user's private channel, but only the delegation-management page subscribed while the central signal map omitted `chain-bottlenecks`. A viewer on a dashboard could therefore wait for its 60-second safety refresh after a delegation change. The shared shell now listens on the exact-user channel and the central map invalidates the bottleneck query. A red-first focused map regression failed on the missing query family and passes after the change. Channel permission parity is inherited from the existing exact-user callback; no delegate-dashboard browser trace was run, so runtime matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

A separate acting-user freshness gap was confirmed in the combined Purchase Request create-and-submit form. `create.tsx` called `purchaseRequestsApi.create()`, then `purchaseRequestsApi.submit()`, but discarded the second response, returned the earlier `draft` snapshot, and navigated without installing an exact detail cache entry. The focused red-first regression showed that the detail query cache was empty after this flow; it now returns the final submit response and passes it through `reconcileRealtimeMutationResponse()` before navigation. The follow-up two-context browser run rendered Pending on the actor detail, then a Finance actor with its socket closed saw Approve disappear 89 ms after PATCH 200 while the VP inbox rendered the next action 892 ms after the response. The 892 ms is one browser sample, not p95. The initial pre-fix run also captured three detail GETs aborted by the client (Nginx 499); this is corroborating symptom evidence, but the exact cancellation trigger was not isolated and is not asserted as a separately confirmed realtime cause.

RFQ source review found a similar but distinct gap: internal RFQ list/detail/edit/compare views did not use the shared list/record hooks, and the existing RFQ lifecycle event contains supplier and quote snapshots, so it was not a safe shared refresh signal. This is a source-discovered omission, not a pre-fix user-visible two-session reproduction. `RequestForQuoteChanged` now stages a content-free after-commit signal from selected RFQ and supplier-quote writes. The 2026-10-30 two-context browser run verified create-list insertion and publish status/action refresh; the publish API-response-to-viewer-render sample was 324 ms. Quote/award flows and related views are still unverified.

The shared QueryClient is configured with 30-second `staleTime` and `refetchOnWindowFocus: false` (`spa/src/lib/queryClient.ts:11-23`). Pages with no matching socket event can remain stale for their own cache policy. The provider now forces active-query catch-up on focus, visibility and reconnect; this selective implementation has not yet been verified across every page. The Purchase Request detail reproduction directly confirms the original user-visible effect.

The Purchase Order submit path demonstrated the producer side of the same coverage gap. Before the fix, `PurchaseOrderService::submit()` staged `PurchaseOrderSubmitted` for its notification listener but omitted `broadcastChain()`, while another authorized user's detail/list received no `chain.refresh` and made no refetch despite a healthy socket. `PurchaseOrderApprovalChainTest::test_submitting_a_purchase_order_stages_one_chain_refresh_with_the_status_change` failed before the fix (zero `ChainStepAdvanced` outbox rows) and passed after the service staged the canonical signal in the status transaction.

The same omission affected Sales Order creation. Before the fix, `SalesOrderService::create()` committed the draft and lines but staged no `ChainStepAdvanced` row, even though the list already subscribed to `entity.sales_order.visible`. A two-context Playwright reproduction confirmed POST 201 for the CRM actor while a System Administrator's open list stayed empty through a four-second observation and showed the row only after manual reload. The new `test_storing_a_sales_order_stages_one_chain_refresh_for_the_committed_record` failed first with zero outbox rows; after the service staged the draft signal after line creation in the same transaction, the 13-test Sales Order route-coverage file passed. Two after-fix browser runs showed response-to-render at 2,370 ms (event +1,733 ms) and 981 ms (event +33 ms; list refetch response +333 ms). The first sample exceeded the proposed 1–2 second target; the second was within it. Two samples do not establish p95 or commit-to-UI latency; record detail, action buttons and related views were not exercised for create. Focused route tests also exposed missing signal staging on update/delete/restore; the CRUD path now asserts exactly one chain row per write. Customer-response submission, proposal resolution, delivery-date proposal, request-customer-confirmation, and no-charge replacement creation now stage a parent refresh, with focused outbox tests for a subset (Sales Order response suite 20 tests / 96 assertions; replacement workflow 1 test / 13 assertions). Browser coverage remains create-only.

The Supply Chain driver run-sheet originally had a subscriber gap. The earlier two-context reschedule reproduction kept the driver detail stale through its ten-second observation; a later source change mounted `useDriverDeliveryRealtime` under `DriverRealtimeBoundary`, and the historical follow-up run verified a normal seeded driver's detail refresh. A fresh source audit found a separate authorization gap in that path: the hook joined `user.{hash}`, whose callback requires `notifications.view`, while driver routes require only the Supply Chain feature and `supply_chain.driver.access`. A valid custom driver role without the unrelated notification permission could therefore be denied the refresh subscription. Delta 321 adds `driver.{userHashId}`, gated by the same feature, active-user check, and driver permission as the driver API; `ChainStepAdvanced` targets that channel and retains the legacy user channel during rollout. The event remains a content-free ping and the hook invalidates only active driver queries. Focused authorization and hook tests are authored but were not run. Current custom-role channel delivery and every route's UI result remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; the historical 15 ms / 1 ms / 499 ms sample applies only to its earlier seeded-driver setup and is not p95 evidence.

Separate red-first backend tests confirmed missing Delivery producers: `DeliveryService::create()` created a scheduled delivery and reservations with zero `ChainStepAdvanced` outbox rows; `delete()` followed by `restore()` likewise staged zero. The service now stages one event after each write in its transaction. Idempotent create retries do not emit duplicates. `DeliveryCreateIdempotencyTest.php` passed 4 tests/12 assertions and `DeliveryDeleteGuardsTest.php` passed 5/10, with the known missing-container-env warnings. This proves outbox producer behavior only. Since no two-context browser test exercised create/archive/restore, their UI freshness and buttons remain `NOT_VERIFIED` in the route matrix.

The attempt-outcome workflow had another omitted producer path: reporting an exception changes status to `return_pending`, depot receipt changes it to `delivered` or `returned`, and a report correction/late recovery changes detail and linked return data. Red-first service assertions confirmed no additional chain event for report, correction, or late recovery. Each successful write now stages one refresh in its transaction; identical request-key replays stage none. The chain status resolver also lacked mappings for the valid enum statuses `return_pending` and `returned`, which prevented the report signal from being staged; both resolve to the last outbound checkpoint `in_transit`, avoiding a false delivered/confirmed checkpoint. The complete attempt-outcome feature file passes (14 tests/166 assertions) and the full chain-definition unit file passes (23/101), with the known container-env/notification warnings. API/UI actor and cross-role behavior for these paths remain unverified.

A second, independent producer gap was confirmed for Purchase Request creation. Before the fix, `PurchaseRequestService::create()` committed the draft and item rows without staging a chain refresh. At about 00:59 UTC, a Purchasing actor received 201 and rendered Draft while the System Administrator's already-subscribed list received only `BadgesChanged`, no `chain.refresh`, and did not show PR-202610-0040 within eight seconds. The new four-assertion service regression failed first (0 chain outbox rows), then passed after create/update/delete/restore each staged one refresh in their transaction. The after-fix two-context run at about 01:02 UTC received `chain.refresh` +49 ms after the actor's API response, the authorized list GET returned at +652 ms, and the row rendered at +1,311 ms without reload. It is one API-response-to-render sample, not commit-to-UI p95. The PR approve/cancel and PO-submit producers were already fixed in the earlier slices; intermediate PR approvals, budget acknowledgement and changed sourcing method are now source-wired but need independent UI tests.

A later live repeat also failed once: the Finance viewer's socket was connected and received `BadgesChanged`, but it received no `chain.refresh` and stayed stale. That run did not timestamp the record/list `subscription_succeeded` frames, so the precise missed-delivery cause cannot be assigned from that trace alone. Code inspection confirmed a separate race: `RealtimeProvider` reports `Live` when the transport connects, while the record/list hook had no catch-up when its authorized private-channel subscription later succeeds. The shared subscription layer now exposes channel readiness; record/list hooks refetch their active query families on initial subscription and reconnect. Unit tests cover initial acknowledgement and reconnect. In the first deterministic delayed-auth run, auth was held 8,070 ms; after release the subscription acknowledged in 168 ms and detail rendered 466 ms later. Since then, failed/pending channel state was made visible as `Reconnecting`, which activates the degraded fallback. An earlier two-context run held detail auth across PO submit for 8,257 ms: the list updated from `chain.refresh`; detail received no event and had no ready subscription, but its authorized API poll rendered Pending approval 3,338 ms after the submit response. Releasing auth yielded an acknowledgement 194 ms later and a current detail render 422 ms after release. After the provider-wide fallback change, a fresh single scenario held detail auth 7,049 ms and rendered detail at +2,232 ms, list at +2,673 ms; this also produced 13 detail and 7 list GETs requiring request-cost profiling. These deliberately degraded runs verify authorized fallback, not healthy-path p95 or universal channel behavior. The earlier normal event sample was 1,262 ms to both viewer renders.

The transport/channel distinction exposed one more source-level risk: the shared subscription registry registered success callbacks but no Echo channel `error` callback, so a rejected private-channel auth request could leave the top-level indicator at `Live` while that channel never became ready. A new subscription regression first failed because `.error()` was never registered. The SPA now tracks transport state plus every mounted private channel's pending/ready/failed state; an auth failure displays `Reconnecting`, invokes authorized refetch callbacks for the shared record/list, badge, notification, permission and inventory hooks, and activates the visibility-aware fallback while the channel is not ready. Tests cover failure → `Reconnecting` → successful resubscription → `Live`. We did not induce a real 403 on the public stack, and not every legacy direct `useEcho` consumer has a page-specific error refetch or degraded fallback; this remains selectively mitigated, not project-wide proven.

Queue timing then isolated a separate, measured latency cause. In one PO submission run, `ChainStepAdvanced` waited 1,987.2 ms in `broadcasts` and executed in 5.0 ms; four queued badge pings in the same burst waited about 2,005–2,024 ms. The old worker combined Redis `block_for=5` with Horizon's default `sleep=3` and had one process. A dedicated `redis_broadcasts` connection now shares the persistent queue Redis DB but uses `block_for=1`; its Horizon supervisor uses `sleep=0` and remains one process on the low-memory dev host. In the next two-context PO run, `ChainStepAdvanced` queue waits were 2.2–3.3 ms and job execution was 5.1–16.0 ms. The different-role E2E measured 1,262 ms from the actor's successful API response until both Finance detail/list pages rendered; the event frames arrived 64/51 ms after the API response and the refetch responses arrived at 529/725 ms. This is a before/after sample, not a percentile or a project-wide SLO proof.

The same browser trace confirmed a client invalidation inefficiency. A single PO event invoked the invalidation API ten times; overlapping prefixes caused three PO-detail GETs, four badge GETs, and a PO-options GET on the detail page. A regression test failed on the former behavior with ten `invalidateQueries` calls. The central invalidation layer now uses one query-cache predicate traversal per event, and PO list/detail/options keys are separate. Live runs show one PO-detail GET and one PO-list GET, with no PO-options GET. Other badge/notification reads still add separate requests. The latest queue sample is fast, but the broader refetch fan-out and p95 remain unmeasured.

The same source audit found missing PO refresh staging on create/update/soft-delete/restore and a PR refresh gap when partial conversion changed visible PR fields. These writes now stage the standard ping, with focused service/outbox tests; the E2E evidence remains submit-only. A focused regression also exposed a separate event-loss defect: the outbox dedupe key used database `updated_at` precision, so two writes in one timestamp tick could collapse into one event. Dedupe now uses a UUID event ID. This cause was test-reproduced, not observed in a live browser trace.

The supplier-catalog audit also confirmed missing signals across listing submission/review, approved-supplier create/update/delete/restore, PO-triggered provisional links, CSV import/rollback, and historical backfill. Red-first tests observed missing outbox rows on submit, PO approval, import and backfill; these write paths now stage through `SupplierCatalogRealtimeService`. The browser reproduction established a separate cross-guard case: supplier sessions use `supplier_portal`, but the SPA had only the internal Sanctum auth endpoint, no supplier vendor channel, and no supplier list subscription. After Purchasing approval, the supplier remained Pending review with Edit visible, received no event, and made no listing GET during five seconds. The fix added a supplier-guard auth route, own-active-vendor channel authorization and a supplier list hook. The two-context retest received auth 200 and the vendor event, made one authorized GET, and rendered Approved with Edit removed 471 ms after the Purchasing response; channel tests deny another vendor. Red-first bulk tests also reproduced two outbox refreshes for two successful rows on bulk supplier submit and approval. Those three bulk submit/approve/reject paths now stage one refresh per successful operation; mixed-vendor internal batches include only the affected vendors' private channels. Focused backend tests pass, but bulk UI behavior, ancillary views and coalescing in other modules remain unverified. The content-free supplier-list ping also omits entity IDs, actor and related references, so it does not yet meet the Phase 2 event envelope and must not be generalized as the standard event without review.

A further delivery producer gap was reproduced on the `POST /api/v1/supply-chain/deliveries/{delivery}/receipt` path. Uploading a receipt to an already-delivered record changed its proof list and receipt-photo field, but the outbox count remained zero. The red-first assertion failed `0 is identical to 1`; `DeliveryService::uploadProof()` now stages one refresh when the upload does not already emit a status-transition refresh. The in-transit path is guarded against duplicate events. The full upload feature file passes 5 tests / 11 assertions (with the existing missing-container-env warnings). This confirms another mutation-level omission, but not a two-role UI pass; both the office and driver receipt routes remain `NOT_VERIFIED` in the matrix. Successful retry of delivery CoC/invoice handoffs had the same producer omission: CoC retry and outbox-listener invoice recovery each updated visible delivery fields with no additional refresh. Both regressions failed before the change (2 existing chain signals, 3 expected), then passed after event staging was added to the transactional retry/manual-required write paths. Invoice replay asserts the already-linked retry adds no duplicate signal. These are service/outbox tests only; the route matrix's UI freshness cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

Delivery proof archive/restore had the same missing producer. The new controller regression failed for both operations with zero outbox rows, then passed after the transactions moved into `DeliveryService` and each staged a chain refresh. The refactor keeps file-presence validation and proof-retention guards in the write path. `DeliveryProofRealtimeTest.php` passes 2 tests / 4 assertions. No two-role UI scenario is claimed; matrix UI cells stay `NOT_VERIFIED`.

A loading-state shipment-plan edit also changed delivery fields without status transition and without an event. The red-first service test failed with zero outbox rows; `saveShipmentPlan()` now emits one refresh in that loading/non-dispatch path while continuing to rely on assignment/status paths for scheduled and dispatch writes. The test passes, but its UI behavior remains unverified.

Delivery cost-handoff retry was another missing producer: service execution created/updated a visible `DeliveryCostHandoff` row but did not refresh the parent delivery query. The red-first service test failed with zero events; the retry now stages one parent ping when the handoff changes and none when the same request key is replayed unchanged. Its focused test passes 1 test / 2 assertions. No cross-role UI behavior is claimed.

The legacy delivery `reserve-stock` recovery route also updated the reservation batch and delivery resource without producing a parent refresh. A route-level regression first failed with no additional chain event; recovery now delegates through `DeliveryService` and stages exactly one event in the reservation transaction, suppressing same-key replay duplicates. The focused API test passes 1 test / 5 assertions. This does not prove inventory dashboard/list invalidation or viewer UI behavior.


A source-level consumer gap was confirmed in Inventory movement history: `InventoryStockChanged` was already emitted after stock ledger writes and the central `inventory_stock` invalidation map already included `['inventory','movements']`, but the `StockMovementsTab` never mounted `useInventoryStockRealtime`. The shell stock listener only covers warehouse managers; ordinary `inventory.view` users on movement history had no listener. Delta 845 mounts the existing hook with the page's `inventory.view` permission and adds a focused page-wiring regression. The targeted page Vitest file passes 2/2 after adjusting duplicate-element selectors in its pre-existing assertions; the shared stock-hook test passes 1/1 with an explicit movement-query invalidation assertion. No second browser delivery was run; movement-list peer behavior remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 2. Procurement summary cache had no write invalidation (broad for P2P summary, reproduced; selective fix verified)

`ProcurementChainController` stores PR, PO, GRN, bill and three-way-match summaries for 60–120 seconds (`api/app/Modules/Purchasing/Controllers/ProcurementChainController.php`). The original global `procurement_chain_pr` key was not invalidated on a pending PR cancellation; a second authenticated API fetch after reload returned the previous aggregate even though the database count changed. The HTTP response was `private, no-cache`; that revalidated the HTTP response but still read Laravel's stale cache. This is a confirmed server-side stale-data source. After the initial fix, a source review found a remaining concurrency race: a request could begin computing the old aggregate before commit, then finish after the after-commit observer forgot the shared key and repopulate that same key with stale data. The summaries now use generation-versioned keys; after-commit Eloquent observers rotate the generation, so an in-flight read can only populate its retired generation. Query-builder/bulk writers are not yet covered, and this narrower concurrent interleaving has not been dynamically reproduced. `ProcurementChainCacheInvalidationTest::test_each_p2p_source_change_invalidates_its_cached_summary_after_commit` now passes with 20 assertions and one PHPUnit warning. It confirms the cached PR/PO/GRN/Bill/three-way summaries remain readable inside the open transaction, their generation rotates only after commit, and the following read returns updated aggregates. The narrower simulated late-fill interleaving remains unverified. Delta 840 targeted a static search across the Purchasing, Inventory, Accounting, and Common application trees and found no direct `DB::table()` mutations of the four aggregate source tables or class-level bulk mutations for those Eloquent models. Raw/dynamic SQL, `withoutEvents`, external writers, and paths outside those trees remain unverified, so writer completeness is still open. The 2026-10-29 after-fix two-context cancellation checks confirmed actor status, other-role detail refetch, removal of the stale Cancel action, and list count refresh. The stronger run exercised the committed outbox path and queued event on the dedicated `broadcasts` supervisor; its record-channel ping arrived and the other-role detail rendered within 1.33 seconds after the actor rendered. This is one sample, not commit-to-UI p95 or a general SLO proof.

### 3. Permission-dependent dashboard cache keys ignored permission changes (confirmed by a red regression; selective fix verified)

`AdminDashboardService::admin()` stored already-gated panel output under `dashboard:admin:{userId}`. `PanelGate` ran only inside the cache miss closure, so the same account could lose `admin.audit_logs.view` or `admin.users.manage` while retaining its dashboard page grant and receive the old cached panels. The new feature regression first failed: after changing the same user's role to a restricted role, `auth_events`, `account_security`, and the other previously cached panels were still returned. Dashboard caches now include a versioned per-viewer scope fingerprint over user/role identity, effective permission slugs, linked employee, and current department. The same scope partition is applied to all bespoke dashboard caches and widget summary/rich payloads. The regression passes after the change; a four-file dashboard suite passed (40 tests, 152 assertions). This does not prove every dashboard data dependency or every permission mutation path; it prevents cross-permission/role/scope reuse for these cache families and leaves old v1 entries to expire on their bounded 30-second TTL.


### 4. Cache invalidation timing and ownership are inconsistent (project-wide risk; selected paths confirmed by source)

Cache writers and invalidators are scattered across controllers, observers and services. There is no registry or project-wide rule that every cache has a source owner, scope, TTL and after-commit invalidation. The current `JournalEntryObserver` defers its tag flush until commit, but its fixed statement keys still allowed a read that began before commit to finish after the flush and refill the active key with an old snapshot. That late-fill interleaving is source-confirmed but was not dynamically reproduced in this no-testing work slice. Financial statements now include a seven-day generation in their 60-second keys; JournalEntry and Account lifecycle writes rotate it after commit. The tag flush remains for prompt cleanup, while late fills can only populate a retired generation. Procurement Chain previously demonstrated the separate no-invalidation failure directly.

Dashboard summary, widget, and Finance keys now include the shared `dashboard:cache:version` generation while retaining user and permission-scope fingerprints where applicable. Badge-backed writes rotate this generation after commit, preventing in-flight old reads from repopulating the active key. Other source writers remain bounded by the entry TTL until their invalidation paths are audited. The audit also found direct badge-version changes and user/role permission-key forgets inside transactions; these could expose an invalidation window where a concurrent read re-caches pre-commit state. Shared `BadgeService::touch()` and `User::flushPermissionsCache()` now defer invalidation until commit, and UserAdmin single/bulk role changes use that rule. Explicit-transaction regression tests fail before and pass after these changes. Journal-entry tag invalidation now also runs after commit, with a regression test. Other aggregate cache families remain unverified.

One confirmed production-dashboard cache race was first found when Eloquent/service writers invoked `forgetCache()` inside a transaction and immediately deleted the fixed `dashboard:production` key. Deferring deletion until commit fixed the pre-commit invalidation, but another stale-fill race remained: a reader that began before commit could finish after the forget and repopulate that same active key. The cache now uses `dashboard:production:v{generation}` plus a seven-day version marker; after-commit invalidation rotates to a unique generation, so late fills remain under retired keys. It also forgets the legacy fixed key. The test was updated to assert the version remains unchanged inside the transaction and changes after commit, but was not run in the current no-tests work slice. Writer completeness and realtime freshness for all production dashboard consumers remain unverified.

`SettingsService` had the same in-flight cache-fill race across application settings, with a one-hour TTL: `get()` could miss, read the old committed row, then repopulate `settings:{key}` after a write's after-commit forget. The read and invalidation path now use `settings:{key}:v{generation}` with a per-setting seven-day marker; `set()`, `updateFromAdmin()`, and `flushCache()` rotate generations after commit and retire the legacy fixed key. Source search found no runtime settings-table writer outside `SettingsService`. The focused regression now models a late stale fill under the old generation, but was not run per the no-tests instruction, so runtime race reproduction and post-change behavior remain unverified.

The DSS detector-parameter cache used the same fixed-key pattern with a one-hour TTL. `DssParameterService::get()` could read the previous parameter value during an update and repopulate it after the setter's `Cache::forget()`. Reads now use detector/key-specific generations; the DssParameter observer registers rotation inside the write transaction so it runs after the outer commit, and removes the legacy key. `DssParameterCacheAfterCommitTest` passes one warning-marked scenario / 7 assertions: the prior snapshot remains selected during an open outer transaction, commit rotates its marker, and a simulated late write under the retired key does not affect the next read. The test warning is the missing `/var/www/.env` in the API test container; no Redis outage or live detector run was exercised.

`SettingsService::set()` had the same ordering defect for `settings:{key}` and its dependent production dashboard key. A focused explicit-transaction regression failed before the fix: after `set()` ran inside an open transaction, the old cache entry was already missing. That permits a concurrent reader to refill the cache from the still-committed old database value, leaving it stale for the one-hour TTL. `set()`, `updateFromAdmin()`, and `flushCache()` now schedule their scoped invalidations after the outermost commit; the focused test confirms the old entry remains available inside the transaction and is removed after commit. A runtime `api/app` source scan found no other settings-table writers; the controller and request references only read settings. Migration-time writes run outside the request cache lifecycle.

### 5. Query-cache identity and authorization-scope changes could retain prior data (security/freshness risk; source fix expanded)

Many React Query keys still omit user/role identity. The auth store now clears the full query cache when the user, role, linked employee/department, delegated approvals, active state, effective permissions or feature set changes; the realtime identity key uses the same scope. Role and user access events refresh `/me`, employee department changes notify the linked user, delegation writes notify affected delegates, and the SPA revalidates auth when a tab returns to focus/visibility. This prevents the identified stale authorization scope from remaining in this client when those refresh paths run, but key scoping and source-writer coverage still need broader module and browser verification. No new cross-user/role/department/delegation scenario was run under the stop-testing instruction.

### 6. Broadcast publication lifecycle is not uniform (partially mitigated; not the reproduced PR failure)

`api/config/queue.php` sets `after_commit=false` for Redis queues. The outbox dispatch job is scheduled after the business commit, and a dedicated `redis_broadcasts` connection shares the persistent queue Redis DB. A 2026-10-30 source sweep found that only three of the 15 direct `ShouldBroadcast` classes implemented `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`. It also found a subtle lane-selection defect: Laravel 12's `BroadcastManager::queue()` reads the event's `$connection` property; `broadcastConnection()` alone does not select a queue connection. The previous default and dedicated connections happened to share a Redis DB and queue name locally, so the dedicated worker could still consume those jobs, but that physical-layout coincidence did not enforce the configured lane. The current source catalog has 125 broadcast classes; all use the shared dedicated-queue trait and implement `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`, with no `ShouldBroadcastNow` class. The class inventory guard and focused transaction test cover the dispatch contract. This is source/test evidence, not a reproduction of a user-visible stale screen for all producers. Producer completeness and mutation-path coverage remain unaudited.

A static scan found all 122 current broadcast classes construct `PrivateChannel`; no event constructs a public `Channel` or `PresenceChannel`. `ChainStepAdvanced` is dispatched through the durable outbox after commit; this was the path for the tested PR/PO transitions. It targets private record and entity-list channels with a content-free refresh payload; record authorization uses the corresponding row policy where one exists, and the list channel checks module plus list-read permission. Exact scope parity for every list channel remains under review. The former shared `badges` channel accepted any authenticated user and exposed event timing across accounts, although its payload contained no counts. Red-first backend regressions confirmed the event targeted that shared channel and any authenticated test account could subscribe. `BadgesChanged` now fans the ping out to individual `badge-user.{userHashId}` private channels; the callback requires an active user whose hash matches the authenticated principal, and SPA badge/inbox hooks join only their own channel. Focused backend channel/fan-out tests and the two affected SPA hook tests pass. No two-user live delivery/latency scenario for this channel change has run. No failed/out-of-order event was observed.

### 8. Broadcast payloads did not consistently follow ping-and-refetch (confirmed source risk; selectively fixed)

The serializer audit found `BadgesChanged` had no `broadcastWith()` whitelist. After queue metadata was added to the shared event trait, Laravel's default serialization could include public event properties beyond the intended refresh signal. `PermissionOverrideChanged` sent a permission slug, old/new override types and a free-text reason although the SPA only refreshed the caller's auth state. `PayrollProgressEvent` sent employee-processing counters and status; its UI patched local state directly from those fields. Those payloads are now explicit and content-limited; payroll progress refetches the authorized resource and invalidates the list on completion. `UserNotificationCreated` still sends only the notification ID/type because the client uses those to fetch the individual item from its authorized endpoint. All 122 current broadcast classes now implement `broadcastWith()`. Their envelopes are not yet one identical schema, but payloads are allowlisted and data-heavy update events remain refresh signals followed by authorized API reads.

The shared SPA provider also previously limited disconnected polling fallback to selected list screens; other mounted query data could stay stale indefinitely if its channel failed and no tab-focus/reconnect occurred. The provider now invalidates all active authorized queries with exponential backoff only while the socket/channel layer is not live, and the page-owned list timers were removed. A focused offline test confirms the first 5-second catch-up. The delayed-auth two-user PO scenario passed after this change: viewer detail rendered through an authorized GET +2,232 ms after submit, list render was within +2,673 ms, and no manual reload was needed. The trace also counted 13 detail and 7 list API GETs, including repeated badge/notification and reference/options reads; the exact request contributors are not yet isolated. Healthy-socket query invalidation remains module-specific, and no full Reverb-offline or worker-stopped multi-user scenario has been measured.

### 7. Outbox pickup on the first queue hop added about 1.2 seconds (confirmed latency cause; selectively fixed)

The 2026-10-30 Sales Order create browser run emitted `chain.refresh`, but viewer render took 2,430 ms from the successful actor API response. Structured queue telemetry showed the two `DispatchOutboxMessage` jobs waited 1,196.5 ms and 1,281.2 ms on the `redis:high` queue; the resulting `ChainStepAdvanced` broadcast itself waited only 22.3 ms on `redis_broadcasts:broadcasts`. This isolates the added delay before broadcast publication. `supervisor-high` used Redis's five-second blocking connection and Horizon's default three-second idle sleep, while the broadcast supervisor already used one-second blocking and zero sleep. A queue-lane regression was made to fail on the old high-lane config, then `DispatchOutboxMessage` and `supervisor-high` were routed through the existing fast Redis alias, retaining a distinct `high` queue. In the next same-scenario two-context run, the outbox waits were 12.3–99.6 ms, broadcast wait 35.6 ms, `chain.refresh` arrived +137 ms, and the viewer rendered +1,286 ms. This is a limited before/after observation rather than a percentile; API refetch and UI render time still account for most of that run. No high-queue load/throughput benchmark has been run. Delta 972 then measured a separate E2E-stack queue-topology problem: its single `queue:work redis_broadcasts --queue=high,broadcasts` worker prioritized 60 outbox dispatch jobs ahead of 22 dashboard-widget broadcasts, and three PR `ChainStepAdvanced` broadcast jobs waited 2.300–3.294 s. The live dev stack has separate Horizon supervisors for `high` and `broadcasts` (Delta 955), so this does not prove the same delay on dev. The E2E Compose topology now separates the workers; the only rerun timed out before a business mutation, so the correction has no passing latency result yet.

### Additional confirmed actor-cache reconciliation gap — Finance profile-update inbox (Delta 966)

The profile-update review page wrote a successful API response over the matching row in every cached list, but did not reconcile the row's new state with each query's filter. A red-first component test held the follow-up list request open after Finance approval and reproduced the approved request still displayed in `awaiting_my_action` with a stale count. The shared success callback now removes the row from action queues when the response revokes every available action for the viewer, and from status-filtered lists when its status no longer matches; it adjusts the visible total and keeps the authorized refetch. The focused mocked-API actor test passes after the change. This is a narrow client reconciliation cause confirmed in one page/test, not a root cause for all record types, server endpoint execution, or peer delivery; only the Finance review route's actor/list/inbox cells are `PARTIAL`.

### Ruled out for the reproduced failures

- Reverb was running and established WSS; private channel authorization POSTs returned 200 in both isolated browser contexts.
- The tested acting user's API request and immediate detail refetch returned the committed Approved state.
- The stale Procurement Chain API response was not a browser/service-worker cache: a full reload made a new API request, and that response still held the stale aggregate. The Laravel `Cache::remember` key explains the observed response.
- The SPA service worker bypasses `/api/` and `/sanctum/`; it was unregistered in Vite development. It did not cause these traces.
- Authenticated API cache headers missing on an unauthorized route were not reproduced: the focused `AuthenticatedApiCacheHeadersTest` passes for both a 200 and a 401 response. The exception-handler callback remains defense in depth; no live 403/422 response-header capture establishes a separate cache root cause.

### Additional post-baseline slice (inventory stock ledger)

`InventoryDashboardService` caches the global stock summary for 30 seconds under `inv:dashboard:summary`. Initially its stock movement/reservation generation rotated only when the asynchronous outbox consumer ran. A red-first regression warmed the summary at `0.00`, held `DispatchOutboxMessage`, committed a receipt worth `50.00`, and read the stale `0.00` again. The mutation services now rotate inventory, Warehouse, Purchasing and stock-widget generations in the source transaction, before registering after-commit outbox dispatch; the consumer only stages refresh pings. The same focused regression now reads `50.00`, and the selected publisher, reservation-staging and rollback tests pass (4 tests / 26 assertions). The channel requires `modules.inventory` and `inventory.view`; clients refetch stock and related active queries through their authorized API calls. One live three-context browser run separately confirms the exercised stock-adjustment approval refreshed Warehouse and System Administrator stock-level lists while their sockets were Live: API-response-to-both-rows-render was 558 ms in that sample. The response's `approved_at` timestamp preceded DB commit, making the ~1,235 ms timestamp-to-render value an approximation. No live dashboard viewer, two-user cache read, every stock writer, cache hit rate, API p95, or latency percentile was measured. A separate missing `Illuminate\Support\Str` import in `InventoryDashboardService` surfaced when the focused check attempted the full summary after a movement. The existing `InventoryDashboardHashIdTest` reproduced it red before the one-line import fix and now passes (1 test / 3 assertions); it remains a separate functional review unit in `FINDINGS.md` and is not part of the cache fix.

### Additional confirmed cache race — stock summary invalidated after queue handoff (2026-11-03)

`StockMovementService::move()` and the reservation source path persisted business data and staged durable outbox rows, but the inventory summary and dashboard generations rotated only later in `PublishInventoryStockChange`, when a queue worker consumed those rows. The source transaction and actor's immediate next API read could therefore race a delayed or stopped worker and return the still-valid cached namespace (30-second TTL). The regression held the outbox dispatch job, committed a 10-unit receipt at `5.00`, and reproduced the warmed cache returning `0.00` instead of `50.00`. Cache retirement now registers its after-commit generation callbacks directly in both source paths, ahead of the outbox job's after-commit queue callback; the listener publishes only the existing minimal refresh signals. The focused cache regression and selected source/publisher commit/rollback tests pass (4/4 selected, 26 assertions). The reservation staging test also confirms its cache rotations have committed while its queued dispatch is held, but does not mutate a reservation quantity. No live API dashboard, peer browser, actual reservation write under held queue, cache hit-rate, or response-latency sample was run.

The after-fix live approval scenario also passes across Finance and requester contexts: the requester's already-open detail and list changed status and removed the Cancel action without reload. It validates only that approval path.

## Cache inventory

`00-cache-call-sites.csv` began as a discovery-time source-hit ledger (349 rows in that snapshot: 97 `api/app`, 195 `api/tests`, and 57 `api/database` hits); it has since been extended and line-reconciled. The current counts are maintained in `cache-registry.md`. The call-site rows are source inventory, not behavioral proof. The identifier-boundary scan avoids counting class names containing `Cache` as facade calls; an earlier `DashboardWidgetCache::version()` reference was a false positive before that helper was renamed. The application cache entry families found in `api/app` are summarized here; key expressions and source locations are recorded for follow-up in [`cache-registry.md`](cache-registry.md). TTLs below are source-defined where present. “No reliable invalidation found” means a repository-wide source scan did not reveal a corresponding invalidation, not that no external operator may clear a key.

| Family / key pattern | Scope and TTL | Invalidation / risk |
|---|---|---|
| `procurement_chain:{aggregate}:v{generation}` plus 7-day generation marker | Global aggregate; 60s / 120s summary TTL | PR/PO/GRN/Bill Eloquent observers rotate the generation after commit. In-flight old reads can populate only a retired generation. Bulk/query-builder paths need audit; the original PR stale read was reproduced before fix. |
| `dashboard:{admin,employee,plant_manager,ppc,accounting,purchasing,quality,warehouse}:v4:{generation}:{userId}:{scopeHash}[:range]` and `dashboard:hr:v5:{generation}:{userId}:{scopeHash}:d{ManilaDate}` | User, role, effective permissions, employee and department; payload 30s, generation markers 7 days | Global dashboard rotation retires snapshots after shared configuration/badge changes; source-scoped generations now cover the registered dashboard source models and relevant bespoke families after commit. Service-specific source invalidators remain in place. Permission/scope fingerprints partition every payload. Direct SQL/quiet/external writers and wall-clock-only boundaries remain audit items. |
| `dashboard:widget:summary:v3:{global}.{widget}:{userId}:{scopeHash}:{key}` and `dashboard:widget:rich:v3:{global}.{widget}:{userId}:{scopeHash}:{kind}:{key}` | User, role, effective permissions, employee and department; payload 30s; global/scoped generation markers 7 days | `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` maps 77 registered source models to applicable bespoke/scalar/rich generations; generation changes retire old namespaces after commit. `DashboardCacheGeneration::touch()` stages one coalesced private `DashboardWidgetDataChanged` signal for global and widget-generation changes. Direct/quiet writers, external writers and time-only boundaries remain audit items. |
| `finance_dashboard:summary:v4:{financeGeneration}:{panelGateSignature}` | Finance caller permission/panel signature; 30s; finance-scoped dashboard generation marker 7 days | A dedicated after-commit generation now rotates for Account, AccountingPeriod, Bill, Budget, BudgetLineItem, BudgetTransfer, CreditNote, Department, FiscalYear, Invoice, JournalEntry, PayrollPeriod, Vendor and setting writes. Source registration precedes realtime observers/outbox staging. Direct SQL and quiet child-table writers remain to audit. |
| `badges.user.{userId}.v{badgeGeneration}.s{inventoryGeneration}` and `badges.version` | User-scoped values; summary TTL; badge and inventory generation markers expire after seven days | Badge observers and direct invalidations rotate unique generations after commit; stock-change refetches also use the current InventoryDashboardCache generation, so low-stock counts cannot be read from a pre-stock snapshot. Observer queues `BadgesChanged`; stock changes use `InventoryStockChanged`. |
| `dashboard:production:v{generation}` + 7-day marker; `inv:dashboard:summary:v{generation}` + 7-day marker | Global aggregates; 30s | Both rotate unique generations after commit, so a pre-commit in-flight fill cannot repopulate the active key. Production invalidation goes through `ProductionDashboardService::forgetCache()`, which rotates the active marker before attempting legacy-key cleanup; SalesOrder, WorkOrder and WorkOrderOutput Eloquent lifecycle writes use the central observer. Inventory rotates from committed stock events. Direct/quiet writer completeness and dashboard UI evidence remain incomplete. |
| `stmt:{type}:v{generation}:{date/range}` tagged `financial_statements` | Global ledger aggregate / date range or as-of date; 60s; generation marker 7 days | JournalEntry and Account observers rotate the generation after commit and flush statement/finance tags. Late fills remain under retired generation keys. Source writer coverage and dynamic verification remain incomplete. |
| `settings:{key}:v{generation}` + `settings:version:{sha256(key)}` | Global settings; value 3600s, generation marker 7 days | `set()`, `updateFromAdmin()`, and `flushCache()` rotate unique per-setting generations after the outermost commit, then attempt legacy-key cleanup. Per-setting cache failures are isolated so shared dashboard and production generations can still rotate before `SettingsChanged` is published. Late pre-commit fills remain under retired generations; no current tests were run. |
| `holidays:{year}:v{generation}`, `holidays:cache_version` | Year; 5 minutes; marker 7 days | Holiday writes rotate the unique generation after commit without reading the old marker first, then attempt legacy-year and tag cleanup. HR and employee dashboard snapshots combine shared and family-scoped generations; holiday writes rotate both dashboard families after commit. Cache reads fail open to the source query and marker loss initializes a fresh UUID, never the old `legacy-v1` namespace. |
| `gov_table:{agency}:rows-v2:v{generation}:active`, `gov_table:{agency}:rows-v2:v{generation}:eff:{date}`, `gov_table:{agency}:ver` | Agency/date; active/effective 300s, marker 7 days | Import/service rotate the unique generation after commit before attempting legacy-key cleanup. The former fixed active key allowed a pre-write reader to refill stale brackets after invalidation; active/effective reads share the generation boundary and cache plain arrays. Cache failures are logged and do not prevent the later table refresh signal. |
| `payroll:progress:{period}` | Period; 3600s | Tracker exposes explicit forget; progress is a transient snapshot. |
| `dss:params:{detectorId}:{key}:v{generation}` + hashed detector/key marker | Detector/key; value 3600s, marker 7 days | The DssParameter observer rotates after the outer transaction commits and removes the legacy fixed key. The focused regression passes one warning-marked scenario / 7 assertions and proves an in-flight stale fill remains under a retired generation. Detector-run lock uses 310s. |
| `mrp:plant-run` | Global lock; 1200s | Lock lease; not response data. |
| `production:idem:{workOrderId}:{idempotencyKey}` | Work order/key; configured idempotency TTL | Best-effort acceleration only; the composite WorkOrderOutput database idempotency key and request fingerprint are authoritative. Cache read/write failures fall back to the durable row and do not change the mutation response. |
| `queue-wait:*` listener dedupe | Event/dedupe-specific; 300s | Listener clears on failure path; bounded TTL. |
| `illuminate:queue:restart` | Framework worker-control timestamp; intentionally permanent | Laravel `queue:restart` and `horizon:terminate` use this control key. It is explicitly allowlisted from unbounded-cache pruning and remains protected from `volatile-lru` because it has no TTL. The one expected permanent infrastructure key is separate from bounded application payloads and the queue-data Redis service. |
| `system:user_id:{sha256(email)}` | Configured service-account identity; 86400s | Email-scoped key, written after commit; cache read failure falls through to the configured account lookup; removes legacy flat key when resolver misses. |
| `backup:remote-artifact:{sha1(bucket/key)}` | Storage bucket/object metadata verdict; configured TTL defaults to 120s | Restore reads through; entry expires by TTL. Catalog probes use `FailOpenCache`, so a cache outage falls through to the storage metadata source. |
| `dash:an:ver:{environment}:{metric-or-*}` plus `dash:an:{metric}:v{global}.{metric}:{scope}:{filterHash}` | Environment, metric, effective company/department/role-derived data scope and normalized filters; marker 7 days; payload TTL follows metric cadence (60s live default, up to 1 day) | Unique metric/global tokens rotate after commit; permission gate executes before cache reads. The `returns.pipeline` scope includes its customer-only predicate; source invalidation remains metric-specific. |


The analytics cache had a permission-scope collision in `returns.pipeline`: `MetricRegistry` grants both `customer_service_officer` and `warehouse_staff` the same `return_management.view` permission, while `ReturnsPipelineAnalytics::build()` filters customer-service users to customer returns. `DomainMetric::scopeLabel()` returned `company` for both users, so identical period/filter requests shared a cache key despite different SQL result populations. The metric now appends `:customer-only` using the same `ReturnManagementAccess::customerReturnsOnly()` predicate that controls its query. The focused `test_returns_pipeline_cache_separates_customer_only_from_company_scope` now passes with six assertions: it inserts customer and supplier returns, confirms the customer-service payload contains only the customer row, confirms warehouse receives both rows, and verifies distinct cache keys/scopes. PHPUnit reports one warning; the assertions completed. The source/seeded permissions establish the collision risk, but a live cache-payload leak was not dynamically reproduced.

`AnalyticsCache::remember()` previously read, ran the SQL callback and wrote with no per-key coordination. Multiple viewers of the same expired metric/scope/filter key could all run the same aggregation concurrently. It now uses a hashed per-key cache lock with a 30-second lease, a one-second acquisition wait, and a second cache read after acquisition. A timed-out waiter rechecks the key then computes without writing, so it does not overwrite the lock owner's eventual fill. Lock-backend errors retain an unlocked fill fallback and are logged. A contention regression source was added but not run; a real concurrent cold-cache load has not been measured.

The discovered frontend and application families now have registry rows, but rows marked **audit** remain incomplete until owner, scope, TTL/size, invalidation order and tests are verified. Queue/session/runtime framework caches remain separate infrastructure entries in Phase 0 and the Redis topology notes. The application source scan found no global `Cache::flush()`; the two tagged flush sites are scoped to their named groups. This catalog status does not prove dynamic package behavior or every future writer.

## Infrastructure findings

- Baseline Redis shared queue/cache on one unbounded process. The updated Compose configuration separates persistent queue Redis (256 MiB, `noeviction`) from disposable cache Redis (96 MiB, `volatile-lru`). This is running locally; production Compose/env are updated but not deployed. After consumers moved locally, 166 keys under the verified legacy cache prefix were deleted from old DB 3; queue DB 2 was untouched. The new cache DB 3 had 119 keys after the recorded E2E snapshot, all with TTL. A later audit found Laravel's `illuminate:queue:restart` worker-control marker without expiry. Current code keeps that exact marker permanent and allowlisted so `volatile-lru` cannot evict it; the audit reports it separately from application entries without TTL. These point-in-time checks do not prove all future writers set TTLs or prevent cache stampedes.
- At discovery no dedicated `broadcasts` lane or worker existed. Queue telemetry reproduced a 1,987.2 ms wait for a PO `ChainStepAdvanced` broadcast with only 5.0 ms worker time. Horizon used its default 3-second idle sleep while Redis used a five-second block. The implementation routes refresh broadcasts through a dedicated `redis_broadcasts` connection and one Horizon worker. Current static scan finds 127 broadcast event classes declaring after-commit dispatch and the dedicated queue, with no `ShouldBroadcastNow` classes; this does not prove each mutation dispatches exactly once. `PayrollProgressEvent` is a documented transient telemetry exception, and `InventoryStockChanged` is emitted synchronously under its durable parent outbox dispatch. Reverb scaling remains disabled, appropriate for the one-node dev stack.
- Reverb WSS and channel auth worked in the reproduced contexts. The local proxy override has the needed `/app` and `/apps` Upgrade path; the checked-in dev proxy path alone is `/ws`. Public Cloudflare WebSocket pass-through remains unverified.
- At discovery, authenticated API responses observed `Cache-Control: no-cache, private`; there was no API-wide `no-store` / validator policy. Auth-protected API routes now receive `private, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate` and `Vary: Cookie, Authorization`, covered by a focused feature test. This browser/intermediary policy does not by itself correct backend cache staleness.
- The deployment source had a cache/worker ordering gap: the Makefile and GitHub workflow started consumers before framework cache generation, the workflow/manual script omitted `event:cache`, and the deploy script/workflow omitted `queue-mail` from rebuild/restart/smoke checks. Production OPcache sets `opcache.validate_timestamps=0`, so API must restart after runtime cache generation. These source findings are now corrected in `Makefile`, `scripts/deploy-update.sh`, and `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`; `realtime:config-check` runs after cache generation. This is a confirmed deployment lifecycle defect in source, but no production deployment was run and it is not claimed as a reproduced cause of current user reports.

## Verification still outstanding

The current route-level matrix contains 758 rows (681 mutating HTTP routes, five state-changing GET routes, 71 scheduled invocations, and one model-create writer); the explicit transition catalog contains 596 edges across 19 modules. The latest parsed runtime counts are recorded above and in `PROGRESS.md`: most UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`, so source wiring and passing focused tests must not be presented as end-to-end coverage. Existing two-context runs cover only named scenarios in the evidence column; they do not establish a project-wide p95 or the 1–2 second SLO.

Still outstanding are multi-user E2E coverage across record families; reconnect and stopped-worker fallback verification; channel-denial coverage across scopes; role-switch/logout cache isolation; unsaved-edit conflict behavior; complete semantic cache invalidation tests; asynchronous/bulk writer verification; and an exact review of every list-channel scope. The cache registry and point-in-time Redis audits document ownership/TTL/infrastructure findings, but a cache hit-rate/API-p95 baseline and proof of semantic freshness for every entry have not been collected.

The Quality outgoing-inspection listener exposed another related-record producer gap. After QC release, `draftReleasedWorkOrder()` may update an existing automatic Delivery by refilling a missing reservation or appending a newly released inspection line. The first-time draft path had a signal, but these existing-record branches did not. The regression passes through both branches and fails with the append signal removed (delivery line is added but no outbox event is staged); the service now stages one refresh for each actual existing-draft update. The listener feature test verifies one added-line signal and no replay duplicate. This establishes backend producer behavior only. The Quality review route and Delivery viewers' UI/action behavior remain unverified.

### 9. Inspection detail/list had no refresh path (confirmed by source and a red-first model regression; slice verified)

`InspectionController` writes an `Inspection` for evidence, verdict, review, cancellation and checklist operations, but the inspection model initially had no realtime lifecycle observer. The detail page consequently had no record subscription and relied on a 15-second interval only before terminal status. Before the observer, `InspectionRealtimeTest` observed zero `InspectionChanged` outbox rows after repeated writes; the first observer implementation produced one row but retained the first action/version. The shared `OutboxService::recordCoalesced()` now refreshes a pending row with the latest metadata. Its regression explicitly asserts one row and the final `updated` action/version (1 test / 5 assertions). A private detail channel checks the Quality feature gate, `quality.inspections.view` and record existence; the private visible-list channel uses the same permission as the list API. The detail/list pages subscribe through the shared hook/map, and successful review/cancel API responses update the acting user's detail cache immediately. Backend event/auth/contract tests and focused SPA tests pass as previously recorded. The actor's review-response rendering is marked PASS from a page-level test. No two-role inspection browser scenario or route-specific mutation test was run, so other UI cells and the full Quality matrix remain unverified. Bulk query-builder writes and child mutations that do not save the parent remain audit gaps.

### 10. NCR detail/list lacked event subscription and mutation producer (source and red-test confirmed; slice selectively fixed)

`spa/src/pages/quality/ncrs/detail.tsx` polled every 15 seconds while open and had no realtime hook; `spa/src/pages/quality/ncrs/index.tsx`, the CAPA effectiveness queue and Quality dashboard had no NCR subscription. `NcrService` changes the parent NCR on actions, disposition, close/cancel, quarantine retry and other workflows, while `NcrAction` rows also change during CAPA verification. Neither model had a registered refresh observer. The red-first `NcrRealtimeTest` failed with zero outbox rows for parent plus CAPA action Eloquent writes, then passed after the shared `NcrRealtimeService` and both observers were registered. The SPA detail/list and CAPA queue now subscribe through the central hooks; the Quality dashboard subscribes only when the current user has `quality.ncr.view`. The invalidation map covers CAPA, MRB/stock and count views. Mutation responses reconcile the actor's in-memory record without relying on the socket. Authorization and broadcast payload contracts pass targeted tests. The actor's cancellation-response rendering is marked PASS from a page-level test. No two-role NCR browser trace or endpoint-specific test for each of the eight matrix routes was run; all cross-role/list and remaining actor/effect UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. Raw bulk/query-builder paths remain an audit gap.

### 11. Calibration and PPAP had no record/list refresh producer (confirmed by source and red-first outbox regression; selectively fixed)

The calibration service mutates register records and recomputes their status, while PPAP transitions mutate parent submissions and child elements, including scheduled expiration. Neither parent nor PPAP child models had a registered realtime observer, there were no matching private detail/list channels, and the calibration screens had no realtime subscription. The regression test reproduced the missing producer: a calibration create and PPAP parent-plus-element writes produced zero refresh outbox rows before observers were registered. The new `QualityRecordRealtimeService` and model observers stage one parent ping per entity/transaction; a single after-commit `QualityRecordChanged` event covers calibration and PPAP, with private detail/list channels authorized by the same read permission and feature gate as the APIs. Calibration list/form subscriptions and actor cache reconciliation now use the shared frontend layer. The backend producer regression passes (12 assertions), and targeted event/channel contract tests have no failures (PHPUnit reports environment warnings). There is no PPAP UI in this SPA, and no two-context calibration/PPAP runtime scenario was run. Query-builder/bulk writers, exact permissions at every deployment role, actor rendering, viewer/list/action behavior and related purchasing/dashboard effects remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; the corresponding matrix cells remain so.

### 12. Inspection specification revisions had no invalidation path (source and red-test confirmed; slice selectively fixed)

Spec upsert, archive and restore mutate the root and revision/item tables, but no spec event observer or private channel exists; neither the editor nor list subscribes. An editor that stays open can therefore retain an old revision, item set and SPC result after another user publishes a spec revision.
