The upsert service's transaction boundary and legacy `InspectionSpecItem` creation hook show why the producer should attach to the model lifecycle, not one HTTP controller. The red-first outbox regression failed with zero refresh rows, then passed after registering a root observer and child-item observer that coalesce one parent ping per PostgreSQL transaction. The authorized private detail/list channel supports archived detail records because the API uses `withTrashed`; the editor/list consume the central realtime hooks and API responses update actor cache. Focused backend tests pass (41 PHPUnit warnings / 350 assertions, warnings from missing container `.env`); frontend invalidation/subscription/editor tests pass (31 tests), and SPA typecheck/targeted ESLint pass. No two-context screen scenario was run; actor, viewer, list, action and inspection-screen effect cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED` in the matrix.

### 13. Grouped final-inspection screen was outside the Inspection invalidation families (source and red-test confirmed; selectively fixed)

The final-inspection UI reads a work-order aggregate containing all outgoing Inspection batches, but neither subscribes to the authorized Inspection list signal nor appears in the `inspection` invalidation map. A per-inspection event could therefore be received by inspection detail viewers while this grouped page retained old status/actions. The root event itself already exists; the missing consumer/invalidation relationship is the cause. The red-first invalidation test failed because the grouped query prefix was absent. The work-order final-inspection page now subscribes to the permission-gated inspection list signal; the central map invalidates that page, production work-order summaries and Quality dashboard queries. The focused map/page tests pass (22 tests), with SPA typecheck and targeted ESLint green. No two-context screen evidence exists; matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 14. NCR template editor initially lacked the record refresh and conflict-safe draft handling (confirmed; focused fix verified)

The original catalog/picker gap was repaired earlier: `QualityRecordRealtimeObserver` stages template changes through the shared outbox, and the list and NCR-create picker consume the permission-gated private list signal through the central invalidation map. A follow-up page audit found the edit route still had no per-record subscription. Its red-first page test failed because no `useRealtimeRecord('ncr_template', id, ...)` handler was registered and the remote-change warning never appeared. The editor now subscribes to the authorized detail channel; it refreshes clean forms, preserves dirty values, pauses saving after a remote update, and offers “Load latest” to explicitly rebase. The focused regression test passes (1 test), including draft preservation and reload. No two-user browser delivery or latency is claimed, and the matrix runtime UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 15. Shipment-lot updates were not connected to open traceability searches (producer and consumer wiring verified; browser behavior unverified)

Shipment-lot create/ensure writes the `shipment_lots` row without a Delivery update, and `TraceabilityPage` queries a term-specific result without subscribing to a lot signal. A user already viewing a trace for the lot number could keep a cached `found=false` result until refresh. The missing producer/consumer relationship was confirmed by a red-first model lifecycle test (zero outbox rows before the observer). `ShipmentLotRealtimeObserver` now stages the content-free signal through authorized private detail/list channels, and the traceability page consumes it through the shared invalidation layer. The producer regression passes (1 test / 5 assertions), channel/event contracts pass in their focused files, and frontend invalidation/subscription tests pass (25 tests). During the producer test, a second confirmed shared-layer defect appeared: multiple writes to one aggregate in one PostgreSQL transaction left the event row carrying the first action and version. `OutboxService::recordCoalesced()` now updates the still-pending row to the latest event metadata and registers only the first after-commit dispatch. Shipment-lot plus the existing Quality services pass together (5 tests / 26 assertions), including the final `updated` action assertion. Matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; no two-context traceability scenario was run.

### 16. Incoming inspection link changed a GRN without notifying its viewers or matching its query key (red-test confirmed; selectively fixed)

Three incoming-inspection creation paths updated `goods_receipt_notes.qc_inspection_id` through the query builder, so the GRN record had no observer callback or chain refresh. Existing GRN list/detail components subscribe to the chain channel, but the invalidation map used `inventory/goods-receipts`, which is not the query prefix used by those components (`inventory/grn`). A red backend producer test observed zero GRN refresh rows, and a red frontend map test showed the actual GRN detail/list keys were absent. The code now changes those links through a row-locked Eloquent helper that keeps each prior null/cancelled guard, a `GoodsReceiptNote` observer stages a content-free after-commit refresh only when the inspection foreign key changes, and the map targets the real `inventory/grn` query family (including the PO-related invalidation). Producer, wire-contract and map tests pass. This verifies the event path and cache-key matching, not a cross-role browser run; every affected UI matrix cell remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 17. Item quality-plan revisions did not refresh other open history pages (red-test confirmed; producer and consumer wired)

`ItemQualityPlanService::createRevision()` changes the active old plan and creates a new revision, while `deactivate()` updates a plan. Neither model lifecycle emitted a refresh. The item revision-history query had no subscription, so another authorized viewer could retain the previous active revision until a manual reload. The red-first lifecycle test failed with zero outbox rows for an in-transaction plan write. The registered observer routes plan changes through the shared Quality publisher, coalesced by parent item and transaction; private item/list channels require the Inventory feature and `inventory.view` used by the history API. The shared invalidation map covers item history and item summary keys, and the page applies the successful API response before refetching. Backend producer, channel authorization, frontend map and hook tests pass. This does not verify real multi-user UI behavior; both write-route rows retain `NOT_VERIFIED` UI cells.


### 18. Return Request detail/list state had no record refresh path (source-audited and producer regression verified; browser behavior unverified)

`ReturnRequest` had no realtime observer and the SPA detail/list did not subscribe. Two additional direct-write bypasses were found: `ReturnRequestService::markInspectionHandoffManual()` performed a query-builder update, and `Quality\InspectionService::replaceInspection()` reassigned the `inspection_id` through the query builder. A red-first `ReturnRequestRealtimeTest` observed no Return Request outbox message for an in-transaction create/update or manual handoff. The shared after-commit event/outbox producer, observer and both Eloquent write conversions now stage the refresh. The list channels distinguish customer-only and all-return scopes; record channel authorization checks the same module/view permission and customer-return restriction used by the detail API. Backend producer, channel, serialization, and frontend hook/map tests pass. The no-fixture constraint prevented a two-context UI check, so this confirms the producer and client wiring, not end-to-end freshness. Other Return Management records, especially `ReturnCase`, remain outside this slice.


### 19. Return Case state and portal timeline changes had no refresh signal (confirmed by source and red producer test; end-to-end UI unverified)

Return Case parent changes were not broadcast, and the list/detail pages had no subscriptions. A reply can change only the append-only timeline while the parent has no dirty fields, and attachment, line and receipt-allocation writes can change detail content independently. The red-first model test proved a case create/update plus public timeline event yielded zero outbox records. Root and child observers now coalesce a content-free `ReturnCaseChanged` event after commit. Internal detail/list authorization requires the module/read permission and applies the same customer-only restriction as the API; a customer-account channel uses the customer guard and tenant match; supplier refresh uses the existing vendor channel and is sent only for a public timeline. Frontend data is invalidated through the central map, and portal/company/user scopes are part of QueryClient keys. Backend producer/workflow/auth/wire tests and focused SPA tests pass. There is no live two-context Return Case scenario, so rendered cross-role status/action freshness and portal reconnect behavior remain unproven.


### 20. Maintenance work orders lacked a record-level freshness path (source and red-test confirmed; wiring selective)

The source inventory found `MaintenanceWorkOrderCreated` was the only broadcast event and only targeted `maintenance.dashboard`. The work-order detail and mobile pages had 15-second polling, the list used a 60-second timer plus ad-hoc Echo, and displayed child writes did not consistently notify open records. `MaintenanceRealtimeTest` failed before the fix because a parent create staged zero outbox rows. Parent and displayed child model observers now stage the same after-commit refresh signal; desktop/mobile/list/dashboard clients consume it through shared hooks. Private record and employee channels follow the existing access scope, and the payload carries no business fields. Focused backend and SPA tests cover the event, channel checks, event contract and invalidation wiring. No live two-role Maintenance browser scenario was run, and the four schedule routes remain deferred; do not treat their matrix cells as passing.


### 21. Maintenance dashboard readers could not authorize its refresh channel (red authorization test; selectively fixed)

The page catalog gates `/dashboard/maintenance` with `dashboard.maintenance.view`, but `maintenance.dashboard` originally required only `maintenance.view`. A `BroadcastAuthorizationTest` using a user with only the dashboard permission reproduced a 403, so dashboard-only viewers would rely on the provider's degraded fallback. The callback now permits either permission while requiring the Maintenance module, and the payload remains a content-free ping. The targeted auth test passes (1 warning / 5 assertions). This proves channel authorization only; rendered dashboard freshness and every widget API scope remain unverified.


### 22. HR separation changes had no viewer freshness signal, and actor responses were discarded (source and red test confirmed; clearance slice wired)

`SeparationService` updated the Clearance aggregate and employee status, and existing `SeparationInitiated`/`ClearanceFullySigned` events served workflow listeners only. The detail page had no record subscription, the clearance list had no event path, and its sign/compute/finalize success handlers discarded returned resources and invalidated only selected local keys. A red-first model regression observed zero `ClearanceChanged` outbox entries. The new coalesced after-commit signal targets the clearance record channel and per-user channels computed by `SeparationService::canView`; payloads contain no checklist/final-pay values. The SPA uses shared record/list hooks and actor response reconciliation; server-computed `available_actions` drives Compute/Finalize. Backend source, channel, scope and serializer tests plus SPA hook/map tests pass. There is no live two-role HR browser run, and the employee-status side effect / other HR mutation paths are not verified.


### 23. Payroll period status and action state had no record/list signal (red producer test; API/UI hooks now wired)

`PayrollProgressEvent` only covered the computation job's progress channel. Approval, correction, finalization, disbursement, void and other period writes did not stage a payroll-period refresh signal, while the period detail UI derived its status/action buttons from its cached API resource. `PayrollPeriodRealtimeTest` reproduced the producer gap: create and two status writes inside a transaction left zero payroll-period refresh outbox rows. An Eloquent observer now stages a transaction-coalesced durable ping. The event contains only event/entity identifiers, action, version, actor hash and timestamp; private record auth uses the same period permission/feature/existence checks as the detail API, and the list channel requires the payroll feature plus `payroll.periods.view`. Payroll list/detail components use the shared hooks/map, and mutation responses reconcile the actor's local period immediately. Backend regression/auth/contract tests and SPA map/subscription tests pass. This only covers `PayrollPeriod` Eloquent writes; payroll rows, disbursement proofs, anomalies, adjustments, statutory tables and their actions remain unverified. The approve transition has one three-context browser sample: HR and Finance viewers refreshed status and action buttons without reload at +2,195 ms from the actor API response; the overall lifecycle E2E later failed on a separate ledger-state assertion, so no full Payroll E2E pass or p95 claim is made.


### 24. Long-lived Horizon workers can keep an obsolete event codec loaded (confirmed in local live run)

After adding `PayrollPeriodChanged`, the API wrote durable outbox rows, but the running Horizon process rejected each with `Event ... PayrollPeriodChanged is not registered for durable publication` and exhausted three attempts. The event was present in the current API container's codec; the old Horizon process had been started before the codec change and did not reload mounted PHP code. Restarting Horizon loaded the current allowlist and resumed the broadcasts supervisor; the repeated two-viewer approval scenario then received the event and refreshed both authorized viewer pages. This is direct evidence of stale long-lived worker code in the local VPS environment. It does not establish a general production incident, but it confirms that hot-reloading the SPA/API alone cannot update queue workers. Deployment/dev workflows need a graceful Horizon restart after code/config changes, plus failed-outbox/queue monitoring. The successful viewer refresh was 2,195 ms from API response to both rendered viewers in one run; no p95 is established.


### 25. Work-order crew changes did not refresh other open crew panels (red test confirmed; selectively fixed)

`WorkOrderAssignmentService::assign()` writes the `WorkOrderCrew` aggregate and its `WorkOrderCrewMember` children in a transaction. The acting SPA invalidated `['production','crew', workOrderId]`, but those models had no refresh observer and another viewer's crew panel had no signal. The red-first regression observed zero outbox events after a crew plus two members were persisted. Parent and member observers now stage one `WorkOrderAssignmentChanged` ping per work order/transaction, after commit, over private detail and list channels. The SPA's existing `work_order` invalidation map now includes crew query keys, so it uses the shared record/list hooks without adding page-level Echo subscriptions. A related channel mismatch was confirmed: `entity.work_order.visible` only required `production.work_orders.view` even though the list route also requires `production.view`; a red authorization test reproduced 200 instead of 403. The callback now checks both. Producer, event codec/contract, channel and map tests pass. No cross-role browser run covers this slice; all UI matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 26. The PHP refresh target could select production Compose defaults on the VPS (live configuration failure; guarded in-place refresh added)

The first live `make dev-refresh-php` execution ran bare `docker compose` while the VPS root `.env` supplied production defaults and the running dev services had been assembled with local runtime override files. It replaced the API and workers with production-configured images; startup guards and missing local Reverb variables put those containers into restart loops, though Nginx and Vite still returned HTTP 200. A follow-up Compose run with only the tracked override created duplicate unstarted workers because the active PHP containers also carried `/tmp/ogami-dev.local.yml`. The API/workers were restored using the active local config set without clearing PostgreSQL or Redis. The target now verifies the API image is `ogami-erp-dev:local` and `APP_ENV=local`, clears Laravel's framework caches, then restarts the existing API/Horizon/mail/Reverb containers by name rather than invoking a partial Compose configuration. The corrected run succeeded; Artisan reported local env, Horizon and `supervisor-broadcasts` running, and `ogamiph.dev` plus `/@vite/client` returned HTTP 200. This is an environment-specific confirmed misconfiguration and not evidence about production deploys.


### 27. Work-order operation transitions had no refresh producer (model-level gap reproduced; signal added)

`WoOperationService` changes operation status, setup timestamps, production timestamps, output, and schedule fields through Eloquent writes. Before the new observer, a valid pause service transition staged no durable realtime event; `WoOperationRealtimeTest` reproduced zero outbox rows (red). A registered `WoOperationRealtimeObserver` now stages a transaction-coalesced `WorkOrderOperationChanged` ping after commit. Its broadcast is content-free and targets the authorized work-order detail/list channels and production dashboard channel; clients refetch from their own API scope. The work-order detail reconciles the acting user's API response into its operation query and invalidates detail/progress queries; the Production dashboard listens through the shared realtime subscription hook. The backend producer and static broadcast contract tests and focused SPA tests pass. This is service/model evidence only: the eight endpoint rows remain unverified for actor HTTP behavior, other-user detail/buttons, lists, linked records, and dashboards; no two-context browser test or route-level test exercised this slice. Search found no direct `DB::table` mutation against `wo_operations` under `api/app`, but generated scheduling paths and future bulk paths still require ongoing review.


### 28. Maintenance schedule CRUD had no realtime producer (model lifecycle gap reproduced; signal added)

The schedule list/detail and create/update/archive/restore pages had no shared real-time subscription. A red-first test performed all four `MaintenanceSchedule` lifecycle operations in one transaction and observed zero outbox messages. `MaintenanceScheduleRealtimeObserver` now stages one transaction-coalesced `MaintenanceScheduleChanged` after-commit signal for Eloquent writes. The private record/list channels require the same `modules.maintenance` and `maintenance.view` checks as the schedule read routes; the wider dashboard channel receives a content-free ping only. The SPA has central schedule query invalidation, record/list subscriptions, a dashboard refresh consumer, and actor response seeding for create/update. Backend producer, authorization and broadcast-contract tests plus frontend invalidation/subscription tests pass. This is model and unit-level evidence: the four HTTP routes and two-user screen behavior remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 29. Production scheduler mutations did not refresh schedule viewers (red producer test; Eloquent and raw-writer paths covered)

`ProductionSchedule` had no registered observer or scheduler-list consumer. The red-first `ProductionScheduleRealtimeTest` created and updated a valid schedule inside one transaction and observed zero durable outbox messages. Source search also found schedule query-builder updates in planner supersede, machine breakdown, reservation release, work-order completion/cancel, and raw delete/reinsert during machine timeline reflow; those writes would bypass a normal model observer. The current path emits a content-minimal `ProductionScheduleChanged` event after commit on authorized private schedule record/list, work-order, and Production dashboard channels. Eloquent bulk writes now materialize and lock rows before updating; timeline reflow explicitly stages the final row refresh after each raw insert inside its transaction. The central invalidation map and shared hook cover the scheduler snapshot, work-order and Production dashboard query families, and the scheduler's unconditional 60-second polling was removed in favor of the provider's disconnected-mode fallback. Tests pass for the producer, machine-breakdown and planner/reflow writer paths, channel permission/module checks, event serialization/queue contract, scheduler behavior and SPA map/hook wiring. No two-role browser or HTTP route scenario was run; every actor/viewer/list/action/related/count/dashboard/inbox cell for the four scheduler mutation routes remains `NOT_VERIFIED`. No backend application cache entry was found for the snapshot; query-cache invalidation is documented in the cache registry.

### 30. MRP machine lifecycle had no shared freshness signal (red producer test; update and status paths verified)

The machine list/detail pages had no shared realtime hooks. `MachineStatusChanged` existed for status-transition workflow side effects and dashboard refresh, but machine model create/update/delete/restore had no durable change producer, and the SPA did not subscribe to the existing machine record channel or any entity-list channel. A red-first `MachineRealtimeTest` created then updated a valid machine in one transaction and observed zero `MachineChanged` outbox rows. The observer now coalesces Eloquent machine lifecycle writes into one after-commit, dedicated-queue `MachineChanged` signal. Restore ignores the intermediate `updated` callback so it emits one `restored` action. Private record and entity-list channels require `modules.mrp` plus `mrp.machines.view`, matching the machine read endpoints; payload contains only refresh/event/entity/action/version/actor/timestamp metadata, and clients refetch through the API. Existing `MachineStatusChanged` and `HandleMachineBreakdown` workflow behavior remain intact. The central invalidation map covers machine detail/list and linked scheduler, production, maintenance and dashboard query families; actor mutation responses update the machine detail cache and visible list row.

Producer regression, authorization, serializer/codec, SPA map/hook and typecheck tests pass. The combined auth/contract run is 65 warning-marked tests / 578 assertions; producer tests are 2 / 9; SPA map/hook tests are 46/46. The warnings are the known missing test-container `.env` warnings, with no assertion failures. Later live two-context Playwright runs verified the machine PUT actor/detail/list behavior (delta 45) and the transition endpoint's actor, other-role detail/list, and state-dependent transition options for idle → offline → idle (delta 49). A further source audit found that status transitions also queued the legacy `MachineStatusChanged` broadcast alongside the observer's canonical `MachineChanged` signal to overlapping detail/dashboard channels; the Production dashboard subscribed to both, creating a redundant invalidation path. A red-first contract test confirmed the legacy event was a second broadcaster, and a frontend hook test confirmed the old subscription. `MachineStatusChanged` is now domain-only for breakdown/restoration listeners, with `MachineChanged` as the sole UI broadcast; the latest cross-role E2E passed after that consolidation (delta 50). A follow-up found a second overlapping breakdown signal: `MachineBreakdownDetected` was broadcast by the queued side-effect listener to the dashboard while `MachineChanged` already covered it. Both new regression assertions failed before consolidation and pass after; the domain event still drives the notification listener, but no longer broadcasts. This second duplicate has source and contract-test evidence only; the two-context browser run did not enter breakdown. These observations do not verify create, archive, restore, linked records, badges, dashboard rendering, or inbox behavior. Application machine state writes in Production, Maintenance, MRP and the breakdown listener use Eloquent updates/saves and reach the registered observer; searches found machine-table query-builder reads but no application raw machine-table update or machine `withoutEvents` path. Demo seeders do write through the query builder and are not runtime mutation paths. No backend machine response cache was found.




### 31. MRP mold writes did not refresh list/detail viewers or related machine pages (red producer test; selective fix verified)

Before the observer, `MoldRealtimeTest::test_mold_create_and_update_coalesce_one_refresh_signal` failed with zero durable `MoldChanged` rows for a valid create plus update in one transaction. Mold shot count/status can also change through Production output and Maintenance completion, and mold-machine compatibility is a pivot write without native model events. The registered observer now coalesces mold create/update/archive/restore writes after commit on the dedicated broadcast queue. `MoldChanged` targets private record/list channels authorized with the `modules.mrp` flag and `mrp.molds.view` permission, plus the existing independently authorized Production dashboard channel; recipients refetch through their API. Compatibility changes touch the mold aggregate and stage refreshes for old/new compatible machine detail viewers. Mold list/detail pages use the shared hooks and central map, including the separate history query and linked scheduler, production and maintenance query families. Actor create/update responses now contain the loaded relation/count shape before seeding the detail cache.

The red producer regression now passes; archive/restore tests, channel allow/deny checks, event payload/codec/dedicated queue contract, map/subscription tests, typecheck and changed-file lint pass. A two-context real-backend mold PUT scenario now passes: System Admin actor, PPC Head detail/list viewers, one event and one refetch per viewer, and 657/726 ms actor-response-to-render samples. Other mold routes and related effects remain unverified. Existing shot-limit alert broadcast events remain in place for backwards-compatible dashboard behavior; the final one-standard-event migration is not complete.


### 32. MRP BOM version changes had no producer; costing invalidation bypassed observers (BOM PUT reproduced live)

`BomService` publishes a new version and deactivates the prior BOM, while `BomCostingService::invalidateForItems()` used a query-builder update for `costed_at`. There was no BOM realtime event or SPA subscription for BOM detail/list and the product's active-BOM summary. The red-first `BomRealtimeTest` observed zero `BomChanged` outbox rows during create/costing; a temporary return to the old bulk update also left zero refresh rows. The service now locks/saves the affected BOM models and the registered observer stages a transaction-coalesced, after-commit `BomChanged` through the durable outbox and dedicated queue. The private record/list/product-summary channels use corresponding API read permissions/scopes, and the payload is only a refresh envelope plus the related product reference.

The first live two-context publication produced duplicate list/product fetches because both old and new BOM versions emitted events. Shared event invalidation now coalesces those invalidations for 100 ms. The repeat showed one authorized refetch each for old detail, BOM list, and CRM product summary; viewer status, active version and state-derived actions changed without reload. One sample rendered 1,249/1,429/1,467 ms after the actor API response; it is not p95 or exact commit-to-UI latency. The active BOM component definition was restored and verified, although historical versions were added. Backend BOM/costing/channel/wire tests passed serially (87 warning-marked tests / 669 assertions) and focused SPA tests passed 51/51. Other BOM routes and counts/dashboards/inbox remain unverified.


### 33. A stopped broadcast worker left connected viewers falsely marked Live (two-context failure reproduced)

The degraded-mode fallback initially watched only Echo transport/channel state. During a controlled two-context test, the dedicated `supervisor-broadcasts` in `ogami-horizon` was paused while Reverb stayed reachable and the viewer had acknowledged the machine list/detail/dashboard private channels. A System Admin's machine status PATCH returned 200, but the Production Manager's connected list retained Idle and the stale `running` transition option for the full 15-second assertion window; detail and dashboard also stayed stale while the toolbar said Live. Playwright failed (expected zero `running` options, received one); trace/screenshots are under `/tmp/ogami-machine-worker-outage-20261030`.

The API now exposes no-store `GET /api/v1/realtime/health`, returning only a healthy/degraded bit based on Redis `high`/`broadcasts` queue depth plus failed or overdue pending `event_outbox` rows (a two-second enqueue grace); Redis or database inspection errors fail closed. It accepts internal SPA, supplier-portal, and customer-portal session guards. The provider uses same-origin fetch rather than the internal Axios client, avoiding portal-to-ERP login redirects when auth expires. It checks delivery independently from the socket, treats backlog/error as Reconnecting, immediately catches active data up, continues visibility-aware exponential fallback, and catches up on focus. Recovery restores Live without resetting healthy Echo subscriptions. Backend service/endpoint tests pass (9 warning-marked tests / 38 assertions), provider tests pass 6/6, and SPA/E2E TypeScript checks pass.

The healthy-worker two-context E2E passed again after `make dev-refresh-php` restarted the API, Horizon, mail worker, and Reverb: across idle → offline → idle, each viewer list/detail/dashboard rendered within 610–757 ms of the actor API response, with one event and one authorized refetch per surface per transition. The paused-worker E2E passed after the outbox-health change (1 scenario / 23.2 s): health reported degraded, no machine refresh event arrived, and viewer list/detail/actions/dashboard refreshed after each page was brought forward and its focus handler invoked, at 729–2,398 ms from the actor response. These are single-scenario samples, not p95 or exact commit-to-UI measurements; the degraded timing includes explicit focus triggers and does not establish a hidden-tab timer SLO.

The new health check also surfaced two historical failed `PayrollPeriodChanged` outbox rows. The running Horizon process had stale loaded codec code and rejected both as unregistered; after a full worker restart, the explicitly refresh-only rows were requeued with `outbox:dispatch --retry-failed`, then both published. The subsequent status audit showed 2,001 published rows, zero failed rows, and empty `high`/`broadcasts` queues. This confirms one local stale-worker/dead-letter recovery and the usefulness of monitoring durable failures; it is not evidence about every deployment. The machine transition matrix records degraded focus catch-up separately; linked records, badges, and inbox remain unverified.


### 34. The approval inbox had no event-driven query invalidation (two-role failure reproduced; selective fix verified)

The Approvals board at `spa/src/pages/approvals/index.tsx` had no realtime subscription and instead polled every 30 seconds. During the baseline two-context Playwright run, Purchasing submitted a ₱60,000 PR; the VP board initially showed it under Awaiting others. Finance approved step 1 while the VP page stayed open. The VP's WebSocket received one `BadgesChanged` refresh frame, but the board made no second `/approvals/board` request during the next 3 seconds and continued showing the old column. A separate request in that same VP session returned the fresh permission-scoped API result with the PR at step 2 in `my_action`. This rules out a stale API/cache response and identifies the missing active-query consumer as the failure point. Baseline trace: `/tmp/ogami-approval-board-repro-20261030-d`.

`BadgesChanged` was later consolidated onto a shared private `badges` channel to remove duplicate delivery. Delta 698 replaces that channel with per-user private `badge-user.{userHashId}` channels; the current `useRealtimeApprovalsBoard` joins only the authenticated viewer's channel, while the central `badges_changed` invalidation map refreshes active `['approvals']` queries. `approvals.visible` remains an unused authorization callback for compatibility. The fixed 30-second board poll was removed; provider degraded-mode catch-up is the fallback. The earlier retest used the same roles, forced the Finance actor's WebSocket closed, received the then-shared refresh signal in the VP page, and moved the card into My action 879 ms after the actor API response. Trace: `/tmp/ogami-approval-board-final-20261030`.

On 2026-11-02, `spa/e2e-real/approval-board-realtime.spec.ts` first failed before approving because its subscription probe still expected `private-approvals.visible`. Source review showed the board now subscribes to `private-badges`, so this was a stale test assertion, not a new application delivery failure. After correcting the probe, the two-context scenario passed twice: the private badge subscription succeeded, Finance approved with its socket forcibly closed, one `BadgesChanged` frame reached the VP, the board made an authorized refetch, and the card moved to step 2 of My action. In the measured run, the actor's action disappeared 157 ms after the API response; the VP board reflected the new card within 740 ms; a fresh authorized API response reported `step_order=2`. This is one measured API-response-to-UI sample, not commit-to-UI p95. Other approval types, regular PR lists, numeric badges, dashboards, linked records and degraded-worker fallback remain unverified.

### 35. Custom approval queues were missing badge/inbox invalidation observers (Payroll Adjustment source regression reproduced)

`ApprovalBoardService` aggregates custom queues whose records do not use `approval_records`. The registered generic `BadgeInvalidationObserver` source list omitted several of these models, including `PayrollAdjustment`; creating or deciding such a record therefore did not stage the `BadgesChanged` outbox signal consumed by the approval board. A red-first backend regression observed no new pending outbox row on PayrollAdjustment create, then passed after registering the observer and verified a second signal on decision. The observer registrations now also cover `StockAdjustment`, `StockCountItem`, `ItemCostReview`, `EmployeeLoan`, and `SupplierItemListing`; those model paths have registration/source coverage but not individual mutation E2E evidence.

A separate live three-context scenario then tested a custom stock-adjustment approval: Warehouse created a pending record, System Administrator watched the board, and Finance approved with its WebSocket closed. The actor's successful API response removed the pending row; the board received two `BadgesChanged` frames and refetched the permission-scoped board, removing the card 836 ms after the actor response. Evidence is `/tmp/ogami-custom-approval-20261030` and test `spa/e2e-real/approval-board-custom-stock-adjustment.spec.ts`. This covers only stock-adjustment inbox refresh; other custom queues, user-specific badge correctness, and unrelated side effects remain unverified. The duplicate frames indicate invalidation fan-out volume should be measured during broader rollout.

### 36. Payroll Adjustment writes had badge signals but no record/list refresh event (red-first outbox test reproduced the missing producer)

Before this slice, `PayrollAdjustment` was registered only with `BadgeInvalidationObserver`. That produced a generic badge/inbox refresh, but there was no dedicated record/list event, private record channel authorization, SPA event mapping, or list/detail subscription. A red-first `PayrollAdjustmentRealtimeTest` attempted create plus pending initialization and observed zero `PayrollAdjustmentChanged` rows in `event_outbox`; the same test passes after registering a dedicated observer and outbox service. A later decision update also stages a refresh. This reproduces the missing producer through a backend regression test; no pre-fix browser stale screen was captured.

`PayrollAdjustmentChanged` now uses the durable after-commit `broadcasts` lane with private `payroll.adjustment.{hashId}` and `entity.payroll_adjustment.visible` channels. Both require `modules.payroll` and `payroll.adjustments.view`, with record existence checked for detail subscriptions. The minimal payload contains refresh metadata and only the related payroll-period hash ID. The shared record hook consumes that relation and invalidates the parent period queries; list and detail pages use the shared hooks. Actor approval/rejection responses reconcile the current list row before the follow-up invalidation.

The rejection path was then exercised through the real SPA in three independent browser contexts. With the actor's socket forcibly closed, the successful reject response updated the actor's row and available action; the HR detail viewer refetched the new status, and the separate board viewer removed the card after an authorized refetch (828 ms after the actor response). This corroborates the new event path for rejection on these observed surfaces. It does not establish p95 latency, pre-fix browser reproduction, list propagation to another role, or aggregate freshness.

One live three-context test (HR maker/detail viewer, separate System Administrator actor and board viewer) passed against `ogamiph.dev`. HR created a pending adjustment; the board displayed its actionable card. System Admin approved with its socket forcibly closed; its list row immediately rendered Approved and removed Approve from the API response, HR's open detail refetched Approved over the private record channel, and the other board context removed the card 936 ms after the actor response. This is one response-to-render sample, not commit-to-UI p95. No pre-fix two-browser incident reproduction was captured, and payroll-period detail, another role's adjustment list, badge totals, dashboards, rejection and other writers remain unverified. The current seeded local roles also have no non-admin adjustment approver; that separate workflow/permission issue is recorded in `FINDINGS.md`.


### 37. Customer invoice portal lacked an authorized invoice refresh path, and balance-only writes could be silent

Before this slice, the customer portal had no private invoice channel/subscription. Red-first service regressions also showed that several invoice writes did not stage a refresh: create/update/prebill approval/cancel, customer payment submission, finance rejection, and credit application where the invoice status stayed `partial`. This is evidence from focused backend tests plus source tracing; the initial missing-refresh customer browser baseline was not captured. The fix stages refreshes in those service paths and conditionally targets `customer.invoice.{customerHashId}` for invoice events with a customer account. The channel is private and authorized by the customer portal guard, the `modules.b2b_portals` flag, active/unlocked account state, and exact active customer-account match. The wire serializer remains content-free (`refresh` and event ID).

The after-fix headless two-context scenario verified customer detail and list updates after finance applied a credit: channel auth 200, authorized refetches, Paid / $0.00, no Submit payment action, no reload. The request-start-to-viewer-update observation was 2,280 ms in the latest pass; one sample includes request/server time and does not establish p95. This selectively verifies one accounting transition and two portal surfaces. Other mutations, list/detail scopes, action semantics, dashboards, badges and inbox remain unverified. The event also does not yet meet the architecture's full standardized envelope because this legacy `ChainStepAdvanced` serialization omits entity/version/actor/related references.

### 38. B2B portal notification bells lacked a private refresh path

`NotificationService::sendPortalInApp()` inserted customer/supplier notices without a broadcast, and `PortalNotificationController` read/delete actions had no cross-tab signal. `NotificationBell` therefore retained a direct 60-second poll for portals even though healthy internal bells were event-driven. A red-first focused regression failed with zero portal refresh events for two newly inserted customer/supplier notices. The service now emits one minimal event per inserted recipient after commit (dedupe conflicts emit none); read, mark-all-read, delete, and clear-read mutations emit the same per-user refresh signal. Private `portal.customer.user.{userHashId}` and `portal.supplier.user.{userHashId}` channels authorize only the matching active portal session and active parent account. The portal bell refetches its own session-scoped API query, and its timer was removed so the shared provider owns reconnect/focus/degraded catch-up.

Focused evidence: the API producer/channel suite passes 4 tests / 24 assertions, and the existing portal notifications suite passes 7 tests / 26 assertions; warnings are the existing test-container `.env` warning. The focused SPA hook and bell tests pass 8/8; targeted ESLint and PHP Pint pass. No two-browser portal WebSocket scenario was run, so portal actor/other-tab render latency and matrix cells remain unverified.

### 39. Incoming-QC handoff updates bypassed the GRN refresh observer

`GrnService::markIncomingQcHandoffGenerated()`, `markIncomingQcHandoffNotRequired()`, `markIncomingQcHandoffManual()`, and `markIncomingQcHandoffPending()` used query-builder updates. These fields appear in `GoodsReceiptNoteResource` and the open GRN detail/list UI, but the writes bypassed `GoodsReceiptNoteRealtimeObserver`, which previously watched only `qc_inspection_id`. A focused producer regression failed with zero outbox rows for a handoff status/message change. The four methods now save the model through Eloquent and the observer emits the existing content-free GRN refresh when handoff status, message, timestamp, or inspection link changes. The focused GRN feature file passes 2 warning-marked tests / 10 assertions. This verifies producer behavior only; no browser trace or aggregate-cache change is claimed.

### 40. Internal notification read/delete mutations left other tabs stale

Internal notification creation emitted `UserNotificationCreated`, but `UserNotificationService::markRead()`, `markAllRead()`, `delete()`, and `deleteAllRead()` changed rows through direct table queries without signaling the user's other open tabs. Those tabs retained stale unread counts and rows until focus/reconnect or a later event. A red-first feature test observed no event after a committed mark-read. The four service paths now emit a minimal `UserNotificationChanged` event after commit only when rows changed, on the existing private `user.{hashId}` channel authorized by `notifications.view`; event payloads contain only event ID, action, optional notification ID, and timestamp. The SPA subscribes through the shared hook and invalidates only the current user's notification query family; creation toast behavior remains on `UserNotificationCreated`. Focused producer tests pass 3 warning-marked tests / 10 assertions, broadcast contract tests pass 28 warning-marked tests / 454 assertions, and focused SPA notification tests pass 9/9. No two-browser notification UI run was performed, so matrix render-latency cells remain unverified.

### 41. Customer portal order and delivery detail relied on 30-second polling

The customer order and delivery detail pages (`spa/src/pages/portal/customer/orders/detail.tsx` and `spa/src/pages/portal/customer/deliveries/detail.tsx`) each declared a 30-second interval for their record and related chain/receipt-group queries. The backend already staged `ChainStepAdvanced` for sales-order and delivery changes through `api/app/Common/Services/ChainBroadcaster.php`, but `api/app/Common/Events/ChainStepAdvanced.php` had no customer-account target, and the customer portal had no subscription to those events. Consequently an open customer portal detail could remain stale between timer ticks; delivery to the portal could also take roughly 30 seconds even when the existing ERP chain event had been published. A red-first `ChainBroadcasterTest` case failed before this change because the generated order/delivery signal had no customer account target. Source tracing also found no portal order/delivery realtime hook.

The event now targets `customer.order.{customerHashId}` only for sales orders and deliveries with a customer account. `api/routes/channels.php` requires the B2B portal module, an active/unlocked/password-ready customer portal user, and exact match with an active customer row. `spa/src/layouts/CustomerPortalLayout.tsx` mounts `spa/src/hooks/useCustomerOrderRealtime.ts`, Echo routes auth through the customer portal endpoint, and the hook invalidates portal order, delivery, receipt-group and chain query prefixes through `spa/src/lib/realtime/invalidationMap.ts`. All four page timers were removed. The payload contains only a refresh marker, event UUID, entity type and hashed entity ID; each page refetches from its existing customer-scoped API.

Focused evidence after the fix: `ChainBroadcasterTest` 7 warning-marked tests / 27 assertions; customer order channel authorization 1 warning-marked test / 4 assertions; `BroadcastEventContractTest` 28 warning-marked tests / 454 assertions; customer order hook and invalidation-map SPA tests 36/36. The first SPA run exposed a test assertion mismatch (full query key versus intentional prefix); the corrected focused run passed. This issue was established by code tracing and a failing producer regression, not by a two-customer browser reproduction. No UI latency, cross-customer isolation browser scenario, or multi-user matrix PASS is claimed.

### 42. Internal O2C detail pages duplicated a healthy realtime refresh path with fixed polling

`spa/src/pages/crm/sales-orders/detail.tsx` polled its active detail and chain queries every 10 seconds, and `spa/src/pages/accounting/invoices/detail.tsx` polled an active invoice every 30 seconds. Both already call `useChainProgress`, which subscribes to their private `chain.refresh` channel, invalidates the query keys on signal, and runs visibility-aware exponential polling only while the shared connection state is degraded. The page intervals therefore generated redundant API requests while realtime was healthy and did not match the desired event-driven freshness policy. New page tests inspected the active TanStack Query options and failed before the change because all three interval callbacks were still configured.

Removed those three page-owned intervals. The authorized event/refetch path and degraded fallback remain in `spa/src/hooks/useChainProgress.tsx` and `spa/src/hooks/useRealtimeRecord.ts`; no query cache is disabled. Focused Sales Order, Invoice and chain-hook tests pass 17/17. This is source-backed redundancy evidence, not a reproduction of a stale UI symptom. No multi-user browser latency or API-load comparison was collected; the current 756-row matrix still requires route-specific runtime evidence.

### 43. Production output changes missed the open work-order list channel

Work-order list rows show `quantity_produced`, but `WorkOrderOutputRecorded` originally broadcast only to `production.wo.{hashId}` and `production.dashboard`. The open list subscribed to `entity.work_order.visible` using `useRealtimeList('work_order', 'visible')` and listened only for `.chain.refresh`; output recordings emitted `.output.recorded`, so the list missed those changes until its 60-second page timer. Red-first unit and hook tests failed because the event did not target the authorized entity list and the central hook did not listen for output events.

`WorkOrderOutputRecorded` now also targets `entity.work_order.visible` with the same `refresh=true` payload. The central `useRealtimeList` hook listens for `.output.recorded` on work-order lists, and the Production list's 60-second interval is removed. The dynamic entity-list callback requires the Production module and the same `production.view` plus `production.work_orders.view` permissions; data is refetched through the work-order API. Focused backend event test and list-channel authorization test each pass 1 warning-marked test / 2 assertions; `useRealtimeList.test.tsx` passes 28/28. No two-role browser delivery or latency sample was collected. Other Production paths and their current matrix cells remain unverified.

### 44. Notification inbox duplicated its layout-owned realtime subscription with a fixed timer

`spa/src/pages/notifications/index.tsx` configured a 30-second interval for portal inbox routes. Both customer and supplier portal layouts already mount the scoped NotificationBell subscription, and notification signals invalidate the same user-scoped query prefix used by the inbox. The shared RealtimeProvider also performs reconnect/focus catch-up and degraded polling. The page timer duplicated requests whenever private event delivery was healthy. Removed the page-owned interval and kept the authorized query/event path. This finding is supported by source tracing; the new query-option regression was not run, and no portal browser trace or latency sample is claimed. This does not prove every notification writer emits a signal.

### 45. Assigned shop-floor work-order views were outside the ERP work-order event channels

The self-service list/detail query keys are `my-work-orders` and related employee-scoped output/history families. They had 30-second fixed intervals but did not subscribe to events. Existing assignment/output/status/schedule events targeted channels whose callbacks require general ERP `production.work_orders.view`; the assigned-worker API instead authorizes an active linked employee and scopes each query to that employee's Production-department crew. Added the private `production.operator.{userHashId}` callback with exact-user, active-account, active-employee, active-Production-department and module checks. Assignment, output, lifecycle-status and schedule producers now include only the current/affected assigned account hashes in their private channels; the payload remains a refresh signal. The SPA hook invalidates only that authenticated user's self-service work-order, history, options, output and report query prefixes, and the two fixed intervals were removed.

This gap and the different API/channel scopes are confirmed by source tracing, not by a pre-fix browser reproduction. Schedule/status/assignment/output, Warehouse handover, equipment, and linked-QC signals have not been observed in a browser. The Machine/Mold model signal services stage a content-free assigned-worker refresh for readiness/display fields; `MoldService::syncCompatibility()` handles its pivot writer explicitly. Inspection root/result-author and linked NCR changes now also signal current assignees when they are tied to in-process work-order QC, which feeds the server's `can_record` result. Every signal refetches through the existing employee-scoped API. Other direct query-builder/pivot/child writers have not been exhaustively audited, and the self-service detail matrix remains unverified.

### 46. Assets had no producer, private channel, or SPA subscription for its mutation routes

The Phase 0 route inventory contains asset create/update/delete/restore, association, disposal request/approval/rejection/cancel, and monthly depreciation mutations. Source tracing found no Assets broadcast event or asset channel callback; `AssetService` performed its writes inside transactions, `restore` wrote directly in the controller, and the Assets SPA had no realtime hook or invalidation-map entry. The actor's forms invalidated some local queries, but other viewers had no event-driven refetch path. This was confirmed by source inspection only; the user requested tests stop, so no runtime two-context reproduction is claimed.

Added `AssetChanged` through the existing durable `OutboxService::recordCoalesced()` path, staged inside each mutation transaction and broadcast after commit on the dedicated `broadcasts` lane. Create, update, association, deletion, restore, disposal workflow transitions, depreciation writes, maintenance-driven status changes, and an approved department transfer now stage refresh signals; one depreciation run groups at most 100 asset IDs per signal to bound the outbox payload. The private record channel checks `modules.assets`, `assets.view`, hash validity and active-row existence; the list channel requires the same feature and permission. Wire payloads contain only event/type/action/version/actor and opaque ID(s), and clients refetch through their authorized API. Asset list/detail queries now use the shared invalidation hooks/map; the acting user's mutation responses are reconciled into the asset detail cache before related queries are invalidated. Asset cache source inspection found no backend `Cache::` entry; the SPA cache remains identity-cleared on user/role/permission/feature changes. All Assets matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`: no test, browser session, or latency measurement was run for this slice.

### 47. Journal-entry mutations had no cross-viewer change signal (source coverage gap; no runtime reproduction in this slice)

The Accounting mutation matrix lists create, update, archive, restore, post and reverse routes. `JournalEntryObserver` previously handled only statement/finance cache invalidation; there was no JournalEntry broadcaster, record/list channel, or SPA subscription for the Journal Entries list/detail. Source tracing found the routes call `JournalEntryService`, which creates, updates, transitions and soft-deletes JournalEntry Eloquent models inside transactions; the application source scan found no raw `journal_entries` table writer that bypasses model lifecycle events. This is a source-confirmed missing propagation path, not a two-user reproduction; the user's stop-testing instruction prevented dynamic verification.

`JournalEntryChanged` now stages one coalesced content-free outbox signal per entry/transaction from the existing observer, after cache-generation callbacks are registered and with the outbox broadcast dispatched after commit. Private record authorization mirrors the API's `accounting.journal.view` permission and `withTrashed` detail scope; the entity list channel uses the same feature/permission gate. SPA detail/list pages subscribe through the shared realtime hooks, which invalidate statement and finance/widget families; create/edit/post/reverse API responses seed actor detail cache where available. All six JournalEntry route rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED` for actor/viewer/list/action UI behavior. Edit forms remain unsubscribed to avoid replacing unsaved journal lines; safe conflict banners, available_actions, and two-user proof remain outstanding.

### 48. Shop-floor active-order views were not subscribed to work-order list signals

`spa/src/pages/factory/ActiveOrders.tsx` used a 30-second fixed refetch for the Production API's active work-order list; `spa/src/pages/factory/QcQuickCheck.tsx` used the same query key without either a timer or realtime hook. The server already emits `ChainStepAdvanced` to `entity.work_order.visible` for lifecycle status changes and `WorkOrderOutputRecorded` to that private channel for quantity progress. The shared list hook already handles both event names, but its central `work_order` invalidation mapping omitted the factory query key and neither floor page mounted the hook. This source trace explains delayed/stale active-order cards and QC selection data; it is not a browser reproduction.

Added the floor query key to the central work-order list invalidation map and mounted `useRealtimeList('work_order', 'visible')` on both floor screens. Removed the active-order page's 30-second interval; if the private channel/worker is degraded, the shared provider catches up active queries with visibility-aware backoff. The list channel authorization uses the same Production module and `production.work_orders.view` requirements as the list API; only a refresh signal crosses the channel, and every refetch goes through the API. Matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; the new frontend test assertion was not run, and no floor-tablet multi-user scenario or latency sample is claimed.

### 49. The central work-order detail invalidation key omitted its `detail` segment

The shared invalidation function builds a record query key as `detailPrefix + entityId`. The `work_order` definition used `['production', 'work-orders']`, but the Production detail and output-entry queries are keyed at `['production', 'work-orders', 'detail', id]`. Existing `useChainProgress` consumers supplied those exact detail keys as extra overrides, which masked the mismatch on the audited detail pages; a consumer relying on the central map alone would miss the record cache entry. Changed the canonical prefix to include `detail` and added a regression assertion. This mismatch is source-verified; no affected current page/browser failure was reproduced, and the assertion was not run under the stop-testing instruction.

### 50. Item master changes did not retire the inventory dashboard cache generation

`InventoryDashboardService::compute()` includes active-item names, active flags, reorder points and safety-stock thresholds in the cached low-stock summary. Its own `inv:dashboard:summary.version` was rotated by `StockMovementCompleted`, but not by item master writes; `BadgeInvalidationObserver` rotated the separate dashboard-widget/badge generation, which does not affect this cache namespace. A concurrent item edit could therefore emit a dashboard refresh while the authorized API refetch still returned the old inventory summary for up to its 30-second TTL. The scheduled safety-stock recompute also used `saveQuietly()`, bypassing the Item badge observer entirely.

Item writes now retire the Inventory summary generation in the earlier-registered `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver`, before `ItemRealtimeObserver` stages `ItemChanged`; the later badge observer only handles the shared badge generation and signal. The safety-stock batch explicitly rotates the Inventory generation, shared dashboard/badge generations, and emits one content-free badge/dashboard ping when the computed threshold value actually changes. The issue and fixes are source-traced; no tests, cache-hit timings or UI reproduction were collected under the stop-testing instruction. Other direct item writers remain subject to audit.

### 51. Leave request transitions had no record/list refresh path

Leave submission and approve-department/approve-HR/reject/cancel transitions are committed in `LeaveRequestService`, which stages recipient notifications but no refresh event for other screens. `LeaveRequest` previously had only the general `BadgeInvalidationObserver`; the leave list, self-service list, leave calendar, and record detail did not subscribe to a leave-request channel. The actor locally invalidated or reconciled after its API action, but another viewer's status, `can_approve` action, calendar, balances, attendance rows, and related dashboard data could remain stale. This is source-confirmed; no two-user reproduction is claimed in the current no-tests slice.

Added `LeaveRequestChanged` to the durable outbox via `LeaveRequestRealtimeObserver`, coalesced per request and transaction. The observer rotates badge/dashboard generations before queuing either refresh signal. Private detail authorization uses the same `leave.view` permission and `ApprovalSourceScope` employee/department/HR row scope as the API; the entity-list channel carries only a content-free refresh ping and each viewer refetches their own scoped endpoint. The central SPA map now covers leave lists/detail, employee leave panels, balances, calendars, attendance and dashboard/inbox families; the leave HR, self-service, calendar, and detail views subscribe through shared hooks. Route matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; no tests or browser sessions were run.

### 52. Leave catalog and balance writes were not refreshing other viewers

Source tracing found that LeaveType create/update/archive/restore had only actor-side list invalidation, while year-end processing, annual rollover, new-hire seeding, approval consumption and cancellation restoration could change EmployeeLeaveBalance rows without a dedicated viewer refresh. The HR dashboard/widget cache generation also did not rotate for balance changes. Added private content-free `LeaveTypeChanged` and `LeaveBalanceChanged` outbox signals. Bulk/year-end/reset/seed paths stage one balance signal per transaction, not one per row, and the dashboard generation rotates before the durable outbox callback can trigger a refetch. `AppLayout` owns the two permission-scoped list subscriptions and the central map invalidates active leave, self-service, HR employee and dashboard queries. This is source-wired only: no mutation, two-user, typecheck or UI behavior was run in this no-tests slice; all matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 53. Payroll statutory cache serialized Eloquent models

`GovernmentContributionTableService` cached complete `Collection<GovernmentContributionTable>` objects for active and effective-dated statutory brackets. Those entries included Eloquent serialization state and used the same generation key format as plain arrays would, so a data-format change could not safely reuse the existing keys. The service now caches only raw attribute arrays under a `rows-v2` key namespace and hydrates the same model-collection return contract for callers. The 300-second TTL bounds leftover v1 entries; generation rotation remains after commit. The existing stale-fill fixture was updated to the v2 key format but, per the user's instruction, neither it nor the payroll suite was run. No cache-size, hit-rate or response-latency measurements were collected.

### 54. Government contribution table writes had cache invalidation but no viewer refresh

The route inventory contains five admin table mutations (update, activate, deactivate, delete, restore) plus CSV import. Source tracing found that these paths rotated the backend bracket-cache generation, but neither the mutation services nor the importer recorded an outbox event; the admin SPA queried `['gov-tables', agency]` without subscribing to a private change channel. Therefore an administrator already viewing the list could retain old bracket values and active-state buttons after another administrator's write. This is source-confirmed, not reproduced in two browser sessions.

`GovernmentContributionTableChanged` is now staged through the durable, transaction-coalesced outbox from both mutation services. Cache generation rotation is registered before outbox dispatch, so a viewer's authorized refetch cannot read the previous generation. The private `entity.government_table.visible` channel checks the same `admin.gov_tables.manage` permission as the API and carries only refresh metadata, action, version and timestamp; bracket values and agency are excluded. The central SPA invalidation map covers the admin list, which subscribes while mounted, and actor responses update the list cache directly. Focused backend and invalidation regression tests were added as source but not run. All six mutation rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED` for actor/viewer/list/button UI behavior; no two-user evidence or latency measurement is claimed.


### 55. De minimis ledger mutations had no cross-viewer list refresh

The mutation inventory lists create, archive and restore for `/api/v1/de-minimis`. `DeMinimisService::record()` creates one or two `DeMinimisBenefit` Eloquent rows, `delete()` soft-deletes the selected row (and may bulk-soft-delete its taxable companion), and the controller restores a row directly. `DeMinimisBenefitRealtimeObserver` now stages one coalesced `DeMinimisBenefitChanged` outbox refresh per transaction for create/update/delete/restore. The private list channel checks the Payroll feature and the same `payroll.adjustments.create` permission as the list API. The payload excludes all employee and compensation details; the modal routes the event to the central query invalidation map and refetches through the viewer's authorized API. The actor continues to invalidate from the mutation response. Backend, channel, broadcast-contract and frontend regression test source was added but not run. The three route-matrix UI rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; no latency, cache-hit or two-user claim is made.


### 56. Payroll anomaly review changes did not refresh the period gate or other reviewers

`PayrollAnomalyController::resolve()` changes `PayrollAnomalyFlag` inside `PayrollAnomalyService::resolve()`, and `detect()` can create flags or bulk-delete orphaned flags during the payroll compute transaction. Before this slice, no anomaly event or SPA subscription existed. The open review panel invalidated only the acting user's `['payroll', periodId, 'anomalies']` query, leaving another reviewer’s list and the open payroll-period detail cache stale even though finalization rejects while unresolved flags exist. This is source-confirmed, not a two-user reproduction.

`PayrollAnomalyService` now stages one coalesced `PayrollAnomalyChanged` outbox signal per changed period transaction after resolve or when detection adds/removes flags. The private channel requires the Payroll feature plus `payroll.anomalies.review`.
