DSS anomaly-summary recommendations are materialized detector output and remain on their existing detector run cadence; this slice does not regenerate them. The acting panel immediately removes the resolved row from its cache using the API response, then reconciles from the API. Regression-test source was updated but not run. Matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; no latency or cache-hit measurement is claimed.


### 57. Department reference writes refreshed only the acting HR tree

`DepartmentController` exposes create, update, archive and restore; before this slice, `DepartmentService` and the controller had no observer, outbox event or private list channel. The HR departments page invalidated its own `['hr', 'departments']` queries, but many open forms and active views held separate department tree/list query keys, with no event to invalidate them. This is a source-confirmed propagation gap; no cross-role reproduction was run.

Added `DepartmentChanged` through an Eloquent observer and durable, transaction-coalesced outbox. The private list channel requires the HR feature and either `hr.departments.view` or `hr.directory.view`, matching the list/tree API read gates. Only refresh metadata and an opaque department hash are broadcast; department name, code, manager and hierarchy remain API-only. `AppLayout` subscribes only for authorized HR/directory readers, and the central map invalidates department references plus known employee, position, attendance, calendar, asset, budgeting, approval and dashboard families. Actor CRUD retains its existing API-success invalidation. Test source and inventory records were added, not run; all four route UI rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 58. Position reference writes did not refresh other HR and recruitment screens

Position create/update/archive/restore mutations and the employee importer can change Position rows. Before this slice there was no Position observer/event/channel, and the position admin page invalidated only its own `['hr', 'positions']` list. Employee forms and job-posting forms use separate query keys, so viewers could retain stale position selectors and job-title references. This is a source-confirmed gap; no multi-user reproduction was run.

Added `PositionChanged` through a registered Eloquent observer and coalesced after-commit outbox service, covering CRUD and importer-created positions. Its private list channel requires `modules.hr` and `hr.positions.view`, and sends only opaque hash/action/version metadata. AppLayout subscribes for authorized readers; the central invalidation map covers HR positions, employee/department references, recruitment, and dashboard query families. Actor mutation callbacks remain API-driven. Regression-test source and route/catalog entries were added but not executed; all four matrix rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 59. Department and position archive/restore writes were not atomic with their realtime outbox signals

Source tracing found department and position `destroy` services and `restore` controllers invoked Eloquent soft-delete/restore without an enclosing database transaction. Their newly registered observers then created the durable outbox row in a separate transaction. A process failure between the model write and outbox insert could therefore leave committed state with no refresh signal; `OutboxService` can only guarantee atomicity when it joins the business transaction. Department and position creation/update already used transactions, and `MasterDataImportService::commit()` wraps employee and importer-created Position rows in one transaction.

Both services now lock and perform archive/restore operations inside `DB::transaction`; restore handlers delegate to those services. The observer consequently writes the outbox row in the same transaction as each soft-delete/restore and its dispatch callback runs after commit. This closes the source-confirmed failure window without changing business rules. No runtime failure was reproduced and no tests were run under the user's stop-testing instruction; the related matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 60. Several AP bill and payment transitions did not emit the refresh signal their screens already consume

`BillDetailPage` subscribes to `chain.bill.{hash}` through `useChainProgress`, and both it and the Bills list listen for the private `ChainStepAdvanced` refresh. The server already emits that signal when a draft is posted and a payment is posted/voided, but source tracing found no corresponding `ChainBroadcaster::broadcastFor()` call for bill creation, cancellation, pending payment submission, an intermediate payment approval, payment rejection, or supplier-invoice field changes. Credit-note application to a bill broadcast only when the bill's status enum changed, despite also changing balance and available actions. Actor callbacks invalidate their own queries, but other authorized viewers have no signal for these transitions, so statuses, payment rows, and buttons can remain stale.

This is a source-confirmed missing-emission gap against existing private channels and consumers; it was not reproduced in two browser contexts. The fix uses the existing minimal `chain.refresh` outbox path inside the owning transaction for those uncovered bill writes, including bill-linked credit-note and supplier-invoice changes. Tests and browser verification remain stopped at the user's direction; the UI matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 61. Credit-note status and balance changes had no realtime producer or page subscription

The Accounting API exposes credit-note create, finalize, apply and void operations. `CreditNoteService` writes those records in transactions, but its only `CreditNoteFinalized` event is consumed by an email listener and is not a broadcaster. The credit-note list and detail components had no realtime hook, record/list channel, or invalidation-map entry. Their actor callbacks invalidate queries locally; other Finance viewers can retain stale draft/finalized/applied/void status and still see actions that the latest server state no longer permits.

This is source-confirmed; no two-user browser reproduction was run. The fix adds a content-free, after-commit outbox refresh from the CreditNote model lifecycle, an API-permission-matched private record/list channel, and central list/detail/linked-query invalidation, including return-management details that embed a linked credit note. Tests and browser verification remain stopped under the user's instruction; matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 62. Chart-of-accounts writes refreshed Redis statements but not other open finance screens

`AccountStatementCacheObserver` rotates the statement and finance-dashboard cache generations after Account writes, but the chart-of-accounts page, account edit page, and finance forms use separate TanStack query families and had no realtime producer or subscription. `AccountService` creates, updates, activates, and deactivates Accounts inside transactions; before this slice, none of those writes emitted a private refresh signal. This is a source-confirmed propagation gap, not a two-user reproduction.

Added `AccountChanged` through a registered model observer and transaction-coalesced outbox, plus private record and list channels requiring the same `modules.accounting` and `accounting.coa.view` boundary as the read APIs. AppLayout subscribes for authorized viewers, and the central invalidation map refreshes chart lists, account selectors, linked finance views and statements. The editor refetch path preserves typed fields, shows a review banner on concurrent changes, and blocks a stale save until the user loads the latest record. Payloads contain only an opaque account hash and refresh metadata. No test/browser scenario was run under the user's instruction; the four route UI rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 63. Budget-transfer decisions left other approvers' actions and budget rows stale

`BudgetTransferService::request()`, `approve()` and `reject()` mutate `BudgetTransfer` in database transactions. The registered `BadgeInvalidationObserver` refreshed badge counts, but there was no budget-transfer broadcaster, record channel or SPA subscription; both list and detail pages invalidated only after the acting user's mutation. Other approvers could therefore retain a pending row and stale `can_approve` action, while budget views could retain the pre-transfer line totals. This is source-confirmed; no two-user reproduction was run.

Added `BudgetTransferChanged` through a registered model observer and transaction-coalesced outbox. Detail-channel authorization mirrors the API's `budgeting.view` or currently eligible-approver rule. The broad private entity channel requires the Budgeting feature and `budgeting.view` or `budgeting.approve`; its payload deliberately omits transfer ID, amount, reason and parties. AppLayout owns the list subscription, the central invalidation map refreshes transfer/budget/badge/approval families, and approve/reject responses reconcile the acting user's screen immediately. No tests or browser scenarios were run under the user's instruction; the three UI matrix rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 64. Budget writes and actuals rebuilds had no shared refresh path

`BudgetService` creates, updates, submits, approves, rejects, closes and deletes budgets inside database transactions, but `Budget` had only the badge observer registered. No private budget detail/list event or SPA subscription existed. The Budget detail page discarded successful action response bodies and invalidated only its own detail query, so the actor waited for a follow-up fetch and other viewers could retain old status/actions for the shared 30-second query stale window. `FiscalYearService` also changed fiscal-year records without refreshing budget selectors or summaries. Finally, `BudgetConsumptionService::refreshHeadersForFiscalYear()` used `DB::table('budgets')->update()`, bypassing model observers during the actuals-sync job. The budget-vs-actual screen additionally polled sync status every five seconds regardless of socket health.

Source evidence: `api/app/Modules/Accounting/Services/BudgetService.php` contains the transactional mutations; `api/app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php` registered `BadgeInvalidationObserver` for `Budget` but no realtime observer; `api/app/Modules/Accounting/Services/BudgetConsumptionService.php` wrote headers through the query builder; `api/app/Modules/Accounting/Services/FiscalYearService.php` performed the fiscal-year writes; and `spa/src/pages/budgeting/detail.tsx`, `create.tsx`, and `budget-vs-actual.tsx` had no Budget event subscriptions or central refresh path. This is a source-confirmed gap. No two-user browser reproduction was run in this slice, so UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

Added `BudgetChanged` through registered Budget and FiscalYear observers and the durable after-commit outbox. Authorized viewers receive content-free private record/list signals; the broad list payload contains no budget ID or financial values, and detail access reuses the API's current `budgeting.view` / eligible-approver check. The actuals header refresh now saves Eloquent models in one transaction so the same observer stages the per-budget refresh; fiscal-year status changes send one record refresh per affected budget so open details recompute available actions, while fiscal-year creation and sync lifecycle changes send list/report invalidations. The frontend central map refreshes budget detail, list, fiscal-year, overview, budget-vs-actual, sync-status, transfer, dashboard, badge and approval query families. Mutation responses now seed the actor's detail cache, detail actions render from server-provided `available_actions`, and stale draft edits send `If-Match` and receive 409 when the loaded version changed. The editor preserves typed fields, shows a review banner and blocks saving until the user loads the current version. The always-on five-second sync polling was removed; the shared provider owns degraded-mode catch-up. No tests, typecheck, build, or browser scenario were run under the stop-testing instruction; the eleven Budget/FiscalYear matrix rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 65. Production work-order details duplicated the shared freshness fallback

`spa/src/pages/production/work-orders/detail.tsx` already mounts `useChainProgress('work_order', ...)`, which subscribes the active record and linked detail queries through the shared realtime layer. Its detail query also had a fixed 60-second refetch interval. The root `RealtimeProvider` already catches active queries up on socket reconnect, focus, and visibility, and runs visibility-aware backoff only while delivery is degraded. The page timer therefore caused extra detail GETs even when realtime was healthy and duplicated degraded catch-up. This is source-confirmed; no request-count or two-user scenario was run.

Removed the page-owned interval. The record event remains the healthy-path refresh, while the shared provider remains the degraded fallback. The work-order cache registry now records this ownership. Runtime completeness of all work-order writers and viewer UI freshness remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 66. Holiday writes only invalidated the acting user's list and left generation keys orphaned

`HolidayService::create()`, `update()`, `delete()` and `restore()` changed holidays and recomputed mutable attendance rows in transactions. The page invalidated `['attendance', 'holidays']` only for the actor; it did not subscribe to a private change signal, so other holiday managers could keep an old year/list and active Calendar or HR dashboard queries had no holiday-specific invalidation. The cache used a global generation so stale values were not selected after a write, but its write path forgot the legacy `holidays:{year}` key rather than the active `holidays:{year}:{generation}` key; old versioned values therefore remained until their one-day TTL. This is source-confirmed, not reproduced in two browser contexts.

Registered `HolidayRealtimeObserver` now stages `HolidayChanged` through the durable after-commit outbox for create/update/archive/restore. The private content-free entity channel permits Attendance readers/managers, `calendar.view`, `dashboard.hr.view`, and linked employees whose authenticated dashboard API exposes their own next-holiday panel. The event carries no actor ID or holiday/attendance data. AppLayout subscribes once for these API audiences, and the central invalidation map refreshes holiday lists, calendar, attendance, leave-calendar and HR/self-service dashboard queries through each user's API. Holiday snapshots use a bounded tag; the observer registers tag removal and generation rotation before outbox dispatch so an event-triggered read uses the new generation. No tests or browser scenario ran under the stop-testing instruction; all four route UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

`api/app/Modules/Production/Services/LateProductionReportService.php` methods `propose()`, `approve()` and `reject()` mutate `LateProductionReport` records through Eloquent, but `api/app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php` had no observer registration for that model and `api/app/Modules/Production/Models/LateProductionReport.php` had no realtime observer. `spa/src/components/production/LateProductionReports.tsx` invalidated the submitter's report list after an API success, while the Production review queue also only invalidated its local list after approve/reject. Neither the employee's other tab nor another approver's open review queue subscribed to these transitions. The central work-order invalidation map also omitted `['late-production-reports']`, despite the review queue living on work-order detail. This is source-confirmed; no two-user reproduction was run because testing is paused.

Registered `LateProductionReportRealtimeObserver` now stages one transaction-coalesced `LateProductionReportChanged` through the durable outbox for create and review updates. The private entity channel requires the Production module plus both permissions required by the review endpoints; work-order detail/list and active operator channels retain their own authorization checks. The event payload is only `refresh` and an event UUID so report details, identifiers, actors, status, quantities and evidence never cross audiences with different API row scopes. The central invalidation map connects the review queue and linked work-order queries; operator tabs invalidate only their current user's assigned-report keys. Submitter/reviewer screens reconcile the API mutation resource immediately. Route matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; tests and browser checks were not run under the stop-testing instruction.


### Ruled-out candidate — Invoice payment submission lacks a cross-role producer

Source review does not support this candidate. `InvoicePaymentSubmissionService::submit()` and `reject()` stage `ChainStepAdvanced` through `ChainBroadcaster::broadcastFor()` inside their owning transaction. `verify()` calls `InvoiceService::recordCollection()` in the same outer transaction; that method stages the invoice refresh after updating invoice balance/status, then the submission status update commits with it. The event is after-commit and publishes to the invoice record/entity channels and, for customer invoices, the matching private customer-invoice channel. Finance invoice pages consume the chain signal; `CustomerPortalLayout` consumes the scoped customer channel and the invoice invalidation family includes portal invoice queries.

This rules out a missing producer/channel/subscriber for submit, verify and reject by source inspection only. No test or two-user browser trace was run, so delivery, action-button recomputation, actor behavior and UI latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. This path is recorded as covered in the source inventory, not as a runtime pass.


### 70. Role and user access changes did not refresh other sessions' in-memory permissions

`RoleService::syncPermissions()` already invalidated the shared `auth:role_perms:{roleId}` Redis entry after commit, but it emitted no signal to active members of that role. `UserAdminService::changeRole()` and `bulkChangeRole()` also cleared permission caches after commit without telling affected browsers to reload `/me`. Separately, `/auth/user` includes the linked employee department, which scopes several API queries, but the auth store did not compare employee/department scope and employee transfers did not notify the linked user. `authStore.applyUser()` cleared the full TanStack QueryClient when a refreshed identity's user, role, permissions or features changed, but without those signals/scope comparisons the old rows and action buttons could remain in an already-open session.

Added an after-commit `RolePermissionsChanged` private role signal, `UserAccessChanged` signals for single/bulk role assignment, account activation/deactivation, and linked employee department changes. User role and permission changes also notify delegates whose active approval scope depends on the changed delegator. User permission override set/remove/restore likewise refreshes those delegates. Channel authorization is exact: the role channel only admits current members of the decoded role; each user-access channel admits only its matching authenticated user. The shared `UserAccessRefreshService` deduplicates recipient IDs and caps fan-out at 50 channels per event; broadcasts carry only `refresh` and an event UUID. `usePermissionSync` reloads the current viewer's `/me` representation, and auth-store/realtime identity comparisons include linked employee and department scope so the QueryClient is cleared and subscriptions rebind on a scope change. Redis role-permission invalidation remains ordered before outbox dispatch. These are source-wired paths; no role/department change reproduction, test, browser trace or auth-update latency measurement was run, so access UI freshness and broadcast authorization remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

The same review found a race in the shared `auth:role_perms:{roleId}` cache: an in-flight read could fetch pre-commit permissions, finish after the `afterCommit` forget, and refill the same stable key. Added `roles.permissions_version`; permission sync and the role-permission seeder increment it in the transaction with the pivot update, and readers use `auth:role_perms:g{generation}:r{roleId}:v{permissions_version}`. Late reads can only populate a retired version key, and restore/completed-migration hooks rotate the shared namespace. The legacy stable key is still forgotten for older workers and expires by its existing five-minute TTL. The additive migration was applied to the running dev database; no concurrency/cache regression was run.


### 71. Approval delegation changes left delegated actions stale in open sessions

`UserResource` computes `delegated_approvals` from current delegation dates and the delegator's current role/permissions. The SPA's `usePermission()` renders approval controls from that field, but `ApprovalDelegationService::create()` and `revoke()` previously changed it without notifying the delegate; `authStore.applyUser()` also did not compare delegated approvals when deciding to clear query data. Effective delegations can also begin/end at midnight without a model write event.

Registered `ApprovalDelegationAccessRealtimeObserver` now stages `UserAccessChanged` for the exact delegate on create, access-relevant update, and delete. Role, active-account, and permission-override changes also notify delegates whose effective approvals depend on the delegator. `UserResource` now reports the next known access-scope boundary (own override expiry, incoming delegation start/end, or a current delegator's custom-approval override expiry); `usePermissionSync` schedules one `/me` refresh at that time and still catches up on focus/visibility return. `applyUser()` and the realtime identity key include `delegated_approvals`, clearing old query results and rebinding subscriptions when `/me` changes. Account deactivation signals the target's existing private channel; if `/me` returns 401/419, the auth store clears that session's query/PWA state. Events carry only the private refresh signal. The delta 362 two-context scenario did not subscribe as the delegate or verify effective permission/action refresh at the delegate, so delegate-channel authorization, scope-boundary behavior, and delegate-side UI remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; manager-list evidence is recorded under root cause #221.


### 72. Concurrent auth refreshes could apply an older permission snapshot after a newer one

`usePermissionSync` requests `/auth/user` on permission/delegation events, channel acknowledgement or errors, a known access boundary, and focus/visibility return. The old `authStore.refresh()` allowed all of those requests to run independently and each response called `applyUser()` unconditionally. A request started before a role/access change could therefore resolve last and replace the newer permission/delegation snapshot, retaining stale query data and action visibility. Bootstrap and login responses had the same stale-session response race. This is confirmed by source control flow; no delayed-response runtime trace or test was run under the user's instruction to stop testing.

`authStore` now tracks session generations, ignores obsolete bootstrap/login/refresh responses, shares an in-flight refresh and schedules one follow-up read when a newer refresh request arrives. Sign-in/sign-out API mutations are serialized so an in-flight logout cannot complete after a newer sign-in request and silently revoke its session. Applying a new auth snapshot clears QueryClient data if identity, role, employee/department scope, active state, permissions, delegated approvals, or features changed. Source inspection then found logout cleared auth/query/local preferences only in `finally`; a slow logout left the old user's data visible until the request completed, and delayed cleanup could erase state after a newer login. Logout now clears old-user memory and local preferences synchronously before its request and performs no identity-changing cleanup after the request; service-worker cache cleanup is app-shell-only and does not delay session clearing. Static review only; no SPA tests, typecheck, browser trace, or timing measurement was run. Auth request ordering remains `NOT_VERIFIED` at runtime.


### 73. A catch-up trigger during an active refetch could be dropped

`RealtimeProvider.catchUp()` returned the current `invalidateQueries()` promise whenever a catch-up was already running, without remembering the second trigger. If focus, reconnect, or a delivery-health transition arrived after an active API read started, the first read could complete with pre-commit data and the later trigger would be discarded. On a healthy socket there is no degraded polling pass to guarantee another read. This is confirmed from the provider's promise control flow; no race was reproduced in a browser.

Catch-up now records one pending follow-up while a pass is active and drains it before releasing the shared promise. Bursts coalesce to one extra invalidation cycle, and a caller arriving after completion starts a new cycle. API refetches remain authorized per active query. No tests, typecheck, or browser verification were run; trigger ordering and request-count impact remain runtime-unverified.


### 74. Logout and user switching cleared only the current tab's in-memory cache

The SPA's TanStack Query cache is tab-local, while the authenticated session cookie is shared across same-origin tabs. Internal `authStore.logout()` cleared the calling tab's QueryClient and preferences only; another open tab could retain API data and controls from the previous user after that shared session changed. A successful sign-in likewise did not force sibling tabs to discard data tied to the prior identity. This is source-confirmed from the one-tab store lifecycle; no multi-tab browser reproduction was run.

Auth state changes now use the shared scoped session-sync path described in root cause 75. It sends only a realm (`internal`, `customer`, `supplier`, or `all`), lifecycle stage and random nonce; the receiving guard refetches its own API identity. Delta 670 verifies internal logout propagation across two same-origin tabs: both reach sign-in, the peer profile control disappears, and its API identity request returns 401 after the actor logout returns 204. Different-user login, cross-guard propagation, direct inspection of in-memory QueryClient values, and ordering under a delayed API response remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 75. The actual sign-in and portal sign-out paths bypassed the first cross-tab signal

Inspection after adding internal auth-store messages found that `LoginPage` calls `authApi.signIn()` and applies internal users with `applyUser()` directly; it does not call `authStore.login()`. Supplier and customer portal layouts call their own logout APIs and clear only the current QueryClient. Thus signals added only to `authStore.login/logout` did not cover the actual login UI or either portal logout path. In addition, a generic signal without a guard scope could incorrectly clear an unrelated internal or portal session. This is confirmed by tracing the application entrypoints; no multi-tab reproduction was run.

Added a shared `authSessionSync` transport with strict realm/stage validation, nonce deduplication, same-origin BroadcastChannel and transient storage fallback, plus one serialized session-mutation queue. The real sign-in page announces an unknown/known realm before its request and the server-selected realm after; internal, customer and supplier logout handlers immediately clear local query/draft/PWA state and notify peer tabs. Logout notifications target `all` because the server endpoints invalidate Laravel's shared session container; each peer then fetches identity through its own guard. The payload contains no identity, permission, or record data. Delta 670 verifies one internal two-tab logout scenario; portal logout and cross-guard session transitions remain `NOT_VERIFIED` in a browser.


### 76. Logout and portal password-change invalidated every guard but signaled only one realm

`AuthService::logout()`, `CustomerAuthController::logout()`, and `SupplierAuthController::logout()` call `session()->invalidate()`, which flushes the browser's shared Laravel session. The first frontend cross-tab implementation signaled only the internal/customer/supplier realm that initiated logout. An open sibling tab in another guard could therefore retain cached query data and its old local identity until its next API request failed. Customer/supplier password-change controllers perform the same whole-session invalidation, but the password-change page had no session-change signal at all. This is confirmed by source tracing; no multi-tab session reproduction was run.

Internal and portal logout now publish `all` lifecycle signals. Portal password-change does the same, serializes its cookie-backed mutation, clears the current query/draft/PWA cache and same-tab internal auth-store identity, then rechecks `/auth/user`; sibling guards also clear and re-read through their own API. Backend logout/password-change behavior remains unchanged. This closes the known sender/subscriber gap at source level; cross-guard propagation and API revalidation are still `NOT_VERIFIED` at runtime.


### 68. Payroll progress cleanup ran before its enclosing state transaction committed

`PayrollPeriodService::claimForCompute()` and `forceUnlock()` call `PayrollProgressTracker::forget()` from inside their database transactions. The tracker previously deleted the Redis progress snapshot immediately, so a later rollback could discard the prior run's progress while the database retained its previous state. The cache deletion could also be observed before the new claim/unlock state was visible. This is a source-confirmed transaction-ordering hazard, not a reproduced user-visible stale-data incident.

`PayrollProgressTracker::forget()` now schedules its bounded key deletion with `DB::afterCommit()`. Cache failure remains best-effort and the one-hour TTL remains the cleanup bound. No test suite or browser scenario was run under the user's stop-testing instruction; Payroll progress UI behavior remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 69. Stock-count changes had no shared session/list refresh path

`StockCountService` mutates `StockCountSession` and `StockCountItem` across create, start, count, variance approval, completion and cancellation. The app registered only `BadgeInvalidationObserver` for `StockCountItem`; there was no session/item realtime observer, private stock-count channel, or stock-count page subscription. The page invalidated/refetched only the acting user's local list/detail, so another warehouse tablet could retain old counted/verified rows, session status and completion actions. Several snapshot rows are inserted with `StockCountItem::insert()`, bypassing Eloquent events, although the corresponding session is also created/updated in that transaction.

Registered session/item observers now stage one `StockCountChanged` outbox refresh per session and PostgreSQL transaction. This includes the raw snapshot insert paths because the parent session write is observed; later item writes are coalesced with that parent event. Private detail and list channels require the Inventory feature and `inventory.stock_count.view`, matching the read API. The wire payload contains only `refresh` and an event UUID; the central invalidation map refetches each viewer's own count data and related stock/count/dashboard/approval queries. The redundant badge observer registration was removed for `StockCountItem`, since the count event already invalidates badge and approval query families. Source wiring is implemented; no route reproduction, tests, typecheck, browser scenario or latency measurement ran under the user's stop-testing instruction, so all six route UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

The acting user's start/count/approve/complete handlers previously discarded the successful mutation representation and waited for an extra detail GET, leaving a window where the screen still showed its prior state. They now reconcile the returned session or item into the matching detail and visible list cache before reporting success; the item response also updates the cached count aggregate from the loaded lines. Cancel retains its existing `204` API contract, applies the known cancelled state locally on success, then refetches. In-flight detail GETs are cancelled before cache seeding to prevent an older response from overwriting the mutation response. The stock-count options lookup has a separate query key outside the list invalidation prefix, avoiding an unnecessary settings GET on every count mutation. This is source-level correction only; matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 77. Offline navigation fallback searched every Cache Storage namespace

The service worker's network-first navigation fallback used `caches.match('/')`, which searches all Cache Storage namespaces for the origin. On a shared origin this could select another app's cached shell, and it bypassed the policy that Ogami reads only its own versioned cache. This was a source-confirmed isolation risk; no user was observed receiving another app's shell. The fallback now opens the current build's `ogami-pwa-{buildId}` cache and reads `/` only from that cache, returning a network error if no owned shell is available. The focused VM-level service-worker policy suite now passes 8/8, including the own-cache navigation fallback and API/Sanctum pass-through cases. No installed-PWA browser scenario was run.


### 78. Accounting-period writes had no realtime or finance-cache invalidation path

`AccountingPeriodService::close()`, `reopen()` and hourly `relockStaleReopenedPeriods()` save the model, but the provider registered no period observer, no period event/channel existed, and neither the accounting-period page nor Finance dashboard subscribed to a period signal. Mutation callbacks invalidated only the actor's list. The page derived Close/Reopen from the locally held `status`, while `FinanceDashboardService` selected its 30-second cached `current_accounting_period` panel using `DashboardCacheGeneration::current()`, which period writes never advanced. This is source-confirmed; no cross-user browser reproduction was run in this slice.

Registered `AccountingPeriodRealtimeObserver` captures model lifecycle changes (including the scheduler's Eloquent save), stages one content-free `AccountingPeriodChanged` through the durable outbox per period/transaction, and registers finance generation rotation before broadcast dispatch after commit. The new private entity channel matches the list feature and permission checks. The central SPA invalidation map refreshes period lists and finance summaries; mutation responses reconcile the acting user's list immediately. `AccountingPeriodResource` now returns version, permissions and available actions, and a stale reopen returns 409. Regression tests were added but not executed under the user's instruction; therefore the root cause fix is source-wired only, and all matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 79. HR training catalog and employee-training changes had no shared refresh path (source-confirmed; not runtime-reproduced)

`TrainingService::create/update/delete()`, the controller's restore path, `EmployeeTrainingService::assign/recordCompletion/cancel()`, and the daily `TrainingExpiryService::check()` Eloquent saves had no model observer or event path. Training catalog pages had only actor-local invalidation; employee-training rows had a badge-only observer and no detail/list subscription. HR action buttons were derived from the locally cached status, and the catalog edit form reset its fields when query data changed, risking loss of unsaved input during a refetch. No direct backend `Cache::` response/data-cache writer was found in `api/app/Modules/HR`; the stale risk was the in-memory SPA query data and missing propagation, with authenticated API reads protected by no-store. This is source-confirmed and not a two-context reproduction.

Registered `EmployeeTrainingRealtimeObserver` and `TrainingCatalogRealtimeObserver` stage coalesced, content-free after-commit outbox pings on private list/detail channels. Assignment now writes its initial Scheduled status in the create statement, yielding one create transition. The central map refreshes HR record/catalog, related employee-training, badge and dashboard query families; personal detail access checks the employee owner or HR row scope. Resources now include version, permissions, and `available_actions`; completion/cancellation revalidate the locked state and return 409 if stale. Actor pages reconcile mutation responses. The catalog form preserves dirty fields and requires explicit reload after a newer remote version. The daily expiry schedule is inventoried and its Eloquent writes reach the observer. Source wiring and regression test files exist, but the user directed tests to stop; no tests, build/typecheck, scheduled run, two-context UI delivery, or latency/cache measurement was performed. All related mutation and scheduled-transition matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 80. MRP scheduled mutations were missing from the transition matrix (source-confirmed inventory gap; not runtime-reproduced)

The daily `mrp:run-daily` and hourly `mrp:reap-stale-runs` schedules were present in `routes/console.php` and command source, but only the command files were listed in the async ledger; the schedule-driven state changes were absent from the mutation and stale-risk matrices. Their model writes already reach `MrpRunRealtimeObserver`, `MrpPlanRealtimeObserver`, `BomRealtimeObserver`/related existing model observers, and `ProductionScheduleRealtimeObserver` where applicable. Added both schedule invocations as explicit transition rows and documented expected cross-record effects. This closes the inventory omission only: no command, worker, browser, or two-user behavior was exercised, and all MRP UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The other scheduled commands still need the same classification.


### 81. Employee master changes, including effective-date salary writes, had no employee refresh signal (source-confirmed; not runtime-reproduced)

`EmployeeAccessScopeRealtimeObserver` ran only when `department_id` changed and only refreshed the linked user's access scope. It did not invalidate employee detail, employee lists/counts, salary-adjustment views, or HR/payroll dashboard queries after a general employee update. `ApplyDueSalaryAdjustments::handle()` calls `SalaryAdjustmentService::applyDueLiveAdjustments()`, whose `applyToLiveRow()` updates `Employee::basic_monthly_salary` / `semi_monthly_rate` with Eloquent, then saves the adjustment. Employee detail displays those salary fields, but the page had no employee record subscription and the list had no employee list subscription. This is a confirmed source gap; the scheduled job and two-user stale screen were not executed in this turn.

Added `EmployeeRealtimeObserver` for employee create/update/delete/restore writes, an outbox-backed `EmployeeChanged` signal dispatched after commit on the dedicated queue, and record/list/user channels. List delivery is split between company-wide viewers with `hr.employees.view_sensitive`, department viewers with `hr.employees.view` scoped to their own department, and a linked user's exact private channel. Record authorization applies the same `DepartmentScope` as employee GET routes. The payload contains identifiers, version/action/actor/time, and related old/current department and position references, not employee fields or compensation values. Employee API responses remain the acting user's source of truth; the SPA now subscribes on employee detail and list pages and invalidates employee, linked payroll, attendance/leave, option, and dashboard query families. Employee query data remains memory-only and authenticated API responses remain no-store. `EmployeeRealtimeTest` passes its producer/contract/privacy assertions (1 warning / 10 assertions); no Employee two-role browser scenario or scheduled salary command execution was run, so matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 82. Salary-adjustment queue status and actions had no shared refresh path (source-confirmed; selected approval path now multi-user verified)

`SalaryAdjustmentService::request()`, `approve()` and `reject()` create/update the `SalaryAdjustment` row, but no model observer or private list/detail subscription notified other queue viewers. A future-dated final approval can change the adjustment status without changing the Employee pay row immediately, so the Employee-only salary signal cannot cover that queue transition. The actor page invalidated its own list after success; other viewers could keep the old pending status and action buttons. This is source-confirmed, not reproduced in two browser contexts.

Added `SalaryAdjustmentRealtimeObserver` and a coalesced after-commit `SalaryAdjustmentChanged` signal for create/update/delete, authorized private list/detail channels, and a central invalidation-map entry for salary-adjustment queues, related employee/payroll views, badges and approvals. The queue now subscribes through the shared list hook. The actor uses the successful API resource to update its active list before the existing reconciliation invalidation. Payloads omit requested/from/to pay, reason and requester details. Channel authorization matches the existing HR feature and `hr.salary_adjustments.view` API gates. Two real-backend three-context Chromium scenarios verified approve and reject: the Admin actor had its socket closed but saw its row/action update from the API response; the HR maker's open detail reflected the new state; and a separate Admin approval board removed the item after refetch. `PayrollAdjustmentChanged` and `BadgesChanged` frames were observed. Approval/rejection board-refetch responses arrived 1,387 ms and 1,185 ms after the actor API responses in these single samples. Creation-side actor rendering, linked records, numeric badges, dashboards and p95 latency remain unverified. See the matrix for per-cell evidence.




### Inventory item cache and subscription boundaries (source-confirmed; no new browser reproduction)

Item mutations now retire the bounded `inv:dashboard:summary` generation through the earlier-registered `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver`; the later badge observer rotates only the shared dashboard/widget generation. The missing record/list contract was separate: `ItemController` returned authorized resources, but Eloquent writes had no Item realtime observer, and the SPA did not subscribe to item catalog changes globally. A separate invalidation-map omission meant `InventoryStockChanged` refreshed stock-level/dashboard query keys but not `['inventory','items', ...]`, although `ItemResource` contains on-hand, usable, safety-stock and stock-status values. The daily safety-stock and ABC jobs also use `saveQuietly()`/query-builder writes, bypassing model observers.

The source wiring now sends one outbox-backed `ItemChanged` for item CRUD, one entity ping for bulk safety-stock/ABC recomputes, and entity pings for linked category/UOM/conversion changes. The root SPA bridge subscribes only when the current user has a permission used by the item list/catalog API; record authorization remains `inventory.view`. Stock events now invalidate item queries. This corrects the identified source coverage gap but does not prove delivery, actor rendering, buttons, cross-role views or latency. The Inventory rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED` until runtime evidence exists.


### Replenishment alert resolution missed the Action Center refresh (source-confirmed)

AutoReplenishmentService::clearBlocked() resolved alert rows with a query-builder update, bypassing Eloquent observers. The scheduled replenishment reconciliation can therefore make an active Action Center alert disappear from storage without invalidating the badge generation or notifying active Action Center viewers. It now emits BadgesChanged and touches the badge generation after commit when at least one alert row changes. This is source evidence only; no scheduled invocation or alert-view browser scenario ran, so the matrix cells remain NOT_VERIFIED.


### 83. Dashboard refetches could return a stale server-side snapshot after machine, mold, maintenance, MRP-run, and asset changes

`DashboardWidgetDataService::summaries()` and `WidgetAnalyticsService::payload()` cached per-user values for 30 seconds under only the global `DashboardCacheGeneration`. `PpcDashboardService::ppc()` used the same global-only generation while reading machine status/availability, mold shot thresholds, maintenance schedules, and the latest MRP run. The existing record events and SPA invalidation map refetched those dashboard queries, but Machine, Mold, MrpRun, MaintenanceSchedule, and Asset writes did not rotate the generation used by the affected cached snapshots. The next API response could therefore return the prior aggregate after a realtime refetch. This is source-confirmed; no browser cache-staleness reproduction was run in this slice.

Added `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` on the source models; it registers source-specific generation rotations with `DB::afterCommit` during lifecycle writes, before the mutation service stages its outbox dispatch callback. It retires source-specific generations for PPC and Plant Manager machine panels, machine summaries and rich OEE gauges, maintenance due counts, asset maintenance summaries, and production output KPI summary/trend data, while `DashboardCacheGeneration::touch()` without a scope retains its existing global behavior. The scoped marker is hashed and TTL-bounded; old snapshot payloads expire within their 30-second TTL. Per-family generations avoid invalidating unrelated users' dashboard payloads on frequent mold shot updates. Added focused regression test source, but did not execute it under the stop-testing instruction. The scheduled/runtime and rendered UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 84. Inventory stock refetches could reuse the previous low-stock badge snapshot

The SPA's `inventory_stock` invalidation map includes `['sidebar', 'badges']`, and `InventoryStockChanged` is emitted after `InventoryDashboardCache` rotates its stock-summary generation. However, `BadgeService::for()` originally keyed its 30-second per-user snapshot only by `badges.version`. A client could receive a stock-change ping and refetch its badge endpoint, but the backend could return the prior cached low-stock or out-of-stock count until that independent badge TTL expired. This is a source-confirmed cache coherence gap; it was not reproduced in a browser session.

Badge snapshot keys now include both the user's badge generation and the after-commit inventory generation. A stock-change refetch therefore uses a fresh per-user key without rotating the shared dashboard generation or broadcasting a second event. The superseded badge snapshot is bounded by its existing 30-second TTL. A focused regression test was added to `InventoryStockRealtimeTest.php` but was not run, per the instruction to stop testing. No cache purge, request-latency measurement, cache-hit measurement, or UI verification was performed.


### 85. Production output pings could refetch stale production KPI widgets

`CoreWidgetAnalytics::outputTrend()`, `DashboardWidgetDataService`'s `production.kpi` summary, and Plant Manager production/OEE panels read `work_order_outputs` and cache their results for 30 seconds. `WorkOrderOutputService` emits a `WorkOrderOutputRecorded` refresh signal and retires the separate Production dashboard cache, but that did not rotate the widget or Plant Manager dashboard generations. After a realtime dashboard invalidation, those APIs could therefore return the old output trend/count. This is a source-confirmed cache gap; no browser reproduction was run.

`DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` now watches WorkOrderOutput lifecycle writes and rotates `dashboard:quality`, `dashboard:plant_manager`, `widget-summary:production.kpi`, and `widget-rich:production.kpi` after commit. The Quality and Plant Manager dashboards retain their shared global generation as well, so existing invalidators still apply. Existing payload TTL bounds old unreachable keys to 30 seconds. A regression test source was added but not run; dashboard UI freshness, request count, cache hit rate and latency remain unverified.


### 86. Downtime updates could refetch stale rich OEE and Plant Manager alert snapshots

`CoreWidgetAnalytics::oeeGauge()` reads `machine_downtimes` for the rich `machine.utilization` and `oee.gauges` widgets. Plant Manager's cached `alerts()` panel also reads open breakdown downtime rows. `MachineDowntime` lifecycle changes already had a maintenance-order refresh observer when linked to a maintenance work order, and production work-order status events refresh production dashboard viewers, but the scoped dashboard cache observer did not rotate either rich widget generation or the Plant Manager snapshot. A successful event-driven refetch could therefore reuse the previous 30-second snapshot. This is source-confirmed; no browser reproduction was run.

`DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` now maps MachineDowntime create/update/delete to the Plant Manager and rich OEE generations, with its `DB::afterCommit` rotation registered before the existing MachineDowntime realtime observer. The change leaves unrelated scalar widget and dashboard generations untouched. Regression coverage source was added but not run. Event delivery, the Plant Manager page's realtime subscription, UI freshness and cache/latency measurements remain unverified.


### 87. Plant Manager dashboard missed the existing production refresh signals

The Plant Manager page queried `['dashboard', 'plant-manager', range]` and refreshed only on a 60-second timer. It did not subscribe to `production.dashboard`, even though that private channel already carried refresh-only signals for machine/work-order changes, output, sales-order confirmation, MRP planning, and mold alerts. Its API refetch could therefore remain delayed until the periodic timer after those changes. The channel originally required `production.view` plus `production.dashboard.view`, while `dashboard.plant_manager.view` is itself sufficient to open this dashboard. This is source-confirmed; no browser trace was run.

The Plant Manager page now uses the shared `useProductionDashboardRealtime` hook. Its event-to-query targets are in the central signal invalidation map, and the channel authorization admits the Plant Manager page permission for these minimal refresh signals; dashboard API panels remain individually permission-gated. The listened event serializers carry only refresh metadata, not record values. This covers events already emitted on `production.dashboard`; other dashboard source families such as delivery and quality still need their own event-to-page coverage audit. No tests, typecheck, browser run, or channel-auth runtime check was performed.


### 88. Stock-change refetches could return the old Warehouse dashboard snapshot

`InventoryStockChanged` rotates `InventoryDashboardCache` and its SPA invalidation map includes dashboard query keys. However, `WarehouseDashboardService` cached stock KPIs and low-stock panels under only the shared dashboard generation, and neither stock movements nor `InventoryDashboardCache` rotated that generation. The Warehouse dashboard page also had no listener for `inventory.dashboard`. A viewer could therefore wait for its 60-second timer, or refetch from another cause and still receive the prior Warehouse snapshot for the remaining 30-second backend TTL. This is source-confirmed; no browser reproduction was run.

`PublishInventoryStockChange` now schedules a `dashboard:warehouse` generation rotation after commit before the stock signal is published, and `WarehouseDashboardService` includes that family marker in its key. The Warehouse page subscribes through the shared stock hook only when `inventory.view` is present; the existing central stock invalidation map refreshes the active dashboard query. The stock hook coalesces burst invalidations. Old unreachable payload keys expire under the existing 30-second TTL. No tests, browser run, cache-hit rate or latency measurement was performed.


### 89. PPC and Quality dashboards missed existing production refresh signals

PPC's machine/planning panels and Quality's output-derived defect Pareto were backed by cached dashboard queries, but neither page subscribed to `production.dashboard`. That channel carried Machine, Mold, MRP-plan, work-order and output refresh signals. Machine/Mold/MRP cache generations were already source-scoped for PPC; `WorkOrderOutput` did not rotate Quality's dashboard generation, and a page refetch would therefore still reuse the prior 30-second result. These are source-confirmed gaps; no browser reproduction was run.

PPC now uses the shared production dashboard hook, and its central signal map invalidates the PPC dashboard, bottleneck and active KPI queries. Quality subscribes only to a separate private `quality.dashboard` channel carrying the output-only `refresh=true` ping. `WorkOrderOutput` retires the `dashboard:quality` generation after commit, and `QualityDashboardService` uses that generation. Channel authorization matches each page permission, and each API continues to gate panel data by its own permissions. Event delivery, panel results, cache hit rate and latency remain unverified; no tests or browser scenarios were run.

Follow-up source audit 279 widened this to a coalesced Quality dashboard signal for the missing linked inputs. See root cause 177 for the DeliveryProof/GRN/WorkOrder/Product/DefectType generation and channel changes; runtime behavior remains NOT_VERIFIED.


### 90. Finance dashboard pages missed existing accounting record refresh signals

The Finance dashboard subscribed to accounting-period changes only through its detail/list page; its own cached dashboard query did not listen for period, journal-entry, or chart-of-account changes. Those source events already refresh their record/list query families, and their observers rotate the shared dashboard cache generation after commit, but the Finance dashboard could stay stale until its 60-second refresh. Source review confirms the missing page subscriptions; no browser reproduction was run.

The Finance page now mounts the shared realtime list hooks for accounting periods, journal entries and accounts when the current user has each matching read permission. The central mappings invalidate the Finance dashboard query and affected statement/widget families; observer generations are rotated before each event is broadcast. This source-wires Finance dashboard refresh without adding a new cache entry. Runtime delivery, panel freshness and latency remain unverified; no tests or browser scenarios were run.


### 91. Stock reservation writes bypassed both stock refresh events and cached widget invalidation

`StockMovementService::reserve()` and `release()` mutate `stock_levels.reserved_quantity`, which feeds `ItemResource` availability and the `material.reservations` widget, but these paths did not create a stock movement or publish `StockMovementCompleted`. The item catalog bridge listens for `InventoryStockChanged`, and the corresponding 30-second low-stock and material-reservation widget caches had separate generations. A reservation-triggered client refetch could therefore miss the event entirely or reuse an old cached aggregate. Source review confirms the write and cache paths; no browser reproduction was run.

Reservation writes now stage a `StockReservationChanged` outbox event, coalesced once per item/location/transaction. The existing stock publisher handles it after commit, rotates inventory, Warehouse, low-stock and material-reservation cache generations, then sends the same content-free private refresh ping used for ledger movements. The event payload visible to clients contains no item, location, or quantity values; viewers refetch through their authorized API. Regression coverage source was updated but not run. End-to-end freshness and latency remain unverified.

### 92. Approval viewers received duplicate copies of the generic badge refresh frame

The three-context Stock Adjustment approval run recorded two `BadgesChanged` frames in the board viewer (`docs/realtime/PROGRESS.md`, delta 165; trace `/tmp/ogami-custom-approval-20261030`). Source inspection at that time confirmed one `BadgesChanged` event broadcast to both private `badges` and `approvals.visible`; the SPA's Sidebar subscribed to `badges`, while the approval board separately subscribed to `approvals.visible`. The shared invalidation scheduler merged the resulting cache work, so the duplicate frames did not require duplicate board refetches, but they added unnecessary transport and handler work. A later change removed the extra `approvals.visible` producer target and had the board consume the shared `badges` ping. Delta 698 supersedes that shared channel with per-user `badge-user.{userHashId}` fan-out, while retaining permission-scoped API refetches and the content-free payload. The unused `approvals.visible` authorization callback remains available for compatibility. No live browser run verifies the new per-user channel delivery or latency.

### 93. Holiday events could refetch dashboard APIs from a still-current 30-second cache

`HolidayChanged` invalidated active `['dashboard','hr']` and `['self-service','dashboard']` queries, but the backing `HrDashboardService` cache keys used only the shared generation. Holiday writes rotated only the separate holiday-list cache generation, so a realtime dashboard refetch could receive the old next-holiday/calendar snapshot for up to 30 seconds. The self-service employee dashboard also had a 60-second page timer and its linked-employee API audience was excluded from the holiday channel despite that endpoint exposing the employee's own `next_holiday`. These are source-confirmed; no two-user browser reproduction was captured.

The HR and employee dashboard keys now combine the existing global generation with family-scoped markers; `HolidayRealtimeService` rotates those two markers after commit before queuing `HolidayChanged`, preserving cache reuse while retiring only affected snapshots. The private holiday channel now authorizes linked employees on the same basis as `/dashboards/employee`, and AppLayout mounts their shared list subscription. The broader refresh payload no longer contains the actor hash ID. The self-service page's fixed 60-second polling was removed; the shared provider handles reconnect/focus and degraded-mode catch-up. No tests or browser run were performed; event delivery, cache freshness and self-service UI remain runtime-unverified.

### 94. Sales Order status changes could leave the production chain-stage snapshot cached

`ProductionDashboardService::chainStageBreakdown()` groups `sales_orders` by current status and its response is cached under a dedicated `dashboard:production` generation for 30 seconds. Sales Order create/update/confirm/cancel lifecycle writes were covered by the newer domain refresh paths and the global dashboard generation, but did not call `ProductionDashboardService::forgetCache()`; that older endpoint could therefore refetch and still return its prior chain-stage totals. A bounded source search found the CRM lifecycle writes use Eloquent and found no `sales_orders` query-builder update in the searched application paths. This does not rule out SQL executed outside those paths or unsearched deployment/import tooling. This is source-confirmed, not reproduced in a browser.

`SalesOrder` is now registered with the central `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver`, which calls the existing `ProductionDashboardService::forgetCache()` after commit for created, updated and deleted rows. This keeps the established generation/late-fill protection and covers Eloquent mutation paths in controllers, services and jobs. No tests or dashboard/browser check were run; other direct/quiet writers and UI freshness remain unverified.


### 95. Settings writes could leave setting-derived dashboard snapshots current

`SettingsService::updateFromAdmin()` wrote and audited settings, then rotated the individual setting cache generation. Most dashboard and widget snapshots use separate per-user dashboard generations, and only a narrow production-settings path retired its own snapshot. A settings update could therefore trigger a client refetch that reused a cached dashboard value computed from the old setting for up to the snapshot TTL. No two-session reproduction was run.

Admin setting updates now register the per-setting and shared dashboard-generation rotations after commit before staging one transaction-coalesced `SettingsChanged` outbox signal. The private `settings.refresh` channel accepts active authenticated ERP users, and the payload contains no key or value; each client refetches its own authorized settings/dashboard API data. Programmatic `SettingsService::set()` also rotates dashboard generations after commit but does not publish user-facing signals. No tests, browser run, cache audit, or performance measurement were performed; Settings UI and cross-user delivery remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 96. Live analytics refetches could reuse a separate metric cache generation

The `returns.pipeline` and `production.wo_lead_time` metrics are declared `live` and are cached by `AnalyticsCache` under per-metric generations. Their source record events invalidate active SPA queries, but the realtime path did not rotate these separate metric generations; an event-driven API refetch could therefore return a cached prior metric value for the remaining 60-second default TTL. This is source-confirmed; no browser reproduction was run.

The central dashboard cache observer now flushes only `returns.pipeline` after ReturnRequest Eloquent lifecycle writes and only `production.wo_lead_time` after WorkOrder Eloquent lifecycle writes. It also retires the affected RMA scalar/rich widget generations for ReturnRequest writes, and the PPC active-work-order, Gantt, and breakdown widget generations plus the legacy ProductionDashboardService generation for WorkOrder writes. The ReturnRequest cache observer is registered before its realtime observer, and WorkOrder changes reach the cache observer before service-level outbox staging, so the version rotation callback precedes publication. Direct SQL/quiet writer coverage, metric UI freshness, and performance remain unverified; no tests, browser/API runs, or cache audits were performed.

### 97. Cache invalidation failures could suppress later after-commit realtime callbacks

Several cache-generation helpers registered `Cache::put()` or legacy-key retirement directly in `DB::afterCommit()`. Laravel runs these callbacks after the database commit; if Redis throws during an earlier cache callback, later callbacks that stage or dispatch the durable realtime signal may not run. Source inspection confirmed this ordering risk in the shared dashboard, badge, inventory, financial-statement, and Production dashboard generation seams. It was not reproduced with an induced Redis failure.

Those five invalidation seams now catch and log cache-write failures so a committed record's later outbox/broadcast callback can proceed. The cache may remain stale until its short payload TTL if Redis itself is unavailable, so the warning log is still an operational signal that needs monitoring. This slice did not inject a cache outage or run tests; other application-specific after-commit cache callbacks remain under source audit.

### 98. Permission and payroll cache cleanup could prevent access/table refresh signals

`User::flushPermissionsCache()`, `RoleService::syncPermissions()`, and `UserAdminService::changeRole()` registered Redis `Cache::forget()` callbacks before later access-refresh outbox callbacks. The government-contribution import similarly registered generation/cache retirement before its table-refresh outbox signal. A cache exception after commit could therefore stop the realtime callback, leaving active users on old permissions or old statutory-table rows until another refresh. This is source-confirmed callback ordering; no Redis failure was induced.

These cache callbacks now catch and log failures while allowing the later `UserAccessChanged`, `RolePermissionsChanged`, or `GovernmentContributionTableChanged` dispatch to proceed. Permission role snapshots remain versioned by the committed database `permissions_version`; legacy cache cleanup is best effort. Payroll’s prior cached generation may remain visible for its bounded five-minute TTL if Redis accepts reads but rejects invalidation writes. The error path and UI recovery were not tested; other after-commit cache call sites remain under audit.

### 99. DSS status freshness depended on a fixed five-second page poll

The DSS admin page polled detector state every five seconds, while DSS parameter writes, recommendation lifecycle changes, run-log updates and recommendation activity had no shared browser refresh event. Decision Center list/detail queries also had no realtime subscription. The page timer masked missing event producers for some views, while other active views could remain stale until focus or manual refresh. This is source-confirmed; no two-session reproduction was run for DSS.

Added a private `dss.refresh` channel gated by the enabled DSS feature and current DSS read/manage/admin permissions. The DSS model observer retires a parameter's detector/key cache generation before staging one transaction-coalesced, content-free `DssChanged` outbox ping; cache failures are logged without blocking publication. Recommendation, run-log and recommendation-event writes use the same signal path. The recommendation-view insert and scheduled/archive query-builder deletes explicitly stage the signal because they bypass Eloquent observers. The admin page, Decision Center list and detail now invalidate their `['dss']` queries from the event. Removed the fixed page timer; the shared `RealtimeProvider` remains the visibility-aware degraded-mode catch-up. Event delivery, UI freshness, and non-DSS source effects remain unverified; no tests or browser runs were performed.

### 100. Recent-record browser storage survived an access-scope change

`CommandPalette.pick()` persists each selected row's URL, label, sublabel and status in `ogami:recent-items`. `recentItemsStore` tied that snapshot only to the user ID and had no expiry. The auth store clears TanStack Query on same-user role/permission/feature changes, but the recent list's `claim()` kept the cached labels and links whenever the user ID was unchanged. A permission downgrade could therefore leave previously visible record metadata in localStorage and the palette after `/me` refreshed, while old history also lingered indefinitely for a still-authorized user. This is source-confirmed; no browser reproduction was run.

The persisted list now also carries an opaque local fingerprint of role, employee/department scope, permissions, delegated approvals, active state and enabled features. `/me` claiming a changed scope clears all prior rows; the fingerprint omits raw permission names and is not used for authorization. Legacy entries without the scope field clear on the next successful auth bootstrap. Entries expire after 30 days and the list remains capped at eight rows for the same owner and unchanged scope. No test or browser run was performed; storage behavior is source-reviewed only.

Delta 349 later removes mutable status snapshots from new recent entries and strips the legacy `status` field on hydration. Record URLs and display labels remain as 30-day navigation history under the existing owner/scope boundary.

### 101. Cache failures could interrupt P2P and statutory-table refresh callbacks

`ProcurementChainCacheObserver` and `GovernmentContributionTableService::stageChange()` rotate version markers after their database writes commit, before later realtime observer/outbox callbacks. Their `Cache::put()`/`Cache::forget()` calls were not guarded. If Redis threw, the record stayed committed but the remaining after-commit callbacks could stop before the P2P or payroll-table refresh signal. This is source-confirmed callback ordering; no Redis failure was induced.

Both generation seams now catch and log invalidation failures and allow later committed-write callbacks to run. If Redis rejects the invalidation, the previous aggregate/bracket cache can remain visible until its bounded TTL; the resulting warning needs operational monitoring. No tests or runtime failure injection were performed.

### 102. Production output cache availability could turn a committed action into an API error

`WorkOrderOutputService` used Redis as a fast idempotency pointer before its durable database lookup, and wrote that pointer after the production output transaction committed. Either `Cache::get()` or `Cache::put()` could throw: the first would reject a valid mutation before the database's composite idempotency check, while the second could return an API error after output, stock and outbox side effects had already committed. That left the acting operator with an ambiguous result even though the database was correct. This is source-confirmed; no Redis outage was injected.

The Redis pointer is now best-effort on read and write. Cache read errors fall through to the existing locked database lookup and persisted request fingerprint; cache write errors are logged while returning the committed output normally. The composite database key remains authoritative. No tests or failure injection were run. The local `make dev-refresh-php` target was completed after this edit; Artisan reports `APP_ENV=local` and Horizon running, while Docker reports API, Horizon, mail worker and Reverb running. This is process status, not end-to-end mutation evidence.

### 103. Production dashboard refresh could beat output cache invalidation

`WorkOrderOutputService` recorded the output and staged `WorkOrderOutputRecorded` inside a transaction, but called `ProductionDashboardService::forgetCache()` only after the transaction returned. A fast outbox worker could publish first; a production dashboard subscriber could then refetch the 30-second legacy dashboard snapshot before its generation changed. `ProductionDashboardService` reads today's output totals and defect history, so the event could arrive while those panels still showed old values. This ordering race is source-confirmed; it was not reproduced with an intentionally delayed cache callback.

`DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` now rotates the legacy production generation from the WorkOrderOutput model lifecycle, registering its after-commit callback before the service stages the outbox event. The post-transaction duplicate cache invalidations were removed from `WorkOrderOutputService`; all Eloquent create/update/delete paths use the same observer. Query-builder writers and dashboard UI results remain unverified; no tests or browser runs were performed.

### 104. Legacy-key cleanup could stop active cache generations from rotating

Several versioned caches performed best-effort legacy cleanup before their active generation moved. `ProductionDashboardService::forgetCache()` and government-contribution invalidation forgot a legacy key before writing the new marker; `HolidayCacheGeneration` first read an old marker so a cache-read failure could prevent rotation; and SettingsService used one catch around setting, dashboard and production invalidations, so one failed Redis operation skipped sibling generations. A cache failure could therefore leave the current generation pointing to an old snapshot even though the mutation's event continued. This is source-confirmed ordering; no Redis failure was induced.

The production and payroll paths now rotate their version markers before legacy cleanup and catch the operations independently. Holiday invalidation rotates its marker without first reading the old value, then cleans legacy/tagged entries best-effort. Settings invalidation isolates per-key generation writes/legacy cleanup so shared dashboard and production generations still rotate. If the marker write itself fails, the old bounded entry can remain until TTL and the warning log is the recovery signal. No tests or failure injection were run.


### 105. Finance refresh pings could refetch the still-current Finance snapshot

`FinanceDashboardService::summary()` cached AR, AP, pending credit notes, current accounting period, payroll pipeline, budget-vs-actual, and forecast panels for 30 seconds under the shared dashboard generation plus a caller gate signature. Most sources already rotate that shared generation, but `CreditNoteRealtimeObserver` and `FiscalYearRealtimeObserver` publish refresh signals without touching it. A viewer could receive the signal, refetch the authorized summary, and still receive the old snapshot for the remaining TTL. This is source-confirmed; no realtime/browser reproduction was run.

The Finance summary now uses a separate `dashboard:finance` generation and a v4 key. The central dashboard cache observer rotates it after commit for transactional and reference-data source models used by the summary, registered before realtime/outbox observers; settings writes also rotate the finance generation. This avoids using broad dashboard churn to keep Finance correct. Query-builder/quiet writes, browser delivery, cache hits and latency remain unverified; no tests were run.


### 106. Finance section query keys missed accounting refresh prefixes

The embedded Finance section stores its summary under `['accounting', 'dashboard', 'summary']`, while generic `BadgesChanged` and several entity invalidations only targeted `['dashboard']` or the unused order `['dashboard', 'accounting']`. Credit-note refreshes were not subscribed from the shared authenticated layout, so a Finance viewer could keep the embedded panel stale despite receiving related record changes elsewhere. This is source-confirmed; no browser run was performed.

The central signal and entity invalidation map now includes the FinanceSection key prefix, and the shared layout subscribes to private Credit Note and Vendor list signals only when the current user has the matching read permission. Budget, bill, invoice, payroll, account, period, and journal invalidations now include the embedded key alongside the direct Finance dashboard key. The backend Finance-only generation from root cause 105 ensures authorized refetches see a current snapshot. No UI or two-user verification was run.

### 107. Vendor master-data writes had no real-time refresh signal

Vendor mutations used the Eloquent model through `VendorService`, but there was no Vendor observer/event or authorized Vendor channel. Finance cached the Vendor name in its AP-due panel, while open Vendor lists/details also had no event-driven invalidation. This is source-confirmed; no two-user reproduction was captured.

Added `VendorChanged` through the durable outbox, a lifecycle observer, and private per-record/entity channels gated by the same `accounting.vendors.view` permission as the API. The payload carries only the content-free refresh fields; the shared SPA layout subscribes through the permission-gated entity channel and invalidates Vendor reads plus Finance/AP dependent queries. The Finance generation rotates before the event is staged. Worker delivery and UI freshness remain unverified; no tests were run.

### 108. Dashboard refetches could reuse unrelated or unrotated 30-second snapshots

`DashboardWidgetDataService::summaries()` and `WidgetAnalyticsService::payload()` cache a per-user/per-scope aggregate for 30 seconds. Their keys included a dashboard generation, but the central observer only rotated that generation for a small subset of source models. Source inspection of the widget queries found missing generation edges for inspections and their result rows, MRP plans, deliveries/shipments, purchasing and budget ledgers, HR/leave/attendance/payroll, finance tiles, loans, complaints, and several other widget sources. A websocket-triggered API refetch could therefore return the old aggregate until TTL expiry. This was a source-confirmed invalidation gap; the live stale-tile scenario was not reproduced in that update. Source disposition: `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` now registers EmployeeLoan and LoanPayment against `widget-summary:loans.outstanding`; the documented 30-second payload TTL bounds exposure if rotation fails. Live Redis freshness and hit-rate were not measured.

The central observer now registers before the domain realtime observers and maps the discovered Eloquent source models to family/widget generations after commit. Accounting and Purchasing dashboard snapshots now also use source-scoped generations instead of relying on the broad global marker. The map does not cover direct query-builder/quiet/bulk writes or every scheduler data source; event delivery, rendered UI freshness, cache performance and remaining writer coverage are `NOT_VERIFIED`. No tests were run.

### 109. Supplier score recomputes bypassed cache observers and viewer refresh

`SupplierPerformanceService::compute()` persists `SupplierPerformanceSnapshot` with `upsert()`, which does not fire Eloquent model observers. The monthly `purchasing:recompute-supplier-performance` command calls that path for each vendor, while the manual recompute UI only invalidated the acting browser's detail query. The Purchasing dashboard and supplier ranking/detail views therefore had no source-driven cache rotation or cross-user refresh from those writes. This is source-confirmed; no scheduler or two-user browser run was performed.

The service now rotates Purchasing and supplier-score widget generations after each committed snapshot, and the scheduled batch emits one private content-free supplier-performance refresh after successful writes. Manual recompute emits the per-vendor refresh from the same transaction. The shared list subscription is gated by the same `purchasing.suppliers.performance.view` permission as the API; recipients refetch through their own APIs. Scheduled/manual delivery and rendered UI remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; no tests were run.

### 110. Dashboard cache outages could fail reads or discard successfully computed panels

Source review of `DashboardWidgetDataService::summaries()` and `WidgetAnalyticsService::payload()` found cache reads outside the source-query error boundary. A cache read exception could fail the dashboard request; a cache write exception could turn a successfully computed scalar into an unavailable tile or a rich payload into an empty result. The same unguarded read-through shape existed in `BadgeService::for()`, `InventoryDashboardCache::remember()`, `AnalyticsCache::remember()`, `ProductionDashboardService::payload()`, `ProcurementChainCache::remember()`, `GovernmentContributionTableService::rememberBrackets()`, and `User::getPermissionSlugsAttribute()`. A cache backend outage could therefore reject reads even when the authorized source query could serve current values. This is source-confirmed; no Redis outage was induced.
