Added `FailOpenCache` for dashboard snapshots, widget summaries, rich analytics, badges, stock summaries, procurement aggregates, payroll table lookups and role permission snapshots. The analytics cache also treats marker, payload read/write and lock failures as cache misses and resolves from the source query. Cache read failures skip snapshots; write failures log a hashed key and still return the computed result. Dashboard, analytics, badge, inventory, payroll and procurement generation-marker read failures select an unshared namespace so an unreadable marker cannot point to an old snapshot. The cache outage path and API responses remain untested. If a generation write fails while Redis continues serving old entries, the registry TTL for that entry remains the upper bound.

### 111. The global freshness timestamp ignored normal live-event refetches

`RealtimeProvider` advanced `lastUpdatedAt` after its reconnect/focus/degraded catch-up pass. Normal entity and shared-signal handlers invalidated queries through `invalidationMap.ts`, but that path did not update the timestamp. An authorized record/list could finish a live refetch while the top-bar indicator still showed the time of an earlier focus or reconnect. This is source-confirmed; no browser interaction was run for this slice.

The timestamp now lives in the shared realtime status store. Both provider catch-up and the central invalidation function update it only after their active-query invalidation/refetch pass resolves. This fixes the indicator at the shared layer without page-specific listeners. Visual behavior and timestamp-to-refetch timing remain unverified.

### 112. Financial statement reads could fail on Redis errors and could reuse the legacy cache namespace

Trial balance, income statement and balance sheet wrapped source queries in tagged `remember()` calls without a cache-failure boundary. A Redis read/write error could fail an authorized report request despite the database being available. Their shared generation reader also defaulted to the literal `legacy-v1` when its marker was missing, which could select a still-live payload under that namespace after marker expiry/eviction. These behaviors are source-confirmed; neither a Redis outage nor marker-eviction interleaving was induced.

The three statement readers now use `FailOpenCache` over the regular cache repository: cache read failures compute from the ledger, and write failures preserve the computed response. The generation marker is initialized atomically with a UUID instead of reusing `legacy-v1`; when Redis cannot supply the marker, each request selects an unshared UUID namespace. Existing generation rotation remains after-commit. If a post-commit marker write itself fails while Redis can still serve old payloads, the 60-second entry TTL remains the bound; that failure case and report latency remain unverified.

### 113. Attendance holiday cache failures could interrupt timekeeping and marker loss could revive legacy rows

`PunchService` and `DTRComputationService` call `HolidayService::forDate()`, which directly read the holiday generation and used a tagged `remember()` without a cache failure boundary. Redis errors could therefore interrupt punch/day computation while the database remained available. A missing holiday marker also selected the literal `legacy-v1` key namespace, and each cached year snapshot could remain for one day. These are source-confirmed paths; no Redis outage or marker eviction was induced.

Holiday reads now use `FailOpenCache` and a five-minute source snapshot TTL. The generation marker uses atomic UUID initialization and a fresh unshared namespace on cache errors, preventing a missing marker from selecting legacy rows. Existing holiday writes still rotate after commit and attempt legacy/tag cleanup before the realtime outbox dispatch. If marker rotation fails while old values remain readable, the five-minute payload TTL bounds the stale window. Punch/DTR behavior, cache load impact and invalidation failure handling remain unverified.

### 114. Redis failure could reject the backup catalog before its remote metadata probe

`BackupService::remoteArtifactMetadata(cached: true)` called `Cache::remember()` before `headRemoteArtifact()`. A cache backend exception could therefore fail a backup catalog request without attempting the actual storage metadata lookup. This is source-confirmed; no Redis or object-storage failure was induced.

The probe now uses `FailOpenCache`: it reuses the 120-second metadata verdict when available and runs the storage `head-object` probe if the cache read fails. A failed cache write still returns the fetched metadata. Restore/download already bypasses this cache and remains read-through. No storage behavior or request latency was tested.

### 115. Evicted cache generation markers could select old payload namespaces again

Settings, inventory/production dashboards, procurement and analytics aggregates, badges, payroll bracket tables, DSS parameters, global/scoped dashboard widgets, financial statements and attendance holidays used stable `initial` or `legacy-v1` values when generation markers were absent. If Redis evicted/expired a marker while a longer-lived payload remained, the next read could select that older namespace again. This risk is source-confirmed; marker eviction was not induced.

Added shared `CacheGeneration::current()`. It atomically initializes a missing marker with a UUID and returns a fresh unshared token on marker-read failure. Migrated the listed generation readers to the shared helper. Marker loss can no longer revive their prior generation keys; if a post-commit rotation write fails but the payload cache still serves, the registered payload TTL remains the bound. No Redis failure injection or runtime freshness measurement was performed.

### 116. DSS parameter reads did not fail open and global fallbacks were cache-sensitive

`DssParameterService::get()` directly read and wrote its cache around a database lookup. A Redis failure could block a detector run even when the parameter row was available. Its detector-specific fallback also recursively reads the global parameter, so caching that fallback under the detector key would leave it stale when only the global marker changes. Source inspection confirmed both constraints; no Redis outage was induced.

DSS cache reads now fall through to the parameter table on cache errors, and cache write errors return the fresh row. Missing/read-failed markers use the shared generation helper. The inherited global value is not cached as a detector-specific row, preserving the existing invalidation relationship. Detector-run behavior and outage recovery remain unverified.

### 117. A Redis read failure could interrupt service-account resolution

`SystemUserResolver::id()` read its email-scoped user ID from Redis before querying the configured service-account row. Unlike its after-commit cache write, that read was not guarded, so a Redis error could block portal audit operations even when the account table was available. This is source-confirmed; no Redis outage was induced.

The resolver now logs a hashed cache key and resolves the account from the database when the cache read fails. Provisioning, user/email validation and after-commit cache writes remain unchanged. No portal audit flow was run under the current stop-testing instruction.


### 118. Scheduled-export status and runner updates had no cross-viewer refresh path

Source review found `ScheduledExportController` CRUD writes had no registered realtime observer or list subscription, while `RunDueScheduledExports` changes `processing`, `last_attempt_at`, `last_run_at`, `next_run_at`, and `last_error` with query-builder updates that bypass Eloquent events. The acting page invalidated after its own mutations, but other owners/admin viewers and scheduled-run changes had no shared refresh signal. The list also reused previous-query placeholder data across any key change, including an identity/access-scope change. Both are source-confirmed risks; no two-context reproduction or scheduler run was performed in this no-tests pass.

Controller writes now stage `ScheduledExportChanged` through the durable outbox in the same database transaction. The scheduled runner explicitly stages coalesced signals inside transactions around its query-builder writes. Private channels enforce the API's owner/admin rule, the payload contains only opaque refresh metadata, and the list uses the shared realtime invalidation map with identity-scoped query keys. Previous-query placeholders are reused only while the user and owner/admin scope match. Runtime delivery and UI freshness remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 119. Payroll compute release paths bypassed the period refresh observer

`PayrollPeriodService::releaseClaim()` is shared by the compute job, failed-job recovery, administrator force-unlock, and hourly stale-run reaper. It changed `PayrollPeriod` with a query-builder `update()`, which bypasses the registered `PayrollPeriodRealtimeObserver`; the returned API/status and active payroll lists could therefore remain stale for other viewers. The direct write also left `updated_at` unchanged, so it could not serve as a monotonic refresh version. This is source-confirmed; no stale run was induced.

The shared method now ensures a transaction, advances `updated_at`, writes the audit row, rotates the PayrollPeriod dashboard and badge cache generations, and stages the existing content-free `PayrollPeriodChanged` event. Cache invalidation is registered before the outbox after-commit callback. This covers all four call paths at their shared mutation boundary. Scheduler delivery, two-user behavior and rendered status/actions remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 120. The local Redis cache contained an unbounded worker-restart marker

The metadata-only `cache:audit` found one key without a TTL: Laravel's framework-owned `illuminate:queue:restart` control marker. It was not a cached record or queue payload. The project already bounds this marker for 30 days when Laravel's `queue:restart` command completes; restarting a container directly does not execute that handler. No cached value was read.

Issued the normal `queue:restart` command so the existing handler refreshed the marker with its 30-day TTL. A follow-up audit found 4 application-cache entries, zero without TTL, zero above 1 MiB, and zero unavailable size readings. The bounded prune dry run found no candidates and deleted nothing. This is point-in-time local evidence; production Redis was not inspected.

On 2026-11-02 the marker recurred. A metadata-only audit counted 185 entries (auth=1, badges=1, dash=2, dashboard=15, dss=15, illuminate=1, inv=1, settings=149) and again identified only `illuminate:queue:restart` as missing a TTL. Running Laravel's supported `queue:restart` refreshed the marker's 30-day TTL and gracefully restarted the worker. The follow-up audit found zero missing TTLs, oversized keys, or unknown sizes; the bounded prune dry run found zero candidates and deleted nothing. No cache entry was deleted, and production Redis was not inspected. Why the marker recurred remains unknown.

### 121. The Finance dashboard relied on a 60-second poll for three source families

`FinanceDashboardService` returns invoice balances/aging, bill balances/aging, and payroll-period pipeline data in the same `['dashboard','finance']` response. The Finance page subscribed to accounting-period, journal-entry, and account events, but not the private invoice, bill, or payroll-period list channels. Those source events already invalidate the Finance query and rotate the scoped backend generation; without a mounted subscriber on the Finance page, viewers depended on its fixed 60-second refetch to see them. This is source-confirmed. No dashboard browser trace or request-rate measurement was run.

The page now subscribes to the missing channels only when the current user's matching API permission is present, and removes the fixed polling interval. The existing provider performs active-query catch-up on reconnect/focus and visibility-aware backoff when socket or broadcast delivery is degraded. The backend Finance snapshot remains bounded at 30 seconds and its scoped generation is invalidated after commit. Live event delivery and rendered freshness remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 122. The Purchasing dashboard originally refreshed by interval instead of source events

`PurchasingDashboardService` derives its pending-PR queue, open-PO KPI, PO status pipeline, upcoming-delivery panel, supplier scores and supplier-risk projection from several source families. The baseline page had no direct realtime hooks and used a 60-second query interval. Current source replaces that periodic refresh with permission-gated PurchaseRequest and PurchaseOrder list subscriptions plus `usePurchasingDashboardRealtime()` for purchasing aggregate and demand-forecast signals (`spa/src/pages/dashboard/purchasing.tsx`, `spa/src/hooks/usePurchasingDashboardRealtime.ts`). `AppLayout` separately mounts the permission-gated `supplier_performance` and Vendor list channels (`spa/src/layouts/AppLayout.tsx`); the generic private-channel callback requires the Purchasing feature and `purchasing.suppliers.performance.view` for supplier snapshots (`api/routes/channels.php`). The central invalidation map routes supplier-performance, Vendor, PR/PO and forecast changes to the active Purchasing dashboard query (`spa/src/lib/realtime/invalidationMap.ts`); snapshot writes retire `dashboard:purchasing` and stage `SupplierPerformanceChanged` after commit (`api/app/Modules/Purchasing/Services/SupplierPerformanceRealtimeService.php`, `api/app/Modules/Purchasing/Events/SupplierPerformanceChanged.php`). The dashboard query has no fixed refetch interval now. A page-only test initially appeared to miss supplier-performance events because it rendered outside `AppLayout`; that harness did not model the production shell and was discarded as evidence. Focused hook tests now cover the supplier-performance event name and dashboard invalidation. Source coverage is present, but channel runtime, two-user UI freshness and time-derived forecast aging remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 123. Employee refresh signals were mounted only on the Employee list page

The SPA had an employee realtime hook that correctly selected company-wide, current-department, or exact-self channels, but only the Employee list mounted it. Other authenticated pages and dashboards reuse employee-derived values (HR headcount/recent hires, attendance/leave panels, payroll/production crew readiness) and therefore had no shell-level subscriber to the already-mapped `EmployeeChanged` invalidation. The hook now mounts once in `AppLayout`, and only while the HR feature is enabled; list/detail scopes remain enforced by the private channel callbacks. It invalidates only active query families, which refetch through the current user's API scope. No UI delivery or cross-role behavior was observed.

### 124. Leave-request changes did not refresh the active HR dashboard

The HR dashboard summary contains pending-leave, leave-calendar, and on-leave counts. `LeaveRequestChanged` invalidates dashboard query families, but before this change the private `entity.leave_request.visible` listener was mounted only on leave, calendar, and selected employee pages. A viewer with the authorized HR dashboard open elsewhere therefore received no event and waited for the dashboard's 60-second query interval. `AppLayout` now mounts the list hook for users with the Leave feature and `leave.view` (including the server's system-admin override), so active dashboard queries refetch through their authorized API. No browser delivery or rendered freshness was run.

### 125. Query clearing did not explicitly reset mounted previous-data observers

The auth store correctly calls `queryClient.clear()` before publishing a changed user/access scope, but many active list queries use `placeholderData: previous => previous`. Those options intentionally keep a previous result at the observer level when its key changes, while many legacy keys do not encode user/role scope. Source inspection establishes a stale-display risk; no cross-user placeholder was reproduced in this no-tests slice. `AuthGuard` now keys the authenticated page subtree by the same server-derived identity/activity/role/department/permission/delegation/feature fingerprint used by the realtime provider. A scope change therefore unmounts old page observers and local record copies before the new scope renders; the auth store still clears the QueryClient and the new page refetches through its API guard.

### 126. Quality dashboard changes originally depended on periodic refresh

`QualityDashboardService` reads Inspection, NonConformanceReport and linked GRN, WorkOrder, WorkOrderOutput, WorkOrderDefect, DefectType, Product and DeliveryProof data. The baseline Quality page had no matching live invalidation for these summary families and used a 60-second refetch. Current source subscribes to authorized Inspection and NCR list channels and to the private `quality.dashboard` aggregate channel only when the Quality and Production features plus `dashboard.quality.view` are enabled (`spa/src/pages/dashboard/quality.tsx`). `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` is registered for the corresponding source models in `AppServiceProvider`; its source map rotates `dashboard:quality` and stages one coalesced `QualityDashboardChanged` signal after commit, so users with dashboard access do not need each underlying record permission (`api/app/Modules/Dashboard/Observers/DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver.php`, `api/app/Modules/Dashboard/Services/QualityDashboardRealtimeService.php`, `api/app/Modules/Dashboard/Events/QualityDashboardChanged.php`). The Quality dashboard query no longer has a fixed 60-second interval. Source coverage is present for these lifecycle-observed models, but direct/quiet/bulk writer completeness, channel runtime, cross-role UI freshness and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 127. Dashboard hooks could create false degraded state when their channel module was disabled

`production.dashboard` authorization requires the Production module plus either production dashboard permissions or the Plant Manager/PPC dashboard grant. The shared hook subscribed unconditionally, and the Quality page likewise always attempted `quality.dashboard`, whose channel also requires Production. With a module disabled, a page still allowed by its own route could receive a private-channel auth error; the shared provider correctly marks any mounted private-channel failure as reconnecting, so this unrelated channel denial could trigger degraded catch-up and make the top-bar status inaccurate. The hooks now gate subscriptions on the same module and permission rules as their channel callbacks; Quality output also requires the Quality module. This is source-confirmed; no disabled-module auth failure was induced.

### 128. Six record-detail routes could retain a different record as placeholder data

Six detail queries used `placeholderData: previous => previous` while their query keys included the route's record ID: CRM Complaint, Payroll Period employee/failure rows, Quality Inspection, Quality NCR, Delivery, and Shipment. When navigation reused the page component for another ID, React Query could keep the previous result visible under the new URL until the authorized request completed, including old status/action information. This is a source-confirmed cross-record display risk; no browser reproduction was run. Removed previous-data placeholders from those ID-scoped detail queries so they use each page's existing loading state until the current record response arrives. List pagination placeholders remain unchanged.

### 129. Browser network recovery could leave active data stale when Echo stayed connected

In `spa/src/lib/realtime/provider.tsx` (online/offline handlers) and `spa/src/lib/realtime/status.ts` (transport/channel readiness), the browser `offline` handler set the transport offline, but its `online` handler only marked it reconnecting when Pusher did not report `connected`; it did not invalidate active queries or restore the transport state when the socket still reported connected. A transient HTTP outage can therefore leave the SPA query cache trusted after HTTP recovers, and the UI can remain Offline despite an open socket. The provider now performs authorized active-query catch-up on every browser `online` event. It preserves private-channel readiness only when the Pusher socket is still connected; a real socket disconnect continues to mark channels pending until their subscription callbacks reauthorize them. This is source-confirmed; no offline/online browser scenario was induced.

### 130. Production execution readiness refreshed by interval despite work-order events

`spa/src/pages/production/work-orders/StartProductionDialog.tsx` polls `['production','execution-readiness', woId]` every 30 seconds. The dialog now subscribes through the shared `useRealtimeRecord` hook, and the existing work-order/crew, schedule, attendance, holiday, stock, inspection, NCR and material-handover paths refresh the authorized readiness query. Delta 452 closes the direct equipment gap for work orders with assigned machine/mold before a crew exists (root cause #258). Delta 453 adds exact active work-order refreshes after product routing and inspection-spec changes. The timer remains because readiness changes at clock boundaries and resource/warehouse dependencies are not fully mapped. This is source-confirmed; no dialog/browser trace was captured.

### 131. Entity audit history could show the previous entity during navigation

`spa/src/pages/admin/audit-logs/entity.tsx` keys the query by model type, model ID and page but used unconditional `placeholderData: previous => previous`. Navigating directly between two entity audit URLs could therefore display the previous entity's audit diffs under the next entity's title until the authorized request completed. Placeholder reuse is now limited to page changes for the same model type and ID; switching either identity shows the route's loading state. This is source-confirmed; no browser reproduction was run.


### 132. Admin Users changes had no refresh event for other administrators

Source review of `UserAdminController`, `UserAdminService`, `AdminUserListResource`, and `AdminUserDetailResource` found that create, profile, activation, lock/unlock, password-reset, role, and bulk-role changes updated `users`, but there was no User lifecycle realtime observer/event. The SPA mutation handlers invalidate the acting administrator's own `['admin-users']` / `['admin-user', id]` queries; `UserAccessChanged` targets the affected account's own user channel and does not notify other Admin Users viewers. A second administrator with a list or detail page open could therefore retain old role/status/action state until another refetch. This is source-confirmed; no two-session reproduction was run in this no-testing slice.

Added an after-commit, outbox-backed `AdminUserChanged` refresh signal for relevant User model lifecycle writes. It reaches a private Admin Users list channel gated by `admin.users.manage` and a per-user channel with the same permission plus target-existence check. The payload contains only refresh metadata and opaque IDs. `UserAdminService::bulkChangeRole()` explicitly stages one signal per changed row because its query-builder update bypasses Eloquent observers. The Admin Users page mounts the list subscription in the authenticated shell, and the detail page subscribes to its record channel. Permission-override changes also notify the affected Admin User detail channel so the system-admin-only overrides panel refetches. These are source changes only: producer dispatch, queue delivery, UI freshness, and cross-role access remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 133. Admin User role choices could stay stale after role metadata changed

The Admin User create, list, and detail pages cache `['admin-user-options']` for five minutes. Those options include role names/IDs and active departments. Before this update, Role create/rename/archive/restore/clone and permission-count changes had no shared role-directory signal; the Role page's mutation handlers only invalidated the acting browser. Another administrator could therefore keep stale role choices or role counts during the cache window. This is source-confirmed; no stale option was reproduced in a browser.

Added a content-free, after-commit `AdminRoleChanged` outbox signal for Role Eloquent lifecycle writes and explicitly staged it for `RoleService::syncPermissions()`, whose permissions-version query update bypasses Eloquent observers. The private Role list channel and record channel require an active user with `admin.roles.manage`; the record callback validates the Role hash/existence. AppLayout mounts the list listener for role managers, and the central invalidation map refreshes role list/detail and `['admin-user-options']` queries. This path is source-wired only; event delivery and reference freshness remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 134. Department changes did not invalidate Admin User department options

`['admin-user-options']` also contains active departments and is cached for five minutes. `DepartmentChanged` existed, but `AppLayout` mounted its private list listener only for HR department/directory readers, while the Admin User options API separately permits `admin.users.manage`. A User manager without the HR read grants could leave an Admin User form open and keep a stale department picker until the query refetched. This is source-confirmed; no stale picker was reproduced.

The central Department invalidation map now includes `['admin-user-options']`. The channel callback and shell subscription also allow active `admin.users.manage` viewers, matching the options endpoint's department-name read scope; the event remains a content-free refresh signal and data refetch remains API-authorized. This correction is source-wired only; realtime and UI behavior remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 135. HR record changes could leave embedded Admin User employee data stale

`AdminUserListResource` embeds the linked employee name, employee number and department label; `AdminUserDetailResource` also embeds the position title. Their API is available to `admin.users.manage`, but the Employee and Position list channels require HR feature/read permissions. A user manager without those grants could keep old linked employee fields in an open Admin User row/detail even though the HR event existed. This is source-confirmed; no cross-role stale-row reproduction was run.

Linked Employee writes now stage the existing permission-scoped `AdminUserChanged` event for their account. Position writes stage one coalesced Admin User directory refresh per database transaction when the changed position is assigned to an employee account. Directory refresh payloads contain no Employee or Position IDs and use only the `entity.admin_user.visible` channel, whose callback requires `admin.users.manage`; the central map refetches active User details/lists and employee-candidate queries through authorized APIs. The original HR channels remain scoped to HR permissions. Source wiring only; event delivery and rendered freshness remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 136. The Admin User employee picker reused candidates from the previous department/search

`spa/src/pages/admin/users/create.tsx` keys employee candidates by department and search but used `placeholderData: previous => previous`. When a manager changed department or search, React Query could continue rendering candidates from the previous key until the new API result arrived. The previous result was API-authorized, but it did not match the current picker scope and could be selected under the new department label. This is source-confirmed; no browser reproduction was run.

Removed the previous-query placeholder from this department/search-scoped picker. It now waits for its own API result before displaying candidates. The candidate query remains in-memory and is invalidated by User creation/Employee changes through the central map. No cache was disabled or persisted. Runtime behavior and request impact remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 137. Additional ID-scoped queries retained prior records as placeholder data

Source search found prior-query placeholders on supplier-performance detail (`['purchasing','supplier-performance', id]`) and stock-card history (`item ID + date range + page`). It also found filter placeholders on supplier delivery schedules (`purchase_order_id`) and cost reviews (`linkedReviewId`). A route or filter change could therefore keep the previous supplier, item's ledger, purchase order's schedules, or linked review's results visible while the new API request was pending. This is source-confirmed; no browser reproduction was run.

Supplier-performance detail now waits for its ID-specific response. Stock-card and supplier schedule pagination placeholders are retained only when their record/filter scope is unchanged; cost-review placeholders are retained only while the linked review ID is unchanged. No cache was persisted or disabled. Runtime navigation behavior remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 138. Assignment suggestions could remain visible while the work order or proposal inputs changed

`CrewPanel` keys its assignment suggestion by work order, debounced staffing/date criteria, and generated/manual mode, but used previous data as a placeholder. During route or criteria changes, an old crew/equipment proposal remained rendered. The editor marked itself busy and disabled the apply action, but the previous proposal could still be mistaken for the current one. This is source-confirmed; no shop-floor browser reproduction was run.

The placeholder now survives only an unchanged suggestion key, and the proposal editor is hidden while the controls are unstable or invalid. A fresh current-key API result is required before showing it. Source wiring only; no UI verification was run.


### 139. Dashboard metrics could show a different metric while a new query was loading

`useMetric` keys its query by metric code, period, date range, and machine, but used unconditional `placeholderData: previous => previous`. Changing a dashboard widget or its filters could render the prior metric's value under the new metric's label while the API checked permission and loaded the requested result. If the new metric was forbidden or failed, the previous value could remain beside the error. Removed the cross-key placeholder; a metric now renders only data belonging to its current query key. Same-key TanStack caching and the server's metric permission check remain intact. This is a source-confirmed stale/scope display risk; no UI reproduction or runtime verification was run.


### 140. Auth changes left custom delivery and production retry drafts in browser storage

Auth changes cleared TanStack Query, the PWA cache and `ogami:formdraft:*`, but custom retry state used separate prefixes. In particular, `NotArrivedPanel` persisted a customer-entered delivery report under `ogami:delivery-trace:{deliveryId}` without a user/account component; delivery-attempt, truck-return, stock-return and production retry payloads also persisted outside the auth cleanup. A later user on the shared terminal could retain or reopen browser-local operational data even though the API would still authorize server reads. `clearAuthSessionDrafts()` now deletes the known app-owned localStorage draft/retry prefixes and the sessionStorage pending-production prefix on logout, new login, identity change, terminal 401, and portal-session paths. The customer delivery-report key also includes the portal user ID. A generation guard prevents an unmounting old form's autosave cleanup from recreating a draft after that clear; same-user drafts remain available on an ordinary page reload. This is source-confirmed storage retention; no browser storage-isolation scenario was run. The user-scoped, non-transactional recent-items preference and unrelated consent/preferences remain governed by their own scope/lifecycle.


### 141. Abandoned form drafts had no expiry

`useFormDraftAutosave` stored a timestamp but accepted every parseable draft regardless of age, so a same-user draft could remain eligible for manual restoration indefinitely. Reads now delete malformed/far-future timestamps and entries older than seven days; active drafts continue to refresh their timestamp during normal autosave. This TTL applies only to ordinary form drafts. Pending idempotent operation payloads remain until success, explicit recovery/discard, or the auth-scope cleanup so automatic expiry cannot make an ambiguous write easier to duplicate. Source-confirmed; no expiration behavior or storage impact was runtime-tested.


### 142. Admin dashboard refetches could read the previous server snapshot

The Admin User realtime map invalidates `['dashboard','admin']`, but `AdminDashboardService::admin()` used only the shared dashboard generation in its backend cache key. The source invalidation observer did not register `User`, `Role`, `LoginHistory`, `AuditLog`, or `Alert`, so a client refetch after a user/status or alert-related ping could still hit the pre-change 30-second API snapshot. Alert condition resolution also uses a query-builder bulk update, which bypasses Eloquent observers. This is source-confirmed from the service key, observer registration, event invalidation map, and writers; no two-session stale read was reproduced. The Admin dashboard now has its own generation scope; those five Eloquent sources rotate it after commit and stage a coalesced, content-free `AdminDashboardChanged` signal to the private `dashboard.admin` channel, authorized by `dashboard.admin.view`. Bulk alert resolution rotates that scope and stages the same ping explicitly after its write. The short-lived active-session and queue-health portions still depend on the existing periodic refresh because their source tables are written outside these model lifecycles. Runtime cache freshness and multi-user delivery remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 143. Admin Activity Feed changes had no real-time signal

`spa/src/pages/admin/activity/index.tsx` fetched `['admin','activity',filters]` on a permanent 60-second interval and contained a source comment that websocket support was future work. There was no feed subscription or event invalidation. The database write inventory found three paths: `ActivityFeedService::record()` uses Eloquent `create()` for ordinary events, uses `insertOrIgnore()` for idempotent events (bypassing model observers), and `SalesOrderFulfillmentController` directly creates one fulfillment activity row. The active page could therefore retain an old activity list until its next timer tick; this source failure was not reproduced in two browser sessions, so UI impact remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.

Eloquent appends now stage a transaction-coalesced `ActivityFeedChanged` outbox ping from an `ActivityEvent` observer; the successful idempotent query-builder insert stages the same signal explicitly, while a duplicate insert does not. The event is queued after commit on the dedicated broadcast lane, sends only `refresh`, event ID, and timestamp to private `admin.activity`, and requires the same active-user `admin.activity.view` permission as the API. The SPA's central signal map invalidates the feed query. Its always-on 60-second timer and previous-filter placeholder were removed; the shared realtime provider owns catch-up and visibility-aware degraded polling. The authenticated API remains `private, no-store`, and the QueryClient is cleared at identity/access-scope changes. Source wiring is reviewed; event delivery, access denial, and UI freshness remain `NOT_VERIFIED` because testing was stopped by instruction.


### 144. CRM customer complaints had no shared realtime producer or subscribers

`CustomerComplaintUpdated` only had an email listener and did not implement broadcasting. The complaint model, the separate `Complaint8DReport` model, and the complaint SLA command had no realtime observer; updating an 8D field does not update the parent complaint row, while `markNcrHandoffManual()` uses a query-builder update that bypasses Eloquent observers. The CRM list had no entity subscription, detail mutations only invalidated the acting browser's detail key, and neither CRM nor the customer portal subscribed to complaint changes. Complaint representations embed linked NCR state, but NCR invalidation did not include complaint queries. Both complaint list screens also reused previous-key query data while new filters loaded.

Added a dedicated, transaction-coalesced `CustomerComplaintChanged` outbox signal. Complaint and 8D report observers cover Eloquent writes from CRM, the customer portal service and the scheduled SLA path; the direct handoff query-builder writer now stages the signal explicitly. Linked NCR writes stage the same complaint refresh. The event is queued after commit on the broadcasts lane and sends only `refresh`, event ID, timestamp, version and action. Its three private channel paths are the CRM detail record, the CRM complaint list scope (`crm.complaints.view`), and the affected customer account using the customer portal guard. All recipients fetch their representation through their existing permission- or tenant-scoped API. The central SPA map covers complaint details, lists, linked NCR invalidations and dashboard families; portal complaint/dashboard queries use the customer-scoped channel, and an open imperative 8D report modal refetches through that same account channel. Successful internal mutations seed the acting detail cache from the API response. CRM and portal list screens no longer show prior-filter results while a new query loads, and static complaint options now have a separate key so record events do not refetch them.

The CRM 8D write path now also returns an opaque row version and accepts an optional `expected_version`. The SPA sends the version it edited; the service compares it under the existing parent/report row locks and returns HTTP 409 for stale writes. The editor preserves local text, blocks further edits/finalization on a detected external change, and requires an explicit reload. Older callers that omit the optional version retain their existing request contract. The complaint resource also provides per-viewer manage permission and state-derived `available_actions` for 8D edit/finalize, NCR retry, resolve, and close; the detail UI now renders those actions from the API response. Existing services still revalidate each action. This guards the 8D write race but is not a project-wide If-Match implementation.

This confirms a source-level missing-producer/subscriber path, not an observed live user failure. No CRM or customer-portal two-context scenario was run. Queue delivery, channel denial, actor response reconciliation, filters, SLA behavior, conflict UX and linked-NCR refresh remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. Server-provided actions are now present for this complaint detail, but the standard stale-state 409 response contract for lifecycle transitions and the project-wide `available_actions` contract remain incomplete.

### 145. Asset QR query data could remain fresh forever in the SPA

`AssetDetailPage` set `staleTime: Infinity` on `['asset', id, 'qr']`. Asset mutations send authorized refresh events that invalidate the active `['asset']` query family, but a client that missed an event while the detail was unmounted could revisit the record and reuse an indefinitely fresh QR payload. Its URL is hash-based, but the response also contains the asset code and display name. Removed the infinite stale time; the mapped asset query now has `staleTime: 0` and forces an authorized refetch on mount, while the asset event still invalidates active views immediately. This is source-confirmed cache behavior, not a reproduced browser incident; no request-count or browser measurement was run.

### 146. Approval type changes could keep actionable cards from the previous type visible

The approval board query key includes `kind`, but its unconditional `placeholderData: previous => previous` kept the old board rendered while a different approval type loaded. That could leave actions for one record family visible under another selected family. Placeholder reuse now checks that the previous query has the same `kind`; changing `kind` renders only that key's data/loading state. The query cache remains keyed by kind and authenticated API refetch remains authoritative. This is source-confirmed filter-scope behavior, not a reproduced user incident; no UI trace was run.

### 147. Product changes had no shared realtime producer or client subscribers

`Product` had no registered realtime observer, Product-specific private channels, or SPA list/detail subscription. The central invalidation map already knew the Product query family, but no Product signal reached it. Product CRUD, financial decisions, image updates, restore and selling-price writes mutate the Eloquent Product; customer access also changes the Product-to-Customer pivot, which can change without a Product model event when visibility itself is unchanged. Other Product viewers and customers with an open order catalog could therefore retain old product fields, prices, availability, or action state. Product lifecycle writes now stage a coalesced after-commit `ProductChanged` through the outbox; the customer-access service explicitly stages after pivot sync. Private Product detail/list events carry refresh metadata only, and the customer portal receives a separate account-scoped `CustomerCatalogChanged` ping before refetching through the account-authorized API. The central map covers CRM Product queries and generic Product option keys. A later dependency audit added Forecasting list, shared demand panel, and accuracy label queries to that map (see root cause 171). This is source-confirmed, not a two-user reproduction; delivery, list/detail rendering, authorization denial, and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 148. Customer order catalog search/date changes could display prior-key products and prices

`portal/customer/orders/new.tsx` keyed its catalog request by debounced search and delivery date but unconditionally reused the previous query as placeholder data. While the next catalog/price request was loading, the page could show results calculated for the old search or delivery date. Selected line objects also kept the price from when the user added the Product, so invalidating a quote could temporarily expose that local snapshot as the current amount. The keys omitted portal user/account identity and relied on auth cleanup to prevent reuse after a principal change. The previous-query placeholder is removed; catalog and order-quote keys now include the current portal user and customer scope, and the account-scoped signal invalidates catalog, quote, and portal pricing queries after Product or Price Agreement changes. When no current server quote exists, line prices and totals display as pending/unavailable instead of falling back to the stored line price. Source-confirmed cache/display risk; no browser trace or cache-isolation scenario was run.

### 149. Price Agreement writes could leave internal agreement pages and customer prices stale

Price Agreement create/update/archive/restore writes changed agreement terms that feed `CustomerPortalService::catalog()` and the live order quote, but there was no PriceAgreement realtime observer, private detail/list channel, or SPA subscription. The existing `PriceAgreement::saved` hook also attached the customer to the Product access pivot without publishing a catalog refresh. A second approver/editor could therefore retain old agreement values, and the affected customer could keep seeing the old price or availability until an unrelated refetch. A registered `PriceAgreementRealtimeObserver` now stages a coalesced after-commit `PriceAgreementChanged` event to the CRM detail/list channels and a separate customer-account catalog refresh for the old/new agreement customer. The list/detail API still performs permission checks on each refetch. This is source-confirmed; no agreement mutation, channel authorization, queue delivery, or cross-user UI scenario was executed.

### 150. CRM and Purchasing list filters could display rows from the previous filter

Product, Price Agreement, Sales Order, Purchase Order, Purchase Request, RFQ, GRN, NCR, Leave Request, and Work Order list queries used unconditional previous-data placeholders. Since their keys include status/search/archive and other filters, switching filters could leave rows from the prior result actionable under the new selection until the request completed. These pages now keep placeholder rows only when the complete query key matches except for the `page` field in that list's filter object. Filter, archive scope, sort, page size, and other key changes show the current query's loading/data state. This is source-confirmed; no filter-navigation browser trace was run.

### 151. Cross-filter placeholder reuse remained in operational and account-sensitive lists

A follow-up source scan found the same pattern across CRM, procurement, inventory, quality, attendance, HR, payroll, accounting, portal, production, maintenance, admin, driver, asset and returns screens. An unconditional previous-query placeholder can keep old statuses, employee/payroll rows, account schedules, inventory quantities or approval actions under newly selected filters while the new request is pending. The shared `sameQueryExceptPage` rule now gates page transitions at 78 query sites in 75 SPA page modules: all other query-key parts (including archive, search, role-scope and status filters) must match. Cross-period reports, search suggestions, entity-tree scopes and other non-paginated changing queries now wait for their current-key response. The remaining explicitly scoped placeholders compare the relevant entity/account/selection fields. The QueryClient and exact-key caching remain enabled. This was source-audited; no browser navigation, user-switch or request-latency trace was run.


### 152. Alert mutations left the Action Center stale between its page timer ticks

The Action Center listens for the private, content-free `BadgesChanged` signal, and the central invalidation map refreshes Action Center, alert-list, unread-count, approval, and dashboard query families from that signal. However, `Alert` was not registered with `BadgeInvalidationObserver`; `AlertEngineService::resolveMissingConditions()` and `pruneResolved()` also use query-builder updates/deletes that bypass Eloquent observers. Thus alert raises, dismiss/read changes, scheduled bulk resolution, and pruning could leave other users' open Action Center or alert views stale until an unrelated event or the page's 60-second interval. This is source-confirmed; no two-user failure was reproduced for Alert.

`Alert` is now observed after commit, and only user-visible alert field changes stage the badge signal, avoiding broadcasts for critical-email bookkeeping updates. The bulk resolve and prune writers now transactionally rotate badge and Admin Dashboard cache generations, then stage durable `BadgesChanged` and `AdminDashboardChanged` outbox events after the query-builder mutation. The Action Center page-owned 60-second interval was removed; its signal refetches authorized API data, while the shared provider owns degraded-mode catch-up. The API now reports the next visible due/priority transition, upcoming production planned-end, or hidden snooze expiry, and the page schedules one one-shot invalidation at that server-provided boundary instead of polling. This path, event delivery, cache freshness, and cross-role UI behavior remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; no runtime test or latency/cache measurement was run.

### 153. Dashboard polling inventory missed source call sites, and hooked pages still duplicated refresh traffic

A fresh static scan of non-test SPA source found 19 active `refetchInterval` declarations before this slice, while `00-polling-call-sites.csv` listed only 10 total rows including one already-removed Action Center timer. Six dashboard query timers were redundant with mounted, permission-scoped realtime hooks: Plant Manager/PPC use the production dashboard signal; Purchasing subscribes to PR/PO list changes; Quality subscribes to Inspection/NCR and quality-output changes; Warehouse subscribes to inventory-stock changes. Removed those six timers and changed their stale “refreshes every 60s” subtitles to “Live updates.” The shared RealtimeProvider still owns disconnected/auth-failure catch-up.

The inventory now contains 13 current SPA polling declarations and 7 historical removed entries. Current source hits cover recovery monitors, backup operations, admin sessions/health/dashboard, HR and generic widget dashboards, metric cadence, and chain bottlenecks. Several are important state monitors without a complete event path yet; they remain active and are explicitly listed instead of being removed without producer coverage. This is a source-audit result, not a reproduced user-visible stale failure. No tests, builds, API calls, browser runs, or performance measurements were run; the event paths for the six changed dashboards and the remaining pollers still need runtime verification.

**Current inventory correction (2026-11-02):** A fresh non-test scan finds 10 source declarations: nine in currently referenced pages/components and one in the unreferenced `RoleDashboard` component. The CSV has 20 total rows: those 10 source hits plus 10 removed historical entries. Thus “13 current / 7 historical” above records the inventory state at the time of delta 153 and is superseded by this scan. It remains a static source classification; it does not demonstrate runtime polling or freshness.

**Delta 359 update:** The 60-second `useMetric` network interval was removed after source review confirmed that the two registered `live` metrics are backed by ReturnRequest and WorkOrder events, each with a post-commit backend `AnalyticsCache` flush and a frontend `badges_changed` invalidation path. The current source scan now finds nine `refetchInterval` declarations: eight in referenced pages/components and one unreferenced RoleDashboard declaration. The 20-row CSV has eleven historical removals. A focused MetricPanel test confirms no timer-driven API reads across 120 seconds and that its stale badge still updates from a local one-shot timer. No socket/browser delivery or multi-user runtime proof was collected.


### 154. HR dashboard refetches could still return a cached snapshot and lacked one shared source signal

`HrDashboardService::hr()` caches permission-gated workforce, attendance, leave, holiday, clearance, profile-update, and payroll panels for 30 seconds. The existing generation map covered Attendance, Employee, LeaveRequest, PayrollPeriod, and Payroll, but did not cover Clearance, SalaryAdjustment, or ProfileUpdateRequest; Department changes also did not rotate this snapshot. The HR dashboard page polled every 60 seconds, while shell subscriptions covered only some of its source families. A query refetch after a missing event could therefore read an old server snapshot, and the page's timer duplicated event-driven reads. This was confirmed by tracing the cache key, source queries, observer map, registered observers, and frontend subscriptions. No dedicated two-user HR dashboard reproduction was run.

`DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` now retires the HR dashboard generation for Clearance, SalaryAdjustment, ProfileUpdateRequest, Department, and Holiday writes, alongside the existing source mappings. It also stages one coalesced `HrDashboardChanged` outbox event per transaction to private `dashboard.hr`; channel access requires the same `dashboard.hr.view` grant as the API, and the payload contains only a refresh flag, event ID, and timestamp. The authenticated dashboard refetch still applies each panel's own permission checks. The HR cache uses a v4 key scoped by viewer/access scope and Manila business date, so the prior v3 key ages out under its 30-second TTL and a new date cannot reuse yesterday's time-based panels. The SPA now refetches from the shared signal and schedules one server-provided next-midnight invalidation instead of a fixed 60-second timer. Source wiring is recorded in the affected matrix rows; event delivery, cache freshness under a live write, two-user UI behavior, API request reduction, and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 155. Attendance writes did not refresh all authorized viewers

Attendance rows change through manual CRUD, biometric import, app punches, leave approval/cancellation, recomputation, and explicit archive/restore. There was no Attendance broadcaster or row/list subscription before this slice, and the self-service employee dashboard only had a cache-generation rotation, not a signal that would make the owner refetch. The new Attendance model observer stages a content-free after-commit outbox event for create/update/delete/restore; the event routes through the permission- and row-scoped attendance channels, authorized active-work-order channels for crew readiness, and the exact employee owner's private channel. The SPA map refreshes attendance, DTR dashboard and self-service query families. Large DTR imports still commit rows separately, so per-transaction coalescing does not prevent a high-volume event burst. This is source-confirmed and source-wired only; no Attendance mutation, two-user scenario, queue delivery, cache freshness, or UI behavior was executed under the current stop-testing instruction.


### 156. Linked production-readiness pings only reached individual operators

Machine, mold, in-process inspection, NCR, and material-handover changes already used `AssignedWorkOrderRefreshService`, but `AssignedWorkOrderChanged` only targeted `production.operator.{user}`. A supervisor or other authorized work-order viewer could keep stale execution-readiness and crew data even while connected to the work-order channel; the service also returned early when an active crew had no linked operator account. The event now carries the work-order hash only for private channel routing, publishes a content-free refresh on the API-authorized `chain.work_order.{hash}` channel even when there are no operator recipients, and retains the personal operator channels. `useRealtimeRecord('work_order')` invalidates the active readiness/crew queries from that signal. The Start Production interval remains because time-based and other upstream readiness changes are not fully mapped. Source-confirmed; no live event or UI scenario was run under the current instruction.


### 157. Child work-order updates did not refresh parent readiness

`WorkOrderService::productionReadiness()` derives a parent work order's readiness from child status and `quantity_good`, but child `WorkOrderStatusChanged` and `WorkOrderOutputRecorded` events only targeted the child's production/detail/list and operator channels. A parent readiness dialog could therefore retain a blocked/ready result after subassembly completion or output changes. Both content-free events now add the parent's exact `chain.work_order` private channel when a parent exists, and the SPA work-order hook handles those event names with the same central invalidation map. The actor's start/resume action still revalidates all dependencies in the API. This is source-confirmed and source-wired; no live subassembly transition or multi-user UI scenario was run under the current instruction.


### 158. Shift-calendar changes notified operators but did not refresh readiness viewers

Employee assignment/working-day changes in `ShiftAssignmentService` and scheduling-rule updates in `ShiftService` call `WorkOrderAssignmentService::notifyStaffingChange()` for active crews. That path only created notifications for production owners; open execution-readiness queries on the work-order detail channel did not refetch. The staffing notification path now stages `AssignedWorkOrderChanged` for each affected active work order, and that event routes to the authorized work-order channel as well as operator channels. Query results remain sourced from the authorized readiness API. Source-confirmed and source-wired; no shift change, event delivery, or multi-user UI scenario was run under the current instruction. Other time-bound/upstream readiness inputs still keep the degraded/fallback inventory open.


### 159. Holiday changes did not refresh production readiness viewers

The work-order execution-readiness API evaluates crew availability against the holiday calendar. `HolidayChanged` previously reached calendar/attendance subscribers only, so a connected production manager could continue seeing readiness computed under the old calendar. The same content-free event now also targets a private `production.readiness` channel whose authorization requires `modules.production` and `production.work_orders.lifecycle`, matching access to the readiness action. The work-order hook invalidates readiness/crew queries and refetches through the existing API. Source-confirmed and source-wired; no live holiday/readiness transition was run under the current no-testing instruction.


### 160. Supplier PO pages did not receive internal chain refreshes

`SupplierResponseService::respond()` and `resolve()` already staged the canonical `ChainStepAdvanced` outbox event for purchase-order state changes, but `ChainStepAdvanced::broadcastOn()` only targeted ERP chain/detail/list channels. Supplier portal users can subscribe only through their authenticated `supplier.vendor.{vendorHashId}` channel, and the supplier layout had no PO listener. The supplier's own response mutation invalidated its detail query after success, but did not seed the updated PO from the response; its frontend API type also described the response-history resource as a full PO resource. Chain events for PO, GRN, and Bill now add the owning vendor's private channel, using the existing active-vendor authorization callback. The payload remains the existing refresh-only event with an opaque record hash; the portal centrally invalidates only the mapped PO/receipt or bill query families and refetches through the vendor-scoped API. Supplier PO, invoice, statement, and dashboard query keys now include vendor identity. Supplier response/acknowledge endpoints add the fresh authorized PO resource to the successful API response, and the actor seeds detail cache immediately from that value. Shipment writes also stage a PO refresh when shipment data changes without moving PO status. Source-confirmed and source-wired only; no supplier/internal two-context trace, event-delivery run, response-contract test, or latency/cache measurement was run under the stop-testing instruction. All affected matrix outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 161. Supplier shipment edits did not notify other PO viewers

`SupplierShipmentService::write()` saves tracking, ETA, shipment-line, dispatch-document, and expected-draft-GRN changes under the purchase-order lock, but shipment edits did not necessarily update the PO row or stage a PO `ChainStepAdvanced` event. Standalone document uploads had the same gap. The supplier actor invalidated its own detail page, while internal PO detail/activity viewers and other supplier tabs had no reason to refetch. Successful shipment create/update and new standalone document upload paths now stage the PO chain refresh in the same transaction after related writes; an idempotent duplicate document replay does not emit another ping. The existing after-commit outbox fans the content-free event to ERP PO viewers and the owning supplier vendor channel. Supplier actor mutations also invalidate mapped PO list/dashboard data from their successful API response. Source-confirmed and source-wired only; no two-context shipment/document mutation, event delivery, or UI latency run was performed; matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 162. Manual backend cache rotation did not invalidate active SPA query caches

`CacheGroupInvalidationService` initially rotated only `DashboardCacheGeneration` for the `dashboards` group, although `BadgeService` keys its snapshots with the separate `badges.version` marker. The cache-group refetch event could therefore make clients refetch the badge API and still receive the previous server snapshot. The service now rotates both markers after commit, and reports failure if either write fails. The group also previously emitted no realtime signal and the Settings mutation handler left active client QueryClient entries untouched; `CacheGroupInvalidated` now targets an allowlisted private channel, is durably staged with the audit row, and the central map invalidates matching query families while the actor invalidates from the API response. Each refetch uses its normal permission-scoped API. This is source-confirmed; focused cache-generation and channel-permission regressions pass, but no live cache clear or two-user incident was reproduced. Delivery, runtime API freshness, and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 163. Admin backup status and available actions depended on page polling

`AdminBackupsPage` queried `['admin','backups']` every five seconds while an operation was active and every 30 seconds otherwise, with no RealtimeProvider subscription. `BackupService` creates `BackupOperation` rows and queue jobs update visible status, artifacts, metadata, and errors; those values control the restore/verify actions in the list. Other authorized viewers therefore had a source-confirmed stale window between timer ticks. The endpoint requires `admin.backups.view`; this establishes the scope for a private refresh channel. This is source evidence, not a two-browser reproduction.

`BackupOperationRealtimeObserver` now stages one coalesced `AdminBackupsChanged` outbox event for create/delete and visible-field updates, after commit; if a restored schema lacks the outbox table, it logs and queues the same after-commit event directly. Scheduled full-backup manifests, newly adopted legacy manifests, and stale-operation reconciliation queue the same content-free event. The private `admin.backups` channel requires an active user with `admin.backups.view`; the SPA invalidates the shared list key and refetches through the authorized API. The actor continues to update from mutation responses. The page-owned timer was removed; reconnect/focus catch-up and the shared provider's degraded fallback remain. The separate break-glass recovery page keeps its two-second token-auth monitor because it must operate without a normal ERP user identity and may run while the database is unavailable. No test, build, API call, browser scenario, queue run, or latency measurement was performed under the current stop-testing instruction; all matrix outcomes remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 164. Automation Recovery listener runs relied on a 30-second timer

`ChainRecoveryPage` originally polled `['chain','listener-runs', attention, page, search]` every 30 seconds and had no realtime subscription. Queue processing, retry, completion, failure and business-outcome transitions are written with `DB::table('chain_listener_runs')` in `ChainListenerRunService`, bypassing Eloquent observers; manual replay and resolution update the same records through `ChainListenerRecoveryService`. The list endpoint is permission-gated by `dashboard.chain_recovery.view`, so authorized operators could see stale queue status, outcome, and resolution actions between polls. The source gap is now wired through explicit queue-lifecycle staging plus an Eloquent observer, a content-free after-commit outbox event, the private permission-gated `chain.listener-runs` channel, and the central SPA invalidation map; the page-owned interval was removed. This is source-confirmed and source-wired, but no two-session transition or live delivery was verified.




### 165. Active Sessions termination did not refresh other administrators

`GET /api/v1/admin/sessions` reads database session rows, and the SPA polls every 30 seconds. `DELETE /api/v1/admin/sessions/{session}` deleted the row using the query builder, so no observer or broadcast ran; other administrators could retain its row and Terminate button until the next refresh, although the endpoint would reject a second delete with 404. This is confirmed by source tracing; the two-admin UI failure was not reproduced.

Successful deletion now stages `AdminSessionsChanged` in the same database transaction as the delete and audit row. The durable outbox dispatches the content-free signal after commit to private `admin.sessions`, which requires the same active `admin.settings.manage` permission as the API. The SPA centrally invalidates the sessions query, and the acting administrator still updates from the mutation response. The session ID is a bearer credential and is deliberately omitted from the event payload. The 30-second refresh remains because ordinary requests update `last_activity` outside the explicit termination endpoint. Runtime authorization, delivery and two-admin rendering remain `NOT_VERIFIED` under the stop-testing instruction.


### 166. Finance chain-bottleneck widget missed source events

The Finance dashboard mounted `ChainBottleneckWidget` with a user/role/audience-scoped query and a 60-second interval. Its page already subscribed to invoice, bill, payroll-period, and journal-entry entity lists, but the central invalidation map did not include `['chain-bottlenecks']` for those sources. Accepted GRN chain events were private to the GRN record and list channels; Finance readers without `inventory.view` could not subscribe, even though the Finance audience monitors accepted GRNs without a bill. Source review confirmed these missing invalidation edges. The central map now includes the financial source families, and GRN chain pings target a new private `dashboard.finance` channel gated by the Finance dashboard permission. The 60-second refresh remains for SLA thresholds that can become overdue without a write. The endpoint now returns `generated_at`, which the widget displays as an “As of” timestamp. No runtime/two-user reproduction or delivery measurement was run.


### 167. Chain recovery signals could refetch a stale dashboard backlog count

`DashboardWidgetDataService::summaries()` caches `chain.recovery_backlog` for 30 seconds under a per-user/per-scope key that includes the `widget-summary:chain.recovery_backlog` generation. The queue lifecycle and outcome paths wrote `chain_listener_runs` with query-builder operations and staged `ChainListenerRunsChanged`; the SPA central map invalidated only `['chain','listener-runs']`. A dashboard viewer with `dashboard.chain_recovery.view` could therefore miss event-driven widget refetch, and a client-side refetch without generation retirement could still read the previous cached count. This is source-confirmed; no stale-count browser reproduction was run.

`ChainListenerRunRealtimeService::stage()` now asks `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` to rotate the scoped backlog generation before staging the outbox refresh. This service seam covers both the query-builder queue lifecycle/outcome paths and the Eloquent recovery observer without registering a second cache observer. `chain_listener_runs_changed` now invalidates both the authorized recovery list and configured dashboard widget query family. The existing 30-second dashboard widget fallback remains because the full widget/source map is still under audit. Regression coverage was added but not run; cache rotation ordering, delivery, and rendered dashboard freshness remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 168. Configurable dashboard widgets missed production source pings and forecast cache retirement

`MachineChanged` already broadcast its refresh-only `chain.refresh` signal on `production.dashboard`, and the central invalidation map could refresh production-specific dashboards. The hook was only mounted by dedicated Production, PPC, and Plant Manager pages; the shared configurable dashboard shell had no subscriber. A viewer with only a configured machine/capacity widget therefore waited on the generic 60-second widget interval. The `forecast.capacity` scalar and rich payloads also used 30-second generation-keyed caches that were not retired by Machine, WorkOrder, or monthly KpiSnapshot writes. Similar source-to-generation omissions existed for other scalar/rich forecasting widgets. This is confirmed by source tracing; no dashboard staleness reproduction was run.

`DashboardShell` now mounts the existing production signal hook for shared/default/role dashboard consumers, and `production_dashboard_changed` centrally invalidates `['dashboard','widget-data']`. Channel authorization and the hook now both include `dashboard.company.view` behind the existing Production-feature gate; the event remains a content-free signal and refetches use the viewer's API. The scoped generation map now covers the source models for forecast headcount, revenue, defect rate, cash flow, capacity, and attendance outputs, including rich payload generations where those providers exist. The generic dashboard interval remains while the full widget map is audited. Regression cases were added but not run; channel authorization, event delivery, cache ordering, rendered state, and request cost remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 169. Purchasing supplier-risk snapshot missed inventory and demand-forecast sources

`PurchasingDashboardService::purchasing()` caches its full per-user, permission-scoped payload for 30 seconds, including `supplier_risk` for viewers with `forecasting.view`. That panel calls `StockOutProjectionService::projectAll()`, which reads `items` and `stock_levels`, recent `stock_movements`, `demand_forecasts`, and product part numbers. Before this slice, `dashboard:purchasing` had no source-generation mapping for Item, Product, or DemandForecast; stock movement/reservation invalidation retired Warehouse and scalar stock widgets but not the Purchasing snapshot. The Purchasing dashboard subscribed to PR/PO entity lists only. Users whose panel access was limited to forecasting permission could therefore retain the cached supplier-risk result without a matching event. This is source-confirmed; no stale supplier-risk browser reproduction was run.

Item and Product writes now rotate the Purchasing generation in the common observer and stage a coalesced Purchasing dashboard signal; stock-ledger/reservation writes retire the same generation and stage that signal. DemandForecast writes rotate the same generation before the dedicated DemandForecastChanged event is staged, avoiding a duplicate PurchasingDashboardChanged event. It uses private `dashboard.purchasing`, authorized by the active `dashboard.purchasing.view` permission used by the API route. The payload contains only refresh, event ID, and timestamp; the SPA invalidates the Purchasing dashboard query and refetches the viewer-scoped API. The 30-second cache is retained as a bound for unobserved writers; no cache family was removed. DemandForecast now also has its own private list/detail event path (see root cause 170); no two-user runtime verification was run. Regression sources were added but not run. Direct/quiet and non-ledger stock-level writers, runtime channel denial/delivery, two-user freshness, cache hits, and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 170. Forecast recompute batches could emit one refresh per row

`ForecastingService::recomputeBatch()` and `DemandForecastController::recompute()` call `compute()` for each product/customer/month, and `compute()` opens and commits an individual transaction. The dashboard signal was transaction-coalesced, so a scheduled run or multi-month API recompute could still emit a burst of refreshes—one per changed forecast row. This is source-confirmed; the scheduled job and API path were not run and no broadcast count was measured.

`DemandForecastRealtimeService::coalesceBatch()` now suppresses per-row forecast events during scheduled and API recompute batches and stages one aggregate `DemandForecastChanged` event after each batch completes. That content-free event reaches the authorized Forecasting list and Purchasing dashboard channels; per-record writes also reach the matching Forecasting record channel. If a later write fails after earlier forecast transactions committed, the `finally` flush still stages the refresh for those partial writes. Per-record manual forecast writes remain transaction-coalesced and immediate. Cache generations continue rotating after each committed source write; the dashboard's 30-second payload bound remains. No test or performance measurement was run; scheduled runtime behavior and event count remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 171. Product changes left Product labels stale in Forecasting read models

`DemandForecastResource` includes the related Product's `part_number` and `name`, `DemandForecastPanel` caches that resource under `['forecasting','demand', ...]`, and `ForecastingService::accuracyByProduct()` returns Product labels under the accuracy query. Product writes already publish `ProductChanged` to the private `entity.product.visible` channel, which admits Forecasting viewers, but the central Product invalidation entry covered only CRM Product queries. An open Forecasting demand list, shared dashboard panel, or per-product accuracy table could therefore retain old labels until another refresh trigger. This is source-confirmed; no browser scenario was run.

The central Product invalidation map now includes the Forecasting list, shared demand panel, and accuracy product queries. Event handling still refetches through the current user's authorized API. Regression coverage was added but not run; delivery and rendered freshness remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 172. Customer lifecycle writes had no realtime path or dashboard generation retirement

Both Accounting and CRM expose Customer create/update/delete/restore endpoints through `CustomerController`; `CustomerService` uses Eloquent writes, including a direct restore route. No Customer broadcaster, observer, or SPA list/record subscription existed. The Customer resource is joined into other reads: the Forecasting customer selector and DemandForecast resource include Customer names, Finance's cached top-overdue panel and Warehouse's cached delivery panel read Customer names, and CRM/Accounting lists use Customer values in linked records. An open directory, selector, portal profile, or cached dashboard could therefore keep stale names or an old active/deleted state after a Customer change. This is source-confirmed; no two-user reproduction was run.

Customer create/update/delete/restore now flows through `CustomerRealtimeObserver` → `CustomerRealtimeService` → durable after-commit `CustomerChanged`. Refresh-only private channels are scoped to directory/Forecasting read permission, the Customer record read permission, or the exact active customer portal account. The SPA central map refreshes Customer directory/detail, cross-module lookup and label queries; PortalLayout listens on the account-specific profile channel. `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` now rotates Finance and Warehouse generations for Customer writes before their API refetches. Multi-row Customer creation in one PostgreSQL transaction coalesces to one list-only signal; updates/deletes/restores retain record and portal channels. Regression cases were added but not run. Direct SQL/quiet writers, channel denial/delivery, role-specific rendering, and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 173. Inventory summary cache omitted linked PR, PO, GRN and setting sources

`InventoryDashboardService::compute()` caches one summary in `inv:dashboard:summary:v{generation}` for 30 seconds. Besides stock and Item thresholds, it contains the count of pending GRNs, the latest open PR/PO linked to each low-stock Item, lot-specific held quantities from DeliveryStockReservation, and the configured consumption-history window. Before this change, the independent generation was retired by stock/reservation publication and Item handling only. PR, PO or GRN events could invalidate the SPA query and immediately refetch the endpoint while its backend cache still served the pre-change snapshot. A change to `inventory.dashboard.consumption_history_days` had the same gap. DeliveryStockReservation rows are created/consumed/released in DeliveryStockReservationService and the stock movement/reservation paths stage StockMovementCompleted or StockReservationChanged in the same transaction; the existing publisher retires InventoryDashboardCache before the private stock ping. Warehouse hierarchy sources and writer paths are covered in cause 174. This is source-confirmed; no stale dashboard response was captured in a browser.

`DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` now retires the Inventory summary generation after commit for GoodsReceiptNote, Item, PurchaseRequest/PurchaseRequestItem and PurchaseOrder/PurchaseOrderItem lifecycle writes. Because this observer is registered before the corresponding realtime observers/services, the cache rotation callback is registered before the outbox dispatch callback. The Item invalidation moved from the later badge observer to this earlier shared observer, preventing an Item event from racing the cache retirement. `SettingsService` now retires the same generation when the consumption-history setting changes. The 30-second cache remains; no cache layer was disabled. No tests, API request, browser scenario, Redis inspection or performance measurement was run, so runtime ordering and visible freshness remain NOT_VERIFIED.


### 174. Warehouse hierarchy writes could leave stock availability and warehouse views stale

`InventoryDashboardService::compute()` reads `Warehouse.is_active`, `WarehouseZone.zone_type`, and each `WarehouseLocation.is_active` / `is_blocked` through `StockLocationSummaryService::availability()`. Those values change usable stock and low-stock classifications. `WarehouseDashboardService` caches low-stock panels under a separate per-user `dashboard:warehouse` generation, and `WarehouseMapService` returns location tree/bin occupancy. Before this slice, `WarehouseService` changed the three hierarchy models with Eloquent transactions, but none had a realtime observer or `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` registration. A client refetch could hit the existing 30-second Inventory or Warehouse snapshot, and other users had no warehouse-layout event to trigger tree/map/dashboard refetches. This is confirmed by source tracing; no two-user reproduction was run.

`DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` now observes Warehouse, WarehouseZone, and WarehouseLocation lifecycle changes, rotating both the Inventory summary generation and the Warehouse dashboard generation after commit. `WarehouseLayoutRealtimeObserver` stages one refresh-only `WarehouseLayoutChanged` event through the durable after-commit outbox, coalesced per PostgreSQL transaction. Its private channel is authorized for the same inventory tree/map, Return Management location, and Warehouse dashboard read grants; APIs continue to enforce each user's own access. The AppLayout signal map invalidates warehouse trees/maps/bin details, stock-count/transfer/picking/return lookups, Inventory and Warehouse dashboards, and linked stock-availability views. `InventoryStockChanged` also reaches a separate manager-authorized private map channel so stock movements refresh occupancy without exposing stock values.
