The existing caches remain enabled. Actor/cross-user screen behavior, channel decisions, and generation ordering remain NOT_VERIFIED because runtime checks were not run.


### 175. Warehouse dashboard source writes could refetch the same cached snapshot

`WarehouseDashboardService::warehouse()` stores a 30-second per-user, permission-scoped snapshot under `dashboard:warehouse:v3:{generation}:{userId}:{permissionScope}`. The snapshot reads pending GRNs, stock issues, low-stock Item and approved-supplier labels, pending TransferOrders, incoming PurchaseOrder/PurchaseOrderItem/Vendor rows, outgoing Delivery/SalesOrder/Customer rows, and Warehouse/Zone/Location occupancy. Before this slice, the separate generation was rotated for Customer and warehouse hierarchy changes, and for stock-ledger writes, but not for the remaining source models. Several had no warehouse-dashboard refresh signal. The SPA could invalidate/refetch and still receive the old generation-keyed response for up to 30 seconds. This is source-confirmed by the service query and generation map; no browser response was captured.

`DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` now rotates `dashboard:warehouse` after commit for GoodsReceiptNote, Item, Inspection, NonConformanceReport, ReturnRequest, PurchaseRequest/Item, PurchaseOrder/Item, Delivery, SalesOrder, Vendor, ApprovedSupplier, and TransferOrder writes. Non-hierarchy source changes stage a transaction-coalesced, content-free `WarehouseDashboardChanged` outbox signal on the private `dashboard.warehouse` channel. Warehouse hierarchy continues through `WarehouseLayoutChanged`. Stock movement/reservation writes use `InventoryStockChanged`, which now has a separate private dashboard audience; the Warehouse dashboard page subscribes with its dashboard permission even when the user lacks `inventory.view`. The 30-second cache remains enabled. Query-builder writes that bypass Eloquent, channel auth/delivery, cache ordering and live freshness remain NOT_VERIFIED.


### 176. Transfer-order create and cancel had no record/list refresh producer

`TransferOrderService::create()`, `execute()` and `cancel()` mutate `TransferOrder` through Eloquent, but the model had no realtime observer or event. Execute also produces stock movement signals, while create and cancel do not, so the existing stock signal could not cover every transfer-order state transition. The SPA Transfer Orders page cached its list and active detail but had no record subscription; remote create/cancel could leave the pending count and actionable state stale. This is source-confirmed; no two-user browser reproduction was run.

`TransferOrderRealtimeObserver` now stages one minimal `TransferOrderChanged` signal through the durable after-commit outbox. Its private detail channel validates the hash and row and both channels require `modules.inventory` plus `inventory.adjust`, matching the list/detail API gate. The AppLayout subscribes to the authorized list channel and the page subscribes to the selected record; the central invalidation map refreshes list/detail, linked stock, and Warehouse dashboard queries. Create/execute responses update the acting user's detail cache immediately; cancellation keeps the existing 204 endpoint and refetches after success. No test, two-user scenario, event delivery or UI latency was verified.


### 177. Quality dashboard inputs could change without retiring its cached snapshot or notifying viewers

`QualityDashboardService::quality()` caches a per-user, permission-scoped snapshot for 30 seconds. Its source queries include certificate-of-conformance `DeliveryProof` rows, GRN and work-order counts, Product labels, and `WorkOrderDefect` counts joined to `DefectType`. Before this change, `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver::SCOPES_BY_MODEL` omitted these inputs from `dashboard:quality` (and several were absent from the observer registration list). A client refetch could therefore read the old generation until TTL expiry. The Quality dashboard also listened to the private `quality.dashboard` channel only for `WorkOrderOutputRecorded`; delivery-proof writes emitted a delivery-chain refresh, which did not reach the Quality dashboard audience. A viewer without the individual entity permission could miss relevant Inspection/NCR list refreshes as well. These source paths are confirmed from the cached service queries, DeliveryService proof lifecycle, cache-scope map, event channels and Quality dashboard subscriptions; this was not browser-reproduced under the current no-testing instruction.

The shared dashboard observer now retires the Quality generation for DeliveryProof, GoodsReceiptNote, WorkOrder, Product, DefectType and WorkOrderDefect lifecycle writes, alongside the already-mapped Inspection/NCR/output inputs. It stages a coalesced `QualityDashboardChanged` refresh-only signal after the generation retirement callback has been registered. The private `quality.dashboard` channel already requires `modules.production` and `dashboard.quality.view`; the SPA invalidates the Quality dashboard and KPI query families, then each viewer refetches through the authenticated dashboard API. The 30-second cache remains enabled. Query-builder bypasses, event authorization/delivery, cache freshness and two-user UI behavior remain NOT_VERIFIED.


### 178. Plant Manager dashboard source writes could leave stale cached cash and defect panels

`PlantManagerDashboardService` reads Account rows for cash position and MTD revenue and joins WorkOrderDefect to DefectType for its defect Pareto. The dashboard snapshot is permission-scoped and cached for 30 seconds, but Account and DefectType were missing from the Plant Manager generation map. Account changes emitted their accounting record/list signal, which does not reach dashboard-only Plant Manager viewers; the existing Production dashboard signal does not cover those Account writes. A client refetch could therefore receive the old snapshot and viewers without record permissions had no source event for the aggregate. The service query, cache scope map, Account event channels and dashboard hook were source-inspected; the multi-user screen behavior was not reproduced.

The common dashboard observer now maps Account and DefectType to `dashboard:plant_manager` and maps WorkOrderDefect to both Quality and Plant Manager. It rotates that generation after commit before staging a coalesced `PlantManagerDashboardChanged` refresh signal. The event uses the private `dashboard.plant_manager` channel, authorized by the Production feature and the exact Plant Manager dashboard permission; the shared dashboard hook invalidates Plant Manager and KPI queries, and values/actions still come from the viewer's authorized API response. The cache remains enabled with its existing bounded TTL. Quiet/query-builder writer coverage, runtime authorization/delivery, cache hits, API latency and two-user freshness remain NOT_VERIFIED; focused source tests were added but not run.

Overtime options use a separate 300-second stale window and invalidate from the settings signal. APIs remain authenticated `private, no-store`. ### 179. HR auxiliary employee records have no refresh signal

The employee detail page caches documents and skills under separate employee-documents and employee-skills queries. Before deltas 323–324, the two document stores and EmployeeSkill assignments lacked shared refresh paths, so peer HR viewers could retain old rows. Both stores now stage content-free, coalesced after-commit signals, each on an employee-scoped private channel and with a central invalidation family. Assignment/upload API responses reconcile the actor cache immediately; delete/revoke responses remove the row before invalidating. The EmployeeDocument and EmployeeSkill source gaps are addressed, but neither path has been browser-reproduced in this pass and all UI outcomes remain NOT_VERIFIED. Skill catalog freshness was a separate source gap and is addressed in delta 325.

EmployeeOnboardingController exposes a separate status resource rendered by OnboardingStepper on Employee detail for HR editors. Before delta 328 its own query had no event subscription. EmployeeOnboarding changes now emit a content-free after-commit signal; linked User creation/reassignment and shift-assignment changes also refresh the exact employee onboarding channel, and Employee changes invalidate the onboarding query centrally. Scheduled reminder_sent_at-only writes are intentionally omitted because they are not projected status. Source wiring is added; channel, delivery and rendered outcomes remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 180. HR approval queues and recruitment lists lacked dedicated refresh paths

ProfileUpdateRequestService previously had only generic dashboard-cache and badge invalidators, leaving its HR/Finance queue and requester history without a shared refresh path. Delta 326 adds scoped after-commit ProfileUpdateRequestChanged delivery and central query invalidation; the queue and requester source gaps are addressed, but runtime UI outcomes remain NOT_VERIFIED. Delta 327 adds RecruitmentChanged observers and a central invalidation family across JobPosting, JobApplication, ApplicationInterview and ApplicationNote. Public application submission now reaches authorized HR list refreshes; candidate PII and tracking codes stay off the wire. Actor handlers reconcile API-returned state and invalidate related queries. This closes the source-wiring gap; runtime authorization, queue delivery and HR cross-role UI remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 181. Linked account changes initially missed HR Employee and account-status queries

Source inspection confirmed that `EmployeeService::show/list` embeds a linked User, while `SystemAccountSection` owns a separate `['employee-account', employeeId]` query. Before delta 329, provisioning, deactivation, role/password and lock changes emitted only AdminUserChanged/access signals; Admin query prefixes did not invalidate HR Employee views. `UserAdminService::bulkChangeRole` also uses a query-builder update and bypasses model observers.

Delta 329 adds linked-User lifecycle observation that stages the existing content-free EmployeeChanged event for the authorized Employee record/list channels, and the Employee invalidation map now includes `employee-account`. Bulk role changes explicitly stage EmployeeChanged for linked Employees after the query-builder update. Provision/create and employee reassignment continue to refresh onboarding through EmployeeOnboardingChanged. This closes the source-wiring gap; channel authorization, queue delivery and rendered peer freshness remain NOT_VERIFIED. `SessionTimeout` updates `User.last_activity` using `saveQuietly()` at most once per minute, bypassing both User observers; the account status's `last_login_at` projection can therefore lag that activity timestamp until a subsequent refetch or other account event.

ProfileUpdateRequest has generic badge/dashboard cache observers and now also has the dedicated scoped HR/Finance/requester refresh path from delta 326. ## Accounting source trace — JournalEntry restore candidate corrected

All 61 Accounting matrix rows now identify their observer/service path, after-commit signal, connected central query families and relevant side effects. BillPayment status is nested in the Bill view and those service mutations emit the parent Bill chain signal, whose central map includes bill detail/list, approvals and badges. Invoice and Bill records themselves are explicitly signaled by their services. Account/JournalEntry writes rotate the versioned statement cache generation after commit. The dunning scheduler changes fields not exposed by the invoice resource and sends emails/recipient notifications. The earlier JournalEntry restore omission was a false-positive source candidate: Laravel's `SoftDeletes::restore()` calls `save()` after changing `deleted_at`, which emits `updated`; JournalEntryObserver handles that event and stages JournalEntryChanged plus cache-generation rotation. Producer wiring is source-confirmed, while actor reconciliation, channel delivery, transition UI, and cache performance remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### JournalEntry restore candidate ruled out by source review

`JournalEntryService::restore()` locks the archived Draft and calls Eloquent `restore()`. Laravel's SoftDeletes implementation sets `deleted_at` to null and calls `save()` before firing the separate `restored` event. That save emits the `updated` model event; `JournalEntryObserver::updated()` calls the shared invalidation path, which stages `JournalEntryChanged` and rotates the financial-statement/dashboard generations after commit. Therefore, the absence of an explicit `restored()` observer method does not leave this route without a refresh signal. This corrects the earlier source-only false positive. Actor UI reconciliation and peer delivery remain `NOT_VERIFIED` because this transition was not exercised in browser contexts.

### 191. Fulfillment-policy writes do not signal the parent SalesOrder view

SalesOrderFulfillmentController updates fulfillment_policy on SalesOrderItem inside a transaction and then calls MrpEngineService::runForSalesOrder. The policy is included in the planning fingerprint, so an actual policy change requires plan regeneration; the MRP service stages ChainBroadcaster::refreshSalesOrder before the transaction commits. The confirmed gap was narrower: ChainStepAdvanced detail/list/customer channels require CRM Sales Order view or an owning customer, while the fulfillment preview GET route also permits Production confirmers; FulfillmentDialog subscribed to no realtime channel and its `sales-order-fulfillment` query key was absent from the central map. FulfillmentDialog subscribes only while open and uses the central sales_order invalidation map, which targets the exact preview query. Its API mutation response seeds the actor cache immediately; remote edits preserve in-progress selections/reason and disable confirmation until the latest preview is explicitly reviewed. ### 192. Customer agreement-document and warranty-override endpoints lack current SPA consumers

CustomerAgreementDocumentController and CustomerWarrantyTermsController have no active SPA API consumer in source search. The warranty values are snapshotted into SalesOrder data at order creation/update, so the reference write does not itself change an existing SalesOrder snapshot. Warranty deletion uses transactional Eloquent model deletes so the observer executes; after-commit durable outbox delivery and existing private customer channels are reused. The focused red-first backend regression passes upload, create/update upsert and delete. No backend cache writer or active SPA cache consumer exists for these subresources. The UI impact remains unresolved and matrix outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; see the current evidence in PROGRESS delta 388.

**Audience refinement (delta 390):** Channel review found that blindly reusing the normal Customer event also put internal agreement-document and warranty writes on `customer.profile.{hashId}`, whose subscribers can fetch customer-profile data but cannot fetch these CRM subresources. A red-first channel assertion failed for all four child mutation scenarios. `CustomerChanged` now has an optional profile-channel audience flag, defaulting to the existing behavior for Customer lifecycle writes; the child observer disables it. Coalesced events union the prior and current audience flags, so an internal child refresh cannot remove a profile signal from a real Customer update in the same transaction. The parent-plus-child transaction regression failed before the union and passes after it. This narrows notification scope without changing the broadcast payload or customer API access. No live portal socket scenario was run.

## Supply Chain source trace — deliveries, inbound shipments and fleet

Delivery service transitions are generally source-wired through the queued, after-commit ChainStepAdvanced outbox path. The private event contains only a refresh signal. Delivery list/detail and selected customer-order consumers use the central delivery mapping. ShipmentLot changes have a separate observer/event. Runtime delivery and all UI result cells remain unverified in this pass.

### 193. Inbound Shipment and Container records have no shared refresh event

ShipmentService and ContainerService persist shipment status/meta, landed-cost totals, document rows and container rows without registered observers or realtime events. Shipment Received can stage a GRN, but that event belongs to a different entity/query family. The SPA shipment list/detail and embedded container queries refresh locally after the acting mutation; no shipment/container entity type, list subscription or central invalidation family was found. Concurrent ImpEx viewers can retain prior status, dates, landed cost, document list or container data. Source-confirmed gap; no multi-user scenario was run.

**Source disposition (delta 319):** Added `ShipmentChanged` through the transaction-coalesced outbox and dedicated after-commit broadcast queue. Shipment, Container, ShipmentDocument and ShipmentLandedCost lifecycle observers coalesce against the parent Shipment per database transaction; the landed-cost bulk-delete path is paired with the parent Shipment save. Private record/list channels match the shipment API read permissions. The central map covers shipment detail/list, its nested container query, dashboard/badge families and related PO/GRN/Bill reads. The detail and list pages now subscribe; actor API responses seed or update their local shipment query before authorized invalidation/refetch. Shipment resources provide current-viewer `available_actions`, permissions, and an updated_at version. Status and metadata writes accept an optional expected version; stale status/version conflicts return 409. Container updates compare both the child and parent versions under locks. The SPA preserves open form input after peer events, pauses submission, and reloads after explicit user choice; a stale API conflict also refreshes while preserving the draft. Source wiring and PHP syntax are verified; runtime delivery, channel authorization, conflict UX, cache freshness, and every matrix UI outcome remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 194. Vehicle fleet queries miss Vehicle writes and Delivery-derived availability

At baseline, VehicleController created, updated, archived and restored Vehicle without an observer/event or fleet subscription. DeliveryService and DeliveryAttemptOutcomeService changed Vehicle status during dispatch, confirmation and physical return. Delivery assignment, status, archive and restore also changed active-delivery eligibility used by the fleet resource without always writing the Vehicle row. Fleet and assignment selectors could therefore retain stale status or available-action state. Source-confirmed gap; no fleet or assignment UI was exercised. Source review ruled out the late-recovery path as a Vehicle writer: `DeliveryAttemptOutcomeService::receiveLate()` does not update Vehicle.

**Source disposition (delta 320):** `VehicleRealtimeObserver` covers Vehicle create/update/delete/restore and AssetService association writes. `DeliveryVehicleRealtimeObserver` covers Delivery assignment/reassignment, status, archive and restore effects on the current and previous Vehicle. Both stage the same transaction-coalesced `VehicleChanged` through the durable after-commit outbox. Private exact-record and list channels require `modules.supply_chain`, an active user and the same `supply_chain.view` permission as fleet APIs. The central map invalidates fleet, available-vehicle selectors, Delivery reads, badges and logistics/dashboard queries. Vehicle resources compute archive eligibility from active Delivery and asset state and return viewer-specific permissions/actions plus an updated_at version. Update/archive/restore compare optional `expected_version` under row locks and return 409 for stale state; actor status/confirmation/truck-return handlers invalidate their local vehicle query family. The existing badge observer now includes Vehicle, retiring the maintenance-badge generation after commit. No Vehicle response-cache family was found. Runtime channel authorization, event delivery, conflict behavior and every UI outcome remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

**Additional confirmed editor gap (delta 504):** the fleet list already consumed the private entity signal and server updates already rejected stale `expected_version` values with 409, but an open edit modal held its selected row in component state and did not subscribe to that vehicle's private record channel. The focused regression failed because opening Edit registered no record callback. The modal now subscribes only while a manager with fleet-view access edits a vehicle; a signal warns, preserves the form, and disables saving until the editor closes and reopens the refreshed row. The existing API 409 remains the final server-side guard. The page tests pass (2 tests), including an actor-side regression that holds the mapped list refetch open and verifies the successful update response appears first. Fleet create/update/archive/restore and stale-409 recovery now use the central vehicle invalidation map, so active Delivery selectors, related Delivery reads, badges, and logistics/dashboard queries refresh through the shared cancellation/coalescing path. This verifies the UI handler/cache behavior in React/Query harnesses, not live Reverb delivery or cross-role UI; matrix behavior cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 195. Driver refresh authorization was coupled to notification permission

The current driver routes are wrapped by `DriverRealtimeBoundary`, which mounts `useDriverDeliveryRealtime`; that hook listens for `.chain.refresh` and invalidates active `['driver']` queries. The earlier “no driver hook” source note was stale and is superseded by this current-tree inspection. Delta 321 adds an exact `driver.{userHashId}` private channel with the driver's API feature/permission and active-user checks, moves the hook to it, and retains the legacy event channel for deployed old bundles. Focused source-contract tests cover the custom-role and payload cases but are not run. This is a source-confirmed authorization mismatch and source-level fix; live custom-role channel authorization and UI refresh remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Payroll source trace — state, payroll rows, and disbursement evidence
