### 199. The compute claim changes period status without an immediate list event

`PayrollPeriodService::claimForCompute()` performs its guarded `Processing` status transition with `PayrollPeriod::query()->...->update()`, bypassing `PayrollPeriodRealtimeObserver`. `claimForComputeAndStage()` records the durable compute request but not a `PayrollPeriodChanged` signal. The actor receives a `PayrollPeriodResource` already showing `processing` and applies it locally. Other period-list viewers subscribe to `entity.payroll_period.visible`, but the first progress signal is `PayrollProgressEvent` on only `payroll.period.{hashId}` after the queued worker starts. The shared list refresh arrives later through terminal `releaseClaim()`. `AutoPayrollPeriodService` also creates a Draft period in one transaction and performs this claim in a subsequent transaction, so a scheduled create event may leave list viewers showing Draft through queue delay and computation. This was source-confirmed; worker delay and UI effect were not reproduced.

**Source disposition:** `claimForCompute()` now explicitly retires the PayrollPeriod dashboard and badge generations and stages one coalesced `PayrollPeriodChanged(status_changed)` outbox signal in the claim transaction after the successful guarded update. This covers both manual and scheduled claims and leaves the API response as the acting screen's immediate source. The existing event is after-commit and reaches authorized detail/list subscribers. Delta 855's focused `PayrollComputeClaimFencingTest::test_claim_token_is_staged_and_round_trips_with_the_request` passes (1 warning / 6 assertions). Peer delivery and UI/button refresh remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 200. Payroll proof rows do not always refresh peer period details

`DisbursementProofController::store()`, `destroy()`, and `restore()` create/archive/restore the child row and then call `DisbursementEvidenceService::syncStatus()`. `DisbursementProof` previously had no realtime observer/event. The parent `PayrollPeriod` is saved only when `disbursement_status` changes between `pending` and `partially_disbursed`; a partial-to-partial change therefore staged no parent refresh even though `PayrollPeriodResource` embeds the proof collection. A new `DisbursementProofRealtimeObserver` stages one coalesced `PayrollPeriodChanged(related_updated)` ping for child create/update/delete, including SoftDeletes restore saves, using the proof timestamp. Delta 855's focused `PayrollPeriodRealtimeTest::test_disbursement_proof_changes_refresh_the_parent_period_when_status_is_unchanged` passes (1 warning / 4 assertions), confirming outbox staging when the derived period status does not change. Peer delivery and finance-viewer UI freshness remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 201. Individual payroll recompute has no payroll-row event or related-period refresh

`PayrollController::recompute()` calls `PayrollCalculatorService::computeForEmployee()`, which replaces the Payroll row and recomputes deductions, loans, adjustments and accruals. No row-specific broadcaster exists; the embedded parent period and employee row queries refresh through `PayrollPeriodChanged(related_updated)`. Initially the controller staged that event only after `computeForEmployee()` committed, leaving a process-failure window where payroll data could commit without a durable refresh. A red-first service regression reproduced the missing outbox row when recomputation was called directly. `PayrollCalculatorService` now stages the parent event inside the same transaction as the payroll replacement for external recomputes; an outbox failure rolls back the data, while internal batch runs keep using their progress and terminal signals. The controller no longer stages a duplicate. The focused regression passes (1 test / 6 assertions; one container warning). The actor still receives the fresh `PayrollResource`; peer delivery, row/list freshness and aggregate rendering remain `NOT_VERIFIED` without a browser run.

The individual Payroll detail still derived its Recompute control from the SPA permission store alone, and the calculator's original state check ran before its data-changing transaction. The detail resource now returns row-level `permissions.recompute` and `available_actions.recompute`, the button renders from that server snapshot, and an external recompute locks and rechecks the parent period before replacing deductions, accruals, loans, or the payroll row. A stale/conflicting period returns the typed 409 and the employee detail invalidates its Payroll row plus central Payroll period query families. This closes the source-level race and control gap; multi-user delivery, runtime button state and stale-action handling remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 202. Payroll action availability was client-derived and state conflicts used 422

`PayrollPeriodResource` originally supplied status/lifecycle fields but no `available_actions` or user-specific permission decision. The period detail page computed controls from cached status plus the SPA permission store. `PayrollPeriodService` already locked and revalidated state on actions, but `PayrollPeriodController` returned stale-state conflicts as HTTP 422. The detail/mutation resource now includes permission-scoped `permissions` and server-computed `available_actions`; expensive eligibility decisions are restricted to individual period resources, not list rows. State-transition conflicts in compute, approve, correction, finalize, GL retry, force-unlock, void, and disbursement now return a typed 409; the detail UI refreshes its mapped queries after 409. Existing locked service validation remains authoritative. The employee Payroll row's Recompute action has the same server-owned availability contract and its calculator rechecks period state under lock. Source wiring is corrected; no Payroll multi-user stale-button scenario was run, and all button/action UI matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

Payroll backend cache entries found in this trace are the agency/effective-date government contribution array cache (300-second payload, 7-day version marker) and the per-period progress snapshot (3600 seconds, cleared on new claim and force-unlock). No other module response cache was found. Transactional Payroll query data is memory-only in the SPA and API responses are `private, no-store`; no cache-hit/latency measurement was collected. The existing cache-registry records government table and progress keys; the embedded period proof and Payroll row query families are added below as client-cache families.


## MRP source trace — plans, equipment and scheduler

All 26 MRP route/scheduler rows now have source wiring notes. BOM, Machine, Mold, MrpPlan and MrpRun observers stage private after-commit events; ProductionSchedule has its own observer and channel set. Compatibility pivot writes explicitly touch the Mold and stage affected Machine signals. MRP query builders only update run heartbeat columns omitted from `MrpRunResource`; terminal run status and summary updates use observed Eloquent saves. MRP has no cached response payload in the module: only the 1200-second plant-run mutex. Matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 203. MRP plan generation can leave CRM-only SalesOrder viewers stale

`MrpEngineService::runForSalesOrder()` updates `sales_orders.mrp_plan_id`, and `SalesOrderResource` embeds `mrp_plan` fields. The MRP observer stages `MrpPlanChanged` to the private MRP plan detail/list channels; that event's related SalesOrder reference cannot refresh CRM-only viewers. Added `ChainBroadcaster::refreshSalesOrder()` stages an additional minimal `ChainStepAdvanced` refresh on the SalesOrder's own private record/list channels and exact customer-account channel, without adding a synthetic chain-step ledger row or exposing MRP plan data. `MrpEngineService` calls it inside the plan transaction after linked supply work, so create, rerun, API-triggered, fulfillment-triggered, and scheduled runs share the same signal path. Source wiring is corrected; no cross-role browser scenario was run and delivery, list/detail freshness, and SLO remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 204. Return-request to linked problem-case refresh is source-wired; runtime still unverified

The initial trace found that `ReturnCaseResource` embeds the linked ReturnRequest status without invalidating case detail. Current `ReturnRequestRealtimeService::stage()` now loads the linked case and stages `ReturnCaseChanged` using the RMA version. That refresh targets the authorized exact case/list channels and, for public customer cases, the exact `customer.account` channel. Internal case pages use `useRealtimeRecord`/`useRealtimeList`; customer case pages use `useReturnCasePortalRealtime`; the case invalidation map covers the case detail/list families. This corrects the earlier source omission without sending RMA fields in the event payload.

Source wiring is present; no linked RMA/case mutation, channel authorization, delivery, or two-context rendering was executed. UI outcomes and runtime freshness remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. This is separate from #185's customer RMA list/detail signal, which also has an exact customer-account subscription in current source.

### 205. Dashboard cache-group clear left badge snapshots in the active generation

`CacheGroupInvalidationService::clear('dashboards')` previously rotated `DashboardCacheGeneration` but not the independent `badges.version` generation used by `BadgeService` cache keys. The clear endpoint then emitted `CacheGroupInvalidated`, so clients could refetch badges and still hit the old backend snapshot. The group now rotates both markers after commit; it reports failure if either write fails. A follow-up red-first regression found that the signal was still dispatched directly without a durable outbox row. The service now stages the operation-keyed signal atomically with the audit row; its publication callback follows the generation-rotation callback. The focused `CacheGroupInvalidationTest` passes under direct PHPUnit (1 test, 7 assertions), checking both generations, event dispatch, published status, and the durable payload. The focused channel-authorization regression passes (1 test, 8 assertions), checking permission-matched access and denying unrelated and unregistered groups. No manual-clear live Redis or two-user UI scenario was performed.
### 206. Database restore left Redis snapshots and open browser caches on the old database

**Status:** source-confirmed gap; source fix added, runtime verification pending.

`BackupService::executeRestore()` and `rollback()` replace PostgreSQL without replaying model observers for every replaced row. Redis generation markers and browser QueryClients therefore survived the replacement. Added `DatabaseRestoreCacheInvalidator`, which rotates the registered data-cache owner generations for dashboards, badges, financial statements, procurement summaries, inventory and production dashboards, analytics, settings, holidays, government contribution tables, DSS parameters, payroll progress, and role-permission snapshots. Settings, DSS, payroll progress, and role permissions also use a restore namespace so removed source rows, deleted payroll periods, or rolled-back `permissions_version` values cannot reuse old per-item cache keys. A failed rotation records owner results and leaves the restore/rollback in maintenance with queues paused.

After successful invalidation and service resume, `DatabaseRestoreCompleted` sends a content-free private reload signal to active internal and portal sessions. The clients reload through their current authenticated API session, dropping in-memory QueryClient data. Channel authorization, Redis rotation, worker delivery, and behavior across two active browser contexts remain dynamically unverified. Old generation payloads remain bounded by owner TTLs; no unbounded key scan or global cache flush is used.

### 207. Settings, DSS, payroll progress, and role permissions need a restore-wide generation

**Status:** source fix and targeted cache regressions pass; runtime Redis rotation/performance remain unmeasured.

Per-key generation invalidation cannot enumerate keys for source rows removed by a restored snapshot. Settings, DSS parameter values, and payroll progress snapshots use restore-wide namespace markers. Role permission keys also need this because the DB-backed `permissions_version` can roll back to a prior number during restore, making a still-live Redis snapshot addressable again. A scoped `RolePermissionCacheGeneration` marker now prefixes role snapshots and is rotated by `DatabaseRestoreCacheInvalidator`; cache failure uses an unshared key or keeps the restore in maintenance. `PermissionCacheFreshnessTest::test_restore_namespace_prevents_reuse_when_permissions_version_repeats` passes (7 assertions), and `DatabaseRestoreCacheInvalidatorTest::test_database_restore_rotates_each_registered_data_cache_owner` passes (21 assertions). Marker-read overhead, actual Redis restore rotation, and orphan payload retention have not been measured.
### 208. Customer portal RMA and linked problem-case pages used separate event audiences

**Status:** source fixes added; regression sources authored, not run.

`ReturnRequestChanged` originally targeted only internal User-guard record/list channels, leaving other customer portal sessions with stale RMA list/detail data. Customer-return pings now also target `customer.account.{customerHashId}`, whose private channel authorizes the exact active customer account. The portal's central realtime signal map invalidates only that account's RMA list/detail query families. When an RMA has a linked ReturnCase, `ReturnRequestRealtimeService` also stages a coalesced `ReturnCaseChanged` signal using the RMA version. That event reaches the existing authorized case detail/list and public customer-account channels, refreshing embedded status. Event bodies still omit record and customer IDs. No two-session or browser result is claimed.

### 209. Restored role permission versions could re-address an old Redis snapshot

`User::permission_slugs` keyed role snapshots by role ID and `roles.permissions_version`. Normal role permission sync increments the DB version transactionally, preventing late pre-commit reads from becoming current after a permission edit. A database restore can roll the version back to a value used before the backup, however, making that old Redis key current again for up to the snapshot TTL. Role snapshots now include a scoped UUID namespace, and restore/completed-migration hooks rotate it after database replacement or migration-time pivot changes. A regression source assertion was added but not run; cache reads, authorization, and restore runtime remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 210. Loan approval-ledger and payment changes had no scoped record/list refresh

`LoanSubmitted` and `LoanDecided` are notification-domain events, not broadcasters. Existing `EmployeeLoan` and `LoanPayment` observers rotated dashboard/badge generations, but did not invalidate `['loans', ...]` detail/list queries or the owner’s self-service loan data. A non-final approval updates only `ApprovalRecord`; it can change the timeline and next action without saving `EmployeeLoan`, so a parent-only observer would still miss a real state transition. The source trace also found payroll recompute/reversal, final-pay settlement, and HR separation reservation paths; these use Eloquent payment or parent writes and need to reach the same signal. This was confirmed by source inspection, not by a reproduced two-browser stale screen.

**Source disposition:** Added `EmployeeLoanChanged`, dispatched through the durable transaction-coalesced outbox after commit. Parent-loan, approval-ledger and payment-ledger observers converge on one service; private record/list/owner channels carry refresh-only payloads. The SPA central invalidation map refreshes authorized loan details/lists, self-service, approvals, badges and dashboard query families. Loan resources now return server-computed actions, row permissions and a version; mutations send the returned version through `If-Match`, and stale versions/actions return typed 409 and the active detail refetches. Existing scoped dashboard and badge cache invalidation remains in place; no new backend cache was introduced. Syntax and source-wiring checks passed, but realtime delivery, channel authorization, cross-role action refresh, UI behavior, cache hit/freshness and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 211. Price-request transitions did not refresh internal or customer portal viewers

`PriceNegotiationService` creates requests from the customer portal, creates/updates proposals internally, handles Finance approval, records the portal's accept/reject decision, withdraws proposals, and expires offers from the scheduled pricing review command. All those paths write `PriceRequest` with Eloquent inside transactions, but the model had no realtime observer/event. Internal pricing-request list/detail/offer pages and the customer pricing page used separate query keys without a PriceRequest listener. Notifications were emitted for selected transitions but did not invalidate the request record/list. The existing dashboard cache observer already rotates the `sales.price_requests` widget generations after PriceRequest changes; there was no separate PriceRequest application response cache.

Added `PriceRequestChanged` through the model observer and durable coalescing outbox, after commit. The signal targets internal detail/list channels authorized by the same CRM price-agreement read/approve/propose permissions as the APIs and a separate customer-portal channel authorized for exactly the active owning customer. Payloads contain only refresh metadata, opaque request/actor hashes, the monotonic request version and an optional opaque accepted-agreement reference; offer values, customer quantities, and notes remain API-only. Central invalidation covers internal request list/detail/review, the customer's portal pricing list and dashboard families. Accepted agreement/catalog changes continue through the existing PriceAgreement/CustomerCatalog signals. Both resources expose server-computed permissions/actions; list controls render from those fields. Proposal edits send the current version and preserve typed values with a conflict banner; approve/respond/withdraw and proposal writes revalidate locked state/version and return 409 for stale state. Actor mutations install API response data before invalidation. Source wiring is corrected; no tests, channel runtime check, portal/internal two-user run, UI result, cache freshness check or latency sample was run in this no-tests slice.


### Delta 323 disposition — Employee document collections

The earlier Employee document gap is now source-wired for both backing stores. EmployeeDocument and Employee-owned Document Eloquent lifecycle events stage one content-free employee-scoped outbox refresh per transaction. The private channel callback requires the documents-view permission, active user, and matching DepartmentScope visibility. The central map invalidates the HR document collection, generic Employee vault collection, and document-type options. EmployeeDocument now has a nullable, backfilled `updated_at` value for event ordering. Tests were authored but not run; no claim is made for migration success, runtime channel authorization, WebSocket delivery or multi-user rendered freshness. The Skill catalog was a separate gap and is addressed in delta 325; other independent HR records remain separate gaps.


### Delta 324 disposition — EmployeeSkill assignment and matrix refresh

EmployeeSkillService assign/update/revoke/restore operations use Eloquent within transactions, and source search found no bulk/query-builder EmployeeSkill writes. `EmployeeSkillRealtimeObserver` now records one coalesced `EmployeeSkillChanged` per employee and transaction. It broadcasts to an exact employee collection channel whose callback applies the same EmployeeCompetenceScope and `hr.employees.trainings.view` permission as the list API, plus a content-free matrix channel requiring the HR feature and one of the two relevant API view permissions. The wire payload omits the employee hash so a matrix viewer with a narrower row scope learns only that a refetch may be needed. The map invalidates `employee-skills`, training-matrix, skill matrix and gap-analysis query prefixes; assignment response data and successful revocation update the actor's in-memory list directly. Focused tests are authored, not run. Runtime authorization, after-commit queue delivery and rendered freshness remain NOT_VERIFIED.


### 212. Skill catalog writes did not refresh catalog or competence readers

`SkillService::create/update/deactivate` changed the Eloquent Skill rows, but no observer/event or `skill` central invalidation family existed. The SPA Skills page invalidated only its acting client's `['hr','skills']` queries after create/deactivate; EmployeeSkill selectors, profile lists, the training matrix and skill matrix/gap-analysis readers could stay stale in other tabs/users. Delta 325 adds a coalesced after-commit SkillChanged outbox signal, private catalog channels with the same `modules.hr` + `hr.trainings.view` read boundary as the catalog API, plus the content-free `entity.employee_skill.visible` channel for competency readers. Central invalidation reaches those query families. The actor applies successful API resources to cached catalog rows before invalidating. This is source wiring, not a two-user reproduction: runtime authorization, broadcast delivery, and rendered freshness remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### Delta 325 disposition — Skill catalog refresh

Skill lifecycle changes now converge on the observed outbox path; payloads contain refresh metadata only and no catalog identifiers or text. Catalog, EmployeeSkill, and competence-matrix readers subscribe through the same HR catalog permission and refetch through the API. There is no backend response cache for SkillService; the affected SPA queries are memory-only and use the shared query defaults. Focused tests were authored but not run.


### Delta 326 disposition — Profile-update approval queue freshness

ProfileUpdateRequest submission and HR/Finance transitions now stage a coalesced after-commit `ProfileUpdateRequestChanged` event through the durable outbox. HR recipients are split between view-all and employee-department scopes; Finance receives signals only for bank requests in Finance-visible states; the requester receives a private exact-user signal. The exact request channel rechecks requester, HR DepartmentScope, or Finance queue visibility. Opaque request/employee references and update metadata are broadcast, while actor identity, encrypted changes, bank values, notes, and remarks stay out of the wire payload; actor identity remains in the outbox for traceability. The central map refreshes review queues, requester history, badges/dashboard and linked Employee queries. The review actor applies the API resource to cached rows before invalidating. Focused tests were authored but not run; authorization at runtime, queue delivery, and rendered outcomes remain NOT_VERIFIED.


### Delta 332 disposition — B2B portal-access directory freshness

Portal account administration changes now converge on `PortalAccessRealtimeService` after audit recording. Account-state writes staged inside the existing transaction share one durable outbox refresh per PostgreSQL transaction; invitation flows stage the refresh after the invitation service has committed. The SPA subscribes to the private directory channel and invalidates the supplier/customer list query families; local actor invalidation remains tied to API success. The channel matches the read permission and the wire body contains no portal-user identifiers or account data. This is source coverage only: queue delivery, two-admin rendering and active portal-session/socket revocation were not tested. No backend cache entry was added or removed.


### Delta 333 disposition — notification retention refresh

The scheduled prune now batches recipient selection by exact notifiable class and user ID, preserving the old read-row cutoff and deletion behavior. Each known inbox batch is deleted with its content-free `NotificationsPruned` event recorded in the same database transaction and published only after commit. A batch fan-out is capped at 50 private channels and maps to the existing internal or portal inbox event name. No notification content or recipient ID appears in the broadcast body. The command was not run, and no queue, browser or performance behavior is claimed. No backend cache entry was added or removed.


### 213. KPI recompute updated peer scorecards without a realtime refresh

`KpiSnapshotService::computeAll()` updates monthly KpiSnapshot rows and catches failures per definition. The model cache observer retires forecast-capacity widget snapshots, but no signal targeted the independent `['kpi','scorecard',year,month]` and `['kpi','trends',codes]` TanStack queries; KpiStrip used a five-minute stale time. The admin compute panel invalidated only its own client on success, and the scheduled command had no client-side path.

Delta 334 stages one `KpiSnapshotChanged` durable outbox refresh after any compute batch that wrote at least one snapshot, including partial-success batches. Its private channel requires an active internal user; this matches the scorecard endpoints' authenticated audience, while each API response remains module-permission filtered. KpiStrip subscribes through the central invalidation map and catches up on subscription. The payload contains refresh metadata only. Source wiring is added; event delivery, authorization and rendered scorecard freshness remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 215. Broadcast jobs shared the general queue Redis database

`config/queue.php` had an isolated `broadcasts` queue name and Horizon supervisor, but `redis_broadcasts` selected the same `default` Redis connection/database as general jobs. Cache eviction could not remove those jobs because the queue Redis service is `noeviction` and cache uses another Redis service; this was an isolation and operational-boundary gap, not an eviction-caused stale-data reproduction. Delta 336 adds a dedicated `broadcasts` Redis connection targeting logical DB 1 by default and preserves `redis_broadcasts_legacy` on DB 0 for an explicit backlog drain. Cache stays on the separate bounded Redis instance, sessions on PostgreSQL. Configuration cache refresh, Horizon restart, legacy queue drain, runtime delivery and resource impact were not exercised; the running deployment may still be using its previous cached configuration.

### 214. User Invite transitions refreshed only the acting admin's list

`UserInviteService::invite()`, `accept()`, and `revoke()` changed rows without an invite-specific outbox event. Acceptance also created a User, whose separate user-directory refresh did not invalidate `['admin','invites']`. The SPA only invalidated the acting browser after issue/revoke; other administrators could retain stale pending/used/revoked lists. Delta 335 stages one `UserInviteChanged` event in each service transaction and routes it through the durable after-commit outbox. Its private channel requires an active user with the same `auth.users.invite` permission as the list endpoint, and the central SPA map invalidates invite queries. This is source wiring only; queue delivery, authorization at runtime, and two-admin UI freshness remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 216. Contact inquiries refreshed only local status screens and generic badges

Public inquiry creation, CRM status changes, and scheduled retention deletion had no signal targeting the CRM inquiry list/detail query family. Generic badge invalidation covered counters only, and status actors invalidated their own QueryClient. Delta 337 stages a content-free `ContactInquiryChanged` event transactionally from the public create service, status service, and retention command. The private channel follows the active-user, `modules.crm` and `crm.inquiries.view` read boundary. The central map invalidates list/detail/option queries; the actor keeps API-success invalidation. Newsletter subscriber retention is unchanged because there is no authenticated ERP reader. No event delivery, channel-auth runtime check, or UI scenario was run; outcomes remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 217. Notification preference writes did not refresh the same user's other tabs

`UserNotificationService::updatePreferences()` upserted per-user notification preferences and returned fresh rows to the acting page, but another open preferences tab had no event to invalidate its `['notification-preferences']` query. Delta 338 stages `UserNotificationPreferencesChanged` in the same transaction and publishes it through the durable after-commit outbox to an exact-user private channel. Channel auth requires the active user to match the target hash and hold `notifications.preferences.manage`, the same permission as the API. The central map invalidates the local preferences family; the actor continues using the successful mutation response/invalidation path. This is source wiring only. Cross-tab channel auth, delivery and rendering remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 218. User display preferences stayed stale in sibling tabs

`PATCH /auth/user/preferences` returned a fresh `UserResource`, and the acting theme/sidebar/table stores updated locally, but another tab had no source event to refetch `/auth/user`. Delta 339 moves the merge/save path into `UserPreferencesService`, wraps it in a transaction, and stages a content-free `UserDisplayPreferencesChanged` event only when a supported preference actually changed. Its private channel matches the exact active user; AppLayout's global `usePermissionSync` handler refetches and applies the server resource without a permission-change toast. Actor behavior remains local/API-driven. Cross-tab channel authorization, delivery and UI state remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 219. Conflict-only production proposals changed scheduler diagnostics without a refresh signal

`CapacityPlanningService::run()` persists new/superseded `ProductionSchedule` rows for successful placements, which already reach the scheduler snapshot through `ProductionScheduleRealtimeObserver`. When placement fails, it instead writes `WorkOrder.scheduling_diagnostic`; the scheduler snapshot includes those conflicts, but WorkOrder had no observer and no schedule row changed to trigger the scheduler list signal. A connected scheduler viewer could therefore retain an old conflict list. Source-confirmed; no two-user reproduction was run.

Delta 341 adds a WorkOrder observer for actual `scheduling_diagnostic` changes and stages one transaction-coalesced `WorkOrderSchedulingDiagnosticChanged` event through the durable after-commit outbox. Its refresh-only payload reaches the existing authorized work-order record/list, production-schedule list, and Production dashboard channels. Existing central invalidation handles work-order and scheduler query families. This is source wiring only; event delivery, channel authorization at runtime, rendered refresh and latency remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 220. Realtime invalidation could replay the schedule-writing suggestion request

`CrewPanel` ran `generateAssignmentSuggestion()`, a POST that persists pending/superseded ProductionSchedule rows, from a TanStack `useQuery` function. The `work_order` and `production_schedule` invalidation families both include `['production','assignment-suggestion']`, and active invalidation refetches matching queries. An open generated-suggestion query could therefore rerun the planner when its own or another user's schedule signal arrived. This is confirmed by source tracing; no browser or network replay was run.

Delta 342 keeps the existing automatic generation for each new work-order/staffing/date key, but records successful keys in the dialog. Realtime invalidation for an already-generated key now calls the read-only suggestion GET; concurrent invalidation during the initial POST shares one in-flight request. Failed generation remains retryable, and changing the proposal inputs still generates a new proposal. This avoids turning refresh signals into business writes while retaining the existing endpoint and workflow. Runtime query scheduling, event delivery, UI rendering and planner request counts remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 221. Delegation changes refreshed delegated permissions but not other open delegation lists (historical gap; fixed and manager list verified)

`ApprovalDelegationAccessRealtimeObserver` sent an exact-user `UserAccessChanged` signal to the delegate, refreshing delegated permissions and action visibility. The Approval Delegations page also lists rows for the delegator and delegate, and users with `admin.delegations.manage_any` see all rows; those open query caches initially had no shared list signal. This source-confirmed gap was recorded before the delta 343 fix. The first browser attempt after that fix exposed a test-harness parsing error, not a product failure: Pusher wraps the custom payload in a JSON string, and the raw-frame listener treated it as an object. The normal manager page had received and applied the update; the test's cleanup assertion also masked the original payload assertion error.

Delta 343 stages `ApprovalDelegationChanged` after create, visible update, and delete through the durable outbox. Only the active delegator and delegate receive exact-user pings; the all-delegations list channel requires the same `admin.delegations.manage_any` permission enforced by the API. The event payload carries refresh metadata only, and the central map invalidates `['approval-delegations']`. Delta 362's two-context Playwright scenario then verified the manager channel returned HTTP 200, create and revoke frames reached the system-admin manager, and the refreshed list showed the active Revoke button then the Revoked state with the button removed. Measured UI-mutation-start-to-peer-frame latency was 450 ms for create and 246 ms for revoke in the passing run. This resolves the specific manager-list runtime gap for one seeded finance/system-admin scenario; actor-side revoke UI, detail pages, delegate permissions, linked views, badges, dashboards, inboxes, and broader role/scope authorization remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 222. Concurrent Production crew and schedule changes returned validation errors without refreshing the open review

`WorkOrderAssignmentService::assign()` rechecked `expected_crew_id` under a work-order lock, and `WorkOrderAssignmentController::apply()` compared the reviewed schedule ID and `updated_at` before and after applying. A changed crew, schedule, or worker eligibility raised `BusinessRuleException`, which the API renders as 422. `AssignmentEditor` left any mutation error in a disabled state, and the dialog did not fetch the current crew/proposal after the conflict. The dedicated crew form also showed the 422 without refreshing current crew/options. This is confirmed by source inspection; no stale-action request or two-user rendering was reproduced.

Delta 345 adds `WorkOrderAssignmentConflictException` for these specific concurrent-state checks and maps it to a 409 response. The assignment review and repair schedule form fetch the current suggestion and crew, then permit another attempt only after that refresh succeeds. Manually selected workers and repair timing/approval inputs remain in local form state; automatic suggestions reconcile to the refreshed proposal. The direct crew form installs the successful API `CrewContext` immediately and, on 409, reloads crew and worker options while preserving selections. The review mutation also installs its returned `CrewContext` before invalidating dependent Production queries. Runtime status/action rendering, conflict handling, and refreshed inputs remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 223. Availability and resource changes did not invalidate open assignment proposals

The shared invalidation map refreshed general Production work-order, crew, or readiness queries for some HR and equipment events, but omitted the active `assignment-suggestion` and `repair-suggestion` proposal queries. Those endpoints recompute worker eligibility from employee accounts, shift assignments, attendance, leave, holidays, equipment reservations and maintenance; the normal assignment proposal also returns material, child-order and inspection readiness blockers. A WebSocket could therefore reach the browser while its proposal and available worker/action state remained cached in the active query. This is a source-confirmed invalidation-map gap; no open-proposal multi-user scenario was run.

Delta 346 adds both proposal query families to central invalidation for their shared dependencies: work orders/schedules, machines/molds, maintenance work orders/schedules, employees, shifts, shift assignments, leave requests, attendance, and holidays. Inventory-stock and inspection-specification events also invalidate the regular assignment proposal, whose response includes material and quality readiness; NCR events invalidate repair proposals whose quantity/state depends on the NCR. This uses the existing private event paths and authorized API refetches. Runtime event delivery and updated proposal/action rendering remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### 224. Confirmed delivery recovery keys remained in browser storage

`DeliveryStockCostPanel` stores one UUID per delivery recovery action under `ogami:delivery-recovery:*`. The request key was reused for ambiguous retries, but neither successful stock reservation nor a completed cost handoff removed it. Those terminal UUIDs therefore stayed in localStorage until an auth-scope cleanup and did not have a TTL. Source review also found that `manual_required` cost handoffs must keep their key: `DeliveryCostRecognitionService::postHandoff()` replays/updates the row by that unique key, and the Delivery resource exposes whether another retry is still allowed. Delta 347 clears reservation keys after the authoritative response reports a reservation and clears cost keys only when the fresh response is `generated` or `not_required`; unresolved/ambiguous outcomes retain their key. This is a source-confirmed browser-storage lifecycle gap, not evidence that a record view served stale business data. No browser storage or retry scenario was run.

### 225. Expired form drafts were removed only when their own page was revisited

`useFormDraftAutosave::readDraft()` enforced the seven-day TTL and deleted an expired entry when its matching form mounted/read it. No global sweep visited other `ogami:formdraft:*` entries, so drafts for forms a user never reopened could remain physically stored past expiry, including while a floor tab stayed open for days. Delta 348 adds a bounded prefix-only sweep at SPA startup and daily for long-lived tabs; it removes recognized autosave envelopes with expired/future/invalid timestamps while leaving entries with another shape, including the delivery-creation idempotency journal, untouched. This closes the source-level lingering-entry path without changing restore behavior or deleting pending operations. No browser storage inspection or expiry scenario was run.

### 226. The command palette showed stale persisted record statuses

`CommandPalette.pick()` copied the status from a `/search` result into `ogami:recent-items`, whose recent section rendered that value as a status chip. The store retained each row for up to 30 days, so later record transitions could leave the “Recent” group showing an old status even though search results came from the API. The owner/scope fingerprint prevented cross-user reuse but did not make the saved status fresh. Delta 349 stops adding status to the recent-item model and search-pick persistence, and strips legacy status fields from persisted entries during hydration before rewriting the sanitized list. Search-result status still comes from `/search` when fetched; the active search query's separate event-invalidation gap is logged as finding #227. This is a source-confirmed stale-display path; no browser scenario was run.

### 227. Active global-search results were not invalidated by record events before delta 351

`CommandPalette` caches `/search` under `['command-palette','search',debouncedTerm]` with the shared 30-second stale time. Before delta 351, `realtimeInvalidationMap` had no global-search family and window-focus refetch was disabled, so a matching record event could leave the active status result stale. This was confirmed by source tracing, not a browser reproduction. Delta 351 adds the search prefix to the centralized invalidation for all fourteen searchable entity types, including related-record and list invalidation paths. Search invalidations are coalesced and rate-limited to one per five seconds per QueryClient; only the active search query refetches through the existing permission- and row-scoped API, while inactive terms are marked stale for next use. The per-QueryClient cap is per tab, not per user: concurrent tabs can aggregate above the endpoint's 30-requests-per-minute limit. Cross-tab request coordination remains open. This closes the source-wiring gap; runtime delivery, request volume under simultaneous typing, and the user-visible latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 228. Deactivated B2B portal users kept open sessions and cached screens

`PortalAccessService::deactivateCustomer()` and `deactivate()` changed the portal principal and removed Sanctum tokens, but only staged `PortalAccessChanged` for the internal portal-access directory. `EnsurePortalGuard` rejected later API reads, while existing portal tabs had no exact-user event subscription or focus/reconnect `/me` check; their in-memory QueryClient snapshots and visible navigation could remain until another browser action. A real-stack reproduction used one internal administrator and two independent tabs for the same customer portal principal: both exact private-channel authorizations returned 200, deactivation returned 200, the next `/me` returned 401, and the pre-fix tabs received zero revocation frames in 8 seconds. The cause was a missing principal-scoped signal and session catch-up path, not a stale API cache. `PortalAccountAccessRevoked` now stages in the deactivation transaction and delivers after commit on the dedicated queue to the exact private customer/supplier principal channel; PortalLayout clears local query/draft/PWA cache and signs out on the ping, with authorized `/me` revalidation on subscription error, reconnect/focus and degraded mode. Two customer tabs passed the real-stack scenario at 640/641 ms from request start after the fix. This is one principal and one measured mutation; supplier UI, other-user denial, p95 latency and broader portal cache behavior remain unverified.

### 229. Long-lived queue workers dead-lettered refresh events from an older codec, keeping every SPA in degraded mode

The `ogami-horizon` container is long-lived and bind-mounts the API source. Durable outbox jobs decode events in the already-running PHP worker through `OutboxEventCodec`; workers do not reload PHP classes when a mounted source file changes. Read-only runtime inspection found 35 `event_outbox` rows at three attempts: 19 `AdminDashboardChanged`, 10 `ActivityFeedChanged`, and 6 `AdminUserChanged`. Their errors were `RuntimeException` entries classified as “event not registered for durable publication.” The current codec allowlist contains all three event classes, and the current `high` queue worker had started after the codec source timestamp; the historical worker's memory was not captured, so stale loaded code is strongly supported by the runtime evidence and worker lifecycle, but not directly observed.

`OutboxDispatcher::dispatch()` decodes before dispatching the event, so these failures occurred before any event listener or broadcast. These three events are refresh-only signals; the wildcard activity projection has no model/request identity to write for them (and skips `ActivityFeedChanged`). Replaying these rows is therefore safe: it schedules authorized refresh pings and does not repeat business mutations. `DispatchOutboxCommand` requires `--retry-failed` to move dead-letter rows back to pending. Until then, `RealtimeQueueHealth::deliveryHealthy()` returns false when any failed row exists, even with empty broadcast queues and no overdue pending rows. The provider displays `Reconnecting` and uses its degraded catch-up path, globally increasing stale exposure while no healthy delivery signal can be reported.

Evidence on 2026-11-02 before recovery: delivery health false; `high=0`, `broadcasts=0`, no overdue pending outbox rows; 35 failed refresh events; supported classes true in the current API runtime. In the two-context scenario the manager socket still authorized and received events, while the page indicator remained `Reconnecting`. The confirmed problem was persistent dead-letter health after a codec/process version mismatch; old worker memory is the causal inference. Ran the supported `outbox:dispatch --retry-failed --limit=100` recovery after reviewing the three content-free event types. All 2,575 outbox rows are now published, the affected failed-row count is 0, both queues are empty, and `deliveryHealthy()` returns true. A subsequent single browser run observed the manager indicator return to `Live` and the list update for create/revoke; request-start-to-peer-frame samples were 443 ms and 201 ms. These two values are not an SLO p95. No business records or cache keys were changed. Use `make dev-refresh-php` after mounted PHP changes in local dev, and terminate/restart Horizon as part of production PHP deploys.

### 230. Critical-stock auto-PO creation omits the PO list/detail refresh signal

`StockMovementService` stages `StockMovementCompleted` in the durable outbox; after-commit dispatch runs `CheckReorderPoint`, which can take `AutoReplenishmentService::checkAndReplenish()`'s critical-item branch and call `AutoPurchaseOrderService::createForCriticalShortage()`. At discovery in delta 371, that writer created a purchase order and set its persisted initial status to `PendingApproval` without calling `ChainBroadcaster` or staging a PO refresh event. The scheduled reconciliation command can reach the same writer for active blocked-item alerts. `PurchaseOrder` has registered observers for P2P aggregate cache invalidation, dashboard cache invalidation and generic badge refresh; there is no registered PurchaseOrder realtime observer. The badge event is a separate approval/count refresh and does not tell an open PO list or detail query to refetch. The PO list listens via `useRealtimeList('purchase_order', 'visible')`; details use `useChainProgress('purchase_order', id)`. Delta 373 adds the missing producer call at the writer.

This was a confirmed source-level producer gap for one indirect PO creation path. The normal auto-PR branch stages its own chain/outbox event, which explains why a broad search of `AutoReplenishmentService` alone can misleadingly suggest all replenishment paths are covered. P2P aggregate/dashboard/badge caches have after-commit invalidation on the PO write, so this path does not establish a backend cached-value defect. A requester who triggered the stock movement may receive that endpoint's response, but it does not contain the separately created PO resource; exact actor-screen behavior depends on the active page and remains unverified. At delta 371, `AutoPurchaseOrderServiceTest` checked creation and approval records but not the chain/outbox ping; `PurchaseOrderWriteRealtimeTest` covered other PO writes. Neither test was run during that source audit. No live stock movement, scheduler invocation, two-user scenario, queue trace or browser update was run. See the delta 373 correction below and `00-direct-status-write-paths.csv`.

Delta 373 fixes the source gap: after writing the PO and approval records, `AutoPurchaseOrderService` now stages `ChainStepAdvanced` through the shared `ChainBroadcaster` before its transaction commits. The event uses the existing private PO record and visible-entity channels; the central invalidation map covers PO detail, list, chain, badge, dashboard and approval queries, which refetch through each viewer's authorized API. The focused `AutoPurchaseOrderServiceTest` failed before the fix with zero PO chain rows and passed after it with exactly one (15 assertions; one PHPUnit warning). This proves the durable refresh record is staged for this writer. It does not prove queue publication, Reverb delivery, or a second user's list/detail update; those UI matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 231. Stock-adjustment peer lists had no refresh subscription for status-only changes

The `StockAdjustmentController` supports create, approve, reject and withdraw. Before delta 374, the SPA page invalidated its own query after mutation success but did not subscribe to any entity or stock signal. The registered `BadgeInvalidationObserver` refreshes sidebar/approval counts, and stock movements emit a separate inventory signal, but neither path reached this page's active list query for pending creation, rejection or withdrawal. The API list is permissioned to either `inventory.adjust` or `inventory.adjust.approve`; no content-free stock-adjustment list channel or producer existed.

Delta 374 adds `StockAdjustmentRealtimeObserver` → `StockAdjustmentRealtimeService` → `OutboxService::recordCoalesced()` → `StockAdjustmentChanged`, with the event staged in the transaction and published through the dedicated broadcasts queue after commit. The event uses `entity.stock_adjustment.visible`, whose authorization matches both list permissions and the Inventory feature, and broadcasts only opaque hashes plus action/version/actor/event metadata. The SPA subscribes through `useRealtimeList` and the central invalidation map invalidates the stock-adjustment query family. The focused pending-create test reproduced zero outbox events before the fix and passed after; the channel allow/deny test and frontend hook test passed. At the time, this did not establish queue delivery or a peer user's rendered adjustment list/buttons. Delta 571 now verifies one three-context Pending→Approved run: the socket-disabled Finance actor reconciled status/buttons from the successful API response, the Warehouse requester list received one authorized event and removed Withdraw, and the System Administrator approval inbox and sidebar approval badge refreshed. The requester render sample was 2,053 ms and the board refetch sample 1,915 ms after the actor response; neither is commit-to-UI or p95. The matrix's historical approve-route `lists` PASS for stock-level quantity remains separate. Detail, linked record screens, dashboards and reject/withdraw UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 232. Supplier cost-review decisions did not refresh the review list or open modal

`ItemCostReviewService` creates reviews from accepted-receipt price changes and updates review state on approve, reject, or renew. Before the fix, `ItemCostReview` had only badge invalidation; neither the record nor cost-review list had a dedicated refresh event, and `cost-reviews.tsx` subscribed to no realtime signal. The reviewer who acted received a fresh API resource, but other reviewers could retain an old list row. A reviewer with the modal open could also retain the old local record and actionable controls after a query refresh.

The red-first backend regression observed no `ItemCostReviewChanged` outbox event before the observer was added. The fix registers `ItemCostReviewRealtimeObserver`, coalesces lifecycle writes in the transaction, dispatches a minimal after-commit event on the dedicated broadcasts queue, and authorizes the private list channel with the same Inventory feature and `inventory.costs.review` read permission as the API. The SPA invalidates the cost-review query family; its acting mutation writes the API response into matching cached pages immediately, while remote refetches update the open modal without erasing draft notes and disable further decisions until the reviewer acknowledges the newer record.

Focused verification passed: backend decision/outbox case (22 assertions), broadcast payload case (10 assertions), private-channel authorization (3 assertions), and cost-review page tests (9). The backend RefreshDatabase test coalesces writes inside its outer transaction, so the outbox assertion verifies the final status and no-op behavior rather than separate committed requests. This confirms source/outbox behavior, permission match and page rendering only. No second-browser delivery, Reverb/queue trace, action-by-action route test, or latency measurement was performed; all UI outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The related user-specific API cache is TanStack memory only; committed events now invalidate it, and no backend cost-review payload cache exists. See delta 375 and the cache registry.


### 233. MRB hold/release records had no authorized list or detail refresh path

Before the fix, `MaterialReviewRecord` was registered only with `BadgeInvalidationObserver`. `QuarantineService` created or updated MRB rows from direct holds, GRN rework, NCR quarantine retries, customer-return dispositions, and release, but no MRB-specific event reached the list or open detail page. The list and detail components had no realtime hooks, so a peer could continue seeing `held` and the Release button after another user released the same record. The service already re-read the row under a database lock and rejected a second transition, but the UI did not receive that fresh state automatically.

The red-first hold test observed no event-outbox row; the channel test returned 403 to an authorized `inventory.mrb.view` reader. Added `MaterialReviewRecordRealtimeObserver` for Eloquent create/update/delete paths and a transaction-coalesced `MaterialReviewRecordChanged` outbox event. It dispatches after commit on the dedicated broadcasts queue to private entity-list and exact-record channels. Both callbacks require an active user, the Inventory feature, and `inventory.mrb.view`; the record callback also decodes the opaque hash and checks that the row exists. Payload contains only refresh, opaque record/actor IDs, action, version, and event ID—no status, disposition, quantity, lot, movement or MRB number.

The central SPA map invalidates the MRB, stock, NCR, effectiveness, inventory dashboard and badge query families; list/detail hooks refetch through the current user's API. Hold/release success seeds the API-returned record into the detail cache, so the acting user does not wait for WebSocket delivery. Focused tests passed for hold/outbox payload (8 assertions), scrap-release `status_changed` coalescing (11 assertions), channel scope (6 assertions), and the shared after-commit/broadcast-queue contract (950 assertions); 106 SPA invalidation/hook tests passed. This verifies source/outbox, channel decisions, and client invalidation, not actual queue publication, Reverb delivery, a peer's rendered button, or commit-to-UI latency. All matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. MRB query data is TanStack memory only; no separate MRB response cache was found. See delta 376 and the cache registry.


### 234. Material-issue slip creates and cancellations did not refresh viewers

`MaterialIssueService::create()` writes a `MaterialIssueSlip` and its lines/movements, then updates the slip total in the same transaction; `cancel()` locks and revalidates the slip, reverses unused stock/releases reservations, and changes its status. Before the fix, these Eloquent parent writes had no slip-specific broadcaster, the list and detail pages subscribed to no slip event, and a permitted peer could keep seeing an issued slip after cancellation. Stock-ledger and reservation refreshes update their own views but do not invalidate the slip record or list.

The red-first regression found no outbox row on create; the auth test returned 403 for `inventory.issue.create`. Added `MaterialIssueSlipRealtimeObserver`, a transaction-coalesced `MaterialIssueSlipChanged` event on the dedicated after-commit queue, and private list/detail channels requiring the Inventory feature and the same `inventory.issue.create` permission as GET list/detail. The record callback also verifies the opaque hash resolves. The payload contains refresh metadata and opaque slip/actor hashes only. The SPA central map covers slip list/detail plus stock, inventory dashboard and Production query families. The create response seeds the actor's detail cache before navigation.

Focused create/cancel event and payload test passed (8 assertions), channel authorization passed (5 assertions), after-commit/dedicated queue contract passed (960 assertions), and 109 SPA invalidation/hook tests passed. The backend test logs that its minimal fixture lacks configured COA account 5010 while stock movements run; this is a test-fixture warning, not proof of a production posting defect. No browser/two-user delivery, queued broadcast, or latency measurement ran; all UI outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The MaterialIssueSlip query data is TanStack memory only, with server HTTP responses uncached; event invalidation now covers active slip query families. See delta 377 and the cache registry.


### 235. Product routing writes had no routing refresh producer or subscribers (source-confirmed, outbox-regressed)

`ProductionRoutingService::create()`, `update()`, `duplicate()`, and `activate()` all performed their routing/version changes and downstream BOM/MRP propagation, but the traced path had no ProductRouting event, observer, private channel, or routing-page subscriber. The pre-fix focused create regression asserted the durable outbox boundary and failed with zero rows. This confirms a propagation gap for routing writes; it does not by itself reproduce a peer browser's stale rendered page.

The shared service propagation now stages one content-free `ProductionRoutingChanged` signal in the same transaction via `OutboxService::recordCoalesced()`. It targets private list and record channels. Authorization now matches the API route group: active user, `modules.production`, `production.view`, and `production.routings.view`, plus record existence for detail. SPA list/detail subscriptions route through the central invalidation map; actor mutations apply the API response without waiting for Echo. The editor preserves unsaved input and requires an explicit reload action after a newer server version arrives.

Focused verification passed: create producer regression (1 assertion), channel access/denial and feature-gate checks (7 assertions), minimal private payload and durable codec round trip (6 assertions), and the routing invalidation-map case (1 test). No two-session browser run or queue/Reverb delivery timing was captured. All four routing matrix UI rows therefore remain NOT_VERIFIED. This cause is limited to ProductRouting and is not evidence that remaining module transitions are covered.


### 236. Work-order resource release and soft-delete visibility changed without a WorkOrder refresh

`WorkOrderService::releaseResources()` clears Machine/Mold ownership and updates `production_released_at`; before this change the resource observers refreshed their own pages but no WorkOrder refresh reached an open work-order detail/list. `delete()` soft-deleted planned work orders and `restore()` made them visible again, also without a WorkOrder signal. The pre-fix focused resource-release regression failed because `event_outbox` had no `ChainStepAdvanced` row, while other resource events were present. This confirms the producer gap, not an end-to-end peer UI failure.

`ChainBroadcaster::refreshWorkOrder()` now stages the existing private chain refresh event through the durable outbox in the same transaction after release, soft delete, and restore. It deliberately uses `OutboxService::record()` rather than creating a chain-step ledger row: these writes do not change the workflow status. The central `work_order` invalidation map refreshes detail, list, readiness, dashboard, and badge query families. The chain/list channels continue to require the production feature and the API's production read permissions.

Focused checks pass: ChainBroadcaster private-channel/content-free contract (6 assertions), paused resource-release regression (10 assertions), and soft-delete/restore outbox checks (4 assertions, including no synthetic chain ledger rows). No two-user browser delivery or latency test ran; matrix UI outcomes remain NOT_VERIFIED.


### 237. Finished-goods receipt retry changed WorkOrder output state without refreshing WorkOrder viewers

`WorkOrderOutputService::retryProductionReceipt()` could successfully create a finished-goods receipt while leaving WorkOrder status unchanged. Its inventory receipt signal did not invalidate the embedded WorkOrder outputs, and the no-good-output branch could change handoff state without a WorkOrder refresh. On expected 422 failures, the SPA displayed a toast but did not refetch the acting user's cached WorkOrder/output state. Source tracing confirmed these missing paths; no peer-browser stale-render scenario was run.

The retry service now stages `ChainBroadcaster::refreshWorkOrder()` in the transaction for changed receipt-handoff state, including successful retries where WorkOrder status is unchanged. When the workflow status changes, the existing WorkOrder status event remains the refresh path. Manual-failure state is only signaled if it actually changes. The central `work_order` invalidation map covers the separate output query prefix. The actor gets the successful API output applied immediately to detail/output caches; failed retries invalidate those keys and refetch.

The focused backend test failed before the fix because no durable WorkOrder refresh row existed after a successful retry, then passed with 4 assertions. SPA map, actor-success cache application, and actor-error-refetch tests pass. This proves producer staging and local query behavior only: no two-user browser, queued broadcast/Reverb receive trace, or latency sample was captured, and matrix outcome cells remain NOT_VERIFIED.


### 238. WorkOrder draft creation could leave lists stale when no routing operations were generated

`WorkOrderService::createDraft()` creates a planned WorkOrder and optionally generates BOM material and routing-operation children. The parent had no created-event observer. When no active routing operations existed, no WorkOrder refresh reached peer list/detail viewers; the operation observer only covered drafts that actually produced operation rows. The red-first no-routing regression confirmed an empty event outbox after create.

The service now stages the existing content-free WorkOrder refresh when its routing generation created no operation rows. When operations are present, their existing transaction-coalesced `WorkOrderOperationChanged` signal already reaches the WorkOrder list/detail audience, so the parent chain refresh is omitted to avoid duplicating that notification. This applies to all callers of `createDraft()`, including ordinary API, MRP and NCR paths; stock planning retains its existing parent/related signals.

The focused no-routing producer test passes (2 assertions) and confirms no synthetic chain-step ledger row. An existing active-routing snapshot test now also asserts exactly one `WorkOrderOperationChanged` row and zero duplicate `ChainStepAdvanced` rows; it passes (4 assertions total). No two-user screen run, queue/Reverb receive trace or latency sample has been captured. UI matrix cells remain NOT_VERIFIED.


## Candidate ruled out — degraded delivery fallback with a connected socket (2026-11-02)

The candidate was that a Reverb socket left connected while Horizon/outbox delivery was degraded would receive only one catch-up, because the recurring fallback loop appeared to depend on transport state. Source tracing rules this out: setRealtimeDeliveryState('degraded') changes the derived provider connection state to reconnecting, and the active-query backoff loop keys off that derived state. The focused RealtimeProvider suite passed all 6 tests, including its fake-timer assertion that active queries catch up again after five seconds while delivery remains degraded. No production fix was indicated. This is code/test evidence; it does not measure a live worker outage or prove every subscriber's degraded behavior.


## Candidate ruled out — stale query reuse after a hooked page remount (2026-11-02)

For pages using the shared realtime hooks, a query's TanStack staleTime does not suppress the subscription catch-up. useRealtimeList and useRealtimeRecord pass their authorized refresh callback as the private-channel subscription-ready callback; the shared registration layer invokes it on initial subscription, reconnect, and when a mounted consumer joins an already-ready shared channel. The targeted useRealtimeList suite passed 39 tests, including the initial subscription invalidation assertion. No source fix was needed. This conclusion applies only to consumers actually using the hooks; pages without an authorized realtime listener remain unverified coverage gaps.



### 239. Restoring a linked PO left its source PR status and action snapshot stale (fixed in the locked restore transaction; live delivery pending)

`PurchaseOrderService::delete()` soft-deletes the draft and calls `reopenSourcePrIfLastLinkFor()`, which runs `syncConversionStatus()` and broadcasts a separate Purchase Request chain refresh. Before the fix, `restore()` restored the same linked PO and broadcast only the Purchase Order event. It did not recompute the PR or publish its refresh. The PR read path only loads relations (`PurchaseRequestService::show()`); it does not reconcile conversion state. `orderedQuantitiesByPrLine()` counts restored, non-cancelled PO lines. The new focused service regression first reproduced this as an Approved PR after restoring the only PO that fully covered its lines, with the PR refresh count stuck at one. Restore now recomputes source PR conversion status after restoring the PO, using the existing transaction and helper, so changed PR status stages its private chain refresh. The test verifies the PR returns to Converted and the expected PR/PO refresh rows exist. See `PurchaseOrderService.php:1416-1454`, `PurchaseOrderService.php:440-449,566-625`, and `PurchaseRequestAccessPolicy.php:256-267`.

The API/service state and outbox producer are regression-tested, but no real browser, API endpoint, queue worker, Reverb delivery or two-user latency scenario ran. The PR detail/list uses its existing authorized `purchase_request` channel and query family; visible peer freshness remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 240. Supplier dispatch child status could leave the PO detail stale (source-confirmed; producer fixed, live delivery unverified)

The PO resource embeds the `SupplierOrderDispatch` ledger, while the detail listens to the parent PO signal. The approval ping can precede the queued dispatch listener’s writes. Before delta 436, `claim()` could create/reclaim Pending without a refresh; `complete()` could persist ManualRequired without one; `recordFailure()` updated Failed by query builder without one; and standalone recovery could confirm or cancel a child row after the PO’s earlier event. That left gaps for attempt count, status, failure reason, and manual-transmission guidance. Focused tests observed the expected PO outbox signal missing in each targeted regression before implementation.

Delta 436 adds `ChainBroadcaster::refreshPurchaseOrder()`, a content-free event on the existing private PO chain/entity/vendor channels. New/reclaimed Pending and ManualRequired are signalled in their owning transactions; Failed is updated and signalled atomically; standalone/recovery Confirmed and Cancelled changes signal only when the status actually changes. PO send/cancel/reject transactions suppress duplicate child signals because their own parent event already covers the update. Eleven targeted feature scenarios (66 assertions) and one broadcaster unit scenario (8 assertions) pass. This verifies backend outbox staging in the test database, not queue-worker/Reverb delivery, frontend refetch/rendering, or the two-user SLO. The matrix therefore retains `NOT_VERIFIED` for live UI outcomes.


## Source-trace update — CustomerComplaint NCR handoff (2026-11-02)

The previously implemented complaint refresh path covers both Eloquent handoff changes and the `markNcrHandoffManual()` query-builder write, which explicitly stages the same refresh in its transaction. The queued recovery listener delegates status changes to those service methods. No new live-path event or cache gap was confirmed in this slice. The demo `GoldenPathDemoSeeder` still writes with `DB::table()->updateOrInsert`, bypassing model observers; this is recorded as a bootstrap/demo-only writer, not represented as a production user mutation. The status transition and direct-writer evidence are in `00-explicit-state-transitions.csv` and `00-direct-status-write-paths.csv`. No action was executed and no client outcome was reproduced; all handoff matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

The status-only peer-list producer gap described in root cause #231 has been addressed in the current tree: Eloquent status writes stage the private after-commit `StockAdjustmentChanged` list ping, and the page invalidates its filtered list query. The stale-risk matrix now records current source wiring while keeping unexercised actor/peer/button cells `NOT_VERIFIED`. The server re-reads the threshold on submit, so this is a stale form preview risk, not a stale server decision. The StockCountSession lifecycle is source-wired through observed service writes, one coalesced session event per transaction, authorized private record/list channels, and a central invalidation map that includes nested count, stock, dashboard and approval query families. Raw stock-count item snapshot inserts are covered by the observed parent session write. Runtime outcomes remain NOT_VERIFIED. ### Delta 408 — StockMovement GL handoff status and retry freshness

Mapped nine initialization/transition rows for `StockMovement.gl_handoff_status`: the shared move path commits Generated, ManualRequired, or NotRequired; ManualRequired retries can resolve to Generated or NotRequired; the GoldenPath raw opening-movement fixture relies on the database default NotStarted; and the one-time legacy migration backfills Generated/NotRequired/ManualRequired. The status writer helpers are `MovementGlPostingService::markManual/markGenerated/markNotRequired`; retry callers include the Inventory API, queued replay listener, and delivery-cost recovery. No StockMovement status observer is registered, and the model disables timestamps.

The initial `StockMovementService::move()` path records `StockMovementCompleted` in its transaction; `PublishInventoryStockChange` retires inventory/dashboard generations and stages the content-free `InventoryStockChanged` broadcast after commit. StockLevelsPage subscribes to that signal and the central map includes the movement query family. The actor's successful GL retry invalidates its local movement list. However, `MovementGlPostingService::retry()` updates the GL handoff without staging a StockMovement/InventoryStockChanged ping. Other movement viewers can retain `manual_required` and the Retry action; WorkOrder details also embed the finished-goods movement status and have no guaranteed refetch for a retry-only change unless a separate auto-completion transition occurs. The live ChainBottleneck API itself has no backend payload cache, but its dashboard widget refreshes every 60 seconds. Logged this source-confirmed gap in `FINDINGS.md` for a separate Inventory/Production PR. No retry action, two-user browser, queue, test, API call, DB/Redis operation or latency measurement was run; all UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

Added nine transition/initialization rows and five direct-writer groups. Current source catalog totals: 243 transition rows, 106 direct status-writer rows, 48 of 83 enum-backed fields without an explicit transition map, and 752 endpoint/scheduled matrix rows. The stock movement list/cache registry now records its owner, identity, TTL and distinct normal-create versus retry invalidation behavior.


### 241. Stock movement GL retry left movement and embedded WorkOrder status stale (producer and action contract fixed; live delivery pending)

`StockMovementService::move()` records `StockMovementCompleted` in the same transaction as the movement and its initial GL handoff. The post-commit publisher invalidates inventory/dashboard generations and emits `InventoryStockChanged`; `StockLevelsPage` subscribes to this event and the central map includes `['inventory','movements']`. Retry was a separate writer path with no corresponding signal. Delta 440 added a meaningful-state comparison to `MovementGlPostingService::retry()` and stages the existing content-free `InventoryStockChanged` event through the durable outbox when journal link, status, or operator message changes. The event uses the private inventory and WorkOrder channels already authorized for those readers, and its existing central invalidation map covers movement lists and linked WorkOrder detail. The focused authenticated retry API test failed before implementation with zero InventoryStockChanged outbox rows, then passed after the change (1 selected test, 9 assertions); codec decoding and payload minimization were asserted.

The original producer regression proved the outbox path only; queue delivery, peer browser behavior and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. A new pair of red-first API regressions then found that the resource omitted user action/version fields and a second retry after Generated was accepted with 200. The resource now projects per-viewer `permissions.retry_gl` and `available_actions`, Retry is offered only for ManualRequired with no journal, and locked retry revalidation returns a typed 409 for stale state. `StockMovement` has no mutable timestamp, so a version column defaults to 1 and increments on meaningful GL handoff writes. The SPA gates Retry from the projection, installs the API result directly into active movement list caches, then invalidates; a 409 refreshes the movement list. The selected backend tests pass (3 tests, 19 assertions), the focused SPA cache/action test passes, targeted typecheck/ESLint/Pint pass. Delta 441 still maps stock signals to chain-bottleneck queries, whose 60-second interval remains degraded catch-up. No live queue, Reverb, two-user browser or latency scenario ran.


### 242. Payroll GL retry accepted a stale Pending action (fixed by locked state revalidation; browser delivery pending)

`PayrollPeriodService::actionContext()` exposes `retry_gl` only when a finalized/disbursed period has no journal and its handoff is ManualRequired or NotRequired. The retry service locked the period and rechecked its payroll status and missing journal, but did not recheck the handoff state before writing Pending and staging a UUID-keyed `PayrollGlPostingRequested`. A new endpoint regression first reproduced the defect: the second request returned 202 instead of 409. The service now checks the locked handoff state against the same ManualRequired/NotRequired set and returns the existing typed conflict for Pending or terminal states. The regression verifies the first response disables `retry_gl`, the second response is a 409, and only one retry outbox row exists. The registered PayrollPeriod observer already publishes writes after commit; this was an action-state guard issue, not a missing broadcast path.

The API action is now dynamically covered sequentially through two authenticated calls, reproducing and preventing duplicate retry publication. The backend lock protects concurrent calls by serializing this same guard. No two-browser screen, live queue worker, Reverb delivery, or latency scenario ran; those UI and delivery outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

The initial PO event can be delivered before the listener creates that row. The selected test did not run the async worker, Reverb, API, or two browser sessions, so delivery timing and visible stale rendering remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. Query-builder insert bypasses and unrelated GRN updates remain outside this fix and are tracked separately in `FINDINGS.md`.

### 247. Material issue refresh maps omitted the reservation-list query family (source fix added; runtime reproduction pending)

`MaterialIssueService::issue()` changes a linked `MaterialReservation` to Issued when fully consumed, or reduces its quantity while keeping it Reserved after a partial issue. `MaterialIssueService::cancel()` can change linked reservations to Released. ### 248. Async production-receipt missing-actor fallback was reported as an event gap (ruled out for schema-valid writes)

The listener contains a fallback when neither the output creator nor a configured system actor resolves. The prior concern predicted a status change without a WorkOrder refresh. However, `work_order_outputs.recorded_by` is a required FK to `users` with restrictive deletion, and the current create paths supply that actor. Attempting to null the field in the isolated feature fixture failed with PostgreSQL SQLSTATE `23502` before the listener could run. The ordinary retry failure branch instead calls `retryProductionReceipt()`, whose service catch marks ManualRequired and stages a WorkOrder refresh. No normal API/database path to the missing-actor branch was established; no product change is warranted without evidence of a constraint-valid producer.

The no-actor fallback is unreachable for schema-valid application writes because `work_order_outputs.recorded_by` is a required FK to `users` with restrictive deletion, and output creation supplies that user. An isolated attempt to null `recorded_by` failed at PostgreSQL's NOT NULL constraint before the listener ran. The earlier source-only concern is closed as a false positive. Ordinary synchronous receipt failures commit `manual_required` together with `WorkOrderOutputRecorded`; receipt retry failures stage a parent WorkOrder refresh from the service transaction. No missing refresh event was confirmed for valid application writes.

The authenticated output API has no backend response cache; query data is in-memory and API reads are `private, no-store`. No listener execution, queue timing, API action, or two-user browser scenario was run, so visible freshness remains `NOT_VERIFIED`. `FINDINGS.md` records the evidence and closure.
