### 249. PPAP stale requests could overwrite a newer review state or approved evidence (fixed, Delta 826)

Before Delta 826, `PpapService::submit()`, `review()`, `approve()`, `reject()`, `update()`, and `updateElement()` evaluated state on the route-bound model before writing without a row lock. A deterministic stale-snapshot regression reproduced a model loaded as Submitted overwriting the row after it advanced to Approved. A second regression reproduced a preloaded Submitted parent permitting an evidence write after the parent advanced to Approved. This established the stale-read/write failure ordering without claiming two simultaneous request processes.

Delta 826 wraps interactive submission writes in transactions, reloads and locks the parent, and compares the current status with the request snapshot. Element writes lock the parent first and child second, preserving the existing Approved evidence freeze. A changed snapshot returns HTTP 409 `ppap_state_conflict`. The new focused pair passes (2 tests / 10 assertions), and existing tests for immutable approved evidence and editable pre-approval evidence pass (2 tests / 13 assertions); all four are warning-marked by the test container's missing `/var/www/.env`. No cache entry is involved. No real multi-user/API/browser race was run, so the PPAP UI freshness matrix remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 250. Calibration daily status recompute can save a value derived from stale schedule dates (source-confirmed; concurrent reproduction pending)

`CalibrationService::recomputeStatuses()` chunks records, derives Active/Due/Overdue from the in-memory `next_calibration_date`, and saves without locking or reloading the row. Interactive date updates and record-calibration writes use `lockForUpdate()`, but the scheduled job can read an older date, then save its derived status after the newer date has committed. That leaves status inconsistent with the current schedule until another edit or daily pass.

The calibration API has no backend result cache; the model observer refreshes changed rows. This is a source-confirmed stale-writer race, not stale cached data. No overlapping job/edit or UI scenario was run, so user-visible behavior remains NOT_VERIFIED. Separate fix is in `FINDINGS.md`.

### 251. General Machine update bypasses the locked status graph and breakdown domain event (source-confirmed; runtime reproduction pending)

`StoreMachineRequest` accepts `status` for machine create and update. `MachineService::update()` applies the request directly to the row; it does not call `transitionStatus()`, whose locked path validates `allowedTransitions()` and records `MachineStatusChanged`. The registered `MachineRealtimeObserver` does stage the generic after-commit `MachineChanged` refresh for the general update, so status detail/list readers receive the common content-free ping. The durable `MachineStatusChanged` listener that pauses a running WorkOrder and opens the breakdown/downtime side effects is not invoked by this general update route.

Thus a user with machine-manage permission can write a status that skips the dedicated transition guard and, for Breakdown, leave the production side effects incomplete while clients receive the generic refresh. The code path is source-confirmed; no machine update/transition, API action, listener, or two-user UI scenario was run, so no visible incident is claimed. This is recorded in `FINDINGS.md` for a separate MRP/Production fix. It is a functional state-transition defect, not a missing record-refresh producer.

No tests, API actions, queue checks, cache operations, DB/Redis writes or browser sessions ran.



### 256. GRN shipment-link updates bypassed the observer's watched fields (fixed)

`GrnService::stageForShipment()` and `createDraftForSupplierShipment()` may update an existing expected GRN by writing only `shipment_id` or `supplier_shipment_id`. The GRN observer previously watched QC/handoff and journal fields, so this Eloquent update staged no GRN refresh or linked PO refresh. A focused Shipment Received test cleared earlier events for the expected draft and failed because the link update produced no `GoodsReceiptNoteChanged` row. A second supplier-dispatch test failed with zero events when `supplier_shipment_id` was temporarily removed from the observer. Delta 443 watches both relationship fields; both tests now pass (2 tests, 8 assertions), including linked PO refresh. Queue/socket/UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 257. GRN handoff refresh omitted the Finance bottleneck audience (fixed)

Status-transition `ChainStepAdvanced` events target `dashboard.finance`, but handoff-only `GoodsReceiptNoteChanged` events previously targeted only GRN readers. Finance dashboard's existing channel listener maps `.chain.refresh` to its authorized chain-bottleneck query, so GL/QC handoff pings could not reach that widget. The focused authenticated GL retry test decoded the actual outbox event and failed because `private-dashboard.finance` was absent; delta 442 added the private channel, whose callback requires Accounting enabled and `dashboard.accounting.view`. The same test passed (1 test, 8 assertions), and the existing channel authorization test passed grant/deny/inactive cases (1 test, 3 assertions). No data fields were added to the event; delivery and UI freshness remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 258. Equipment readiness refresh skipped assigned work orders until a crew existed (fixed at producer)

`MachineRealtimeService` and `MoldRealtimeService` stage the linked work-order signal after relevant equipment changes. Before delta 452, `AssignedWorkOrderRefreshService::stageForResource()` found those work orders only by starting at an active `WorkOrderCrew` row. A work order with `machine_id` or `mold_id` set but no active crew was therefore omitted even though execution readiness reads machine/mold status and compatibility. A red-first `MachineRealtimeTest` changed the assigned machine status and found zero `AssignedWorkOrderChanged` outbox rows. The producer now queries work orders directly by the assigned resource ID and unions the existing active NCR-rework crew matches. Focused Machine and Mold producer tests pass (6 tests, 22 assertions); each emitted signal targets the existing private work-order chain channel and contains only refresh metadata.

This fixes the source path for direct equipment assignments with or without an active crew. It does not prove queue, Reverb, channel subscription, or UI delivery, and it does not remove the dialog's 30-second timer because time boundaries and other upstream readiness inputs remain incompletely mapped. The matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 259. Product-level routing and inspection-spec edits did not refresh active work-order readiness (fixed at producer)

`WorkOrderService::executionReadiness()` calls `WorkOrder::assertFinishFirstRoutingCompatible()` and `InspectionSpecService::forProduct()`. Routing changes already emitted `ProductionRoutingChanged`, while inspection-spec and child-item writes emitted `QualityRecordChanged`; those signals reached routing/spec viewers but did not target active work-order channels. Two red-first producer regressions created a confirmed work order for a product, changed the product's routing/spec and observed zero `AssignedWorkOrderChanged` rows.

Delta 453 adds `AssignedWorkOrderRefreshService::stageForProduct()` for confirmed, in-progress and paused work orders. `ProductionRoutingRealtimeService` and the shared Quality publisher for `InspectionSpec` use this method, so a parent-spec edit or child-item edit stages one coalesced content-free signal for each active work order of that product. Focused routing and inspection-spec tests pass (2 tests, 7 assertions). This verifies durable producer rows only; browser/channel delivery, readiness rendering and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

The Start Production dialog's 30-second interval remains because readiness also depends on clock boundaries and resource/warehouse inputs, including maintenance windows and overlapping schedules, which are not completely mapped to the work-order refresh channel. No dialog browser trace was captured.


### 260. Equipment reservation changes did not refresh other affected work-order readiness (fixed at producer)

`WorkOrderService::executionReadiness()` reads both confirmed `MaintenanceWindow` reservations and overlapping `ProductionSchedule` rows. Before delta 454, `ProductionScheduleRealtimeService` staged the changed schedule's own event only; creating a reservation did not refresh other work orders assigned to the same equipment. The maintenance-window child observer refreshed only its owning maintenance WO, while `MaintenanceWindowService::release()` changed `state` with a query-builder update and emitted no event for affected production work orders. Red-first focused tests observed zero `AssignedWorkOrderChanged` outbox rows after window confirmation, window release, and schedule creation. A fourth red test confirmed schedule reassignment omitted the work order linked to the previous equipment.

Delta 454 fans reservation availability changes to work orders linked to both current and previous machine/mold IDs. Maintenance-window create/update/delete model paths and the service's bulk release path are covered; production-schedule fan-out runs only when reservation-relevant fields change. Existing content-free `AssignedWorkOrderChanged` signals coalesce by work order and transaction and use the authorized record/operator channels. The four focused regression cases pass (4 tests, 6 assertions). This verifies outbox producers only; queue/Reverb delivery, two-user UI, latency and performance remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The Start Production timer remains while clock/crew and other readiness inputs are mapped.


### 261. Finished-goods setup changes did not refresh active production readiness (fixed at producer and consumer)

`FinishedGoodInventoryService::assertReady()` reads the product part number/unit, a matching finished-goods Item's active/type/unit mapping, and the existence of an active unblocked location under a Finished Goods zone in an active warehouse. Product and Item events previously refreshed their own inventory/catalog audiences but did not refresh active WOs for that product. Warehouse-layout changes did not reach the production lifecycle channel or the work-order readiness hook. Red-first tests observed zero `AssignedWorkOrderChanged` rows after product unit and matching item availability changes; an FG location block event lacked `private-production.readiness`; the SPA hook test found no listener for that event.

Delta 455 makes ProductRealtimeService refresh active WOs by product and ItemRealtimeService match current/previous item codes to affected products when mapping fields change. Finished-Goods-impacting warehouse/zone/location changes now add a content-free ping on the existing `production.readiness` private channel, authorized by `production.work_orders.lifecycle`; open WO hooks subscribe and invalidate their own readiness query keys. Focused API checks pass (3 tests, 6 assertions) and the SPA realtime hook file passes 40 tests. The event includes no warehouse, location, item or product details in its broadcast payload.

This verifies service/outbox and hook invalidation only. Queue/Reverb delivery, two-user UI, stale visible frame and commit-to-UI latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. Other readiness inputs and the Start Production timer remain under audit.


### 262. Employee and HR shift eligibility changes omitted active-work-order readiness refreshes (fixed at producers)

`WorkOrderService::executionReadiness()` validates the active crew against employee status, linked user role/account state, the Production department, employee shift assignments, working days and the referenced shift definition. Before this slice, `ProductionStaffingObserver` already refreshed work orders for employee/account changes, and the shift business services explicitly called `notifyStaffingChange()`. The model-level Department/Role/Shift/EmployeeShiftAssignment realtime observers did not consistently reach the work-order readiness audience. Red-first tests reproduced no `AssignedWorkOrderChanged` outbox row after a Production department deactivation, role-slug change, direct EmployeeShiftAssignment write or direct Shift schedule edit; the existing employee-status path passed via `ProductionStaffingObserver`.

Delta 456 adds a shared `AssignedWorkOrderRefreshService::stageForEmployees()` lookup over active assigned crews. Department code/active/delete/restore changes refresh affected employees' active work orders; role slug/delete/restore changes do the same for linked users; assignment lifecycle changes refresh current and previous employee crews; shift start/end/active/delete/restore changes refresh employees assigned to that shift. These reuse the authorized, content-free `AssignedWorkOrderChanged` private work-order signal. Service-level shift assignment and observer fan-out share the existing coalescing key, and the focused API case confirms exactly one work-order signal for the transaction.

The same reassignment regression found a second producer bug: after an assignment had loaded its employee relation, changing `employee_id` left `loadMissing('employee')` pointing at the former employee. The assignment event therefore targeted the stale employee and the new employee's work order received no refresh. `ShiftAssignmentRealtimeService` now resolves the current employee from persisted `employee_id`, reads Laravel's prior-value snapshot for reassignment, and routes the refresh to both current and previous private employee channels. The payload remains limited to refresh/event/action/version/time metadata.

The focused staffing-readiness feature file passes 8 tests / 12 assertions, including old/new crew fan-out, minimal payload keys and single-event coalescing. The first red run failed on all four previously uncovered readiness paths; the status path passed through its existing observer. This is producer/outbox evidence only: no API route, browser/two-user screen, queue worker, Reverb delivery, cache metric or latency scenario ran. Matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; the Start Production visibility-aware 30-second fallback remains because clock-derived readiness changes without a database event and broader runtime delivery coverage is incomplete.

### 263. Recruitment stale actions could remain visible after a missed refresh (server projection and conflict recovery wired; runtime delivery unverified)

Recruitment propagation is source-wired: `JobApplicationRealtimeObserver`, `ApplicationInterviewRealtimeObserver`, and `ApplicationNoteRealtimeObserver` stage a private, after-commit `RecruitmentChanged` ping, while application/posting pages subscribe through the shared hooks. Before delta 463, stage requests did not carry the stage the viewer had rendered. A stale screening client could reach current-stage interview validation and receive a 422 field error instead of a state conflict; the advance/reject handlers then left the detail query untouched. Before delta 465, the Recruitment resources omitted server-computed actions, permissions and versions, and the posting detail derived its controls from local status and permission state.

Delta 463 adds an optional `expected_stage` to the application stage endpoint and sends it from updated SPA clients. `AdvanceApplicationRequest` validates interview fields against the expected stage, and `RecruitmentService` compares that stage with the row-locked current stage before applying advance/reject. A mismatch returns typed HTTP 409 `recruitment_state_conflict` without changing state or sending candidate mail. On conflict, the SPA refreshes application detail/list and linked posting queries; a plain confirmation closes, while interview draft fields remain intact.

Delta 465 closes the server-action projection gap for both Recruitment resources. `RecruitmentApplicationActions` and `RecruitmentPostingActions` return the current viewer's permissions, state-valid `available_actions`, and a version derived from `updated_at`; posting archive availability also checks the loaded application count. The application list eager-loads interview eligibility, and the nested posting relation eager-loads its application count, avoiding per-row resource queries. Posting detail buttons now render from the API action list. Updated clients send the rendered version on posting edit, archive, restore and status actions; locked service methods compare it and return typed 409 on stale versions, while older requests without a version remain supported. Application arrival is a related-state exception: it may make a draft unarchivable without advancing the posting's `updated_at`, so the locked archive service also returns typed 409 for a versioned stale archive action. Application advance/reject continues to send `expected_stage` and revalidate the row-locked state.

The actor's posting status response is written directly into its detail query. A component regression found that immediately invalidating that same detail query could replace the returned state with an older refetch; exact-detail invalidation was removed while posting lists and linked application lists still refresh. A typed posting conflict invalidates those active views, and the posting detail adopts the latest server action projection. The edit form includes the version in its write and reports a 409 without invalidating the form query, preserving typed input. It fetches current posting metadata through the authorized API and offers a review link; its component regression confirms the typed title survives that fetch.

Evidence: the `RecruitmentPostingTest` feature file passes 9 tests / 42 assertions, including manager versus view-only action projection, application-aware archive availability, restored/status actions, and a stale-version HTTP 409. The posting detail test first failed while the stale Publish button was still rendered and passes after the action projection change; five focused Recruitment page test files pass 16 tests, including a 409 edit conflict that retains the typed title while fetching the latest posting. The earlier application suite passes 9 tests, including stale-stage conflict recovery and preserving interview drafts. These use PHPUnit's testing database and mocked browser API responses. No Recruitment two-context browser, live socket delivery, or cross-role rendered timing scenario was run because the running stack is production-configured with SMTP and a live database. Event delivery, commit-to-screen latency, and the 1–2 second SLO remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 264. Failed cache-generation writes could keep old payload namespaces reachable (bounded retry and fallback fixed; repeated failure remains)

`CacheGeneration::current()` uses the generation marker to form the payload key. Several owners rotated markers with a direct `Cache::put()` inside best-effort after-commit callbacks. If that write returned `false` or threw while Redis remained readable, those callbacks logged and continued; a subsequent API refetch still read the old marker and could return the old cached snapshot. This affected shared dashboard/widget/badge data, settings, analytics, statements, holidays, role permissions, payroll tables/progress, DSS parameters, and procurement/inventory/production aggregates. This was source-confirmed; the Redis write-failure path had not been induced.

Delta 464 adds `CacheGeneration::rotate()` as the common write path. Delta 466 adds one immediate retry of the same idempotent generation write before attempting to forget the prior marker; a store that returns `false` from `forget()` is accepted only when a follow-up read confirms the marker is absent. A missing marker then initializes to a fresh UUID namespace through the existing atomic `current()` path. All generation-marker writers found in the reviewed application sources use this helper; payload writes are unchanged. The helper does not delete every old payload key immediately: retired entries remain bounded by their owner TTL. If both marker writes and marker retirement fail while cache reads continue to work, the old namespace may remain reachable until that TTL; this remains error-logged and requires operational retry/availability work.

Evidence: the original red-first `DashboardCacheGenerationFailureTest` seeded `old-generation` plus a stale payload, forced `Cache::put()` to return false, and observed the stale snapshot. Delta 464 then verified retirement and source fallback. Delta 466 adds two focused cases: a transient rejected write succeeds on the bounded retry, and two rejected writes retire the old marker; the exact PHPUnit file passes 2 tests / 11 assertions. These use a mocked repository, not live Redis. A 13-file targeted PHPUnit invocation completed 29 tests / 118 assertions but reported one unrelated test error (`StatementCacheAfterCommitTest` calls a nonexistent `JournalEntryObserver::saved()` hook) and two Government Table outbox-count failures; those remain separate. No live Redis fault injection, two-user view, cache hit rate, API p95 or latency measurement ran. Runtime cache freshness and the repeated write-plus-retirement failure remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 265. Overtime approvers could read the list API but could not subscribe to its refresh channel (fixed; standard-role browser flow verified, direct-only browser scope remains unverified)

The Attendance overtime list route permits either `attendance.view` or direct `attendance.ot.approve`, and `OvertimeListRoutePermissionTest` confirms an approver with only the latter can read the list/options. Before delta 467, `entity.overtime_request.visible` authorized only `attendance.view`, and the list page subscribed only when `attendance.view` was granted. A focused channel test reproduced 403 for the API-authorized approver, and a focused SPA test observed the page subscribing with no audience despite that approver's list access. The refresh event is content-free; list refetch remains API-scoped by `OvertimeDecisionPolicy`.

Delta 467 aligns the private list-channel callback and SPA subscription gate with the direct permission alternatives on the list API. It deliberately does not use the SPA's broader delegated-approval helper because the list endpoint itself does not accept delegated-only access. The exact API channel regression passes (1 test / 4 assertions), and the focused Overtime list component regression passes (1 test). Unrelated viewers remain denied, and disabling the Attendance module denies the channel. No business rule or record visibility changed.

OvertimeRequest observer/outbox events, per-viewer actions, actor reconciliation, typed 409 state revalidation, and the central invalidation map were already source-wired in delta 313. The list authorization mismatch was the remaining source gap found here. Delta 468 adds a real-chromium run against `https://ogamiph.dev` with two independent browser contexts (employee requester and department-head approver). Both private channels subscribed and each client received two `attendance.overtime.changed` frames. The requester's API response rendered Pending in 66 ms; the other-role approval list rendered the new row 1,023 ms after that response. After the approver received HTTP 200, the requester list moved to Approved in 905 ms without reload. This is one scenario, not a p95/SLO claim; the browser approver also had `attendance.view`, so the direct-`attendance.ot.approve`-only channel case remains verified by focused API/component tests but not real-browser delivery. Other Overtime transitions and detail/badge/dashboard/general approval-inbox views remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 266. Standalone Quality dashboard did not consume Inspection refreshes (focused component reproduction and fix; live delivery unverified)

The standalone `/quality/dashboard` page reads inspection pass-rate and Pareto queries, but before delta 469 it subscribed only to NCR changes. Inspection mutations already produced the private `.inspection.refresh` signal, and the central invalidation map already had an Inspection entry; however, that map used `['quality','dashboard']` while this page's live query keys are `['quality','pass-rate']` and `['quality','pareto', ...]`. Thus even adding a listener alone did not refresh the metrics.

The red-first component regression rendered a 50.0% pass rate, emitted `.inspection.refresh` on `entity.inspection.visible`, and observed the dashboard remain at 50.0%. After adding the authorized `useRealtimeList('inspection', ...)` subscription, the test remained red at 50.0%, which isolated the separate invalidation-prefix mismatch. Delta 469 adds the inspection-list subscription gated by `quality.inspections.view` and adds the exact pass-rate/Pareto prefixes to the central invalidation map. The focused test then passed, refetching the mocked API and rendering 75.0%. The signal carries refresh metadata only; the subsequent metric fetch remains the authorized API request. No backend cache entry or business rule changed.

This confirms the consumer/invalidation gap in a component-level transport simulation. It does not exercise any Inspection mutation endpoint, channel authorization against a live API, Reverb, a second browser/role, latency, or actual cached API data. All Inspection route matrix outcome cells therefore remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; only the source wiring and focused consumer regression are evidenced.

### 267. Stale Inspection result versions returned validation errors instead of conflicts (typed 409 and refresh recovery fixed; live delivery unverified)

`InspectionService::assertResultVersion()` rejected a missing or outdated result version with `ValidationException::withMessages()`. The API therefore returned HTTP 422 for a stale checklist/result action even though the row-locked server state had changed. A red-first `ChecklistSubmissionTest` submitted expected version 2 against the current version and observed 422 with the `expected_result_version` validation error; the freshness contract requires a state conflict that tells the client to reload.

Delta 470 adds `InspectionStateConflictException`, rendered for API requests as HTTP 409 with code `inspection_state_conflict` and the existing `expected_result_version` error key. Result-version assertions still run against the locked row and no state/business transition changed. The stale checklist submission, stale cancellation, and counted-batch version regressions pass (3 tests / 14 assertions). On the SPA, matching conflicts invalidate the central Inspection query families; detail review/cancel/replace closes stale review confirmation while the authorized detail reloads, and checklist/counted/full-count editors retain their local entries for review. The focused Inspection detail and decision-panel suite passes 48 tests. Its new review-conflict test confirms the latest `passed` state is loaded and the stale “Approve pass” action disappears.

The failures were reproduced through API/component tests, not with two live users. No Inspection socket delivery, live cache response, role-scoped browser, latency, or p95 measurement ran. The 9 `InspectionController` route rows have the typed-conflict contract and source consumers noted in the matrix, but every actor/peer/list/action/count/dashboard/inbox UI outcome remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Post-baseline cache marker isolation — 2026-11-02

Static config/source evidence confirmed that production generation-marker reads and rotations used the default `CACHE_STORE=redis`, which resolves to the same `redis-cache` / `allkeys-lru` store as disposable payloads (`api/config/cache.php`, `api/config/database.php`, and the local Docker cache service). If a marker rotation's retry and retirement both fail while an old marker remains readable, future reads can continue selecting its stale payload namespace; the existing `DashboardCacheGenerationFailureTest` covered retry and successful retirement but not that dual-failure boundary. This is a confirmed failure mode in the cache design, but it was not independently traced to the successful PR approval browser delay or reproduced by forcing live Redis eviction/failure.

**Current disposition (delta 478):** marker reads and rotations now use the named `generations` store backed by the persistent Redis service (DB 4 by default); disposable payloads remain on the bounded `redis-cache` store (DB 3). Thus LRU eviction of payloads cannot also evict the marker needed to select the current payload namespace. Focused failure-path tests cover retry and retirement, and Redis metadata scanning checks both stores. This removes the confirmed shared-eviction design risk in source/config; live Redis eviction/failure was not induced, and a simultaneous marker-store write plus retirement failure remains a fail-open namespace risk rather than a proven runtime incident.

The focused regression was first run red because there was no dedicated generation store or `currentDefault()` path. The fix routes every production generation read/rotation through the named `generations` cache store on the persistent non-evicting Redis instance, logical DB 4 by default; payload data remains in the bounded LRU store. Cache metadata audit/prune now cover and identify both stores. Delta 480 then verified 151 exact legacy marker copies in the former payload store by matching their key names to active generation-store markers and confirming UUID-shaped string values in both stores; it individually removed those 151 keys. The post-cleanup scan found no duplicates. Retired payload snapshots and any marker without a current owner remain bounded by TTL. The targeted five-file test run passed 11 tests / 55 assertions; PHPUnit emitted environment warnings because `/var/www/.env` is absent in the container. No Redis eviction or total marker-store outage was injected, so those infrastructure failure cases remain unverified.

### 268. Initial connection and initial health failure duplicated authorized catch-up reads (provider regression reproduced; request fan-out reduced)

`RealtimeProvider` previously invalidated every active React Query query when Echo first connected and again when the first health check reported degraded. At the same time, each page's private channel callback already invoked its own authorized catch-up after the channel acknowledged. That duplicated initial reads across the whole active page and could amplify API concurrency during mount. The health probe also ran every two seconds in every visible tab. Before the fix, new provider tests reproduced a global invalidation on first connect and on the initial degraded health result. After that fix, first connection relied on the page's authorized channel-ready catch-up; global catch-up runs after an actual reconnect, delivery-state transition, recovery, focus, or browser-online signal. Initial degraded delivery enters the existing visibility-aware active-query fallback instead of triggering an immediate second global fetch. At that stage the visible-tab health cadence changed to ten seconds with a two-second request timeout; Delta 677 below returns it to two seconds after a separate regression reproduced delayed stalled-worker detection.

The focused provider suite passes 7/7 tests, including initial-connect suppression, reconnect catch-up, no duplicate initial-degradation refetch, and degraded fallback/recovery. In the subsequent four-context final-approval scenario, the peer made three successful detail reads and one aborted stale request; an earlier run of the same scenario had eight successful detail responses. The post-action authorized GET started 113 ms after the actor response and completed in 1.452 s; the peer rendered Approved and removed Cancel in 1.726 s. These are single-run request counts and timings, not a controlled benchmark or p95. The API still warns at its eight-child PHP-FPM cap during this four-context load (root cause #269), so concurrency pressure has not been eliminated.

### 269. PHP-FPM worker pool saturated during the multi-context approval flow (runtime warning confirmed; latency attribution partial)

The upstream API image used the PHP-FPM pool default `pm.max_children=5`. During the first four-context final-approval run the API container logged `server reached pm.max_children setting (5)`; an authorized post-event detail GET took 3.177 s. This supports pool saturation as a capacity bottleneck, although one browser timing sample does not isolate FPM from queueing, page activity, database time, or network latency.

Delta 484 addresses repeated `pm.max_children=8` warnings in the live dev stack through 18:08 UTC: the local dev Dockerfile now allows ten children and up to five spare workers; the dynamic minimum/start count remains two. Production stays at eight because its separate image permits 256 MiB per PHP request and production-host peak memory has not been profiled. Before changing the dev pool, the live host reported 15.41 GiB total memory with 11 GiB available, the API cgroup had no memory ceiling and used about 95 MiB, and four idle FPM workers measured about 20–22 MiB PSS each. These are idle dev-host measurements, not peak/production estimates; PHP `memory_limit` remains 128 MiB in dev. The local API image rebuilt and its container was recreated; the Dockerfile assertion and runtime `php-fpm -tt` both confirm max_children=10, start/min spare=2, and max spare=5. The SPA root and Vite client remained HTTP 200 at `ogamiph.dev`. No post-change multi-user latency sample or API p95 was collected, so this removes the observed eight-worker cap but does not yet prove faster peer rendering or the 1–2 s SLO. The production pool was not changed.


### 270. Near-live aggregate refreshes contended with live record detail (reproduced; central scheduler now prioritizes detail)

In one four-context final-approval run, the System Administrator's open Purchase Request rendered Approved 2.998 seconds after the VP response. Its trace showed three `/dashboards/badges` requests starting 57–75 ms after the response and taking 2.09–2.87 seconds; peer record-detail requests started about 182 ms after the response and took 1.62–1.98 seconds. Adjacent API logs showed the PHP-FPM pool reaching `pm.max_children=10`. This established simultaneous near-live refetch fan-out and worker saturation during the miss; it did not isolate PHP-FPM as the sole cause of the detail latency. Source review confirmed `scheduleRealtimeChangeInvalidation()` put detail, lists, related records, counts and dashboards into one 100 ms lane, while shared signals used the same lane.

A red-first `invalidationMap.test.ts` regression reproduced both badge and detail queries starting together. The central scheduler now coalesces live detail/additional record keys in a 100 ms lane and near-live list/related/count/shared-signal prefixes in a 300 ms lane. The focused realtime invalidation file passes 77/77 tests. In the next four-context run, the actor socket was disabled; the peer's authorized no-store detail GET started +166 ms after the response, returned `approved` / `actions.can_cancel=false` in 911 ms, and the UI rendered Approved in 1.359 s, removing Cancel at 1.097 s. Two `chain.refresh` frames were received. Source and the durable ledger show that the queued purchase-order conversion listener can separately mark an approved PR conversion pending and then manual_required, staging a refresh for each write; the latest tagged approved request had three approved-state refresh messages published. The browser trace did not capture event IDs or wait for the final conversion state, so the exact later-frame mapping and receipt of all signals remain unverified. This is one passing sample following the 2.998 s miss, not proof of a stable p95. PHP-FPM warnings at the 10-worker ceiling continued in adjacent logs; their contribution in the passing run is not isolated. The response was `private, no-store`, `CF-Cache-Status: DYNAMIC`, and not served by a service worker, ruling out those cache layers for this response only.

### 271. Successful save redirects were stopped by the shared dirty-form guard (reproduced; redirect release wired across discovered forms)

In the initial Purchase Request E2E attempt, POST 201 and submit 200 committed a submitted row, but the creator remained on the form with the unsaved-change confirmation open and no detail GET followed. `useFormSafety` supplied `when = formState.isDirty && !saved`; TanStack Query invokes mutation `onSuccess` before updating `isSuccess`, so a success-callback `navigate()` ran while the guard was still active. The failed four-context run reproduced the user's actor-screen symptom independently of WebSocket delivery.

The shared guard now returns a one-shot navigation allowance, exposed by `useFormSafety.allowNavigationAfterSave()`. A repository AST inventory found 50 success-callback redirects across 47 `useFormSafety` pages; each now releases the guard immediately before navigation. The focused guard regression was red before the API existed and passed 3/3 afterward. The four-context Purchase Request scenario then reached the detail page and completed approval with the actor socket disabled. No individual browser verification was run for the other redirected forms. A static audit also inventoried 12 direct `useUnsavedChangesGuard` files: five UI files enable in-app navigation protection, and none performs navigation in a mutation `onSuccess`; the only direct-guard success redirect uses browser-exit-only protection. No matching sequencing case was found there.


### 272. Personal dashboard layout writes did not refresh sibling tabs (fixed at transaction producer and SPA consumer)

`DashboardLayoutService::saveUserLayout()` and `resetUserLayout()` changed caller-owned rows under a version check and returned a fresh result to the actor, but emitted no realtime event. The actor's successful save/reset invalidated its own React Query cache; other tabs for the same account had no refresh signal, leaving the layout and `layout_version` stale until navigation/refresh and causing a later save to receive 409. This is per-user preference state: other accounts must keep their independent layouts and are not entitled to the owner's signal.

Red-first evidence: the new exact-file `DashboardLayoutTest` first observed zero outbox rows after a changed layout save. A separate test reproduced the early-return gap where saving an empty layout deleted prior rows but staged no refresh. The picker component regression reproduced a same-account layout prop update replacing the in-progress draft with the remote layout. Delta 487 adds an after-commit, outbox-backed `DashboardLayoutChanged` signal on the existing exact-self private preferences channel; only changed canonical content stages a signal. The broadcast is content-free and omits the user's hash ID and layout values. The frontend's central map invalidates active personal layout and widget-data queries. Save/reset responses seed the actor's identity-scoped layout cache immediately. Dashboard layout/widget cache keys now include `realtimeIdentityKey` (user, role, employee scope, permissions, delegations and features), and the editor preserves typed draft state while showing a conflict banner and disabling stale saves after a remote update.

Evidence: four focused backend feature cases pass (4 tests / 14 assertions); the broadcast after-commit/dedicated-queue and inventory guards pass (2 tests / 1,221 assertions); the exact private preferences-channel authorization case passes (1 test / 3 assertions), allowing the owner and denying a different account; focused DashboardPicker and invalidation-map SPA tests pass (84 tests), including cache-key isolation across users and same-user permission changes. No backend layout cache was found and no cache was removed. These tests verify producer, authorization target/source contract, key design and client behavior in isolation. No two-tab live Reverb test or commit-to-render latency measurement was run; matrix runtime cells remain PARTIAL/NOT_VERIFIED as recorded.

### 273. Dashboard layout and widget queries shared keys across permission identities (key collision risk fixed; no cross-user render leak reproduced)

The default and role dashboard shells previously used the same React Query keys `['dashboard','layout',{rich:true}]` and `['dashboard','widget-data',widgetKeys]` for every authenticated user. Both responses depend on the viewer's widget permissions and personal layout. `authStore.applyUser()` already clears the entire QueryClient whenever user, role, employee scope, permissions, delegations or features change, which is a safety guard; no wrong-user cached render was reproduced. However, an identity-free key violated the cache-scope contract and made isolation depend exclusively on all access-change paths clearing at the right time.

Delta 487 adds the shared `dashboardLayoutQueryKeys` factory, scoped by `realtimeIdentityKey(user)` for the permission-filtered widget catalog, layout and widget payloads. Its focused regression verifies different cache keys for different identities; the picker response-cache test verifies writes target the same current-user key. Existing auth-store clearing remains defense in depth. No browser user-switch scenario or cache-hit/performance benchmark ran, and this finding is a source-confirmed cache-ownership risk rather than a claim of observed disclosure.


### 274. Broad status-action invalidation could replace the actor's fresh response (reproduced; shared hook corrected)

`useOptimisticStatusAction` installs the successful server record into the exact detail key, then invalidates `invalidateKey` on settle. Leave actions pass the broad `['leaves']` prefix, which also matches `['leaves','request',id]`; the redundant active detail request could replace the returned `approved` state and `available_actions` with an older response. The same hook is used by Leave, Overtime, and Purchase Request detail actions, so the source pattern affected 11 approve/reject/cancel/submit controls. This is a client cache race; the server continues to own status and action eligibility.

Red-first evidence: the new hook test mounted an active Leave detail query and a Leave list under the same prefix. It installed a fresh `approved` mutation response, then the family invalidation refetched the detail mock with its prior `pending` state; the test failed with the actor cache reverted and `available_actions.approve=true`. Delta 488 excludes only the exact detail query already reconciled from that invalidation predicate, while keeping related list queries active and invalidated. When the mutation response has no fresh record, the detail is still invalidated for reconciliation. The focused hook file passes 2 tests after the fix. This reproduces the shared client race in an automated query-cache harness; no live Leave action, browser, cross-user socket delivery, or API latency sample ran, so route UI outcomes remain as marked in the matrix.

### 275. Fresh mutation responses could be overwritten by an older same-family read (shared race reproduced; confirmed callsites reconciled)

The same cache race existed outside the status-action hook. Several mutation handlers called `setQueryData()` with a successful API response and then invalidated a key prefix that also selected that exact detail. An already-running detail request could finish after the mutation and write its older status, relation, or available-actions projection over the new response. Direct invalidation also left matching older list/detail reads eligible for TanStack's request deduplication, so an invalidation could reuse a fetch that had started before the commit. This mechanism was reproduced in `queryInvalidation.test.tsx`: with a pending detail fetch, a fresh mutation snapshot was installed and an older `pending` response then overwrote it. The test failed before the fix and passes after exact-key cancellation.

Delta 489 extends the central invalidation implementation in `spa/src/lib/realtime/invalidationMap.ts`. `invalidateRealtimeChange()` and `invalidateRealtimeSignal()` accept optional exact keys to preserve. The common prefix invalidator cancels an in-flight read for each preserved exact key with `revert:false`, then excludes that exact key while refreshing other active matching lists, linked queries, counts, and dashboards. It also cancels older active reads for invalidated prefixes with `revert:false` before refetching, so a locally installed mutation response or partial response is not reverted while the authorized current read is in progress. `invalidateQueryPrefixesExcept()` exposes the same cancellation policy for families without a registered entity signal. WebSocket handlers use the unchanged no-exclusion path and still refetch through each viewer's authorized API.

Source-reviewed mutation consumers now use this shared path in Assets disposal/association/create/update; Quality inspection, calibration, inspection-spec and NCR actions; Inventory item, material-issue, MRB and quality-plan writes; Production routing, Work Order lifecycle/receipt/operation, crew assignment and late-production decisions; Recruitment application stage/interview/note actions; Accounting journal entries; customer price and delivery actions; Supply Chain shipment plans; Maintenance assignment; Warehouse transfers; and personal dashboard save/reset. The Phase 0 matrix annotates 72 corresponding mutation rows. Full API resources are kept in their exact actor detail keys while related families refresh; partial child responses still trigger an authorized refetch. This was source-checked for the listed callsites, not route-by-route reproduced in the browser.

Verification: the query-family regression failed before the fix because an older in-flight detail response replaced the fresh mutation value; it passes after exact-read cancellation. The central invalidation map, generic query invalidation and shared status-action hook files pass **83 focused SPA tests**; targeted ESLint passes on touched files. A focused Work Order failed-receipt retry test passes after narrowing the error reconciliation to its detail/output families. A larger multi-file UI run had one Asset association interaction timeout that passed when isolated; a separate full Work Order detail-file run produced Pusher-constructor/test failures beyond the focused retry case. These noisy suites are not counted as passes, and no broad API suite was run. No cross-role browser, live Reverb delivery, cache hit rate, API p95, or commit-to-UI measurement was collected. Matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 276. Exact cache-key exclusions used JSON property order instead of TanStack key equality (reproduced; matcher corrected)

The central invalidator compared each query-key segment with `JSON.stringify()`. TanStack Query's default hash sorts plain-object fields, so these keys are identical to TanStack but not to the local matcher: `['work-orders','detail',{id,view}]` and `['work-orders','detail',{view,id}]`. When a successful mutation response was installed under the first key and the second spelling was supplied as an excluded exact key, the invalidator failed to recognize it and refetched the detail, reopening the actor-response overwrite race. The same local prefix helper could also fail to match structurally equivalent object segments.

Red-first evidence: a focused React Query test mounted the first key, installed a fresh `complete` response, then excluded the same key with reversed object-field order. Before the fix, the detail query ran once during invalidation and the test failed. `keyStartsWith()` now delegates to TanStack's `partialMatchKey`; exact exclusions require structural matches in both directions and equal key lengths. This aligns prefix and exact matching with TanStack's query-key semantics without changing retention or authorization.

Verification: the new regression passes, and the focused generic invalidation, central map and shared status-action files pass **84 tests**. Targeted ESLint and `git diff --check` pass. This is a reproduced cache-reconciliation defect, not evidence of a live user-facing incident; no browser session, API, WebSocket, cache-hit or latency measurement ran. The 72 route outcome rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 277. Payroll Adjustment actor reconciliation left an older detail request live (reproduced; page migrated)

Payroll Adjustment approval/rejection wrote the full API response to `['payroll-adjustment', id]` but directly invalidated only `['payroll-adjustments']`. The list invalidation did not cancel an already-running detail GET. If that GET returned its pre-decision record after the mutation, the actor's fresh `Approved` view reverted to `Pending` and restored the Approve button. The backend's row lock and pending-state validation remained correct; this was a frontend cache race.

Red-first evidence: the page test kept `adjustmentsApi.show()` pending, seeded the pending server record so the Approve action was visible, completed approval, then resolved the old GET with the pending record. Before the fix the cache and button reverted. `refresh()` now uses the registered `payroll_adjustment` realtime invalidation map, cancels older in-flight reads for the affected record/list families, preserves the exact detail key containing the mutation response, and refreshes lists and related aggregates through authorized API reads.

The focused `payroll/adjustments/detail.test.tsx` passes **2 tests**, including the red/green regression; targeted ESLint and `git diff --check` pass. The actor regression is page-level but uses a local React Query harness; no live payroll API action, peer browser, Reverb delivery, or latency measurement ran. Approve/reject matrix UI outcomes therefore remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 278. Cancelling a stale read with `revert:false` left the fresh detail query in error state (reproduced; central cancellation corrected)

The delta 489 stale-read protection used TanStack `cancelQueries(..., {revert:false})` after callers had seeded a fresh mutation response. TanStack stopped the old request from writing but dispatched its cancellation as a query error; detail pages that render `isError` then showed an unavailable/error state despite having the fresh record. The Goods Receipt Note page regression exposed this immediately after the shared invalidator was adopted.

Red-first evidence: the generic in-flight query test observed the preserved data but found `query.state.status === 'error'` after cancellation. The GRN mutation page test showed “Failed to load GRN” after a successful rejection response while an older detail request was pending. The central invalidator now snapshots data for excluded exact queries, cancels using TanStack's normal revert behavior to restore an idle non-error state, reapplies the snapshot with its original `dataUpdatedAt`, then invalidates the remaining active families. Matching list/detail reads also use normal revert before their current authorized refetch.

Verification: the in-flight cache test and all **7** GRN detail tests pass, including the stale response and canceled-query error regressions; targeted lint and `git diff --check` pass. No API call, browser, peer delivery, or performance measurement ran. Runtime matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Additional source-confirmed production-list gap — attendance-driven crew changes (Delta 493)

An attendance change for an employee assigned to an active work order already emitted `AttendanceChanged` to `chain.work_order.{hashId}`. Detail consumers listened to that per-record channel. Production list consumers instead subscribed to `entity.work_order.visible`, and the attendance event did not send to that list channel. Cross-role production viewers may lack `attendance.view`, so they also cannot receive the separate attendance list ping. This left work-order list, crew and readiness queries without an event path for that viewer even though the detail path was wired.

The event now adds the private work-order entity channel only when the changed Attendance row affects at least one active work order. Existing channel authorization requires production module access and both list-route permissions. The payload continues to carry refresh metadata only; query results are refetched from the viewer's authorized API. The SPA list hook handles the attendance event, and the central work-order map covers the dependent query families. Red/green producer and hook tests prove the source contract and handler path. A headless Chromium attempt authenticated Production and admin contexts, rendered the Production list, and observed its private entity-channel subscription. The dev database had no active work-order crew member with current attendance, so the scenario stopped before any mutation; no event delivery or refetch was attempted. The cause remains confirmed from source plus focused test reproduction, not a captured stale screen/network trace; matrix outcome cells stay `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Cross-cutting frontend invalidation path bypasses (Delta 494)

The Phase 0 hook scan found private realtime listeners outside the entity map that called `invalidateQueries()` directly. This did not prove a separate stale frame, but it meant those handlers bypassed the common in-flight cancellation and coalescing behavior and split related keys into multiple cache traversals. The listeners now route their scoped key families through `scheduleRealtimeQueryInvalidation()` or the entity map's count/list scheduler. Focused tests pass 133/133 across the affected shared hook families. This is a consistency and stale-read risk disposition, not an independently reproduced user symptom; no runtime/UI outcome was promoted.


### 279. Leave action controls and stale decisions lacked a complete server freshness contract

`LeaveRequestResource` exposed `can_approve` but not a complete `available_actions`, `permissions`, or full-precision version. List and detail reads supplied the approval projection, but create and action responses did not consistently compute it for the current actor. Leave detail buttons fell back to status plus `can_approve`; successful actions invalidated only the broad `leaves` family instead of the central linked-query map. A stale department/HR approval or rejection raised a general business-rule exception and returned HTTP 422, while a missed signal could leave linked active views stale after the API refused the action.

Red-first source/API tests found missing action/version fields and a replayed approval returned 422. After correcting a separate observer import failure (tracked in `FINDINGS.md`), the targeted API tests pass **2 tests / 14 assertions**. The focused SPA tests cover an explicit empty action array overriding the legacy approval flag and a stale 409 refreshing detail plus linked calendar; the three focused files pass **8 tests**. These are focused API/component reproductions, not a captured two-user browser failure.

Mutation responses now attach the acting user's server-computed approval projection; the resource provides actions, permissions and a microsecond version; stale status decisions return typed HTTP 409; and Leave mutations use the central invalidation map while retaining the authoritative actor response. The explicit action array is authoritative when present, and the legacy `can_approve` fallback remains for rolling compatibility. UI outcome cells stay `NOT_VERIFIED`: no live Leave event delivery, peer action-button change or commit-to-render timing was measured. No cache retention or backend result-cache policy changed.

### 280. Overtime and Purchase Request status actions skipped mapped related-query refreshes

The shared `useOptimisticStatusAction` reconciled the acting user's detail response, but its Overtime and Purchase Request callsites supplied no entity-map reference. They therefore invalidated only local list prefixes. The maps already declare linked Overtime attendance/self-service/approval/dashboard families and Purchase Request chain/PO/Bill/count/dashboard families. This left those active actor-side related views outside the mutation refresh path even though peer event handlers use the central map.

The source gap was confirmed by comparing each hook callsite to `invalidationMap.ts`. All three Overtime detail decisions and all four Purchase Request detail decisions now pass their entity and ID to the shared status hook; successful full-resource responses preserve the exact actor detail while the map refreshes linked active families, and a conflict refetches the detail too. A focused hook regression verifies the Purchase Request detail is retained while its list and linked Bill query refetch; the hook file passes **4 tests**. No live action/browser or peer delivery was run in this delta; prior matrix evidence remains unchanged, including existing PASS/PARTIAL cells for Purchase Request approval/cancellation and Overtime approval.

### 281. P2P child changes did not refresh parent details and lists that depend on their status

`ChainBroadcaster` sent each PO, GRN or Bill event to the changed record and its entity-list channel. Purchase Order details embed GRN/Bill state, and Purchase Request details embed converted PO/GRN/Bill summaries. A viewer may be authorized for a parent record but lack permission for its child module's private channels, leaving the embedded status stale even though the frontend parent map includes those linked queries.

`ChainBroadcaster` now adds only authorized parent channel targets: PO changes reach their linked PR detail and list; GRN and Bill changes reach their linked PO and PR details and lists. The existing PO/PR callbacks match the corresponding API read policies. Parent IDs remain private constructor metadata and do not appear in `broadcastWith()`. Parent hooks then run the central invalidation map and refetch through each viewer's API. A focused `ChainBroadcasterTest` passes (12 tests / 79 assertions), and the focused SPA record-hook regression passes (1 test). No live two-user P2P scenario ran; matrix UI outcomes remain unchanged.

### 282. Delivery outcome and receipt actors discarded the API's fresh Delivery resource

`DeliveryAttemptPanel` received the updated Delivery/DriverDelivery from report and amend mutations, but its success handler discarded the resource and only invalidated broad query prefixes. The driver report regression reproduced a successful API mutation while the mounted run sheet continued to show its prior status/outcome because its refetch returned the prior mock state. `DriverPhotoCapture` had the same response-discard pattern: after upload it invalidated `['driver']` and navigated back, forcing the actor detail to depend on another GET even though the upload already returned the fresh DriverDelivery. The actor therefore had no response-first guarantee, and the broad local invalidations bypassed the shared stale-read cancellation and related-query map.

`DeliveryAttemptPanel` now synchronously writes the returned record into the exact actor detail key (internal Delivery or driver DriverDelivery), then schedules the central `delivery` invalidation without waiting for related reads and excludes that authoritative exact key. Its definitive-error refresh also uses the central map. `DriverPhotoCapture` follows the same response-first pattern before navigating; its test leaves the subsequent GET pending and verifies the updated Delivered state is already rendered. The focused `driverDelivery.test.tsx` passes **12/12**, and `DeliveryAttemptPanel.test.tsx` passes **1/1** for the internal Delivery detail cache; both driver actor regressions failed before their respective fixes. This reproduces and fixes the actor cache behavior at the component boundary, not a live production incident. Peer Reverb delivery, linked active-query behavior on real routes and commit-to-render latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 283. Sales Order confirmation discarded its authoritative record response

`SalesOrderController::confirm()` returns the fresh `SalesOrderResource`, but the detail page ignored `result.data` and invalidated the detail, list and chain keys independently. The detail regression reproduced the stale actor view: confirmation returned a `confirmed` resource, while the page's read still returned the earlier `draft` resource and the actionable Confirm button remained. Cancellation had the same discarded-response path. These page-local invalidations also skipped mapped linked queries, including MRP plans/runs and invoice finalization's embedded Sales Order summary.

The shared `reconcileRealtimeMutationResponse()` now installs a full successful API record into the exact actor key, cancels/protects an older exact read, and refreshes mapped active families while excluding that authoritative key. Sales Order create/edit/confirm/cancel and Delivery outcome/receipt consumers use the same helper; partial-response Sales Order proposal, cancellation-response and MRP-retry paths use the map without a response seed. Sales Order invalidation includes MRP runs/plans; invoice invalidation includes Sales Order summaries. The new page regression failed before the response was installed and passes after; focused verification passes **7** Sales Order detail tests, **82** invalidation-map tests, **12** driver-delivery tests, and **1** DeliveryAttemptPanel test. Two live two-context Sales Order create/list runs passed, one after the shared response reconciler was wired into the create page; neither exercises confirm/cancel or peer detail/actions. This verifies the actor cache contract and one create/list delivery path, not a confirmation multi-user SLO; those matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 284. Invoice finalization discarded the authoritative state and retained an actionable button

`InvoiceController::finalize()` returns the updated Invoice resource, but the detail page discarded it and invalidated the broad invoice prefix. A red-first UI test performed a successful finalization response while the detail GET still returned Draft; the page stayed Draft and retained Finalize. Prebill approval and cancellation had the same full-resource response pattern, while collection returned a child resource and also bypassed mapped linked-query invalidation.

Invoice finalize/prebill approval/cancel now use `reconcileRealtimeMutationResponse()` to seed the exact actor Invoice key and centrally refresh active mapped queries; collection uses the central `invoice` map because its response is a Collection child. The Invoice map refreshes linked Sales Order summaries, journal/customer/portal/badge/dashboard families. The finalization regression passed after the fix; the full focused `accounting/invoices/detail.test.tsx` file passes **10/10**. No Invoice mutation was run in two browser contexts, and peer button/status refresh, channel delivery and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 285. Purchase Order detail mutations discarded full API responses and bypassed linked invalidation

The internal Purchase Order detail page received full `PurchaseOrder` resources from submit, approve, reject, send, cancel, close, short-close, budget acknowledgment, and delivery-date update, but discarded them and invalidated a page-local query prefix. A slow or stale detail GET could therefore leave the actor on the old status with an obsolete server-provided action. These local prefixes also missed the registered entity map, including MRP dependencies after short-close. Supplier-response accept/reject returned a child `PurchaseOrderResponse`; those handlers invalidated broad PO queries without immediately reconciling the response state into the open detail.

The detail now uses `purchaseOrderQueryKeys.detail(id)` and `reconcileRealtimeMutationResponse()` for full PO responses, then centrally refreshes active PO lists, chain, GRN/Bill/delivery/MRP and count/dashboard/approval families while protecting the exact actor key. The child-response handlers synchronously update the matching `latest_response` from the API response, then use the same PO invalidation map to fetch the authorized full PO state. Posting a draft Bill now routes through the Bill map, which includes embedded PO detail. The PO map includes MRP plans/runs for short-close effects.

Red-first page evidence: before the full-response migration, the submit test returned `pending_approval` from the API while the rendered page remained Draft with Submit available; it now renders the returned state without another detail GET. A focused child-response regression verifies Accepted replaces Pending and its Accept action disappears while the authorized PO refetch is deliberately left pending. The PO detail test file passes **2/2**, and the central invalidation map file passes **82/82**. Targeted ESLint passes for the touched PO, Invoice, Sales Order, Delivery, and shared invalidation files. No live PO mutation, two-context PO detail/action scenario, Reverb delivery, API latency, or cache hit-rate was measured; peer UI and route-level runtime outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 286. HR employee edit discarded its full API response before returning to the profile

`employeesApi.update()` returns the updated `Employee`, but the edit page discarded it, invalidated the same employee detail query, and navigated to the profile. If the old detail GET remained in flight or the replacement GET was slow, the profile could reuse the stale cached employee while the mutation had already succeeded. This mutation also coordinates optional shift assignment changes, whose separate shift query remains refreshed by the Employee map's attendance family.

Red-first page evidence: the edit-page test returned an `on_leave` Employee from the update API, held the old `active` detail read open, and found the exact cache still contained `active` after navigation began. The page now installs the full response through `reconcileRealtimeMutationResponse()` under `['hr','employee',id]` and refreshes related Employee, attendance/leave, production, payroll and dashboard families through the central map. The focused `hr/employees/edit.test.tsx` passes **1/1** after the fix. This is a component/query-cache reproduction, not a live HR API mutation or cross-user browser scenario; peer delivery and route-level UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 287. CRM Customer edit invalidated away its own updated profile response

`crmCustomersApi.update()` returned the full Customer resource, but the edit page discarded that value, invalidated the broad CRM customer prefix (including the exact detail key), and navigated to the profile. `ActionCenterTaskController::update()` returns each updated task ID, state, assignee and snooze deadline, but `ActionCenterPage` discarded that response and only invalidated local query prefixes. The API response has an explicit `ActionCenterTaskUpdate[]` type. `action-center/index.test.tsx` passes **1/1** after the stale-state regression failed before the fix. The page now reconciles the child response's number/status/total into the matching embedded CreditNote, protects that exact RMA detail during cancellation, then uses the central `credit_note` map to refresh other active Accounting, return, ledger, dashboard and badge families through their authorized APIs. `return-management/detail.test.tsx` passes **4/4**, including the stale-child regression. ### 290. Bill and Bill Payment responses were discarded by the AP detail page

The Bill detail page's `cancel` and `postDraft` endpoints return a complete Bill, while payment create/approve/reject/void endpoints return a `BillPayment` embedded in that Bill. Before the fix, the cache stayed Draft/Unpaid and the payment row had no Posted state. Payment responses update or append the matching embedded child immediately, then `invalidateRealtimeChange()` cancels older reads and refreshes the authorized parent Bill and linked query families. `accounting/bills/detail.test.tsx` passes **6/6** after the three stale-response cases failed before the change. This is a focused SPA/API-mock reproduction, not a live AP mutation or two-user broadcast run; latency, cache-hit rate, API p95 and peer UI outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 294. Contact Inquiry status updates discarded the actor's full API response

`inquiriesApi.updateStatus()` returns a complete `ContactInquiry`, but the detail page ignored it and only invalidated the broad CRM inquiry prefix. When the authorized follow-up GET was pending, the actor continued to see the prior status and Close button after the API had returned Closed. The focused file passes 2/2. This is component/query-cache evidence, not a live route, Reverb or two-context result; matrix route UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 297. Bill state did not refresh its parent GRN detail

### 301. Leave approval lists ignored successful record responses, including bulk results

The department approval, HR approval, and rejection APIs return full viewer-authorized `LeaveRequest` resources. ### 302. LeaveType events had no mounted consumers on the Leave pages and forms

The backend already emits a content-free after-commit `LeaveTypeChanged` event to private `entity.leave_type.visible`, and the SPA invalidation map covers Leave type, self-service type and leave-request option queries. Yet neither the mounted `LeavesPage` (which contains `LeaveTypesManager`) nor the separate `CreateLeavePage` and `SelfServiceLeavePage` mounted `useRealtimeList('leave_type', 'visible')`. An authorized user could therefore edit a type while active request lists and forms retained old labels or options; the forms' five-minute reference query stale time gave no timely refetch guarantee. Red-first page assertions confirmed the missing consumers. The shared hook is now mounted at the Leave parent page and both separate forms, using the existing channel policy (`modules.leave` and `leave.view`) and central map. The focused Leave list/type, self-service and realtime-list check passes 63/63. This verifies the source and hook invalidation path, not live WebSocket delivery; all four route-level UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 303. ImportBatch list subscribed to a channel Laravel does not define

`ImportsPage` subscribed through the common `useRealtimeList('import_batch', 'visible')` hook, which generated `entity.import_batch.visible`. The standard `ImportBatchChanged` event broadcasts on `entity.admin_import_batch.visible`; `routes/channels.php` defines authorization only for that actual name. Because the prior hook test asserted the frontend-generated name rather than the backend event/channel contract, it passed while import managers had no authorized matching channel for commit/rollback batch refreshes. The hook now maps `import_batch` to Laravel's exact channel. The test was changed first and failed with the old channel; the focused realtime-list file passes 43/43 after the fix. The real channel is private and requires active `admin.import.manage`. This verifies code and hook contract, not live WebSocket receipt; import route UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 304. Production Routing subscribers used the SPA entity alias as the private channel name

`ProductionRoutingChanged::broadcastOn()` publishes the list signal on `entity.production_routing.visible`; `routes/channels.php` authorizes that private channel for active users with the Production feature and both `production.view` and `production.routings.view`. `RoutingsListPage` and the routing editor use the public realtime entity type `product_routing`. The common hook composed `entity.product_routing.visible`, for which Laravel has no authorization callback. Routing list viewers could therefore miss create/update/activate/duplicate refreshes, and routing detail viewers' aggregate catch-up subscription used the same invalid channel. The exact record channel `production.routing.{hashId}` remained correct for changes to that same routing ID, but does not replace the list signal or catch-up when another version of the same product changes.

The new hook contract test first failed and showed `entity.product_routing.visible` where the backend channel was expected. A shared alias map now resolves `product_routing` to `production_routing` for both `useRealtimeList` and detail catch-up; it also retains the ImportBatch mapping. Focused SPA hook coverage passes **45/45**, targeted ESLint and SPA typecheck pass, and the existing backend authorization test passes **1 test / 7 assertions**. A static reconciliation now matches all 75 backend private entity-list channels to frontend list consumers. This confirms the channel contract and frontend registration, not live Routing delivery or rendered UI. Routing route matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 305. Warehouse dashboard listener used the wrong broadcast event name

`WarehouseDashboardChanged::broadcastAs()` returns `dashboard.warehouse.changed` and sends a content-free refresh ping on the private `dashboard.warehouse` channel. `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` retires the `dashboard:warehouse` cache generation and stages the transaction-coalesced ping after source writes. The channel authorization requires `dashboard.warehouse.view`. `AppLayout` subscribed to that correct channel but passed `.warehouse.dashboard.changed` to `useRealtimeSignal`, so the handler never matched the server event and the central `warehouse_dashboard_changed` invalidation map did not run. Active Warehouse dashboard queries could stay stale in the SPA cache until another refetch trigger, even though the server-side generation had rotated.

Evidence: a new source-contract regression scans Laravel `broadcastAs()` return values against runtime SPA source string literals. It failed before the fix with exactly `dashboard.warehouse.changed` missing. The scanner parses all 122 current methods, including the one ternary method with two possible names, and compares 111 unique names; unrecognized return shapes fail the test. The shell now listens for `.dashboard.warehouse.changed`. The payload-audit CSV had the same reversed event name and was corrected to match the event class. The contract regression and the focused realtime-list hook file pass **46/46**; targeted ESLint and SPA typecheck pass. The fix changes no payload, channel, authorization rule, or cache key. This proves static contract alignment only: the event delivery and Warehouse dashboard render were not reproduced in a browser, so affected dashboard matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### Live list sample: Purchase Request cancellation, 2026-11-03

This is one list-only timing sample; it does not establish a percentile or cover actor UI, detail, available actions, linked records, badges or dashboards in this run.

### 317. Payroll Adjustment UI action controls were not driven by record-specific server actions

`PayrollAdjustmentResource` returned status and data but omitted `permissions` and `available_actions`. Both adjustment pages combined `status === pending` with the client's general permission/delegation snapshot. That could show a control when the current record was self-authored or no longer actionable, and it could misrepresent record-specific delegated approval eligibility. The write service already revalidated the pending state, maker exclusion, and permission/delegation while holding the row lock, so the risk was stale or misleading controls rather than bypassing the server gate.

Evidence: the new focused API test failed first with `data.permissions.view` missing (`null`); a red-first detail component test then found Approve rendered even when the API fixture set both available actions false. The resource now returns user-scoped `permissions` and state/maker/delegation-aware `available_actions`. Delegated grant IDs are memoized on the current Request so list serialization performs at most one delegation lookup per permission rather than one per row. Both list and detail controls consume those API booleans and disable while either decision is in flight. The focused API file passes 2 tests / 22 assertions (with two existing test-environment warnings because `/var/www/.env` is absent), the detail page passes 3/3, and post-change approve/reject three-context browser scenarios pass with the actor socket disabled. Related records, numeric badge counts, dashboards, and other Payroll Adjustment state transitions remain unverified.
