The form now reconciles the response into that detail key and refreshes mapped Payroll Adjustment families before returning to the existing list route. The focused page test passes 1/1; the create UI's immediate rendered list row remains unverified.


### 319. Supplier Listing controls were status-derived and successful actor responses were discarded

`SupplierItemListingResource` returned the row status but no current-user `permissions` or `available_actions`. The review page rendered approve/reject from `status === pending` plus the client permission snapshot, and the bulk selector used Pending status alone. The write service did correctly recheck the active permission/delegation and Pending state, so the confirmed defect was stale or misleading controls and an avoidable actor refresh dependency, not a server authorization bypass. On single approve/reject, the SPA discarded the full resource response and only invalidated its active list; if that refetch was delayed, the actor continued to see the old status and actions. Bulk endpoints preserve a narrower `{id,status,message}` result contract.

Red-first evidence: `SupplierListingAvailableActionsTest` failed because the resource omitted `permissions.view`; it now passes 1 test / 15 assertions and verifies reviewer, view-only, and post-approval action states. `index.realtime.test.tsx` first showed an unavailable pending row still had Approve/Reject and that the actor's row stayed Pending while the next list request was unresolved. A bulk-selection regression failed when its server-action filter was removed, then passed after restoration. The final SPA test file passes 4/4. Single mutation responses now reconcile cached filter/page variants before mapped invalidation; successful bulk status outcomes update current rows before settlement invalidation. Targeted ESLint and SPA typecheck pass. A post-change real two-context approval scenario passed: the supplier portal refetched Approved and removed Edit 523 ms after the Purchasing API response. This is one response-to-render sample, not commit-to-UI or p95; rejection, bulk, detail, badges, dashboards and inbox remain unverified. No Supplier Listing backend response cache was found and no cache was removed.

### 320. Item cost-review action controls were inferred from client permissions and status

`ItemCostReviewResource` returned status/version but no `permissions` or `available_actions`. The SPA inferred Review/Renew controls from the current status and the browser's local permission/delegation snapshot, so its actions were not part of the authorized API representation and could drift from the server's current access scope. The decision and renewal services already recheck permission/delegation and state under a database lock; this is an action-projection gap, not a write-authorization bypass. The existing ItemCostReview observer, private reviewer channel and central invalidation map already refresh the list; this pass did not reproduce a missing broadcast or live peer-render failure.

Red-first evidence: `ItemCostReviewAvailableActionsTest` failed at the absent `data.permissions.view`, then passed with current-user review/approval permissions and state-aware approve/reject/renew flags (1 test / 22 assertions), including a delegated approver. `cost-reviews.realtime.test.tsx` rendered Review despite an explicit API `available_actions` denial when the browser permission mock said approve; it now passes after the page reads the server action fields. Existing ItemCostReview workflow tests pass 3 tests / 34 assertions; targeted Pint and ESLint pass. One post-change real two-context browser scenario passed (1 / 16.3 s): a Finance actor approved while a delegated Purchasing viewer had the same review open. The viewer received `inventory.cost-review.changed` on `private-entity.item_cost_review.visible`, made an authorized refetch, rendered Approved in its list and modal, and lost Approve/Reject; the actor rendered Approved and lost Review. The response-to-viewer-render sample was 1,749 ms, not commit-to-UI or p95. The decide route's actor/detail/list/action cells are now PASS; related records, badges, dashboards and inbox remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. No cache entry was removed or cache/performance metric measured.

Delta 558 follow-up: a final frontend source scan found that this page still fell back to local permission plus status if the server omitted `available_actions`. A red-first missing-field test reproduced the fail-open row label; now absence of the server projection fails closed to Details with no decision buttons (focused page tests 2/2; targeted ESLint passes). Matrix runtime results remain unchanged because no live scenario was added. No event path, cache or business rule changed.

### 321. Invoice prebill approval controls did not use the server's record-specific permission result

`InvoiceResource` omitted `available_actions`, and the invoice detail page inferred `Approve prebill` from pending status, a client-side maker check, and the user's general permission snapshot. The write service separately checks the active delegation excluding the invoice maker, requires a reason, excludes an already approved invoice, and enforces segregation of duties against edits by the approver/delegators since the last approval. That mismatch can expose a button to a delegate whose only delegator is this invoice's maker, while the API correctly returns 403. This is a misleading/stale control, not a server authorization bypass.

Red-first evidence: `InvoicePrebillApprovalTest::test_prebill_resource_hides_delegated_approval_when_the_invoice_maker_is_the_delegator` first failed because `data.available_actions.approve_prebill` was null; the test proves the delegate has the broad delegated approval permission but the server denies this record and still forbids the POST. The invoice detail component regression independently showed the button despite an explicit API `false`. `InvoiceService::canApprovePrebill()` now computes the action through the same checks as the write path, and `InvoiceResource` returns that viewer-scoped result. The write path retains its row lock and full validation, sharing the segregation-of-duties predicate. The UI uses only the returned action. Focused API tests pass 2 tests / 18 assertions and the invoice detail component passes 12/12; whole-SPA typecheck and targeted Pint pass. There was no live two-user browser scenario, so all invoice route matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; no invoice cache entry was found or changed and no cache/performance metric was collected.

### 322. Budget Transfer detail conflated approve and reject eligibility

`BudgetTransferResource` exposed `can_approve`, which correctly includes the different-role approval rule. `BudgetTransferService::reject()` has a distinct rule: any active user with the approval permission/delegation may reject a pending transfer they did not request. The detail page used `can_approve` and the browser permission snapshot as a single gate for both buttons, so a same-role reviewer could be authorized by the server to reject but see neither action. The list also gated controls with local permission state instead of a per-record API action result. Write-time state and permission checks already existed and remain unchanged.

Red-first evidence: a new API test failed because the response omitted `available_actions`; it now verifies a Finance same-role reviewer receives `approve=false`, `reject=true`, successfully rejects, and receives `reject=false` in the mutation response (1 test / 7 assertions). A red-first detail test could not find Reject when the fixture gave the API `reject=true` and local permission false; it now passes 1/1. Three adjacent HTTP/delegation API scenarios pass 36 assertions. The controller now projects the distinct approval/rejection rules, the resource returns both action flags, and list/detail controls consume them. Targeted ESLint and Pint pass; final whole-SPA typecheck is recorded in Delta 557. No live multi-user browser transition or latency/cache measurement was run, and the route matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. No cache entry or business rule was changed.


## Delta 552 — Loan bulk-approval candidate ruled out at source

The stale matrix wording had claimed that Loan bulk approval had no record/list event. Source inspection found the existing `LoanApprovalRecordRealtimeObserver`, which calls `EmployeeLoanRealtimeService::stageApprovalChange()` for each loan approval-ledger write, including intermediate steps that leave the parent status Pending. That service bumps the parent version and coalesces a content-free `EmployeeLoanChanged` signal in the transaction outbox. The event targets private detail, permission-gated list, and exact borrower channels; `employee_loan` is present in the central invalidation map. A focused `LoanApprovalChainTest` endpoint regression passed (1 test / 9 assertions) and decoded those channels and payload keys. This candidate was a documentation error, not a confirmed stale-screen root cause. Cross-user delivery/render and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; no code or cache behavior changed.


### 323. Leave detail could infer approval and cancellation from status when action fields were absent

`LeaveRequestResource` always returns `available_actions`, but `LeaveDetailPage` fell back to status, the legacy `can_approve` field, and local owner identity when it was missing. In a mixed API/frontend version, a stale or incomplete response could restore Approve/Reject/Cancel controls despite the action projection being unavailable. A red-first detail regression reproduced Approve for a pending-department request with `can_approve=true` and no `available_actions`; the page now fails closed. Focused detail tests pass 4/4. This is a client action-projection contract risk; server mutation checks and leave realtime/cache behavior were not changed. No live route scenario was run.

### 324. Supplier Listing row and bulk actions inferred eligibility from Pending status

`SupplierItemListingResource` computes approve/reject per viewer, but the SPA fell back to `status === pending` when the action object was absent. That affected both row controls and selection filtering for bulk approve/reject. A red-first SPA test reproduced the row Approve control without server action flags; the updated page passes 5/5 and verifies the same row cannot be included in a bulk operation. Server checks and existing live single-approve evidence are unchanged; bulk runtime outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. No cache changed.

### 325. Fleet table reconstructed archive eligibility without the Vehicle resource

`VehicleResource` restricts archive when a vehicle has an asset association or active delivery and always returns its action list. `FleetPage` inferred `update/archive` for any non-deleted row if `available_actions` was absent. A red-first list component test reproduced Edit under that missing-field fixture; the UI now uses an empty action set and the focused suite passes 3/3. This corrects misleading controls but does not replace the server's write validation or prove live delivery. No cache or event path changed.

### 326. Shipment detail used local permission as a substitute for record-specific actions

`ShipmentResource` projects document/container/metadata/landed-cost actions using both permission and state restrictions. The SPA fell back to general local permission when those action fields were absent, which could show Add container or document/cost controls after the server had withheld the record-specific projection. A red-first component test reproduced Add container with local permission true and no server action object; the page now fails closed, and the focused test passes 1/1. Targeted ESLint and accumulated whole-SPA typecheck pass after restoring `usePermission` only for the read-only GRN link. The resource and mutation guards were already in place. No live browser scenario, cache change, or latency measurement was performed.


### 327. Leave list rebuilt approval eligibility from legacy permission and status fields

The list page retained a fallback that inferred approve/reject from `can_approve` and the status when `available_actions` was missing. Its actor reconciliation also kept a row in the awaiting-approval result from the legacy flag. This contradicted the server-projected action contract and could show an action in a mixed-version deployment without the current record-specific projection. A red-first list component test reproduced Approve for a pending-department row with `can_approve=true` and no action list; the focused suite passes 5/5 after both row eligibility and reconciliation fail closed; targeted ESLint and the accumulated whole-SPA typecheck pass. The detail page was independently corrected in Delta 559. Server rules, event producers and caches were unchanged; no live route scenario was run.


### 328. Purchase Order detail reconstructed workflow buttons from status and local permission

`PurchaseOrderResource` supplies an `actions` map computed by `PurchaseOrderAccessPolicy`, including current approval-step role, self-approval, vendor segregation-of-duties, and transition rules. The detail page fell back to status plus `usePermission()` when the map was missing, so a stale or old API shape could expose PO workflow actions the current server projection did not authorize. A red-first component regression reproduced Approve for a pending PO with no action map; the client now defaults every action to false. Focused PO detail tests pass 3/3. Server mutation validation remains authoritative and unchanged. There was no live PO browser transition, cache change, or latency measurement; matrix UI runtime outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 329. Delivery confirmation combined server readiness with client permission

`DeliveryResource.can_confirm` described only readiness: delivered status, acceptable proof, and no blocking return case. `DeliveryDetailPage` combined it with the browser's cached `supply_chain.deliveries.confirm` permission, so the record response lacked the viewer-specific decision and could show Confirm to a viewer whose current API role did not allow it. The write route still enforced permission and `DeliveryService::confirm()` revalidated readiness, so this was a stale-control/projection gap rather than an authorization bypass. A red-first API test saw no `available_actions.confirm`; the resource now combines the same readiness predicate with the current viewer's permission. The view-only regression passes with readiness true but action false (1 API test / 6 assertions). A red-first React test showed Confirm from the old `can_confirm` field alone; final focused detail coverage passes 18/18. The full focused DeliveryConfirm feature file passes 5/5 (16 assertions), with its pre-existing missing-revenue-account fixture logs. No live two-user delivery confirmation or latency/cache measurement was run; matrix runtime cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 330. Bill payment approval controls fell back to a general client permission

`BillPaymentResource::canDecide()` already returns a viewer- and approval-step-specific `can_decide` value, including pending-state, maker exclusion, permission and current-step role/delegation checks. Bill detail nevertheless used the SPA's broader `accounting.bills.payment_approve` permission when the response omitted `can_decide`. A red-first component regression with a pending payment, missing `can_decide`, and that client permission present rendered Approve (and Reject). The page now requires `p.can_decide === true`, so an absent projection fails closed. The focused Bill detail file passes 7/7 and targeted ESLint passes. This reproduces a stale/partial-response UI eligibility defect; the service's server-side write validation remains unchanged. No live bill transition, cross-role browser scenario, cache behavior, or latency was measured; matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 331. Inspection detail used the client permission snapshot when `can_manage` was absent

`InspectionResource` always returns `can_manage` for the authenticated viewer. Inspection detail previously substituted the SPA permission snapshot if that field was absent, allowing the editable checklist form to render from stale browser authorization state. A red-first test used an in-progress inspection, omitted `can_manage`, and retained the mocked broad permission; it rendered `Save draft`. The page now enables checklist management only when `data.can_manage === true`. The focused inspection detail file passes 15/15; positive fixtures explicitly include the API field. Targeted ESLint and the accumulated SPA typecheck pass. This changes client control visibility only; API routes and service validation are unchanged. No live inspection mutation, peer update or latency/cache measurement was performed; route UI matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 332. Maintenance work logging inferred assignment eligibility from a broader action list

`MaintenanceWorkOrderResource` returns `can_record_work` using `MaintenanceWorkAccess::canRecord()`, which requires an active eligible assignee in the right state. The API's `available_actions` can also include `submit_repair` for a maintenance supervisor managing an assigned employee's repair; that does not make the supervisor eligible for the separate `work-records` endpoint, whose executor guard requires the actor to be the assignee (except System Admin). Desktop `WorkLogControl`, the mobile work-log/parts controls, the desktop spare-part sheet, and the part-return control fell back from missing `can_record_work` to a broad `maintenance.wo.complete` permission plus `complete/submit_repair` action. The API route executor checks assignment for work logs, part requests and returns; its repair-submission action can also be granted to a supervisor who is not that assignee. Red-first paired desktop/phone tests reproduced both Record work and Return buttons with the server-specific flag absent and a submit-repair action present; a second regression reproduced Request spare parts. All those controls now require explicit `can_record_work: true`, while separate supervisor reconciliation and repair-submission actions retain their own gates. The focused completion-gate file passes 6/6, the part-reconciliation file passes 8/8, and targeted ESLint passes. The repair-submission action remains separately authorized; no API rule changed. No live role scenario, work-order route, cache or latency was tested; route UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 333. Stale Stock Adjustment decisions returned 422 and left the actor's row actionable

`StockAdjustmentService::approve()`, `reject()` and `withdraw()` lock and re-read the authoritative row, so the state guard prevented duplicate movement or terminal-state mutation. However, the stale-state branch threw a generic `BusinessRuleException`, which each controller translated to HTTP 422. The list page reported that error without closing the decision dialog or refreshing the currently displayed row. A red-first API test reproduced the second-approval response as 422 after another checker had approved the adjustment. The service now throws a typed `StockAdjustmentStateConflictException`; all three mutation endpoints map it to HTTP 409 with a stable code and status error. The page closes the stale action and refetches on 409. Focused API coverage passes (2 tests / 12 assertions), including stale approve, reject and withdraw routes; the Stock Adjustment page suite passes 4/4 including a mocked 409 refresh. Live two-user conflict behavior remains unverified.


### 334. Stale Stock Count actions returned 422 despite locked state checks

`StockCountService` locked and re-read sessions and count lines, but stale start/count/variance-approval/completion/cancellation states threw generic `BusinessRuleException`; `StockCountController` converted those to 422. The mounted Stock Count page already refetches after rejected 4xx actions and retains an open count form, but the API did not distinguish a stale state precondition from a form/business-rule refusal. A red-first HTTP regression reproduced a second start against an active session as 422. A typed `StockCountStateConflictException` now maps stale session/line guards to HTTP 409 with `stock_count_state_conflict`; scope, tolerance, segregation-of-duties and input-rule failures remain on the existing 422 path. The new API regression verifies stale start, count-line approval/count, completion and cancellation routes (1 test / 20 assertions); the full `StockCountEmptyScopeTest` file passes 2 tests / 36 assertions, including the unchanged empty-scope 422 path. No separate frontend component test, two-user browser scenario, live peer refresh or cache measurement was run.


### 335. Stale Transfer Order decisions left the action dialog and row untouched

`TransferOrderService::execute()` and `cancel()` lock and re-read the order before checking Pending, preventing duplicate stock movements and cancellation after execution. Those stale guards threw generic `BusinessRuleException`, which `TransferOrderController` mapped to 422. The SPA reported the error but did not close the confirmation or refetch the transfer list/detail. A red-first API regression executed an order, then reproduced the second execute response as 422. The service now throws `TransferOrderStateConflictException` for stale execute/cancel, the endpoints return a coded 409, and the page closes the dialog and refetches its authorized list/detail. Focused API coverage passes 1 test / 8 assertions. No SPA component test, live peer event, two-user conflict or cache/latency measure ran.


### 336. Transfer Order buttons used local permission and status instead of viewer-scoped actions

`TransferOrderResource` returned the current status but no `available_actions`. The page reconstructed both actions from `status === 'pending'` and the browser permission snapshot, suppressing Execute for the creator in one additional client-side check. A red-first API test reproduced a missing `available_actions.execute` value; a red-first component test showed that the page still rendered Execute and Cancel when the projection was absent. The resource now computes `execute` from the current API viewer's `inventory.adjust` permission, Pending state and maker-checker rule, and `cancel` from the same permission plus Pending state. The page renders only explicitly true actions and disables a confirmation if a refetch removes its action. Focused API coverage passes 8 tests / 31 assertions and the page component passes 3/3. The list/detail TanStack Query keys now include user ID and role; the focused regression first observed an unscoped detail key and now verifies both scoped keys. These tests verify the response and component contract, not live event delivery or a two-user route; Transfer Order runtime matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 337. Transfer Order detail events did not match the viewer-scoped query key (fixed)

The shared invalidation map emits the Transfer Order detail prefix `['inventory','transfer-order',hashId]`. Delta 577 placed viewer identity before the ID (`['inventory','transfer-order',userId,role,hashId`), so the record event handler ran but prefix matching missed the active detail query. This was reproduced in two contexts: the Warehouse viewer received both private record and list events but had no second detail GET within five seconds. A red-first QueryClient regression independently observed one detail read instead of the expected two. The detail key now follows the canonical order `[family, recordId, userId, role]`, preserving viewer isolation while matching the central map. The complete invalidation-map file passes 85/85. The rerun two-role Chromium flow received one record and one list event, refetched both views, rendered Transferred and removed stale action buttons; peer completion was 1,493 ms after actor HTTP response. This is one dev sample and does not establish commit-to-UI p95. Delta 579 adds a stock-level fan-out sample: the active authorized stock query refetched and per-location values changed, but visible table rendering was not separately asserted, so related-record coverage is PARTIAL. The observed stock GET response was 2,530 ms after the actor response timestamp, while a prior trace measured 1,096 ms; neither establishes commit-to-UI p95 or a stable cause. Dashboard views and other role transitions remain unverified.


### 338. Cloudflare gave the service-worker script a four-hour browser TTL (fixed at origin)

Before the change, the active local TLS origin returned `Cache-Control: no-cache` for `/sw.js`, while the public `ogamiph.dev` response returned `Cache-Control: max-age=14400` and `cf-cache-status: REVALIDATED`. The active ignored `.dev-runtime/nginx.conf` sent this path through the generic Vite proxy without a no-store edge directive. A browser could therefore retain worker code for four hours even though the origin intended revalidation; this is a confirmed deployment-cache contract violation and a plausible cause of clients running old worker behavior, but no stale record screen was directly reproduced from an old worker in this slice. The tracked dev Nginx template and active runtime override now use an exact `/sw.js` route with browser `no-store` and Cloudflare/CDN `no-store`; the production worker route matches. After config validation and reload, the public response changed to `Cache-Control: no-store, no-cache, must-revalidate, max-age=0` and `cf-cache-status: BYPASS` twice. API responses and app-shell headers were rechecked. The prior Cloudflare object was not manually purged because no Cloudflare API credentials were available; normal requests bypass it and its prior TTL will expire.

## Delta 588 — event channel names compared with authorization registrations (2026-11-03 UTC)

A source-level name check scanned the 122 `ShouldBroadcast` event classes and extracted 262 direct private-channel literal/prefix expressions. It compared them with the 166 `Broadcast::channel()` declarations in `api/routes/channels.php` (164 single-line literal patterns plus two source lines inside a registration loop). The only unmatched expression by the simple literal-pattern scan was `job_posting.{dynamic}`; source inspection confirmed that the loop registers both `job_posting.{hashId}` and `job_application.{hashId}`, plus the matching list channels. No missing registration was found among the extracted channel expressions. This rules out a broad channel-name registration omission in the inspected source snapshot; it does not test callback policy parity, dynamic channel expressions outside the extraction patterns, runtime subscription authorization, or per-transition recipient coverage. No test, code, cache, or database operation ran.


### 339. Inspection detail Cancel and checklist-replacement controls trusted the stale SPA permission snapshot

`InspectionResource` returns `can_manage` for the current API viewer, but Inspection detail still gated Cancel and checklist replacement with `usePermission()`. A red-first component case returned `can_manage: false` and `can_replace_checklist: true` while the mocked client permission remained true; the UI rendered Cancel, and the test failed on that visible control (15/16 tests passed before the fix). Both controls now require the explicit `can_manage === true` server field; checklist replacement also retains the server-projected `can_replace_checklist` condition. The focused detail component file passes 16/16 and targeted ESLint passes. API mutation authorization and business rules were not changed. After the fix, the public Vite-transformed Inspection module returned HTTP 200 and contained both explicit `can_manage === true` predicates. That confirms HMR served the changed source, not the rendered interaction. This is a mocked stale-permission reproduction, not a live cross-role or role-change browser scenario; Inspection route matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 340. Ten broadcast refresh payloads omitted event identity (architecture contract gap; symptom causality unproven)

A source scan of the 122 broadcaster `broadcastWith()` methods found 10 payloads without `event_id`: SalesOrderConfirmed, WorkOrderOutputRecorded, WorkOrderStatusChanged, MrpPlanGenerated, MoldShotLimitNearing, MoldShotLimitReached, BadgesChanged, PermissionOverrideChanged, UserNotificationCreated and PortalNotificationChanged. The red-first contract test failed at construction with `Unknown named parameter $eventId`; the shared event contract file then passed 38 tests / 1,497 assertions after the fix. Added `HasBroadcastEventId`, generated a UUID at event construction, and included it in each affected payload. Existing channel audiences and payload content remain the same apart from this opaque trace ID. Round-trip tests cover new and legacy outbox payloads for Badges and Permission Override events; targeted Sales Order and MRP tests cover model event codec compatibility. This adds identity for tracing queued signals, but the SPA currently does not consume IDs for ordering, deduplication or catch-up, so this gap was not shown to cause a stale screen. A literal-key scan across the whole catalog found `entity_type` in 84/122 payloads, `entity_id` in 20/122, `action` in 89/122, `version` in 86/122, `actor_id` in 48/122, `timestamp` in 80/122, and related references in 8/122; dynamic payload construction may be missed, and aggregate signals may not need a record ID. Event ID coverage is not evidence that the full Phase 2 contract is complete. No browser, socket delivery, two-user, latency or cache scenario ran, and no cache entries changed.


## Root-cause follow-up 591 — Work Order status event envelope (2026-11-03 UTC)

The Work Order lifecycle event already used the durable after-commit outbox and private detail/dashboard/parent/operator channels, but its `broadcastWith()` contained only `refresh` and `event_id`. Added the shared content-free envelope and passed the authenticated actor as an opaque hash from `WorkOrderService::recordStatusChange()`. The broadcast now includes entity type, opaque WorkOrder hash, `status_changed`, UTC `updated_at` version (timestamp fallback), opaque actor hash or null, the opaque parent WorkOrder reference when present, event ID, and timestamp; it omits from/to status, reason, raw IDs, quantities and model fields. The staged event remains codec-compatible with the existing outbox path. This closes one payload-contract gap, but does not establish that the prior refresh-only payload caused a stale screen.

Reproduction/evidence: a red-first payload regression failed before the implementation with `Unknown named parameter $actorHashId`; after the change, `DashboardBroadcastPayloadTest.php` passed 7 tests / 21 assertions, `BroadcastEventContractTest.php` passed 38 / 1,497, and the filtered committed Work Order transition/outbox feature test passed 1 / 10. Pint passed on the six scoped PHP files and `git diff --check` passed. No full suite ran. No two-context browser/socket trace was run, so delivery and UI behavior remain NOT_VERIFIED. No cache entry or business record was changed.


## Root-cause check 592 — accounting cache invalidation commit ordering (2026-11-03 UTC)

Verified the candidate that statement cache invalidation might run before commit. `FinancialStatementCacheGeneration::rotateAfterCommit()` and `DashboardCacheGeneration::touch()` register their marker rotations with `DB::afterCommit()`; both Account and Journal Entry observers call those APIs. The observers also register legacy tag cleanup with `DB::afterCommit()`. `JournalEntryObserver` registers these cache callbacks before staging its outbox refresh, so a post-commit client refetch is not intentionally sent ahead of marker rotation. This candidate is ruled out for these Eloquent observer paths; direct SQL or quiet writes that bypass these observers remain a separate coverage question. The targeted `StatementCacheAfterCommitTest.php` passed 1 test / 2 assertions; no code or cache data changed. This is a source-timing result, not a concurrent runtime proof or measured cache freshness result.


## Root-cause follow-up 593 — Work Order parent invalidation reference (2026-11-03 UTC)

The Work Order status signal also refreshes a child’s parent readiness channel. The new envelope initially emitted `related: []`, so generic consumers had no parent record reference. Added an optional trailing `parentWorkOrderHashId` snapshot to the event, populated it in `WorkOrderService::recordStatusChange()`, use the same opaque hash for the parent channel and emit it as `related: [{entity_type: work_order, hash_id: ...}]`. Legacy/direct event construction remains compatible and produces an empty list when no parent is supplied. No raw database ID is included. This improves the event contract but does not establish a user-visible stale-data failure.

A red-first payload test failed with `Unknown named parameter $parentWorkOrderHashId`; the full focused dashboard payload file passes 7 tests / 22 assertions. The filtered committed Work Order outbox test passes 1 / 10 and verifies parent-reference persistence through the codec. Pint passed on six scoped files and `git diff --check` passed. No full suite, live socket/two-user test, or cache change ran; Work Order runtime delivery and UI matrix cells remain NOT_VERIFIED.


## Root-cause check 594 — Work Order detail channel cross-check (2026-11-03 UTC)

A source comparison initially showed that `WorkOrderStatusChanged` broadcasts on `production.wo.{hash}`, while `useRealtimeRecord('work_order')` subscribes to `chain.work_order.{hash}` with `.chain.refresh`. Traced the paired producer before classifying it as a defect: `WorkOrderService::recordStatusChange()` stages both `WorkOrderStatusChanged` and `ChainStepAdvanced` in the same transaction, and `ChainStepAdvanced` broadcasts `.chain.refresh` to the exact work-order channel that the detail hook listens to. The filtered committed-transition feature test verifies both durable outbox rows are present. This rules out a missing event-name/channel pair for the traced WorkOrderService status transitions. It does not verify every direct/bulk status writer or live subscription/delivery, and UI matrix cells remain NOT_VERIFIED. No code, cache, or business data changed in this check.


## Root-cause 595 — WorkOrderOutput outbox replay rejected newer WorkOrder state — output event envelope and outbox replay recovery (2026-11-03 UTC)

`WorkOrderOutputRecorded` had a refresh-only wire payload, and its durable outbox record pinned the WorkOrder row version. A focused regression advanced the WorkOrder `updated_at` after output staging but before codec decode; `OutboxEventCodec` reproduced `Outbox model ... changed after publication`, which prevents that queued refresh from broadcasting. This is a concrete event-delivery failure mode for rapid follow-up updates, reproduced at the codec boundary rather than through a live worker/socket.

Scope/rank: high within rapid WorkOrder output delivery because a later write to the same aggregate before dispatch can suppress the refresh. The source scan found the same pinned-model pattern in three broadcast event classes across Production output and MRP Mold thresholds; the direct codec failure was reproduced on WorkOrderOutputRecorded, and the two Mold threshold signals were subsequently migrated and checked through the same decoder. This does not establish the same failure in unrelated event classes.

The event now uses the shared envelope (`entity_type=work_order`, opaque WorkOrder hash, `action=updated`, UTC version, opaque actor hash derived from `recorded_by`, optional parent hash, event ID and timestamp). It still excludes quantities, defect data, operator identity and model fields. Because its only consumer is a content-free refresh broadcast, it implements `ToleratesNewerModelState`; after a later WorkOrder update the stale event decodes as a refresh of current authorized API state. The WorkOrder output list/detail/dashboard/operator channels and event name are unchanged.

Evidence: the first red regression failed on the missing timestamp constructor field; the new version-race feature regression then failed with `changed after publication`. After the change, the focused `WorkOrderOutputIdempotencyKeyTest` scenario passes 1 test / 9 assertions, including outbox encode/decode after a newer WorkOrder version. The focused realtime unit set passes 47 tests / 1,529 assertions; Pint passes on nine scoped files and `git diff --check` passes. No full suite, live Reverb delivery, two-user UI, cache performance test or cache mutation ran. This source/codec fix does not establish end-to-end SLO behavior; matrix UI cells remain NOT_VERIFIED.


## Root-cause follow-up 596 — Mold threshold outbox replay tolerance (2026-11-03 UTC)

The outbox audit found that `MoldShotLimitNearing` and `MoldShotLimitReached` retain a version-pinned Mold model but send only refresh metadata. The same `OutboxEventCodec` newer-row rejection reproduced by the WorkOrder output regression applied to these model-bearing outbox events. Added the shared content-free envelope and `ToleratesNewerModelState`: warning uses `action=updated`, reached uses `status_changed`, both carry the opaque Mold hash/version/event ID/timestamp and a null actor; threshold values and shot counts remain API-only. The private dashboard audience and event names are unchanged. PayrollProgressEvent also carries a model but dispatches directly through Laravel's queued broadcast/SerializesModels path, not the durable codec, so it is not affected by this particular rejection.

The red-first contract assertion failed because Mold threshold events did not opt into newer-state tolerance. After the change, `DashboardBroadcastPayloadTest` passes 9 tests / 27 assertions, and `MoldRealtimeTest::mold_threshold_outbox_signals_survive_a_newer_mold_version` passes 1 test / 8 assertions for both event classes after advancing the Mold row. The combined focused realtime unit set passes 48 / 1,533; Pint passes on 12 scoped files and `git diff --check` passes. No live Reverb/two-user run, full suite or cache change ran. Generic `MoldChanged` already refreshes record/list/dashboard views; this closes a queued signal failure mode and does not prove that it caused a stale UI. Runtime UI cells remain NOT_VERIFIED.

## Root-cause follow-up 597 — Sales Order confirmation payload contract (2026-11-03 UTC)

`SalesOrderConfirmed` had a refresh-only wire payload, but source tracing confirmed the service already stages it in the durable outbox within the confirmation transaction, and `SalesOrderService::confirm()` separately stages the chain refresh used by Sales Order detail/list consumers. The Reverb payload now carries the shared minimal envelope (opaque record/actor references, action, UTC version, empty related references, event ID and timestamp); internal email snapshot fields remain off-wire. The focused envelope and outbox compatibility tests pass.

This closes one event-contract inconsistency, not a confirmed stale UI cause: the prior `refresh=true` signal already requested a dashboard refresh, and no two-context browser run tested confirmation status/actions/delivery in this slice. Keep all Sales Order confirmation UI matrix cells `NOT_VERIFIED`. No cache behavior changed.

## Root-cause follow-up 600 — shared chain event lacks ordering metadata (2026-11-03 UTC)

The central `ChainStepAdvanced` event had no action, version, actor reference or event timestamp, even though one producer covers eight high-impact chain entities and many record transitions. The central producer now adds the shared envelope metadata and the route/payload inventories reflect the change. Red-first evidence failed on the missing event timestamp; after the change, the focused producer/queue/broadcast-contract tests pass 54 / 1,600 and Pint passes on four PHP files.

This is a confirmed event-contract omission, not a reproduced stale-screen cause: consumers use the signal to refetch authorized API data, and no live delivery or UI regression was exercised in this slice. Keep chain runtime/UI matrix cells unchanged.


## Root-cause follow-up 606 — Contact Inquiry stale controls and identity scope (2026-11-03 UTC)

**Confirmed in the focused reproduction:** Contact Inquiry responses omitted `version` and server-computed `available_actions`. The page derived eligibility from status plus a local permission check, so Closed still offered “Mark in progress” even though the service's locked transition graph rejects that edge. The API accepted status without a client-observed-state precondition, so a stale action surfaced as generic 422 rather than a typed 409 that directs the client to refresh. Red-first API tests failed on the missing projection and conflict contract; the component test failed because the Closed response left the invalid button visible. No change to business transitions was needed.

The resource now computes management permission and allowed actions from the current viewer and status, includes `updated_at` as `version`, and the detail page renders only those server actions. The API optionally validates `expected_status` under its existing row lock and returns `contact_inquiry_state_conflict`/409 for stale requests; legacy requests without that field keep their existing contract. The actor installs the successful API response immediately, and the 409 handler refetches through the authorized detail API. The focused API regression passes 2 tests / 16 assertions; the SPA detail component file passes 5/5; a private channel authorization regression passes 1 test / 3 assertions for permission allow/deny and feature gating. These checks do not prove other transitions or other-user delivery.

**Cache-scope hardening:** inquiry resources vary by viewer because IP address and user-agent are present only for managers. The prior list/detail TanStack keys did not encode identity. List and detail keys now include `realtimeIdentityKey` (user, role, employee scope, permissions, delegated approvals and features); an SPA regression confirms a user switch gets a separate redacted cache entry while retaining the former user's distinct entry. No live cross-account leak was reproduced, and this is frontend in-memory cache isolation; the API remains private/no-store and no backend response cache exists.

The two-user, three-browser-context dev-stack run marks this Contact Inquiry status route PASS for actor response, peer detail, list, and button/action outcomes. The actor was System Administrator with its socket disabled; the viewer was Customer Service Officer. After the event-envelope update and Horizon restart, the browser verified the private payload `entity_id` exactly matched the inquiry hash. One sample measured 547 ms event-to-detail DOM, 529 ms event-to-list DOM, 561 ms actor-response-to-peer detail DOM, and 395 ms actor-response-to-peer list DOM. This does not establish p95 or outcomes for another transition or environment. Related-record, badge, dashboard and approval-inbox cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. Whole-SPA `tsc --noEmit` also exposed an unrelated existing TS2769 in `quality/inspections/detail.test.tsx:159`; see `FINDINGS.md`.DOC


## Root-cause follow-up 607 — Horizon published stale loaded event code (2026-11-03 UTC)

**Reproduced boundary:** the status mutation committed an outbox row whose serialized `ContactInquiryChanged` payload contained the new inquiry `entityHashId`. Decoding that row with the current API code produced `entity_id` equal to the inquiry's opaque hash. The first browser run nevertheless recorded the Reverb WebSocket frame without `entity_id`, even though the viewer had subscribed and received the status event. Horizon had been up for roughly five hours, predating the edited event class. This isolated the loss to the long-lived worker/broadcast path rather than database write, outbox serialization or the current event implementation.

Restarting Horizon loaded the current class into its queue workers. Repeating the same two-user, three-browser-context scenario then delivered `entity_id` matching the exact inquiry hash in both viewer contexts and passed the status/action/list checks. The frame payload remained refresh metadata only; no inquiry content or PII was added. This confirms stale loaded PHP worker code in the dev runtime as a cause of the missing event envelope, not a universal explanation for every stale UI report. Delta 733 adds a dev-only Compose Watch target that restarts Horizon and the mail worker on mounted PHP source/config/bootstrap changes, and Reverb on config changes. A temporary source probe caused both worker restarts without rebuilding; `horizon:status` and live SPA/Vite HTTP checks passed afterward. This watcher is active only while its `make dev-watch-php` process runs; production continues to require the documented deploy/restart sequence.


## Root-cause follow-up 608 — Inspection event omitted standardized identity metadata

`InspectionChanged::broadcastWith()` previously omitted `entity_type`, `entity_id`, `actor_id`, and `timestamp`, despite targeting private per-record and list channels. Its outbox event retained the internal integer actor ID but did not provide the SPA a privacy-safe actor reference. The red-first broadcast contract test reproduced the missing constructor contract (`Unknown named parameter $actorHashId`). The event now emits the standardized refresh envelope using opaque inspection and actor hashes; the integer field remains optional solely to decode pending legacy outbox rows and is never serialized to Reverb. The observer supplies the current user's hash, while existing after-commit delivery and private channel authorization are unchanged.

This is a confirmed event-contract defect, not evidence that the missing metadata prevented an Inspection refetch or caused a stale screen. Focused contract tests pass 3 tests / 1,349 assertions, and the coalesced Inspection lifecycle test passes 1 / 5. No live two-user Quality scenario ran, so Inspection actor/peer/list/action UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Root-cause follow-up 609 — Legacy Inspection measurement endpoint accepted stale writes

`PATCH /api/v1/quality/inspections/{inspection}/measurements` serialized requests on the Inspection row and rejected terminal states, but it had no client-observed version check and did not advance the resource's `result_version`. Red-first API reproduction sent two writes with the same version 0: the first saved a passing measurement; the second stale request returned 200 and changed that evidence to failing. Thus row locking prevented simultaneous writes but still allowed a later stale editor to overwrite the prior committed result.

The endpoint now accepts optional `expected_result_version`, compares it with the locked row, and advances `result_version` for each accepted write. A stale version returns `409 inspection_state_conflict`; callers that omit the field retain the old endpoint contract. The SPA sends the current version and, on conflict, refetches through the authorized realtime invalidation path while retaining typed measurement values. Focused API verification passes the new route case (1 / 6), lifecycle concurrency file (4 / 13), and measurement contract file (7 / 19); the SPA panel file passes 15/15. This confirms and fixes the stale-write path at the API boundary. No live two-user Inspection scenario ran, so real-time delivery and actor/peer UI freshness remain unverified.


## Root-cause follow-up 610 — Legacy Inspection item-result route ignored client version

`RecordInspectionItemResultsRequest` already accepted an optional `expected_result_version` for legacy inspections, but `InspectionController::recordItemResults()` stripped the field unless the inspection used checklist capture. The legacy `InspectionService::recordItemResults()` branch also left `result_version` unchanged. Red-first reproduction sent two item-result writes from version 0: both returned 200 and the second changed the saved PASS decision to FAIL. This is a confirmed stale-write/data-concurrency defect, not proof of a WebSocket transport failure.

The controller now forwards the optional precondition for all inspection modes. The locked legacy item-result service compares it and increments the version on accepted draft or submit writes; the legacy batch-result endpoint applies the same optional comparison and increments on outcome submission. The SPA sends the latest response version through its multi-request lot-result flow and retains selections/refetches authorized detail and list data on 409. The exact API regression passes 1 / 8 assertions, the lifecycle concurrency file passes 5 / 21, and the component suite passes 16/16; targeted formatting, syntax and ESLint checks pass. Old callers without the field retain compatibility. No two-user Inspection UI scenario ran, so Inspection actor/peer/list/action runtime cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Root-cause follow-up 611 — Legacy Inspection Cancel accepted a stale screen version

**Reproduced:** a measurement write from version 0 committed as version 1; Cancel from the old screen with `expected_result_version: 0` still returned 200 and moved the record to Cancelled. The cancellation service only checked versions for checklist/count capture, and the legacy detail UI omitted the available `result_version`. This is a stale-action/data-concurrency defect.

Cancellation now checks a supplied version for every capture mode while holding the row lock, then advances `result_version` on the accepted transition. Inspection detail sends that value for all Cancel and Review actions; the legacy review path now checks a supplied version and advances it on disposition. The stale cancel regression passes 1 / 6, the focused concurrency file passes 6 / 27, and the detail component file passes 17/17, including the actor cache update. No live two-user Quality UI scenario ran; matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. Backward compatibility is preserved for old requests that omit the optional version.


## Root-cause follow-up 612 — Legacy Inspection lot-result request dropped version preconditions

`RecordLotResultRequest` did not validate or include `expected_result_version` in its decoded payload, and `InspectionService::recordLotResult()` did not compare a client-observed result version before updating checklist and measurement rows. A two-request regression sent version 0 twice: the second request returned 200 and overwrote the earlier PASS measurement. This confirms a stale-write defect in the legacy lot-result API; it is not a reproduced socket-delivery problem.

The request now accepts an optional integer version and the service checks it against the locked Inspection row. Accepted lot evidence advances `result_version` if nested measurement persistence has not already advanced it. The focused route regression passes 1 / 6 assertions and verifies the original row is retained after the stale 409. No current SPA caller or live multi-user browser scenario exists for this legacy route; route UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Root-cause follow-up 613 — PHP worker code can lag mounted source

The running API/Horizon processes were older than the `InspectionChanged` source edit, so the event worker could publish a prior payload even while the SPA and request source files were current. The project had already reproduced the same failure mode on Contact Inquiry in Delta 607. Ran the local-only guarded `make dev-refresh-php` target; it confirmed local image and `APP_ENV=local`, cleared framework config/route/event/view artifacts, and restarted API/Horizon/mail/Reverb without rebuilding or clearing Redis/DB data. API environment, Horizon running/healthy, public SPA/Vite and transformed Inspection module were verified afterward. This is process-freshness verification, not evidence that an Inspection event reached a browser; live Quality matrix cells stay `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Root-cause follow-up 614 — event refetch could be canceled without retry

A live Inspection cancel run showed the authorized record GET returning `status=cancelled`, and both private record/list event frames arrived, but one peer detail query remained Draft. Query-cache tracing and a red-first central invalidation regression isolated the failure: the event handler started a detail refetch, then a concurrent reconnect/focus catch-up canceled that request. TanStack retained the previous Draft data and left the active query invalidated, while the invalidator returned without another read. This confirmed a client refetch race for this active query; it did not indicate an API response cache, missing event, or channel authorization failure in this scenario.

The shared invalidator now performs one trailing event-driven invalidation when a matching active query is idle and still invalidated after the first pass. The regression failed before the change (two calls, stale data) and passes after (third call installs Cancelled); the focused invalidation-map file passes 87/87. Inspection list and detail query families are now disjoint, settings invalidation targets the correct options key, and Inspection API reads pass their AbortSignal through Axios. A final live `qc_inspector`→`system_admin` cancel scenario with the actor socket blocked passed: actor UI 795 ms from action start, peer detail 901 ms and list 1,242 ms from API response. These are one-sample timings, not p95. Only this route's actor/detail/list and status-derived button-update outcomes are now PASS. `InspectionResource` now also provides server-computed `available_actions`, and the header controls render from that projection; related screens and other transitions remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Root-cause follow-up 615 — degraded catch-up could starve a slow initial query

The SPA catch-up helper canceled every active in-flight query except a query currently tagged as an event-owned refresh. While the socket was reconnecting, the provider's degraded-mode loop could repeatedly cancel a slow first detail read, keeping the record screen empty or on its loading state. The failed browser attempt exposed the blank peer main region; trace showed an aborted detail read followed by a successful authorized Draft response. A red-first QueryClient test made the cause deterministic: `signal.aborted` became true when catch-up ran during an ordinary detail read.

Catch-up now invalidates active queries with cancellation disabled. It records non-event active reads that were already in flight and issues one trailing authorized fetch after that pass settles. Event-owned reads are not duplicated; the bounded stale-and-idle retry remains. The new regression fails before the change and passes afterward; the complete focused invalidation-map file passes 89/89, and provider tests pass 7/7. A live `qc_inspector` actor with blocked WebSocket and `system_admin` detail/list viewers passed one cancel scenario: actor 595 ms from action to UI, peer detail/list 1,014/1,315 ms from API response, event-to-peer 968/1,199 ms. These are samples, not p95.

The same slice adds a viewer-scoped `available_actions` projection to InspectionResource and makes its header buttons render from the projection. Focused maker/checker API and detail component regressions pass (2/9 assertions and 18/18). This closes the Inspection header action projection for the exercised cancel path only; full Inspection transition, linked-query, dashboard, badge, and inbox coverage remains open. This is a frontend request-race root cause; no backend response cache or Redis entry was removed or changed.


## Root-cause follow-up 619 — reconcile User Invite producer and subscription evidence

Older matrix rows said invite creation, public acceptance, and revocation had no shared invite refresh. Current source shows those transitions already stage `UserInviteChanged` through `UserInviteRealtimeService` inside the mutation transaction. It targets a private directory channel authorized only for active users with the invite API permission, and the SPA invalidation map covers the list and nested selector query prefixes. The apparent current gap was stale coverage documentation, not a missing producer in the present checkout.

Added focused route-level verification: all three transitions write a refresh outbox row with the expected action and minimal broadcast payload; the channel accepts an active system administrator, denies an active employee without invite permission, and rejects an inactive administrator; the SPA invalidation map reaches filtered invite lists and employee/role selectors. `UserInviteRealtimeTest.php` passes 4 cases / 46 assertions (the runner reported four warnings whose details were not captured); the focused invalidation-map suite passes 90/90. The three matrix source notes now describe current wiring. No two-admin browser scenario ran, so UI delivery and render outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; this does not establish invite event delivery latency or p95.

## Root-cause follow-up 622 — Horizon's restart marker bypassed the TTL cleanup

The live cache audit found one unbounded key, `redis:illuminate:queue:restart`, in the LRU-evictable application cache DB. Source tracing confirmed that Laravel Horizon's `horizon:terminate` writes this framework coordination key forever, while the earlier listener only bounded writes from `queue:restart`. The old daily unbounded-key prune could also delete it because the key shares the app cache prefix. This explains the marker's recurrence after prior cleanup and exposes a worker-restart reliability risk under cache pressure; it does not prove a lost worker restart or explain a stale business record. The fix now retains the marker after both commands, exempts only this exact store/key from the no-TTL cleanup rule, protects it with `volatile-lru`, and prevents direct prune deletion. Focused tests pass 7 cases / 34 assertions; dev Redis reports `volatile-lru`, the marker remains TTL `-1`, audit reports zero unbounded application entries plus one expected persistent marker, and prune apply found/deleted zero keys. No multi-user record flow, forced cache-pressure eviction, production Redis, cache hit-rate, or API p95 was measured.

## Cache hygiene follow-up 630 — orphaned Horizon keys in an inactive Redis DB (2026-11-03 UTC)

The local persistent Redis DB 0 held 39 `ogami_erp_horizon:` keys after the effective Horizon/default Redis connection had moved to DB 2. All 39 matched the exact legacy namespace, 38 lacked TTL, and no other DB 0 keys existed. Runtime Laravel config in the API and Horizon containers pointed Horizon to DB 2; broadcast jobs use DB 1, cache payloads use separate Redis DB 3, markers use DB 4, sessions use PostgreSQL, and Reverb scaling is disabled. This confirms an orphaned local operational-telemetry keyspace, not stale business data or a user-facing record cache. It was removed with an exact-prefix/count guard; afterward DB 0 disappeared from `INFO keyspace` and Horizon remained running. Active cache audit/prune stayed clean (514 scanned; zero unbounded app entries; one expected infrastructure marker; zero prune candidates). The stale-key migration timing is inferred from the namespace being present only in the inactive DB; sample env files still default `REDIS_DB=0`, so other environments require their own runtime check. No application cache or API data cache was removed, and no API latency impact was measured.


## Runtime follow-up 631 — Purchase Request final approval and UI timing (2026-11-03 UTC)

One real-backend Chromium run used three roles: VP actor, System Administrator detail viewer, and Purchasing list viewer. The actor WebSocket was forcibly closed. Its `PATCH /api/v1/purchasing/purchase-requests/{id}/approve` returned 200 with `status=approved`; the actor rendered Approved and removed Approve from that response. The viewer received three `chain.refresh` frames at -30 ms, +97 ms and +466 ms relative to the actor response. The event invalidation map coalesced them into one observed authorized detail refetch: it began at +197 ms, returned `status=approved` and `actions.can_cancel=false` at +1,614 ms, and the in-page MutationObserver recorded Cancel removal at +1,650 ms. The Purchasing list had rendered the PR as Approved by +2,142 ms, when Playwright's locator completed, after two matching API reads returned Approved. This one run passes the 2 s detail/action target and the near-live list target; the list time is an upper bound that includes runner scheduling, and neither result establishes p95 or broad module coverage.

The authenticated record and list responses were `Cache-Control: max-age=0, must-revalidate, no-store, private`; Cloudflare reported `DYNAMIC`, Age was absent, and Playwright reported `fromServiceWorker=false`. This sample did not show stale HTTP, proxy, or service-worker data. No cache key/value changed. An earlier Playwright assertion used `getByText('Approved')`, which can match an approval-timeline step, and measured after cross-process locator waits; one later sample measured 2,531 ms by that runner clock while its in-page observer recorded the action disappearing at 1,873 ms. The E2E now scopes status assertions to the PageHeader and measures the SLO from the browser DOM mutation. The earlier failed action-count run remains ambiguous and is not treated as a separate confirmed product failure. Earlier slow samples recorded in the route matrix still prevent a p95 claim.

A read-only outbox check for synthetic PR-202611-0040 found three distinct `ChainStepAdvanced` rows around final approval/conversion handling, each published once (`attempts=1`), plus one `PurchaseRequestApproved` application event. Source tracing shows `markApproved()` sets conversion state to Pending, the queued consolidation listener calls `markPoConversionPending()`, and an unsuccessful automatic conversion can then set Manual Required. This supports a producer-side burst of separate aggregate refreshes, rather than a known queue retry duplicate. The browser test recorded event names and times but not event IDs, so no one-to-one mapping between the three frames and three rows is claimed. Its network trace recorded one overlapping detail GET started +67 ms and canceled +201 ms, followed by one successful authorized GET started +197 ms and completed +1,614 ms. This cancellation is the existing invalidator behavior: it aborts an active read when a record signal arrives so a response that may predate the committed change cannot win. The three frames did not result in three observed successful reads in the measured window. Because the browser log closed after the successful render, it does not rule out a later queued catch-up read. No stale UI was attributed to the burst in the passing sample; server-side event coalescing and fuller request instrumentation remain optimization/measurement candidates.


## Cache hygiene follow-up 633 — retired UUID payloads had no scheduled cleanup (2026-11-03 UTC)

Source review found that generation rotation made prior cache payloads unreachable immediately, but the scheduled `cache:audit` and `cache:prune-unbounded` jobs did not remove TTL-bearing entries from those retired namespaces. The old payloads eventually expired under their owner TTLs, so this was a retention gap rather than evidence that a viewer could still read stale data. A read-only local dev scan found zero retired-generation payloads; the earlier one-time Horizon cleanup was a separate operational keyspace and was not repeated.

Added `cache:prune-retired-generations`, dry-run by default and scheduled daily at 04:30 with a 500-key bound. It reads UUID values only from the dedicated generation-marker store, recognizes the registered generation-key families and their direct, composite, dashboard `v3`/`v4`, holiday year-suffix, role-permission, and badge secondary-token layouts, and scans only the application payload cache prefix. Apply mode deletes a key only when it contains a UUID absent from the active marker snapshot and Redis still reports a positive TTL; generation markers, unbounded keys, and other Redis databases are not candidates. The command emits aggregate counts only. Focused parser, command, and actual Redis-reader tests cover dry-run, active/retired discrimination, TTL checks and prefix-scoped deletion. Live dry-run found zero candidates and deleted nothing. No data values or business rows changed; cache hit rate, API latency and memory impact were not measured.

### 341. Coalesced Item catalog refreshes did not reach open Item details (fixed)

`SafetyStockRecomputeService` persists Item changes with `saveQuietly()` and `AbcClassificationService` uses query-builder bulk updates, then each stages one `ItemChanged` catalog signal with `itemHashId: null` to avoid one event per item. `ItemChanged` sends that content-free signal only on `entity.item.visible`. Item lists subscribe through `useRealtimeList`, but `useRealtimeRecord('item', ...)` previously listened only on `inventory.item.{hashId}`; unlike the existing customer, demand-forecast and product-routing batch cases, item detail hooks were absent from the shared list-channel catch-up subscription. A new hook regression test failed before the fix because no `entity.item.visible` subscription was registered, then passed after the shared hook began subscribing item details to the authorized catalog channel and routing its refresh signal through the same invalidation map. The full focused `useRealtimeList.test.tsx` passed (46 tests). This proves the frontend event-to-query path, not a two-user browser delivery or a general item-detail SLO; item matrix cells remain unverified for end-to-end freshness. The refresh payload contains no item data, and each detail is refetched through the authorized API. No cache policy or business rules changed.

### 342. Position changes refreshed the Admin User directory but not open user details (fixed)

When a Position is updated, `PositionRealtimeService::stage()` emits a directory-only `AdminUserChanged` with `userHashId: null` if linked employee accounts exist. `AdminUserChanged` then publishes only to `entity.admin_user.visible`; the Admin Users list subscribed through `AppLayout`, but `useRealtimeRecord('admin_user', ...)` did not subscribe to that list channel. The Admin User detail resource includes linked Employee position data, so a manager viewing a linked account could retain an old position title after a Position edit. A focused hook test failed before the fix because `entity.admin_user.visible` was not registered, then passed after adding `admin_user` to the shared batch-detail catch-up subscriptions. The focused hook suite passes 47 tests. This is source and component-hook evidence; no two-user browser delivery/render or SLO measurement was run. The payload remains content-free and the detail reload uses the existing authorized API. No backend behavior or cache policy changed.

### 343. Position changes did not refresh linked Employee detail viewers (fixed)

`PositionChanged` originally broadcast only to the `entity.position.visible` list channel. `EmployeeDetailPage` reads the Position title embedded in `['hr','employee',id]` but its shared record hook listened only to that Employee's channel. Position readers saw their list invalidate; an Employee viewer without Position-directory permission could retain a stale embedded title. A focused hook test failed before the fix because there was no exact Position channel subscription. `PositionChanged` now also sends its refresh-only event on `position.{hashId}`; authorization allows a Position reader or an active Employee reader only when `DepartmentScope` contains an Employee linked to that exact Position. The Employee detail supplies its linked Position ID to the shared `useRealtimeRecord` hook, which invalidates its own Employee detail key through the central map. The test was red before the code path existed and now passes. Backend `PositionRealtimeTest` passes 1 test / 48 assertions, the focused `BroadcastAuthorizationTest` passes 1 / 6 assertions, and the frontend hook file passes 48/48. The event payload adds no employee, Position-title or compensation data. These are source, API/channel and hook tests; no two-user browser delivery or rendered SLO sample was collected, so the Position matrix rows remain NOT_VERIFIED for live freshness.

### 344. Payroll anomaly review modal retained an actionable stale record (fixed)

`AnomalyReviewPanel` stored the selected `PayrollAnomalyFlag` object in modal state. When another reviewer resolved the anomaly and the authorized unresolved-list query refreshed, the list no longer contained the flag but the modal kept the copied unresolved object and continued to offer an enabled “Mark reviewed” action. A focused component regression reproduced this by opening the modal, typing remarks, and replacing the query result with an empty unresolved list; before the fix the modal still displayed the old `zero_pay` flag and its action remained enabled. The panel now stores only the selected flag ID and derives the displayed record from current query data. While a refresh is running or failed, or if the target is no longer present/unresolved, the action is disabled and the modal explains the condition while preserving typed remarks. Mutation errors invalidate the anomaly query so the next action decision is based on a fresh authorized response. The focused regression passes and targeted ESLint passes. This verifies the modal/query-cache state transition only; no second browser context, socket delivery, server response timing, or Payroll anomaly action was exercised, so matrix cells remain NOT_VERIFIED. No cache policy or business rule changed.


### 345. Payroll Adjustment confirmation dialogs retained stale list rows (fixed)

The Payroll Adjustment table used server-provided `available_actions`, but the approve confirmation retained the selected `PayrollAdjustment` object and the reject modal retained the same copied row. A focused red-first component test opened approval, replaced the active list query with an empty result representing another actor's committed transition, and observed that the old employee/amount description and enabled confirmation remained. Both dialogs now store only the adjustment ID and derive the target/action from current authorized list data. They disable submission while the query is fetching or errored, after the row disappears, or when the current server resource no longer allows that action; the reject reason remains intact. Mutation failures invalidate the list for a fresh state check. The focused regression passes 1/1 and targeted ESLint passes. One real-backend Chromium run used two reviewer browser contexts authenticated as the same System Administrator account: the actor socket was disabled, the peer received the event and an authorized list response with the updated action state, and its open dialog disabled Approve 756 ms after the actor response. This proves separate-context catch-up only; no distinct-user/cross-role dialog scenario or p95 was measured. Existing API state validation and business rules are unchanged.

### 346. Leave rejection modal retained a stale request and action (fixed)

The Leave table renders its row actions from the latest resource `available_actions`, but the rejection modal stored a copied `LeaveRequest`. A red-first focused component regression opened Reject with a pending request, entered a reason, and replaced the active list query row with `approved` and no available actions. Before the fix the modal kept the old request and prompt, so it did not explain that the action had gone stale. The modal now stores the request ID, derives the current request/action projection from the active authorized list response, and disables submission while that query is fetching or errored, when the row is absent, or when the latest resource omits `reject`. The dialog keeps the typed reason and shows a clear stale/unavailable message; rejection failures invalidate the Leave request list. The focused regression failed before and passes after the change. This proves the component/query-data path only; no Leave mutation, two-user browser scenario, WebSocket delivery, or latency sample ran, so all Leave rejection runtime matrix cells remain NOT_VERIFIED. The server still revalidates the action, and no business/cache policy changed.

### 347. HR salary approval confirmation remained actionable during realtime refetch (fixed)

The HR Employees page subscribes to the `salary_adjustment` private list channel, and its event invalidation targets the `['hr', 'salary-adjustments']` query family. `SalaryAdjustmentsTab` previously left an open Approve/Reject confirmation enabled while that list was refetching; the dialog kept a copied row and could submit its stale action before the fresh action projection arrived. Its mutation error handler also left the list untouched. A focused regression opened the dialog, invalidated the active list while holding the refetch pending, and failed because Confirm remained enabled. The tab now stores only the adjustment ID, resolves it from current query data, disables confirmation while fetching or errored and when the latest `available_actions` no longer allows the choice, and invalidates the list after mutation errors. The focused salary adjustment suite passes 4/4 after the change. This verifies the client in-flight refresh window only; no approval API request, other browser/user, event delivery, or latency sample ran. Server validation and business rules were not changed.

### 348. Stock Adjustment dialogs remained actionable during realtime refetch (fixed)

The Stock Adjustment list already subscribes to the authorized entity event and renders row actions from the server `available_actions` map. However, its approval, rejection, and withdrawal dialogs kept full row copies, and none disabled confirmation while the list query was refetching. A red-first regression opened approval, held the active event-invalidation refetch, and observed an enabled Confirm button. A second reproduced the rejection dialog gap with a valid reason already entered. The page now stores IDs, reads current rows/actions from the authorized list query, disables all three dialog actions while fetching or errored and when the target/action is unavailable, and refetches after mutation errors. `ReasonDialog` gained an optional disabled prop used by the rejection path. The focused Stock Adjustment page suite passes 6/6, including actor response reconciliation and stale-409 handling. ### 349. Leave approval confirmation ignored list-query freshness (fixed)

The Leave table rendered Approve from the current server `available_actions`, but its confirmation stored only the request ID and did not consult the active list query's fetch/error state or the refreshed request action projection. A red-first component regression opened department approval, held an active list invalidation refetch pending, and observed that Confirm remained enabled. The approval confirmation now resolves the request from the live authorized list query and blocks/guards confirmation while fetching or errored, or when `approve_dept`/`approve_hr` is no longer in the response. Mutation errors invalidate the Leave request list. The focused Leave page suite passes 7/7, including this in-flight case and the prior stale rejection case. ### 350. Payroll anomaly dialog did not recheck parent period eligibility (fixed)

The anomaly record could remain unresolved in its list while the parent payroll period changed to a state where review is forbidden. The modal validated the anomaly query target but its confirm action did not re-check the current `periodStatus`/maker eligibility supplied by the parent. A red-first component regression opened the dialog with a computed period, then rerendered it with draft status and observed the action stayed enabled. The modal now shows the current blocked reason and both disables and guards submission while that condition holds. The focused Payroll Anomaly panel tests pass 2/2, including the pre-existing stale-target/remarks case. ### 351. Supplier Listing review actions trusted stale row snapshots (fixed)

The Supplier Listings page already rendered row actions from `available_actions` and reconciled successful API responses, but it retained a copied listing in its single-reject modal and passed selected copies into bulk rejection. Its row and bulk approval buttons also stayed active during list invalidation. Red-first regressions reproduced an enabled single-reject confirmation during refetch, an enabled row Approve control, bulk actions active during refetch, and a bulk-reject confirmation still enabled after the authorized list removed the reject action. The page now guards row actions during fetch/error, holds single reject by ID, derives selected bulk-reject rows from selected IDs and the current query, and disables/guards confirmation if any selected row is missing or no longer rejectable. Mutation failures invalidate the affected listing IDs. The reusable `DataTable` bulk action contract now accepts an optional disabled state. The focused Supplier Listing suite passes 9/9. ### 352. Purchase Request conversion modal trusted its opening list row (fixed)

The PR list can open conversion from an approved row, but `ConvertPrToPoModal` previously queried fresh sourcing lines while keeping the opening row's old conversion status and never checking the authorized PR detail `actions.can_convert` projection. A red-first component test supplied a current server detail response with `status=converted` and `actions.can_convert=false`; the modal still left Create Purchase Order enabled. The modal now fetches the current detail under the shared PR detail query key, waits for detail and sourcing reads to settle, renders a clear unavailable/error state, and disables/guards conversion unless the server says `can_convert=true`. Mutation errors use the central PR realtime invalidation map. The focused component regression passes 1/1. No conversion API call, two-user browser, WebSocket delivery, or latency sample ran, so PR conversion runtime matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 353. Stock Count variance confirmation retained a counted item snapshot (fixed)

The Stock Count variance dialog stored a full `StockCountItem`; when the active authorized session query changed to Completed and the line changed from Counted to Adjusted, the open modal continued to describe and offer approval for the old variance. A red-first component test replaces the active query cache entry after opening the dialog and waits for the dialog to reflect the settled React Query update; the prior implementation retained the old description and action. The modal now stores the item ID, derives it from the active session query, and disables/guards approval unless the query is fresh, the session remains in progress, the line remains Counted and above the configured tolerance, and the current role/maker-checker conditions still permit approval. It shows checking, unverified, or unavailable status as appropriate. The focused regression passes 1/1 and targeted ESLint passes. No API action, two-user browser, socket delivery, or latency sample ran, so Stock Count runtime matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 354. Transient Echo bootstrap failure stranded active private subscriptions (fixed)

`RealtimeProvider` made a single `getEcho()` initialization attempt.
