If the lazy Echo/Pusher import or construction rejected transiently, the provider switched to Offline and retained only HTTP catch-up; it did not retry until the user/session identity changed. A red-first provider regression simulated one rejected initialization followed by a valid connected client and observed one call and a permanent Offline state. Tracing the registration layer found that `openChannel()` cleared its rejected promise but left mounted handlers with no channel; even a later successful Echo initialization would not reattach those registrations without a component remount.

The provider now retries initialization with bounded exponential backoff, retries immediately on browser-online/focus/visible signals, and cancels its retry timer on cleanup. After Echo initializes, the shared registry retries active channel registrations that have no channel or in-flight open. The provider regression failed before and passes after; a second regression verified the active private listener is attached after the transient failure. Focused tests pass: provider 8/8 and subscriptions 5/5; targeted ESLint and a direct trailing-whitespace check pass. This is unit-level evidence only: no browser-context, live Reverb outage, per-record UI, latency, or production deployment was tested. The fallback HTTP catch-up remains the only demonstrated recovery behavior during initialization failure; overall matrix runtime statuses remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 355. Overtime rejection confirmation retained a stale list-row snapshot (fixed)

The Overtime list rendered action buttons from the server's `available_actions`, but its rejection modal retained a copied `OvertimeRequest`. A red-first component test opened Reject for a pending row, entered a reason, then replaced the authorized list cache row with `status=approved` and `available_actions.reject=false`; the old dialog remained actionable. The page now holds approve/reject targets by ID and derives both records and action eligibility from the current query response. It disables and guards confirmation while the query is fetching or errored, if the row is missing, or if the latest action projection no longer allows the transition. The user's typed rejection reason remains in the open dialog. The same current-state guard now also covers approve confirmation, and the server's existing locked state validation remains authoritative.

The red-first rejection regression passes with the focused page suite (2/2); targeted ESLint and a trailing-whitespace check pass. No Overtime API action, two-user browser, live socket, cache-backend behavior, or latency sample was run. The approval dialog follows the shared mechanism but was not independently exercised in this slice; all unrun Overtime runtime matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 356. Customer Pricing withdrawal dialog trusted its copied row action (fixed)

The Pricing Requests page kept a copied `PriceRequest` in modal state, and its Withdraw button was disabled only until a reason was entered. A red-first component regression opened the modal for a `pending_finance` request, replaced the active authorized list result with an `offered` version whose `available_actions.can_withdraw` was false, and observed that Withdraw stayed enabled. The page now stores only the request ID and resolves the selected request from current list data. It disables/guards Approve and Withdraw unless that row is present, its list query has settled successfully, and the latest viewer-specific action projection allows the operation. The separate review query remains required for current Finance cost/signature data. Typed notes remain in the modal during reconciliation; missing or inaccessible rows show an unavailable state.

The isolated stale-withdraw regression passes 1/1 and targeted ESLint passes. The combined pre-existing `reviews.test.tsx` file run had 8 failures / 2 passes, several reporting duplicate responsive controls for singular role queries; no pre-change baseline was run, so the combined file is not reported green. No pricing API mutation, second browser, socket event, cache backend, or latency sample was run; matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 357. Bill payment rejection dialog retained a stale decision (fixed)

The Bill detail page's Reject confirmation used the payment snapshot that opened the dialog. A red-first component regression opened Reject for a pending payment, replaced the active authorized Bill detail cache row with a Posted payment and `can_decide=false`, and observed that Confirm reject stayed enabled. The page now retains payment IDs and derives current payment/action eligibility from the latest Bill detail query. Reject and Void confirmation are disabled and guarded while the detail query is fetching or errored, when the target disappears, or when current state/action fields disallow the operation; approve also rechecks its current row before calling the API. The rejection reason is retained during refresh. The isolated red-first rejection regression passes 1/1 and targeted ESLint passes.

This confirms a client stale-confirmation defect in the in-flight query reconciliation path. The approve and void guards were not independently exercised, and the component regression does not establish that a live peer event reached the query invalidator. No server mutation, other user/browser, WebSocket event, cache operation, or latency sample ran. Bill payment actor/peer/list/button matrix outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 358. Failed private-channel authorization stranded active subscriptions (fixed)

The channel error handler marked a private-channel registration failed but retained the Echo channel handle. `retryRealtimeSubscriptions()` only reopened registrations with no channel handle. A red-first registry test simulated an active channel auth error, invoked the retry function, and observed only one `Echo.private()` call; the active channel remained failed and could not recover through that path. Because the provider derives its global status from failed channel states, visibility-aware HTTP catch-up continued, but real-time delivery stayed unavailable until transport/channel lifecycle recovery.

The registry now records failure, leaves the failed active channel, resets listener attachment state and reopens it through Echo. The provider invokes this for failed registrations after transport reconnect and on browser focus/online recovery. The focused registry suite passes 6/6, provider suite 8/8, and targeted ESLint passes.

This is a confirmed frontend connection-recovery root cause affecting any active private-channel subscriber after an isolated authorization error. The reproduction is unit-level only; no live Reverb authorization outage or cross-user UI was run. The fallback catch-up remains active during failure.


### 359. Recovery operation ID survived logout in tab storage (fixed)

`RecoveryPage` persists the current operation identifier at `ogami.recovery.operation_id` so the authorized recovery API can resume status display after a page reload. The shared auth-scope cleanup cleared QueryClient, service-worker caches and enumerated app drafts but did not include this sessionStorage key. A red-first auth-store test wrote the key, logged out, and confirmed that the identifier remained in the same tab. The next recovery session could therefore inherit an old operation target, although every API read still required a fresh in-memory recovery token.

`clearAuthSessionDrafts()` now removes the key on logout, new login and identity/access-scope changes, alongside pending production session data. The regression failed before the change and passes afterward (1 focused test), and targeted ESLint passes. This fixes stale cross-session UI state, not an API data-authorization bypass. No browser recovery operation, other user, or API call was run.


### 360. Return Management split dialog kept a stale received quantity (fixed)

The split dialog stored a copied `ReturnRequestItem`; when the active authorized RMA detail query changed, its quantity validation still used the old received total. A red-first component regression opened the dialog at 100, entered 2, then changed the current query row to a received total of 1; Split line remained enabled before the fix. The dialog now stores only the line ID and resolves the current row from the RMA query, gating submission on current query success/fetch state, RMA status/disposition, permission and received total. It preserves typed input, reports the unavailable/current-state reason and invalidates the central RMA query on mutation errors. The regression passes, and `detail.test.tsx` passes 5/5 with targeted ESLint.

This confirms a stale client action snapshot, separate from event delivery and cache invalidation. No API mutation, second user, live WebSocket event, cache backend, or latency was exercised; the split route's runtime matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 361. Admin Role Archive confirmation retained stale eligibility (fixed)

The Roles page stored the complete Role object when opening Archive. After the active authorized list query updated the same custom role from `users_count=0` to `users_count=1`, the open confirmation still enabled Archive. A red-first component regression reproduced the enabled stale confirmation. Archive and Restore now use the shared `useQueryRecordTarget` hook to retain IDs and derive the current query row; fetch/error/missing-target and current eligibility state gate and guard confirmation. A successful bodyless action removes the row immediately from cached role lists before central invalidation; errors also invalidate role detail/list and user-role option families.

The focused regression passes 1/1 and targeted ESLint passes. This is controlled client/query-state evidence only: no Role API mutation, peer browser, live channel event or latency sample ran. Restore shares the guard but was not independently tested; the relevant route matrix outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 362. HR Training Archive confirmation retained a stale server action (fixed)

The Training list confirmation stored a copied `Training` resource. A red-first component regression opened Archive with `available_actions` including `archive`, changed the active authorized list row to `available_actions=[]`, and observed the modal still enabled. Archive and Restore now use the shared `useQueryRecordTarget` hook to bind their targets to the current list row; the server action projection, query fetch/error state and target existence gate and guard submission. Successful bodyless actions remove the actor row before central invalidation; failures invalidate the Training family.

The focused regression passes 1/1 and targeted ESLint passes. This is local component/query-state evidence only; no Training API mutation, second user, live event or latency sample ran. Restore shares the guard but was not independently exercised; route matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 363. Scheduled Export confirmation used stale rows and lacked server actions (fixed)

The Scheduled Exports page stored copied `ScheduledExport` objects for Archive and Restore. A red-first component regression confirmed that removing the target from the active list left Archive enabled. A second regression kept the row in the combined `trashed=with` result but changed its current action from Archive to Restore; the dialog still remained enabled. Source tracing found that `ScheduledExportResource` exposed neither soft-delete state nor available actions, so the client could not distinguish the two states.

The resource now supplies `available_actions.update/archive/restore` from the model's soft-delete state. A focused API contract test verifies active and archived list resources. The SPA renders Status/Edit/Archive/Restore from those server flags; the shared `useQueryRecordTarget` keeps open dialogs attached to the latest authorized query and gates submission on query freshness plus the current action projection. Successful bodyless archive/restore calls immediately remove rows that leave the current filter, then use the central `scheduled_export` invalidation map; errors invalidate the same family.

Both red-first regressions pass in the focused SPA file (2/2); the API contract test passes (1 test, 5 assertions), and targeted ESLint/PHP syntax checks pass. Pint flagged the resource's existing formatting convention; its broad reformat was reverted to avoid unrelated churn. No archive/restore API mutation, second user, live WebSocket, backend cache behavior, or latency was tested. Scheduled Export route matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 364. Scheduled Export actor count stayed stale after a 204 archive (fixed)

The actor's Archive/Restore API returns no resource body. Delta 663 removed the schedule row from the current filtered list immediately after success, but it left `meta.total` unchanged until the central invalidation refetched. A red-first component regression held that refetch pending and observed the empty state beside a stale `1 schedules` header. The shared `removeScheduledExport()` page reconciler now filters the row and decrements total in one cache update for active archive and archived-only restore. The focused SPA page file passes 3/3, and targeted ESLint/diff checks pass.

This confirms immediate actor cache state only. No bodyless action endpoint, peer client, WebSocket delivery or latency was exercised; Scheduled Export runtime matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 365. Shared read-through cache accepted a null TTL (preventive policy gap closed)

`FailOpenCache::remember()` accepted a nullable TTL, and the installed Laravel 11 `Repository::put()` implementation treats `null` as a call to `forever()`. The current application call sites pass bounded integer or date-based TTLs, so this was not evidence of an existing stale/permanent business-data entry. A red-first unit regression failed before the change because the shared helper accepted null and evaluated the resolver. The helper now excludes null from its parameter type; the focused test passes (1 test / 2 assertions) and confirms the resolver does not run for an invalid TTL. This is a future cache-safety guard, not a reproduced user-visible stale-data incident; no cache was removed and no performance impact was measured.

### 102. A confirmed protected-request 401 cleared only the discovering tab

`api/client.ts` retries one protected 401 after an `/auth/user` probe; when the probe confirms the shared session is gone, it previously cleared only the current `QueryClient` and navigated to sign-in. Each same-origin tab owns a separate in-memory cache, so sibling tabs could keep the prior identity and cached records until their own next request. The source gap is confirmed by a red-first interceptor regression: before the change, a confirmed session loss produced zero peer signals.

The interceptor now publishes one minimal `all/changed` auth-session signal after the confirming probe. Receivers clear their own user-scoped caches and revalidate through their own guard APIs; duplicate protected 401s coalesce within the page. The actor still clears locally and navigates without relying on Echo. Focused client tests pass 19/19. The explicit logout two-tab Playwright scenario passes, but does not exercise the interceptor fallback. An attempted out-of-band session-revocation browser scenario timed out during concurrent dashboard requests, so cross-tab UI delivery for this exact branch remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 366. Start Production refreshed readiness on a fixed timer while realtime was healthy (fixed for clock-only changes)

The dialog's `execution-readiness` query declared a 30-second React Query interval. A red-first component regression advanced a visible dialog by 30 seconds and observed a second API request. Database-driven readiness changes already use authorized private work-order/readiness signals, while elapsed time changes schedule state, crew availability, and resource overlap without a write.

The API now computes an aggregate `next_refresh_at` for execution schedule thresholds, relevant equipment reservation/maintenance intervals, assigned-crew shift/attendance boundaries, and plant-local midnight. It exposes no attendance or reservation details in that field. The client issues one visible-tab refetch at the returned boundary; fixed polling is removed and shared realtime catch-up remains responsible for transport degradation, focus, and reconnect. The focused API test passes (1 test / 2 assertions), the focused SPA timer test passes (1 selected test), and targeted ESLint/PHP syntax checks pass. No browser, two-user or live Reverb run was performed; related UI matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`, and request-volume/API-latency impact was not measured.


### 367. Clearing the SPA cache did not fence in-flight mutation responses (fixed)

The shared `QueryClient.clear()` removed current query data, but TanStack mutation execution continues independently. A red-first regression held a real app-cache mutation response, cleared the QueryClient, then resolved it; the old mutation's `onSuccess` wrote the previous session's record back into an unscoped cache key, and the mutation promise returned that record to awaiting component code. This confirmed a cache-scope race that ordinary logout-clear tests, which mock the QueryClient and have no pending mutations, did not cover.



### 368. A connected socket could hide a stalled broadcast queue for ten seconds (detection tightened)

The provider can have an open Echo socket while Horizon/broadcast workers stop draining their queue. Delivery state is checked separately through a no-store endpoint; it only returns an aggregate healthy/degraded value. The then-current provider scheduled the next check ten seconds after each successful request. A red-first test held the socket connected, returned healthy once and degraded next, advanced the fake clock by two seconds, and observed that the provider still reported Live without making the next health request. This confirms the detection timer, not a real queue outage or a cross-user stale UI.

Delta 677 changes the visible-tab health cadence to two seconds. Initial channel-ready catch-up remains scoped to each authorized subscription; global active-query catch-up remains tied to later health transitions, with the existing visibility-aware exponential fallback while degraded. The focused provider suite passes 9/9 and targeted ESLint passes. Health checks now run up to 30 times per visible tab per minute rather than 6; request/cache impact, a live stopped-worker recovery and browser latency remain unmeasured. Matrix outcomes are unchanged.


### 369. Overtime state transitions ignored a stale client record version (fixed)

Overtime approval and owner cancellation locked and revalidated the current status, so a repeated action after a terminal transition was rejected. However, while a record remained Pending, callers could submit decisions against an old record snapshot because the API exposed no monotonic version and ignored `If-Match`. A focused API regression sent `If-Match: "0"` to cancel an existing version-1 Pending request; before the fix, the endpoint returned 200 and changed its state. This confirmed a stale-write contract gap, not a missing event or response-cache failure.

Delta 680 adds and backfills `overtime_requests.version`, returns it in authorized resources, increments it on Eloquent updates, and checks the supplied version after acquiring the record lock for approve/reject/cancel/restore and each row in bulk approval. Stale versions return typed HTTP 409; legacy clients without `If-Match` retain existing behavior. SPA callers now send their resource version. The focused stale/current cancel cases pass (2 tests / 8 assertions), the bulk stale-version test passes (1 / 4), and the event payload contract passes (1 / 1). Actor response behavior is API-contract tested; no two-user browser or live socket path ran, so Overtime matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 370. Stock Count responses omitted viewer-specific action projections (contract gap fixed)

Stock Count session/item resources returned state and related actors but no `available_actions`. The SPA reconstructed Start, Cancel, Complete, Count and variance Approve controls locally from permissions, status and options. A red-first focused API test observed a missing action field on a newly created session. This is a confirmed response/UI contract gap that can leave action eligibility incomplete after a refetch; it is not a reproduced live cross-user delivery failure. The existing service already row-locks state and returns HTTP 409 for stale transition preconditions, so the older matrix claim that these conflicts returned 422 was stale documentation.

Delta 689 adds server-computed session and item action lists scoped to the authenticated viewer, with state, permission/delegation and maker-checker checks, and uses those fields for the SPA lifecycle/variance controls. The focused API test passes for a manager, independent checker and view-only user (1 test / 14 assertions); the Stock Count SPA stale-action test passes 1/1 and targeted ESLint/Pint pass. The project typecheck was stopped after 90 seconds and about 1.6 GiB RSS without diagnostics. No mutation, second browser, WebSocket receipt or latency was measured; all Stock Count runtime matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 371. Stock Count submit could be silently dropped by a concurrent detail refetch (fixed)

A two-context Chromium run first showed the Count dialog open and its Record click completed without a count API request. The trace showed Stock Count detail reads occurring during that action, and the modal remained open without a validation error. Source tracing found the form's valid-submit callback returned early whenever `sessionUnavailable` was true; that flag includes `sessionQuery.isFetching`. Thus a refresh that began after the form submit event was dispatched could silently discard the user's action. This is distinct from missing event delivery or stale server data.

A red-first focused component regression mounted the page with its realtime hooks mocked, opened Recount, began a pending detail refetch and submitted the form; before the fix `stockCountApi.recordCount` was not called. The redundant submit-time fetch guard is removed while the visible button remains disabled during a known refetch. The API locks and revalidates the current session and returns its existing success or typed 409 result. The focused SPA test file passes 2/2. A follow-up two-context browser run passes: one UI count returned 200; the admin peer received the private signal and fetched `counted`; actor and peer rendered Counted/Recount without reload. One actor-response-to-peer-render sample was 1,058 ms, not a percentile. This fix is logged as a separate functional review unit in FINDINGS.md. No other Stock Count surface is promoted.


## Root-cause follow-up 698 — shared badge channel exposed cross-user event timing (2026-11-03 UTC)

`BadgesChanged` previously targeted one private `badges` channel authorized for every authenticated internal user. Its payload had no data values, but every subscriber could observe other users' badge/approval event timing. This violated least-privilege channel scope and could cause unnecessary client invalidations. The event now fans out to one `badge-user.{userHashId}` private channel per active internal user; the authorization callback requires the authenticated user's decoded ID to equal the channel ID. Payload remains a content-free refresh signal with event ID. `useBadges`, approval board, generic badge invalidation, and Action Center subscribe using the current user's hash. Three E2E probes were updated to the new channel.

The focused fan-out and authorization tests passed (2 tests / 5 assertions), and the focused badge, approval-board and Action Center frontend tests passed (5 tests). The failing-first evidence was collected before implementation: the fan-out test expected per-user channels but observed `private-badges`, and channel auth returned 403 for the unregistered new channel. Post-change there is no multi-user live delivery or latency sample. This is a confirmed source/auth-scope issue; route matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### Delta 704 — extend exact-principal revocation to token and credential changes

The pre-existing PortalAccountAccessRevoked signal covered explicit deactivation, but source inspection found `PortalAccessService::revokeTokens()` and `revokeCustomerTokens()` deleted active credentials without staging that personal signal. `PortalInvitationService` also rotates passwords and deletes tokens during resend/re-invitation without a session update. Open portal tabs could retain cached content until their next authorized API request. Both token-revocation methods now stage the existing opaque personal refresh ping in the same transaction as token deletion; both invitation credential-rotation transactions do the same after writing the new credential and deleting tokens. The event remains after-commit and contains no identity or business fields. A red-first targeted regression failed for both token-revocation routes, then customer/supplier token and customer-resend focused cases passed (3 tests / 13 assertions). The existing two-tab real-stack evidence still covers only customer deactivation; token revocation, supplier session UI and p95 remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 372. Admin session termination failed on PostgreSQL and did not signal the revoked user's tab (fixed in delta 709)

`SessionController::destroy()` selected a session through `LEFT JOIN users` and `LEFT JOIN roles`, then used generic `lockForUpdate()`. The focused HTTP request reproduced PostgreSQL SQLSTATE `0A000` because PostgreSQL cannot apply `FOR UPDATE` to the nullable side of an outer join; the endpoint failed before deleting the session or creating events. The query now uses `FOR UPDATE OF sessions`, which serializes the row it deletes without locking nullable joined rows.



### 373. Payroll progress subscription did not catch up on channel readiness or failure (fixed in delta 721)

`useRealtimeEvent` and the shared subscription registry already supported channel-ready and channel-error callbacks, but the compatibility `useEcho` wrapper accepted only the event handler and discarded those lifecycle hooks. Payroll period detail was the only application consumer of `useEcho`; it refetched after progress events but did not immediately catch up when its private channel first became authorized or failed while the socket remained connected. A focused red-first hook regression reproduced the dropped callback (expected one ready callback, received zero).

`useEcho` now forwards optional ready/error callbacks, and Payroll period detail refetches its authorized period query on either lifecycle outcome. The event handler remains a content-free signal followed by authorized API reads. The focused SPA hook test passes (2 tests), and targeted ESLint passes for the wrapper, regression test, and Payroll detail page. No two-user browser or live authorization-failure scenario ran; peer UI and latency cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 374. User preference and cache-generation subscriptions missed channel-ready catch-up (fixed in delta 721)

The source audit of every application `useRealtimeEvent` consumer found that the user display-preference channel had neither a readiness refetch nor an error refetch, and the cache-generation channel had an error fallback but no readiness catch-up. The separate role-permission channel also lacked a readiness refresh. These channels could become authorized after a relevant change had already occurred, leaving active preference/auth state or cached query families behind until a later event, focus, or reconnect. Red-first focused assertions observed missing callbacks for preference and cache-generation subscriptions; the role-channel omission was then covered by a ready-callback regression.

`usePermissionSync` now reloads server-provided auth/preferences on readiness or failure for the preference channel and on readiness for the role channel. `useCacheGroupRealtime` schedules its registered family invalidation on both readiness and failure. The focused user-catch-up file passes 6 tests, including the ready/error cases; no live 403, role-change browser session, or cache-generation broadcast was induced. These tests verify callback wiring and local invalidation scheduling, not cross-user delivery or measured freshness.


### 375. Purchase Request final approval now refreshes the tested cross-role detail and list; latency remains variable (2026-11-03)

A four-context real-backend Chromium run used Purchasing, Finance, System Administrator and VP contexts. With the VP actor socket disabled, the API response updated the actor to Approved. The peer detail's authorized, private/no-store GET returned Approved and `actions.can_cancel=false`; the detail rendered and removed Cancel in 1.620 seconds after the actor response, and the Purchasing list rendered Approved in 2.220 seconds. Three private `chain.refresh` frames were received. This confirms cross-role detail, server-action and list refresh for this tested final-approval path without reload. Earlier runs measured 2.998, 3.508, 3.628 and 4.047 seconds, so the mixed evidence does not establish a stable 1–2 second SLO or p95; retain PARTIAL for detail/actions.

The tested detail response was `private, no-store`, `CF-Cache-Status: DYNAMIC`, had no `Age` header, and was not served by a service worker. This rules out those HTTP cache layers for this request only. The prior 4.047-second run's API GET took 3.769 seconds while PHP-FPM logged its 10-child cap; an isolated budget snapshot took 94.3 ms cold and 19.4–20.8 ms warm. These observations do not isolate the source of that GET latency. A separate red-first provider regression reproduced three healthy-delivery probes within five seconds; healthy checks now run every five seconds and degraded checks remain every two seconds. This reduces healthy-state probe frequency by 60%, but the uncontrolled E2E sample does not establish that it caused the faster refetch. Cache freshness across other record/cache families, API p95, and cache hit rate remain unmeasured.

A read-only PHP CLI profile measured the Purchase Request eager-load at 24.9 ms, action projection at 2.4 ms / one query, and budget snapshot at 54 ms. It observed 25 SQL queries / 74.3 ms total across those calls and the initial user/record lookup. Because this ran outside PHP-FPM and HTTP, it is not a reproduction of the slow browser request. It does not isolate the 3.769 s GET or prove a query regression; server request timing and the FPM wait remain unmeasured.


### 376. Development PHP-FPM saturation delayed authorized realtime refetches (dev ceiling raised; residual latency remains)

A four-context final Purchase Request approval run on the local dev stack logged PHP-FPM's configured `pm.max_children=10` ceiling. During the same run, the active Nginx proxy recorded 2.3–5.0 second upstream response/header durations on Purchase Request detail reads and up to 5.1 seconds on its options endpoint, with FastCGI connect time near zero; aborted browser reads also appeared as 499s. An earlier two-context sample's detail GET took 3.769 seconds. The responses carried `private, no-store`, had Cloudflare `DYNAMIC`, and bypassed the service worker. This confirms worker-pool saturation in the tested dev burst and rules out those HTTP cache layers for those requests; it does not separate application time from FPM queue wait or establish a backend-cache defect.

The dev image now allows 16 FPM children. On the observed host snapshot, 9.9 GiB was available and running workers used roughly 56–63 MiB RSS. `php-fpm -t` and the rebuilt service config passed; the focused final-approval E2E then passed once with peer render at 1.821 seconds and no `pm.max_children` warning in that run's log window. Earlier 10-worker runs measured 2.259 seconds or failed before the action during an initial detail load. The sample is uncontrolled, so do not attribute the pass solely to the capacity change. Other upstream reads still reached 4.545 seconds at 16 children, leaving PHP endpoint latency partly unresolved. Production's separate FPM image and the route matrix remain unchanged/unverified.
### 377. Invoice payment review controls were inferred from client state (fixed, Delta 749)

`InvoicePaymentSubmissionResource` exposed no `available_actions`; the Finance dialog therefore combined a client permission snapshot with the submitted row's status to decide whether Verify/Reject should appear. `InvoiceResource` also omitted a server projection for the parent Review Payment button. The service already locks and revalidates the invoice and payment row on every decision, so this was a stale-control/response-contract gap rather than an established authorization bypass. The focused API regression now verifies that an authorized Finance user receives both review actions, the portal receives neither, and the verified response removes them; a focused component test verifies hidden controls for a non-actionable server response and immediate actor cache reconciliation. No two-browser reproduction or live event receipt was run, so the invoice UI matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.
### 378. Purchase Order Short-close remained outside the server action map (fixed, Delta 751)

Purchase Order primary lifecycle actions already used `PurchaseOrderAccessPolicy::actionsFor()`, but Short-close visibility combined a state-only API flag with the SPA permission snapshot. The response now includes `actions.can_short_close` from the route's `purchasing.po.create` permission and existing `isShortClosable()` predicate; the UI renders from it. The mutation route and service still enforce all prior permission/state checks. A red-first API regression failed on the missing action and passes after the change; the existing focused detail component file also passes. This contract gap did not establish a live reported-symptom reproduction, and no second browser/event delivery run occurred. Route UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 379. Purchase Order supplier-response buttons could outlive the server's action decision (fixed in delta 753)

The detail UI combined pending response status with the client permission snapshot rather than using a server action projection. A focused component regression reproduced Accept remaining visible while the response payload denied both actions. `PurchaseOrderAccessPolicy::responseActionsFor()` now supplies flags to both embedded PO and response resources; the page renders each decision control from its flag and installs the mutation response into its detail cache. The API regression verifies true flags for an authorized pending response and false flags after acceptance (1 test / 7 assertions); the component suite passes 4/4. This confirms the server contract and actor response reconciliation in a focused harness, not cross-user event receipt. Runtime UI matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.
### 380. NCR Cancel was shown without checking the server's additional cancel guards (fixed, Delta 758)

`spa/src/pages/quality/ncrs/detail.tsx` rendered Cancel from nonterminal status plus the SPA permission snapshot. `NcrService::cancel()` additionally rejects late incoming NCRs and NCRs with a settled MRB decision, including after acquiring the row lock. `NcrResource` did not expose the viewer-specific eligibility. A red-first component regression reproduced the stale button when the API payload denied cancellation; a red-first feature regression failed because `available_actions.can_cancel` was absent. The resource now projects the same cancel eligibility conditions, and the UI uses that server flag. The service still revalidates on every action. Focused SPA coverage passes 5/5 and the API projection test passes 1/8 assertions; targeted lint and PHP syntax checks pass. No live two-user event trace or cancellation attempt from a peer role ran. The relevant route UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 381. Sales Order primary controls were derived from local state (action contract gap fixed; peer symptom unverified)

Red-first SPA regressions supplied false server action flags while local permission/state allowed the controls; Confirm and Edit still rendered. API regressions found `available_actions.can_confirm`, `can_edit`, and `can_cancel` absent. The SalesOrder resource now computes all three per viewer using the existing permission and state rules, and the detail controls use those flags. Focused API evidence passes (1 test / 12 assertions); the detail component file passes 9/9. Existing controller/service guards remain authoritative. This reproduces and fixes the response/UI action-contract gap, but does not establish a stale control during a two-user WebSocket failure. The route's live actor/peer/list/button outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 382. Asset Edit remained visible for a server-immutable disposed asset (action contract gap fixed; peer freshness unverified)

The Asset detail page rendered Edit from the SPA permission snapshot alone, but `AssetService::update()` already locks and rejects disposed assets. A red-first component test reproduced Edit remaining visible when the API action flag was false, and a red-first feature test confirmed `AssetResource` omitted that field. The resource now returns viewer/state-scoped `available_actions.can_edit`; the page renders Edit only from it. Focused API coverage passes 1 test / 4 assertions with a PHPUnit warning, and the Asset detail component file passes 7/7; targeted ESLint, PHP syntax, and Pint checks pass. This fixes the response/UI contract but does not establish a live cross-user WebSocket symptom. Asset route UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.
## Root-cause follow-up 787 — long Action Center boundaries stopped refreshing (2026-11-04 UTC)

- **Evidence:** `ActionCenterTaskController` permits snoozes up to 30 days. The Action Center page previously scheduled `Math.min(delay + 25, 2_147_000_000)` and invalidated only once. The new focused test set a 30-day boundary and failed because the callback ran at the first capped timeout, about 5.2 days early. The boundary string does not change on that early refetch, so the effect does not arm a second timeout.
- **Impact:** A snoozed Action Center item or other time-derived status could remain stale after the capped early refresh until another event, focus, or navigation occurred.
- **Classification:** Confirmed frontend timer-boundary freshness defect; the reproduction is a deterministic unit test, not a two-user browser reproduction.
- **Disposition:** `scheduleBoundaryInvalidation()` now re-arms one bounded timeout at a time and invalidates only when the target boundary is reached. The focused test passes 1/1 and targeted ESLint passes. No browser/background-tab timing was exercised and no route-matrix runtime cells were promoted.

## Root-cause follow-up 788 — Journal Entry controls did not use server action eligibility

- **Evidence:** `JournalEntryResource` returned status/record data without `available_actions`; the detail component used the SPA permission snapshot and status to derive lifecycle controls. A focused feature test failed on the absent action field. A repeated Post after posting reproduced HTTP 422 for a stale state request. The first conflict UI regression also failed because it refreshed the detail but did not invalidate the active journal list.
- **Impact:** A stale detail snapshot could continue to show an action that current state or maker-checker rules reject. The actor also received an ambiguous validation-style response for a concurrent/stale state.
- **Classification:** Confirmed response/UI contract gap and state-conflict response inconsistency for Journal Entries. No two-user browser reproduction establishes this as the cause of peer WebSocket staleness.
- **Disposition:** The resource now projects viewer/state-scoped actions; the detail page uses them and sends typed 409 conflicts through the central invalidation map so active lists and details refresh. The API regression passes 1 test / 21 assertions and the SPA detail tests pass 3/3. Route actor/peer/list/button cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED` pending live scenario evidence.



## Root-cause follow-up 790 — Vendor detail had no listener and the generic hook selected the wrong private channel

`VendorChanged` broadcasts `accounting.vendor.{hashId}` as `.accounting.vendor.changed`; `routes/channels.php` authorizes that exact record channel using `accounting.vendors.view`. Vendor detail did not call `useRealtimeRecord`. A red-first component regression first observed no Vendor listener. After adding the page hook, the same test showed that the generic hook subscribed to `chain.vendor.vendor-1`, which does not match the backend channel. The central hook now maps `vendor` to `accounting.vendor.{id}`, and Vendor detail subscribes through it. The focused regression passes and verifies that the event handler triggers an authorized API refetch and the visible name changes. This proves page registration, channel-name alignment, and handler behavior in a component harness only; no browser socket, second user, list, related record, or latency scenario ran. Vendor route runtime matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Root-cause follow-up 793 — dashboard snapshots had no visible generation time

- **Evidence:** the shared shell rendered no age/timestamp when a page omitted `updatedAt`, and several dashboard API response shapes omitted a top-level snapshot time. The red-first `DashboardShell` regression failed on missing “As of” text. Snapshot services use 30-second `FailOpenCache` entries, so stamping after `remember()` would falsely make an old cache hit appear newly generated; `generated_at` is now stored in the cache-miss result. Dashboard response-key schema versions were advanced to bypass timestamp-less entries after deployment.
- **Impact:** users could not see how old a cached dashboard snapshot was. This did not reproduce stale data and does not explain the record-status/button symptoms.
- **Classification:** confirmed freshness-visibility/response-contract gap, not a confirmed stale-data incident.
- **Disposition:** shared shell displays page-provided `updatedAt` or API `generated_at` for fresh and stale values. Cached internal dashboards, Finance and portal dashboards now expose a source time; Production and configurable dashboard layout already exposed one. The focused shell test passes 6/6; one Admin service test passes 10 assertions with the known missing `/var/www/.env` warning; PHP syntax passes for ten service files, targeted ESLint passes, and SPA `npm run typecheck` passes. No cache was removed, prior payloads remain bounded by the existing 30-second TTL, and no route-matrix cell is promoted without a browser/two-user run.

## Residual architecture gap 794 — normal dashboard widget polling

`RoleDashboardShell.tsx` and `pages/dashboard/default.tsx` poll active widget data every 60 seconds even while Reverb is healthy. This conflicts with the target degraded-only polling policy and adds periodic API load. It may mask missing widget source signals, but no reported stale-data failure was reproduced from the interval itself. The provider already catches up active queries while disconnected; all per-widget event sources and authorized consumers still need coverage before the normal interval can safely be removed. This is source-confirmed and remains a migration item, not a confirmed symptom root cause; no matrix cells were changed.

Delta 949 re-audited the current widget catalog against cache-generation inputs. Static summary keys map to an observer scope or an explicit source hook (including stock low-stock/reservation scopes and Alert's shared generation); dynamic `kpi.*` scopes are derived from KPI definition/snapshot writes. The generic ping reaches active dashboard viewers who can read at least one catalog widget, then each API request enforces the viewer's current panel grants. This closes the key-to-scope and broad-audience source questions, but it does not prove every query-builder writer calls its invalidator, cover time-only due/SLA boundary changes, or prove live delivery. The 60-second interval therefore remains until those writer and clock-boundary paths are mapped; the shared provider remains the disconnected/reconnect fallback.

## Root-cause follow-up 795 — PPC cache invalidation had no matching client refresh event

- **Evidence:** the `MaintenanceWorkOrder` model is mapped to `dashboard:ppc` in `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver::SCOPES_BY_MODEL`, and the observer rotates that cache generation after commit. The focused test confirmed the other side of the gap: before the fix, the same source write called `OutboxService::recordCoalesced()` zero times for the PPC dashboard. PPC's shared production hook listened to `production.dashboard`, whose existing producers did not cover every source mapped to the PPC snapshot. The frontend invalidation map already had a `['dashboard','ppc']` query family, but there was no authorized PPC-specific event to trigger it.
- **Impact:** PPC viewers could remain on a stale snapshot until manual navigation, focus/reconnect catch-up, or the generic widget interval, even though a future API read would use the rotated generation.
- **Classification:** confirmed server producer/client subscription gap for the `dashboard:ppc` aggregate. It is a source-level reproduction, not a two-user browser reproduction.
- **Disposition:** every source model mapped to `dashboard:ppc` now stages a coalesced `PpcDashboardChanged` outbox signal after the generation-rotation callback is registered. The event is content-free, after-commit, on the dedicated broadcast queue, and targets private `dashboard.ppc`; the channel matches the PPC dashboard API permission. The shared `DashboardShell` installs a permission-gated hook and the central invalidation map refreshes the PPC snapshot, widget data and the PPC page's chain-bottleneck query. The focused observer/envelope/codec check passes; channel authorization passes for allowed/denied users; SPA map/hook checks pass 2/2. PHPUnit prints the existing missing `/var/www/.env` container warning. No Reverb frame or second-browser behavior was exercised, so the PPC runtime matrix remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Root-cause follow-up 796 — Finance cache generation had no shared dashboard refresh event

- **Evidence:** `Bill` is mapped to `dashboard:finance`, so `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` rotates the scoped Finance snapshot generation. The red-first regression expected the Bill write to stage both its Plant Manager and Finance aggregate events; before the fix, the actual outbox call contained only the Plant Manager signal. Finance's source entity-list subscriptions are conditional on additional record grants, while the dashboard route itself requires only `dashboard.accounting.view`. The Finance API applies panel-level permissions, including aggregate panels that can be visible without every underlying record-list permission.
- **Impact:** a Finance viewer without the relevant source-list grant could keep a stale open summary even though a new API read would use the rotated cache generation.
- **Classification:** confirmed aggregate signal coverage gap for the Finance dashboard; the before-state was reproduced at the observer boundary, not in a two-user browser.
- **Disposition:** every model scope mapped to `dashboard:finance` now stages one coalesced `FinanceDashboardChanged` outbox signal per transaction. It uses the existing private `dashboard.finance` channel, whose active-user, accounting-feature and `dashboard.accounting.view` checks match the dashboard route. The payload is refresh metadata only; the frontend subscribes from the shared shell and central invalidation refreshes Finance, widget data and chain-bottleneck query families. Observer/outbox/event-contract and existing channel authorization checks pass; SPA map/hook checks pass. Test commands report the existing missing `/var/www/.env` warning. No live Reverb delivery, role comparison or latency was measured; Finance matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Root-cause follow-up 797 — Purchasing aggregate joined data was outside its dashboard signal fanout

- **Evidence:** `PurchasingDashboardService` includes Department names in the cached PR action queue and Vendor names in its upcoming-delivery/supplier panels. `Department` and `Vendor` both rotate `dashboard:purchasing`, but `DepartmentChanged` broadcasts only to `entity.department.visible` (HR department readers), and the generic `PurchasingDashboardRealtimeService` branch only selected Item/Product. A red-first observer regression expecting the aggregate signal for Department and Vendor failed before the change.
- **Impact:** a Purchasing dashboard reader without HR Department/vendor record permission could keep old joined labels after a source update; a refetch could also return the old 30-second snapshot until its generation changed on a later read or expiry.
- **Classification:** confirmed aggregate refresh producer gap for these two source model types; no two-user stale-page reproduction was run.
- **Disposition:** `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` now stages the existing coalesced `PurchasingDashboardChanged` event when any Item, Product, Department or Vendor source with the `dashboard:purchasing` scope changes. The event already targets private `dashboard.purchasing`, the channel matches the dashboard permission, and the existing SPA hook invalidates the dashboard query. The focused observer regression passes (1 warning-marked test / 2 assertions); the existing Purchasing hook suite passes 2/2. PHPUnit reports the known missing `/var/www/.env` container warning. No socket delivery or latency was measured; matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Root-cause follow-up 798 — employee dashboard cache generations had no permitted refresh path for personal HR/payroll sources

- **Evidence:** `HrDashboardService::employee()` reads current-month Attendance, remaining `EmployeeLeaveBalance`, pending `LeaveRequest`, latest `Payroll`, and the next `Holiday`; the cache generation rotates for these sources. Attendance/Employee/Holiday already have employee-readable signals. Leave-request/balance list channels require leave-view/approval permissions, and payroll-period channels require payroll-period view permission, so ordinary linked employees cannot rely on those channels. A red-first observer regression for `PayrollPeriod` showed the employee generation was included in the cache scope but the observer staged only Finance and HR dashboard events.
- **Impact:** the authorized `/dashboards/employee` snapshot could remain cached in an open self-service tab after a leave, balance, payroll, or payroll-period write; direct data refresh was limited by the 30-second cache TTL when no separate user notice reached the client.
- **Classification:** confirmed source-level producer/channel-audience gap; this was reproduced at the observer boundary, not with two browser sessions.
- **Disposition:** added a content-free after-commit `EmployeeDashboardChanged` signal. `LeaveRequest`, `EmployeeLeaveBalance`, and `Payroll` target the linked active employee's private channel; `PayrollPeriod` uses a private channel authorized to linked employees because period lifecycle changes can affect every snapshot. `LeaveBalanceRealtimeService` also stages owner-targeted signals for row saves/initialization and a linked-employee signal for bulk year-end/reset jobs that bypass model observers. The callbacks match the authenticated employee dashboard scope; payloads carry refresh metadata only. The shared shell subscribes and the central map invalidates active self-service/dashboard queries. Focused observer, event-contract and channel-authorization checks pass (three PHPUnit warnings / eleven assertions); the SPA invalidation map and typecheck pass. No live delivery, two-user behavior or latency was verified; employee matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Root-cause follow-up 801 — rich widget generations were not paired with scalar generations

`DashboardWidgetDataService` caches a scalar under `widget-summary:{key}`; `WidgetAnalyticsService` independently caches rich data under `widget-rich:{key}`. The source map had several scalar generations but not the matching rich generation. The regression used an Invoice write and `finance.ar_aging`: before the fix, `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver::invalidateSource()` changed the scalar generation but the rich generation remained identical. After the fix, source scopes retire both generations for registered same-key rich renderers; rich-only Executive/Quality widgets are mapped to their transactional sources, and `KpiDefinition`/`KpiSnapshot` changes resolve dynamic KPI keys by definition code. Static reconciliation found all 28 literal rich handler keys in the pairing catalog, including `approvals.pending`; `ApprovalDelegation` now explicitly retires both approval widget generations, while `kpi.*` uses the dynamic resolver. Focused Invoice, KPI, and delegation observer regressions pass (3 tests / 6 assertions) with the known missing-container-`.env` warning; the frontend delegation invalidation regression also passes (1 selected test, 93 skipped). No two-user dashboard UI, socket delivery, cache hit rate, API latency, or matrix outcome change is claimed. The shared 60-second generic-widget intervals remain pending complete producer/consumer and time-boundary verification.

## Bounded freshness exception — unauthenticated database recovery progress

Source references: `spa/src/pages/auth/recovery.tsx`, `api/routes/api.php` (`auth:sanctum` broadcast auth and `recovery.access` routes), `api/app/Common/Middleware/RequireRecoveryAccess.php` (`private, no-store`), and `api/app/Common/Services/BackupManifestStore.php` (atomically replaced sidecar). Existing callbacks in `api/routes/channels.php` take authenticated ERP/portal users; no recovery bearer channel exists.

The recovery page has no ERP/portal principal to authorize through Laravel's normal `/broadcasting/auth` endpoint. Its operation state is held in an atomically replaced, file-backed sidecar so restore progress can still be read while the primary database is offline or replaced. The short-lived recovery bearer token is validated by `recovery.access`, and that endpoint returns `Cache-Control: no-store, private`. The UI checks only the current operation every two seconds while active and stops on terminal/error state. No private recovery channel exists, and no broad/public channel is used. Replacing the poll safely requires a dedicated token-aware channel authorization path bound to the active operation ID; until then this single recovery screen is an explicit, bounded exception rather than a missing ERP record event.

## Root-cause follow-up 808 — aggregate dashboard signals missed configurable widget queries

- **Evidence:** the backend source observer retires both aggregate and per-widget cache generations, then stages after-commit dashboard signals. Before the fix, the central SPA map for Admin, HR, employee, Plant Manager, Purchasing, and Quality did not include the configurable `['dashboard', 'widget-data']` query family. A red-first invalidation-map regression reproduced the missing Admin widget invalidation. Further source tracing found Admin, Purchasing, and Quality signals subscribed only from their dedicated pages; the generic pages share `DashboardShell` and did not have those subscriptions.
- **Impact:** a viewer with a configurable widget layout could keep a prior widget value after a dashboard source write, even though the server-side generation had rotated and a dedicated dashboard signal was published. The connected 60-second refresh eventually reconciled the widget, but did not meet event-driven near-live freshness.
- **Disposition:** the central signal map now invalidates widget-data for all six aggregate signal families. The shared `DashboardShell` owns the permission/feature-gated Admin, Purchasing, and Quality private listeners; the three page-local registrations were removed. The content-free event prompts an authorized API refetch, whose per-widget gates remain authoritative. The focused SPA invalidation-map suite passes 95/95, targeted ESLint passes, and `npm run typecheck` passes. No live socket, two-user rendering, or latency sample was run; matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The 60-second generic widget refresh remains as coverage protection for incomplete source families.

## Root-cause follow-up 809 — Purchase Request stale state responses bypassed conflict recovery

- **Evidence:** `PurchaseRequestService` locks and rechecks status for Submit/Approve/Reject/Cancel, but the status guards threw generic `BusinessRuleException` (mapped to 422). Submit and Cancel also ran state-sensitive `canManageDraft`/`canCancel` checks before the locked guard. A red-first endpoint regression repeated Submit after a successful Submit and observed 403 before the locked-state check, so the stale action never produced the 409 signal that the shared SPA status-action hook uses to refetch.
- **Impact:** an owner acting on a stale Submit/Cancel control could receive a forbidden/validation-style response and miss the shared 409 recovery path; a concurrent Approve/Reject whose locked status changed also returned 422.
- **Disposition:** `PurchaseRequestAccessPolicy::canManageRequest()` now checks row scope/ownership separately from current state. Locked stale-state guards for Submit/Approve/Reject/Cancel throw `PurchaseRequestStateConflictException`; the controller returns the standard JSON conflict envelope with code `purchase_request_state_conflict`. Existing action authorization remains in force. The focused repeat-Submit API regression passes (1 test / 4 assertions, PHPUnit warning status); PHP syntax checks pass. Pint passes on the new exception, policy, and controller, while reporting pre-existing style issues in the touched service/test files. No Purchase Request page change was needed because `useOptimisticStatusAction` already refetches the detail and invalidates linked query families on 409. No two-user browser/socket test or full module suite ran, and route matrix UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; this is a confirmed response-path gap, not a reproduced cross-user WebSocket failure.

## Root-cause follow-up 810 — Purchase Order stale actions left the detail page actionable

- **Evidence:** `PurchaseOrderService::submit()` had a locked status guard but used a state-sensitive `canManageDraft()` before that check. Repeating Submit after success reproduced HTTP 403 instead of entering stale-state recovery. Approve/Reject/Cancel locked guards also returned generic 422 business-rule responses. The detail page's mutation error handlers only toasted and did not invalidate the record; when a conflict refresh arrived, an open confirmation dialog could still retain its stale Submit control. A red-first component regression failed because the current Pending approval response did not replace the draft/action view.
- **Impact:** a buyer could be denied on a stale Submit without the client refetching, and stale approval/confirmation controls could remain actionable while the user reviewed the message.
- **Disposition:** `PurchaseOrderAccessPolicy::canManagePurchaseOrder()` now separates buyer permission/row ownership from Draft status. Locked stale-state checks in Submit/Approve/Reject/Cancel throw `PurchaseOrderStateConflictException`; the controller returns HTTP 409 with a stable error code. The PO detail page routes typed state conflicts through the central purchase-order invalidation map and closes pending status dialogs. Red-first endpoint reproduction observed 403; after the fix the focused endpoint regression passes (1 test / 3 assertions). The cross-buyer draft-submit regression passes (1 / 3), and the detail component file passes 5/5; API tests are warning-marked by a missing `/var/www/.env` container read. PHP syntax, targeted ESLint, and Pint for the six exception/policy/controller files pass. No live socket or two-user test ran; Purchase Order matrix runtime cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED` and this does not prove the original cross-user broadcast symptom is resolved.

## Root-cause follow-up 811 — stale Bill posting errors did not refresh AP actions

- **Evidence:** `BillService::postDraft()` re-reads and locks the Bill, then returned a generic `BusinessRuleException` when the persisted status was no longer Draft. `BillController` converted it to HTTP 422. A focused endpoint regression reproduced 422 when posting a Bill already in Unpaid state. The Bill detail error handler only toasted, leaving the action snapshot in place; a red-first SPA regression observed no second authorized detail fetch after the conflict.
- **Impact:** after another actor posts the Bill, the open AP detail could keep its stale post/override action until navigation or manual refresh.
- **Disposition:** the locked Draft guard now throws `BillStateConflictException`; the controller returns a minimal 409 envelope with code `bill_state_conflict`. The page closes the stale posting confirmation/override dialog and refreshes through the central Bill invalidation map. The focused API regression passes (1 test / 2 assertions; known `/var/www/.env` PHPUnit warning), and the selected component regression passes (1 test; 8 skipped by filter). PHP syntax and targeted ESLint pass. Pint passes for the new exception; the pre-existing BillController fully-qualified-strict-types style issue remains. No two-user socket delivery, cache change, latency measure, or matrix outcome change was made; Bill freshness cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Root-cause follow-up 812 — stale Sales Order confirmation did not refresh state and actions

- **Evidence:** `SalesOrderService::confirm()` reloaded and locked the order, but when its state had already advanced it raised a generic business-rule error. The confirm endpoint returned HTTP 422. A focused API regression first reproduced that 422. The Sales Order detail page displayed the error without refetching the record, leaving the confirmation snapshot and its controls stale.
- **Impact:** a user opening an order after another actor confirmed it could retain stale status/actions and receive a generic validation response on a stale confirmation attempt.
- **Disposition:** the locked state guard now throws `SalesOrderStateConflictException`; `SalesOrderController` returns HTTP 409 with `sales_order_state_conflict`. The detail page closes its stale confirmation dialog and refetches through the existing authorized realtime query path. The focused API regression passes (1 test / 3 assertions; known `/var/www/.env` PHPUnit warning); the selected component regression passes (1 test, 9 skipped). Targeted ESLint, PHP syntax, `git diff --check`, and `ogamiph.dev` plus `/@vite/client` HTTP 200 checks pass. This reproduces and fixes the actor's stale-action response path; it does not prove WebSocket delivery or another user's screen refresh. Sales Order matrix runtime cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Root-cause follow-up 813 — shared SPA query defaults held unoverridden server data fresh for 30 seconds

- **Evidence:** the application-wide TanStack Query client configured `staleTime: 30_000`. A red-first focused regression against the shared client observed `30000`, confirming that API-backed data without a local override could be considered fresh for 30 seconds after it entered the query cache. On screen remount during that window, React Query could reuse the cached snapshot without revalidating through the user's authorized API.
- **Impact:** missed or inactive-page invalidations could leave a previously viewed record or list looking current on remount. This is a source-confirmed stale-risk mechanism; no specific user screen or two-user socket failure was reproduced for this delta.
- **Disposition:** the shared default is now `staleTime: 0`; the in-memory cache remains enabled for rendering, deduplication, and offline behavior. Explicit per-query lifetimes remain and require their own freshness/invalidation contract. The focused policy regression passes (1/1), targeted ESLint and `git diff --check` pass. Added remount request volume/API p95 was not measured; no record matrix cells are promoted.

## Root-cause follow-up 814 — dashboard attention and badge snapshots had explicit stale windows

- **Evidence:** `DashboardAttentionList` explicitly set `staleTime: 30_000`, overriding the newly corrected shared default. A red-first component regression seeded a fresh Action Center response and remounted the widget; it displayed the cached heading but made zero authorized API calls. `useBadges` separately set `staleTime: 15_000`; its red-first hook regression observed 15000 instead of the near-live target of zero.
- **Impact:** if a refresh signal was missed while these consumers were inactive, Action Center tasks and sidebar counts could remain cached as fresh on return, leaving badges and attention cues behind current server state.
- **Disposition:** both queries now set `staleTime: 0`, preserving in-memory caching and their existing after-commit event invalidation. The focused dashboard, badge, and shared-query tests pass (10/10); targeted ESLint and `git diff --check` pass. No two-user event, latency, or added-request-rate measurement was performed; related matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Root-cause follow-up 815 — customer portal invoice signals stayed fresh for 30 seconds

- **Evidence:** `PortalLayout` explicitly configured a 30-second `staleTime` for the customer invoice-signal count query. A red-first component regression seeded a current cached response and mounted the customer portal; after the portal event-hook boundary was isolated, the regression showed that the API was not called.
- **Impact:** if an invoice signal arrived while the portal was disconnected or its refresh signal was missed, a returning customer could continue seeing an old unpaid/overdue count until that explicit freshness window expired.
- **Disposition:** the portal query now uses `staleTime: 0`, retains the in-memory cache, and continues to receive the customer-authorized invoice signal API plus existing event invalidation. Focused SPA tests pass 13/13 across PortalLayout, dashboard attention, badge, and query-client freshness; targeted ESLint and `git diff --check` pass. This does not prove peer WebSocket delivery or performance; matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Root-cause follow-up 816 — chain-bottleneck updates lacked a shared source signal

- **Evidence:** `ChainBottleneckService` computes 16 detector families from SalesOrder, WorkOrder, Inspection, Delivery, Invoice, PurchaseRequest, Bill, GRN, WorkOrderOutput, StockMovement, ReturnRequest, CustomerComplaint and PayrollPeriod rows, plus `GrnItem` and `ReturnRequestItem` existence dependencies. `ChainBottleneckController` also joins current `ApprovalRecord` rows to derive the `next_approver` audience, while the widget's automation summary reads `ChainListenerRun`. The widget had only a one-minute periodic query. The invalidation map already named `chain_bottlenecks_changed`, but no backend producer or matching listener/channel existed; Finance's old `.chain.refresh` listener did not match a bottleneck event producer. A second source trace found `ComplaintService::markNcrHandoffManual()` changes a detector source using a query-builder update that bypasses model observers, then only staged the record-specific complaint event.
- **Impact:** viewers could retain old counts/statuses until the next minute refresh after a relevant write, including viewers unable to join a source record channel. Wall-clock SLA crossings also need periodic recomputation when no row changes.
- **Disposition:** mapped model writes now stage a transaction-coalesced, content-free `ChainBottlenecksChanged` through the durable outbox after commit. The query-builder complaint handoff now calls the same cache invalidation/source-signal observer explicitly. The dedicated private channel requires an active user and the same `dashboard.view_bottlenecks` permission as the API. Observer registration includes the detector sources, line-item dependencies, `ApprovalRecord`, and `ChainListenerRun` automation-health rows. The widget listens through `useRealtimeSignal` and the central invalidation map; `staleTime` is zero. The 60-second periodic recomputation remains for SLA threshold crossings without a write. Focused source, writer, channel authorization, broadcast inventory, invalidation-map and widget tests pass; PHPUnit reports the known `/var/www/.env` warning. No live socket/two-user trace or latency sample was run, so matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Root-cause follow-up 817 — explicit per-query TTLs bypassed live-data remount revalidation

- **Evidence:** `KpiStrip` set a five-minute `staleTime` despite the shared zero-stale default and existing KPI event/invalidation map. A red-first component regression seeded a scorecard at 70%, isolated its realtime hook, and observed no `kpiApi.scorecard` call on mount; the old value rendered. This confirms the per-query override bypassed the global default.
- **Impact:** other mapped live query families with positive page-level `staleTime` could likewise remain considered fresh after a missed event while unmounted.
- **Disposition:** QueryClient's central `refetchOnMount` policy derives from the entity, aggregate-signal and cache-group invalidation maps and returns `always` for matching keys despite positive `staleTime`. Option/look-up keys remain on their existing TTLs. The in-memory result is preserved and replaced by the authorized API response, but can remain visible while that request is pending. Focused KPI/query-policy tests pass 6/6; targeted ESLint and SPA typecheck pass. Request-rate/API-p95 impact, live peer delivery and matrix outcomes remain unmeasured/`NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Root-cause follow-up 818 — Admin audit-log queries had no realtime producer or subscriber

- **Evidence:** `AuditLog` is the shared append-only sink for model audit traits and direct service creates. Both Admin audit-log query families lacked a realtime hook and central invalidation entry. A red-first focused API test created two audit rows and confirmed no audit-log outbox signal existed.
- **Impact:** authorized audit-log viewers could retain a prior list or trail until remount/manual refresh even when the source business mutation committed successfully.
- **Disposition:** `AuditLog::created` now stages one transaction-coalesced outbox ping. The private `admin.audit-logs` callback requires active `admin.audit_logs.view`, the same grant used by the API; event data is limited to refresh metadata. The list and entity-trail pages invalidate `['admin','audit-logs']` and refetch through authenticated endpoints. Producer/payload test passes 7 assertions and SPA map test passes 1/1. Focused Broadcast auth test passes with Reverb configured and channels registered; it verifies matching permission allow, unrelated permission deny, and inactive-user rejection. No two-user delivery or matrix outcome is claimed.

### 383. Employee DTR query was omitted from attendance event invalidation (confirmed and fixed; one browser retest passed)

The original HR Officer correction committed and broadcast the existing attendance_changed signal. The already-open Employee DTR stayed stale because the central invalidation map omitted the DTR page's self-service query prefix. The original HR03-MISSING-PUNCH trace observed the stale row for eight seconds while a read-only database check confirmed the corrected values.

The central map now includes that prefix, and a red-first regression fails before and passes after the change. HR03-MISSING-PUNCH attempt 08 then passed in separate Employee and HR browser contexts: the Employee's open DTR showed 14:00 and 7.5 hours without reload, and the explicit reload step retained the values. The outbox held one published AttendanceChanged create and one published update; the dedicated broadcast worker completed both. The interval from the HR correction step's completion to the Employee observation step's completion was 1.654 seconds, one sample and not p95. A raw browser WebSocket frame was not archived, so the UI/outbox correlation is recorded without claiming direct frame capture. Only the Employee DTR and HR list surfaces are promoted; separate record-detail, action-button, linked-record, badge, dashboard, and approval-inbox behavior remains unverified.



### 384. Critical alert email status could stay stale after claim or delivery outcome (confirmed and fixed)

A focused red-first feature regression reproduced the missing refresh: AlertEngineService::claimCriticalEmail() updated the visible alert email_status to sending, and updateCriticalEmailDeliveryState() wrote sent, failed, or terminal_failed through the query builder. The Alert observer deliberately skips email-only changes. Before the fix, the failed-delivery scenario produced only the alert-creation BadgesChanged outbox row; neither claim nor final outcome generated a refresh. The Alerts page renders failed and terminal-failed delivery status, so a viewer could keep the old row until a manual refresh or another invalidation.

The claim and final outcome now explicitly call BadgeService::touch() and record a content-free BadgesChanged outbox signal in the same transaction as each status write. Badge and shared dashboard generations rotate after commit; the outbox dispatcher publishes after commit on the existing authorized per-user channel, and the central invalidation map refetches alerts through each viewer's API. The selected API regression passes 5 assertions, including exactly one ping for the claim and one for the failure outcome (PHPUnit marks the run WARN because it intentionally exercises/logs delivery failure and the existing container warning). The status/transition CSV now includes the default state and six delivery edges. No scheduled execution or separate-user browser test was run; all runtime matrix cells remain NOT_VERIFIED.

## Root-cause follow-up 849 — legacy broadcast auth bypassed the inactive-session guard

- **Evidence:** The live `route:list --path=broadcasting/auth -v` showed Laravel's `/broadcasting/auth` compatibility route had only the `web` middleware group. The generic `entity.{entityType}.{scope}` callback grants a private refresh subscription from feature and permission checks but does not repeat `is_active`. The existing `SessionTimeout` inactive-account guard is in the API middleware group, so it did not run on that compatibility route. A focused regression against an inactive user with both Work Order list permissions reproduced HTTP 200; the active-user control also received 200.
- **Impact:** An inactive user whose existing web session had not yet been invalidated could continue subscribing to generic private entity refresh pings through the alternate endpoint. Payloads remain content-free and each subsequent API read is separately authorized, but subscription access still violated the active-principal rule and exposed change timing.
- **Disposition:** `bootstrap/app.php` now registers Laravel's compatibility route via `withBroadcasting()` using `api` and `auth:sanctum`; the existing global `SessionTimeout` and password checks run before channel authorization. The focused test passes (one warning-marked test, two assertions), preserving active access and returning 401 to the inactive principal. Runtime route listing confirms the middleware stack. The SPA's `/api/v1/broadcasting/auth` and B2B-specific routes are unchanged. No matrix outcome changed; no direct frame or two-user scenario was run.

### Delta 858 — live HTTP-cache and broadcast-worker snapshot

The active dev site returned HTTP 200 for `/` (`no-cache`) and `/sw.js` (`no-store, no-cache`; Cloudflare `BYPASS`). An unauthenticated request to `/api/v1/auth/user` returned 401 with `private, no-store` and `Vary: Cookie, Authorization`; health returned 200 with `no-cache, private`. Horizon was running and the dedicated `redis_broadcasts:broadcasts` queue reported zero pending, delayed, or reserved jobs. A current source scan found 127 broadcast event classes, all with after-commit and dedicated-queue markers; none used `ShouldBroadcastNow`, and the scan found no `new Channel` or `new PresenceChannel` constructors in event files. This does not verify authenticated 200 headers at the edge, Reverb frames, subscriptions, or UI freshness; no matrix cells changed.

### Delta 859 — stale Horizon telemetry reappeared in retired Redis DB 0

Redis DB 0 contained 11 keys under the legacy `ogami_erp_horizon:*` prefix. Every key was idle for over two hours, DB 0 contained no other key, and current API/Horizon config resolves the active default connection to DB 2; no application client was connected to DB 0. The exact prefix/count/database-size guarded `UNLINK` removed those 11 operational telemetry keys. DB 0 is empty afterward, Horizon is still running, the broadcast queue is empty, and the cache audit remains clean. This is not a business-response cache or user-visible stale-data root cause, but the old telemetry writer is unidentified and recurrence needs monitoring. Queue, payload-cache, generation, session, and business data were preserved.

### Delta 861 — isolated E2E containers respawned after the user asked to stop testing

Twelve `ogami-erp-e2e` containers reappeared without an observable Playwright/runner process. Their restart policies were disabled and only that Compose project was stopped; its volumes and the 10-service development stack were preserved. `ogamiph.dev` and API health returned HTTP 200 afterward. No E2E result is counted. The launcher is still unknown, so the stopped stack could reappear again.

### Delta 863 — portal notification refresh publication was not durable

- **Evidence:** `PortalNotificationController` updated/deleted the authenticated principal's notification rows, then used `DB::afterCommit()` to dispatch `PortalNotificationChanged` directly. `NotificationService::sendPortalInApp()` similarly dispatched created-notification signals after commit. The event was absent from `OutboxEventCodec`, so no durable retry record existed if the process stopped between the transaction commit and its callback. A focused read-action regression failed because the committed change had no `event_outbox` row.
- **Impact:** a portal viewer's other tabs could miss inbox refresh after a committed read/delete/create if direct post-commit dispatch was interrupted; the API-owned notification row remained correct and authorized.
- **Disposition:** `PortalNotificationChanged` is now in the allowlisted codec. `NotificationService` stages create events inside the same transaction as inserted notification rows; the controller stages single/bulk read/delete events inside the corresponding write transaction via `NotificationService`. The existing outbox dispatcher queues broadcast publication after commit and scheduled recovery remains available. Recipient realm, exact private channel, event name and refresh-only payload are preserved. Five selected feature cases pass (27 assertions); syntax checks pass. No portal two-user browser delivery, latency sample, or matrix cell promotion occurred.
