### Delta 864 — internal notification updates still use after-commit event dispatch

`UserNotificationService` wraps read/read-all/delete/clear-read writes in transactions and dispatches `UserNotificationChanged`; `NotificationService::send()` dispatches `UserNotificationCreated` from a post-commit callback. Both events use the authenticated user's private channel, minimal payloads, `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`, and the dedicated broadcast queue. Neither event is currently in `OutboxEventCodec`, so those internal inbox pings have no durable outbox recovery record if the process stops after commit and before queue publication. This is a confirmed source-level reliability gap, not a reproduced stale-screen cause: no app-process interruption or lost internal-notification frame was observed, and the shared provider has reconnect/focus catch-up. No code was changed because preserving the existing event assertions and publication behavior requires a deliberate migration of the notification feature tests; no test or matrix outcome changed in this documentation pass.

### Delta 865 — internal notification refresh publication now uses the durable outbox

The Delta 864 gap is fixed. `UserNotificationChanged` and `UserNotificationCreated` are now in the explicit outbox codec. Internal notification rows and create-event rows commit together; read/read-all/delete/clear actions stage their refresh row in the same transaction as the state change. After commit, the outbox dispatcher emits the existing event onto the dedicated broadcast queue. The acting call still observes the same event after its transaction, while a process interruption leaves a pending outbox row for scheduled recovery. A red-first regression failed because the created notification had no outbox entry. The first implementation made a 25-recipient send issue 227 SQL queries; bounded batch insert/claim/publish now satisfies the five-query ceiling asserted by the existing performance regression. Three selected feature tests and two selected hardening tests pass (19 assertions total), and PHP syntax checks pass for the touched files. This confirms create/read producer and compatibility behavior only; no two-user browser delivery, broadcast outage recovery, latency sample, or matrix promotion was run.

### Delta 866 — restore and cache-group completion use durable publication

- **Restore evidence:** source tracing found successful restore and rollback finish cache-generation invalidation, clear maintenance and resume queues before `BackupService::dispatchDatabaseRestoreCompleted()`. That helper directly dispatched the reload event; if the post-restore dispatch failed, it logged critical but wrote no durable retry record. The event was also missing from `OutboxEventCodec`, so the common outbox could not recover it. No destructive restore was run and no client-side failure was reproduced; this is a confirmed source-level reliability gap, not a confirmed cause of the reported stale screens.
- **Cache-group evidence:** `CacheGroupInvalidationService` rotated cache generations and directly dispatched `CacheGroupInvalidated` after its audit transaction. A red-first feature regression confirmed the action left no `event_outbox` row.
- **Disposition:** `BackupService` records `DatabaseRestoreCompleted` through `OutboxService` with an operation-specific dedupe key and after-commit inline outbox publication. `CacheGroupInvalidationService` now inserts its deduplicated event row atomically with the audit row and publishes it after the earlier registered generation-rotation callback, retaining its allowlisted groups, private channel and payload. The explicit codec allowlist supports recovery for both. The 127-class source scan now has one intentional outbox exclusion: `PayrollProgressEvent` is ephemeral progress telemetry backed by a period-scoped one-hour snapshot and authorized API catch-up; all durable refresh broadcasters are codec-supported. The restore codec regression passes (1 test / 4 assertions), cache-group producer/outbox passes (1 test / 7 assertions), and channel authorization passes (1 test / 8 assertions); PHP syntax and targeted Pint checks pass. No restore/rollback, two-user reload, worker outage, or matrix UI result was tested; matrix counts remain unchanged.

Delta 852 reran the final test method name `test_inactive_user_cannot_authorize_generic_entity_channel_via_legacy_endpoint`; it passed with 2 assertions and one PHPUnit warning. This closes the test-rename verification gap only, not broader cross-role socket/UI coverage.


### 385. Stock Count refresh signal omitted ordering and actor metadata (source-confirmed contract gap; user-visible impact not reproduced)

`StockCountRealtimeService` already staged a coalesced, after-commit `StockCountChanged` outbox event with the session hash, action and model timestamp. Its `broadcastWith()` discarded those fields and sent only `refresh` and `event_id`. This meant Stock Count subscribers could refetch, but the signal did not satisfy the shared event envelope or provide event-level version/actor diagnostics. The private detail/list channels and API authorization were already in place. No two-user/browser trace tied this omission to a reported stale screen, so it is not claimed as a reproduced user-visible cause.

The event now emits the shared minimal envelope with session entity type/hash, action, version, pseudonymous actor hash, event ID, related refs and timestamp. Count values, variances, lots and notes remain API-only. New constructor fields are optional and follow the existing event ID argument so previously stored outbox payloads still decode. The focused contract regression exists but was not run under the instruction to stop tests; PHP syntax, direct payload construction, legacy outbox decode/current roundtrip and whitespace checks passed. Delivery, ordering behavior in a browser and matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 386. Four Inventory refresh events omitted common envelope metadata (source-confirmed contract gap; no stale UI reproduction)

`StockAdjustmentChanged`, `ItemCostReviewChanged`, `MaterialIssueSlipChanged`, and `MaterialReviewRecordChanged` each broadcast a content-free refresh signal with entity/action/version/actor and event ID, but omitted the shared `related` and `timestamp` fields. Their private channels and authorized API refetch behavior were already present. Source review confirms the `material_issue_slip` invalidation map includes `['inventory','reservations']`; an older payload-audit note claiming this prefix was omitted was stale and is corrected. No cross-user stale-screen reproduction was established for the missing envelope fields.

All four events now use `BroadcastEventEnvelope::refresh()` and append an optional timestamp constructor parameter after the existing event ID, preserving decoding of stored outbox rows. Channel names and payload content boundaries are unchanged. One focused contract test failed before the edit with `Unknown named parameter $timestamp`, then passed with 24 assertions; PHPUnit emitted its existing warning that `/var/www/.env` is absent in the container. The test did not use the database. No peer delivery or matrix result was promoted.


### 387. Inventory stock aggregate ping omitted the shared refresh envelope (source-confirmed contract gap; user-visible impact not reproduced)

`InventoryStockChanged` reached private inventory, warehouse, item, work-order, maintenance and NCR audiences after commit, but its payload contained only event ID, aggregate type, action, version and `occurred_at`. It omitted `refresh`, `entity_id`, `actor_id`, `related` and the common `timestamp` key. This is an aggregate signal spanning multiple mutation sources, so it has no single record or actor; nullable IDs and empty related references are intentional. The existing SPA handlers use the event as an invalidation ping and do not consume stock data from the payload. No stale screen was dynamically reproduced from these missing metadata fields.

The event now uses `BroadcastEventEnvelope::refresh()` with `entity_id: null`, `actor_id: null`, and an empty related list. It retains the old `occurred_at` field as an alias for compatibility. A focused contract test failed before the fix on the missing envelope keys. The final selected Inventory envelope/GRN contract check passes 3 tests / 51 assertions. PHPUnit reports the known container warning that `/var/www/.env` is absent; the tests do not use the database. Delivery and matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 388. GRN, item, transfer-order and warehouse-layout events omitted shared entity metadata (source-confirmed contract gap; user-visible impact not reproduced)

`GoodsReceiptNoteChanged` and `ItemChanged` omitted entity type/ID and timestamp; both held numeric actor IDs internally but did not publish an opaque actor hash. `TransferOrderChanged` used `hash_id` without actor or timestamp metadata, while `WarehouseLayoutChanged` lacked entity/actor fields and an explicit `related` list. The events were already after-commit on the dedicated queue, and their private channels/refetch handlers were in place. The GRN listener checks `entity_type === 'grn'` for supplier receipt invalidation, so the new envelope deliberately retains that established type. TransferOrderChanged retains its old `hash_id` field alongside `entity_id`. No stale UI reproduction was made.

These four events now use `BroadcastEventEnvelope::refresh()`. Actor hashes are sourced from the authenticated user; internal numeric IDs are not broadcast. Item catalog and warehouse-layout aggregate pings use null entity IDs. Optional constructor fields were appended to keep stored outbox payloads decodable. A focused test failed before implementation on the missing actor-hash constructor argument. The final selected Inventory envelope/GRN contract check passes 3 tests / 51 assertions; the test container reports its existing missing `/var/www/.env` warning. No database or browser scenario ran, and matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 389. Purchasing RFQ and supplier-catalog pings omitted standard event metadata (source-confirmed contract gap; stale UI impact not reproduced)

The internal `RequestForQuoteChanged` event carried only refresh/event/action/version, omitting its opaque RFQ ID, actor hash, related refs and timestamp. `SupplierCatalogChanged` carried only action/version/event ID even though it targets both internal catalog lists and vendor-private channels. `SupplierRfqChanged` included `hash_id` but omitted `entity_id`, actor and related refs. All three already used after-commit durable outbox publication and private channels. Vendor-facing signals deliberately receive a null actor ID because an internal actor identifier is not needed or appropriate for the external audience; their channel routing hashes are not included in the wire payload. No stale screen was dynamically reproduced from these metadata gaps.

The three events now use the shared envelope. Internal RFQ changes include an opaque actor hash and RFQ hash; supplier catalog uses an aggregate type with null entity/actor IDs; vendor RFQ keeps null actor plus both `entity_id` and its legacy `hash_id` alias. Existing event names, private channels, sealed-quote API boundaries and old outbox constructor decoding remain intact. The focused RFQ/catalog test failed before the change on missing constructor fields and now passes with the selected two-test contract check (13 assertions); warnings only report the missing `/var/www/.env` test-container file. No database or browser flow ran; matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 390. Supplier performance refresh omitted the shared related-reference field (source-confirmed contract gap; stale UI impact not reproduced)

`SupplierPerformanceChanged` already carried a content-free refresh, opaque vendor ID when scoped, action/version, opaque actor hash and timestamp. It omitted `related`, so its wire shape differed from the common envelope. The batch event intentionally uses a null entity ID because it refreshes a period-wide snapshot; the per-vendor event uses the vendor hash. Its producer already rotates purchasing dashboard generations before after-commit outbox publication. No user-visible stale screen was reproduced from the missing empty related list.

The event now uses `BroadcastEventEnvelope::refresh()` and emits `related: []`; no query semantics, channels, cache keys or payload business data changed. The focused supplier event contract failed before the fix and passed afterward as part of the two selected Purchasing signal tests (16 assertions total); the container reports its known missing `/var/www/.env` warning. No database/browser scenario ran, and matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 391. Quality inspection, NCR and record pings omitted common refresh metadata (source-confirmed contract gap; stale UI impact not reproduced)

`InspectionChanged` already carried inspection/actor hashes, action, version and timestamp but omitted the shared empty `related` field. `NcrChanged` and `QualityRecordChanged` sent only refresh/event/action/version, omitting entity type/hash, actor hash and timestamp; their constructor's numeric actor ID remained server-side. Private record/list channels and authorized refetch handlers were already wired. NCR's linked complaint and work-order refreshes are independently staged by the same mutation service. No stale UI was dynamically reproduced from these omissions.

All three now use `BroadcastEventEnvelope::refresh()`. The numeric actor IDs remain internal; only authenticated opaque user hashes can be broadcast. Optional actor/timestamp fields were appended for outbox compatibility. The selected Quality contract checks pass (4 tests / 19 assertions), including decoding the legacy inspection/NCR/quality payloads; PHPUnit emits only the known missing `/var/www/.env` warning. No database or browser scenario ran, and matrix results remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 392. Production, MRP and Maintenance record pings omitted common envelope fields (source-confirmed contract gap; no stale UI reproduction)

`MachineChanged`, `MoldChanged`, `MrpPlanChanged`, `MrpRunChanged`, `ProductionScheduleChanged`, `WorkOrderAssignmentChanged`, `WorkOrderSchedulingDiagnosticChanged`, `ProductionRoutingChanged`, and `MaintenanceWorkOrderChanged` already carried private refresh signals and entity/action/version metadata, but several used only `hash_id` and omitted `entity_id`; most also omitted an empty `related` field. The production schedule, assignment, and maintenance work-order events retain their current private channel sets and authorized API refetch behavior. MRP Plan and Production Routing already identify their related Sales Order/Product by opaque hashes. Source review found no payload values consumed by the UI; no stale screen was reproduced from the missing envelope fields.

These nine payloads now use `BroadcastEventEnvelope::refresh()` and include the standard `entity_id` and `related` fields while retaining their prior `hash_id` aliases and existing related references. Event names, channels, constructor order, and API-only field boundaries are unchanged. A focused contract regression failed before the fix on missing `entity_id`, then passed with 83 assertions across the new envelope case and existing production-schedule channel/payload assertions. No database or browser scenario ran; delivery and route-matrix outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The intentionally ping-only Production operation, assigned-work-order, late-report and Maintenance schedule events remain content-free because their audiences span different record/API scopes.


### 393. Inventory pending-issue widget viewers lacked an authorized change signal (source-confirmed propagation gap; runtime symptom not reproduced)

`MaterialIssueSlip` writes already retired the `inventory.pending_issues` dashboard cache generation after commit. Its record/list event targeted `entity.material_issue_slip.visible`, whose authorization requires `inventory.issue.create`; the dashboard widget is granted to `inventory.view` users. Those inventory readers therefore could fetch the aggregate but could not subscribe to the event that announced its change. The default and role dashboard pages still poll widget data every 60 seconds, which could mask this gap. Source and permission mapping confirm the audience mismatch; no two-user browser trace established a stale widget on screen.

The original narrow fix emitted an Inventory-only widget ping. Follow-up source review found the same audience mismatch could apply to any configurable widget whose viewer lacks the source record channel. Delta 892 replaces that one-off path with a central signal staged by `DashboardCacheGeneration` for all widget-summary/widget-rich generations; details and verification are recorded in root-cause follow-up #394.

### 394. Widget generation changes had no central real-time signal (source-confirmed propagation gap; UI symptom not reproduced)

`DashboardWidgetDataService` caches each configurable summary under `widget-summary:{key}`, while rich payloads use `widget-rich:{key}`. `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` maps many business models to generation rotations, and some services rotate these scopes directly. Before this change those cache writes did not guarantee a signal to dashboard viewers; only selected model-specific dashboard events existed. A user could therefore have permission to read a widget but lack permission for the source entity channel and retain a stale tile until the 30-second data TTL or dashboard polling refresh.

`DashboardCacheGeneration::touch()` now stages `DashboardWidgetDataChanged` for every global, summary, and rich generation rotation through the durable, transaction-coalesced outbox. The event broadcasts only a null-entity/null-actor refresh envelope on `dashboard.widgets`; authorization uses `DashboardLayoutService::listAvailableWidgets()` so it follows the catalog's current permission visibility rule. The central SPA invalidation map refreshes widget data and the inventory dashboard through each viewer's authorized API. Layout data has its own `dashboard_layout_changed` signal, so source-data pings no longer refetch the unrelated layout query (Delta 950). The old Inventory-only widget event was removed. Focused red-first unit, channel-authorization, material-issue outbox and SPA map checks were added; the global-generation case is also covered (Delta 896). No WebSocket frame, two-user rendered latency, or whole-dashboard UI result is claimed; associated matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 395. Broadcast event payload shapes did not share the required refresh envelope (source-confirmed contract gap; stale UI impact not reproduced)

The 128-row `00-broadcast-payload-audit.csv` showed only 33 source `broadcastWith()` payloads included all nine standard refresh fields. Several aggregate and legacy notifications omitted `entity_id`, `actor_id`, `related`, or `timestamp`; some still used `hash_id` or `related_entities`. Consumers mostly invalidate and refetch, so this mismatch did not itself reproduce a stale record screen, but it left the requested event identity/version contract dependent on individual event implementations.

`StandardizedPusherBroadcaster` now completes the refresh envelope immediately before every configured Reverb/Pusher send. It preserves the event name, channels, and existing payload fields; fills absent values from content-free aliases or server time; converts only string opaque identifiers into standard IDs; and filters numeric/malformed `entity_id`, `actor_id`, `entity_ids`, and related references. A source check of installed Reverb 1.x showed that `max_message_size` applies to client WebSocket frames, while the server's HTTP parser enforces `max_request_size` on the complete Pusher request buffer. The first guard used the former setting and would not protect outbound broadcast requests. The Reverb driver now estimates the Pusher event request body, reserves 1 KiB for the HTTP request line/headers, and rejects above `reverb.servers.reverb.max_request_size` before sending. The Pusher fallback receives the shared envelope but does not use the Reverb-specific limit. This covers all current `ShouldBroadcast` event classes when sent through Reverb without changing individual dispatch sites. A focused test proves normalization, legacy related-reference handling, batched opaque-ID filtering, the configured Reverb broadcaster, the final Pusher payload, and the request-limit rejection (6 selected cases / 15 assertions; PHPUnit reports six warnings because `/var/www/.env` is absent in the container). No production traffic sample, oversized-event observation, raw browser frame, multi-user UI result, or latency sample was captured; this confirms the configured guard contract, not peer-delivery proof.


### 396. Decision Center mutation responses were discarded before detail reconciliation (reproduced actor-side freshness bug)

`dssApi.act()` and `dssApi.outcome()` return the updated `Recommendation`. In `RecommendationDetails.tsx`, both TanStack mutation `onSuccess` callbacks previously ignored the return value, showed a toast, and invalidated the broad `['dss']` query family. The route's detail query is `['dss', 'recommendations', id]`; while that authorized refetch was pending, React continued rendering the previously cached detail and its client-derived action buttons. A focused component regression reproduced this deterministically by returning `status: accepted` from the action API and holding the subsequent `dssApi.show()` response: before the fix the acting user's detail still showed “New” and “Accept.”

Both handlers now seed the exact detail query with the API response before preserving the existing broad invalidation, so the actor immediately sees the server response without relying on Echo. The regression passes with the refetch still held and observes the updated “Acknowledged” state, removal of “Accept,” and appearance of “Mark as executed.” This establishes only the local actor response-reconciliation path for Decision Center actions. It does not establish other mutations, cross-user channel delivery, multi-user latency, or any other matrix cell beyond the focused actor/button partial evidence; those remain unverified.


### 397. Decision Center action eligibility was inferred in the SPA instead of returned by the API (source-confirmed contract gap; focused lifecycle verified)

`RecommendationResource` returned recommendation status but omitted `permissions` and `available_actions`. `RecommendationDetails.tsx` derived accept, dismiss, snooze, execute, follow-up and outcome controls from locally interpreted status, expiry and snooze timestamps. The mutation service already re-locks the row and validates each action, so this was an API/UI state projection gap rather than an authorization bypass. It could make controls drift from server policy as policy rules evolve, and stale cached status directly determined which action remained visible.

The resource now returns `permissions.view` / `permissions.act` and six server-computed action flags. Eligibility follows the existing action preconditions, including expiry and due-snooze behavior; `RecommendationService::act()` continues to lock and revalidate the state on every request. The SPA renders decision and outcome controls only from those flags, retaining the existing snooze-date validation. The lifecycle feature test first failed because the permission projection was absent, then passed open → accepted → executed action projections (1 selected test / 31 assertions; PHPUnit emitted its known missing `/var/www/.env` warning). A component regression first failed because an open status still showed Accept when the API set every action false; the selected actor/action component tests now pass (2 selected, 1 skipped). Targeted ESLint passes. `npx tsc --noEmit` was stopped after roughly one minute without diagnostics to keep verification bounded; no typecheck result is claimed. No live API/browser, second user, channel delivery, or latency sample was captured; DSS peer and remaining matrix cells stay unverified.


### 398. Quality Calibration controls were not based on server-projected permissions/actions (source-confirmed contract gap; focused API/UI contract verified)

`CalibrationController::index()` returned the paginated records without a collection-level create permission. `CalibrationRecordResource` exposed neither current-viewer permissions nor available actions, while the SPA used local `usePermission()` checks for New/Edit/Record controls. That left this page outside the server-computed action contract and made its visible controls depend on a separate client permission interpretation. The service continues to enforce its existing policies and locks each update/record operation; this change does not alter those business rules.

The list response now includes `permissions.create`; each resource includes `permissions.view/manage` and `available_actions.update/record_calibration` computed for the authenticated viewer. The SPA renders the New and row actions from those response flags, and the record modal checks the latest listed row's action flag before submission. The focused API regression failed first on the missing create projection, then passed for a manager and view-only account (1 selected test / 10 assertions; known missing-container-`.env` warning). The focused component regression failed first when server-authorized controls were hidden by a denied client permission hook, then passed (1 test); targeted ESLint and PHP syntax checks passed. No live API/browser, second viewer, WebSocket receipt, or latency sample was captured. The route-matrix outcomes remain unchanged and unverified.


### 399. PPAP responses omitted viewer permissions and action eligibility (source-confirmed API contract gap; focused API contract verified)

`PpapSubmissionResource` returned status and checklist content without current-viewer permissions or available actions, and the PPAP index response omitted create permission. The service already validates each operation under its existing transaction and lock: submit requires Draft plus an element; review/approve require Submitted or Under Review and a non-submitter; approval additionally requires every element accepted or not applicable; updates are blocked for Approved submissions. The missing projection meant clients could not render the server's current decision from the same representation they refetch.

The index now includes viewer-scoped `permissions.create`; submissions expose `permissions.view/manage` and action flags derived from the existing service preconditions and loaded elements; child elements expose viewer/manage permissions and a viewer-scoped update flag using an eagerly loaded parent relation to avoid per-element queries. No service state transition or authorization rule changed. A focused API regression first failed because `permissions.create` was absent, then failed on missing child action/view fields, then passed for a manager and view-only user (1 test / 16 assertions; PHPUnit emitted one environment warning). Source search found no PPAP route/page under `spa/src`, so no button rendering or browser consumer can be verified here. The seven PPAP route rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED` for actor/peer UI, list, related, badge, dashboard, and inbox behavior; the matrix now records the absent SPA consumer as the reason. No two-user delivery or latency sample was collected.


### 400. Purchase Request detail used a legacy action-field name (API/UI contract gap fixed; freshness behavior not newly reproduced)

`PurchaseRequestResource` already computed per-viewer action eligibility through `PurchaseRequestAccessPolicy::actionsFor()`, but returned it only as `actions`, unlike the canonical `available_actions` response field used by the real-time UI contract. The Purchase Request detail was coupled to that legacy field and also repeated status checks around some buttons. A focused API regression failed because the detail response had no `available_actions`; after the change it verifies that the canonical field is present and exactly matches the existing policy result. The resource computes the policy once, emits the same flags under both keys for compatibility, and the detail controls now render solely from `available_actions`. Existing service/policy checks still validate every mutation. This confirms the response/UI contract path only: no pre-change stale screen, new multi-user event, or latency failure was reproduced, so existing Purchase Request matrix outcomes remain unchanged.


### 401. Purchase Order detail exposed server actions only through the legacy field (API/UI contract gap fixed; no new peer reproduction)

`PurchaseOrderResource` already called `PurchaseOrderAccessPolicy::actionsFor()` for route-scoped detail responses and returned `null` for list rows, but used only the noncanonical `actions` field. The detail already rendered lifecycle buttons from that map, while one short-close status condition and the PDF control were outside the map. The existing API regression was changed to require the canonical field and equality with the compatibility field; it failed before implementation because `available_actions` was absent. The resource now computes the existing map once and exposes both keys; the detail consumes the canonical map for lifecycle, short-close, print and vendor-segregation messaging, and removes the redundant Draft status check around the update control. The focused API contract passes (1 test / 11 assertions; one known PHPUnit environment warning), and changed-file ESLint, PHP syntax, and live Vite transformation pass. No business eligibility or server-side action rule changed. This is contract evidence only; no new actor/peer browser trace or latency result exists, so matrix outcomes remain unchanged.


### 402. RFQ actions were exposed through a legacy field and consumed by three pages (API/UI contract gap fixed; delivery remains unverified)

`RequestForQuoteResource` computed actions from `RequestForQuoteAccessPolicy`, but named the projection only `actions`; detail, comparison and manual-quote pages all read that key. The policy already encodes the RFQ state and permission preconditions, and the service revalidates them under its existing write path. A focused workflow regression failed when it asserted `available_actions.can_close_now` on the buyer detail response. The resource now creates one conditional projection and exposes it under both canonical `available_actions` and legacy `actions`, preserving omission when the invitation relation is not loaded. The three SPA consumers use the canonical field; redundant Open-state checks were removed where the server action already captures that precondition. The selected supplier-to-draft-PO workflow now passes (1 test / 50 assertions; one known PHPUnit environment warning), with changed-file ESLint, PHP syntax, and live Vite transformation passing. No business transition changed, and no two-user browser, event delivery, or latency result was added; RFQ matrix outcomes remain unchanged.
### 403. Return Management approval controls used the legacy action field (API/UI contract gap fixed; live stale screen not reproduced)

`ReturnRequestResource` computed whether the authenticated viewer may decide the current approval step, including current-role and submitter checks, but exposed the result only as `actions`. The detail page coupled approval button visibility to that legacy key and a second browser-side permission check. The focused API regression failed because `available_actions` was absent; it now confirms the canonical and compatibility maps agree for both an unauthorized role and the assigned finance approver. A focused SPA regression failed when the server canonical flags were true and the legacy flags false, then passed after the page switched its approval controls to `available_actions`. The same approval decision is reused for both response keys. Delta 915 also adds server `can_cancel` eligibility to both keys and loads the approval relation on every RMA mutation response. Delta 916 moved Submit/Receive/Inspect/Dispose/Complete, Edit Draft, Quality handoff retry, and request-level Split visibility to server flags. The Dispose projection suppresses known missing/nonterminal return-inspection evidence. Exact per-line Split preconditions and disposition-specific Quality eligibility are still revalidated by the service and are not completely represented by the summary flags. No peer delivery or latency sample was captured. Return Management matrix outcomes remain unchanged.


### 404. Return Management showed Cancel after physical receipt (reproduced UI/action mismatch; cancellation rule preserved)

The detail page offered Cancel for every `approved` RMA when the viewer had `return_management.manage`, but `ReturnRequestService::cancel()` rejects cancellation after `received_at`, a receipt/reversal, or stock movement effects. Thus an RMA could retain an actionable Cancel button after receipt even though every action attempt was rejected by the existing locked server guard. A red-first component regression reproduced the visible button when the API action flag was false; a red-first API regression found the detail response had no cancellation flag. The service now exposes the same status/physical-effects eligibility predicate to the resource and retains it in the mutation guard. The API computes viewer-scoped `available_actions.can_cancel`; the detail hides Cancel unless that server flag is true. Mutation responses load the relations required to recompute the flags for the acting user. The final selected API run passes the unauthorized/current approver, submit response, and post-receipt cases (3 tests / 36 assertions, with existing container/fixture warnings); focused UI checks pass (2 selected component cases). No business rule changed. This is component/API evidence, not a two-user browser/socket reproduction; all route matrix runtime outcomes remain unchanged.


### 405. Return Management workflow buttons trusted local state instead of the API action projection (reproduced UI/API contract mismatch; main controls fixed in Delta 916)

The detail page built Submit/Receive/Inspect/Dispose/Complete controls from the locally held status and SPA permission snapshot; Edit Draft and Quality retry did the same. The API had no canonical flags for those actions, so a client could retain/show a button from a stale status snapshot even when the server would refuse the action. A red-first component regression used an approved RMA with `available_actions.can_receive=false` and reproduced a visible Record Receipt button before the migration. The detail response now supplies viewer-scoped flags for the main workflow actions and the page reads those flags. Completion projection calls the same customer-MRB check as the locked service path. The selected API projection test passes 1 test / 7 assertions; the focused RMA detail component file passes 8/8, and changed-file syntax/style/ESLint/diff checks pass. This does not prove live API mutation recovery, cross-user WebSocket delivery, or complete per-line Split and disposition-specific Quality eligibility; the service continues to revalidate those details. Matrix runtime cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 406. RMA stale status actions returned 422 and left the actor's page stale (reproduced; typed 409 and refresh recovery added in Delta 917)

`ReturnRequestService::ensureStatus()` rejected a repeated or delayed write with `BusinessRuleException`, which the API rendered as 422. A red-first API regression submitted the same RMA twice: the second request returned 422 (`Expected status draft, got pending_approval`) instead of the required conflict. The detail mutation handlers only toasted failures, so a missed socket event could leave the actor's old status and action buttons visible after that rejection. RMA status mismatches and stale cancel/retry/reject/split/dispose states now use `ReturnRequestStateConflictException`, rendered as 409 with `return_request_state_conflict`; ordinary business-rule validation remains 422. A red-first UI regression confirmed a 409 did not refetch the RMA. The detail now invalidates its central `return_request` families and closes plain confirmations after 409; rejection and disposition dialogs preserve entered values while disabling stale submission; the edit form subscribes, preserves dirty fields on newer snapshots, and disables Save when the server reports `can_edit=false`. The repeated-submit API regression passes (1 test / 3 assertions; existing notification/container warning) and the focused Return Management detail/disposition UI files pass 11/11. No cross-user browser, peer event trace, latency sample or matrix promotion occurred. This change affects conflict response/recovery only; business state rules remain unchanged.


### 407. Supplier RFQ stale writes returned 422 without refreshing the portal (reproduced; typed 409 recovery added in Delta 920)

`SupplierQuoteService` locks and rechecks the RFQ before saving, withdrawing, or uploading quotation documents. When an invited supplier submitted a write after the RFQ moved to Closed, `assertAcceptingQuotes()` threw a generic `BusinessRuleException`; `SupplierRfqController` translated it to 422. The supplier quote editor and detail page only displayed a toast on mutation errors, so if the vendor-scoped `SupplierRfqChanged` signal was missed, the old `can_quote` state and Withdraw/Submit controls remained until a manual refresh. A focused red-first API test changed an invited fixture's status to Closed after the supplier had loaded the open invitation, then attempted a valid draft save; the endpoint returned 422 instead of the expected typed 409. The route remains under the supplier portal guard and the service verifies the invitation. No other vendor's quote or competitor data is included. The typed conflict now returns HTTP 409 `supplier_quote_state_conflict`; the supplier portal refreshes its authorized RFQ data through the central `supplier_rfqs_changed` map after a 409 and disables actions during refetch. Invitation denial and content/PDF validation remain 422. The red-first focused API test now passes (1 warning / 3 assertions; the warning is the existing missing-container-`.env` notice). No two-browser portal scenario was run; this proves conflict recovery wiring, not WebSocket delivery or end-user latency.


### 408. Buyer RFQ publish from a stale detail screen returned 422 without refetching (reproduced; fixed in Delta 921)

The red-first focused API regression created a Draft RFQ, advanced its persisted status to Open after the buyer had loaded the draft, and then posted `publish`. The API returned HTTP 422 (`Only draft RFQs can be published.`) instead of the expected 409 state conflict. `RequestForQuoteService::publish()` correctly locks and revalidates the row, but threw generic `BusinessRuleException`; `RequestForQuoteController::respond()` translated it to 422. The buyer RFQ detail's mutation error handler only toasted the message, so a missed event left its status/action projection unchanged. The test failed at the expected-status assertion before this finding was written; no implementation change had yet been made. No two-browser delivery or latency trace was captured.

Delta 921 adds `RequestForQuoteStateConflictException`, renders it as 409 `request_for_quote_state_conflict`, and reuses it for stale RFQ edit/publish/extend/close/cancel/document and award lifecycle guards. Permission checks still run before state disclosure, and invalid input remains 422. The RFQ create/edit, detail, comparison, and manual-quote pages invalidate through the central request-for-quote map on conflict. Edit and manual-quote forms retain entered values after a lifecycle change; lifecycle actions disable during refetch. Successful full-RFQ mutation responses now reconcile into the actor's detail cache immediately before linked families are invalidated. The focused publish API test passes (1 test / 5 assertions, with the test container's existing missing-`.env` warning); targeted Pint, PHP syntax, ESLint, Vite module transforms, live HTTP, and diff checks pass. This does not add a peer WebSocket trace or new matrix evidence; the stale-risk matrix outcomes remain unchanged.

### 409. Decision Center actions from a stale snapshot returned 422 (reproduced and fixed in Delta 922)

`RecommendationService::act()` locks and rechecks the current recommendation and `applyAccept()` rejects an already-accepted row, so the server prevents a duplicate transition. However, `RecommendationActionRequest` had no expected-version input, `RecommendationResource` returned only second-precision `updated_at` rather than a monotonic version, and a stale duplicate action therefore fell through to generic `BusinessRuleException` and HTTP 422. `RecommendationDetails.tsx` only called `reportMutationError()` on failure, so the status and action projection could remain stale if the refresh signal was missed. A focused API regression used two separate authorized user identities: the first accepted the record and the second submitted Accept with the earlier snapshot; it reproduced HTTP 422 where a state conflict was expected (failure at `RecommendationLifecycleTest.php:353`). This was an API-level two-identity reproduction, not two browser contexts and not evidence of WebSocket delivery. The authorization check on the locked row must remain before any version-conflict detail is returned.

Delta 922 adds a monotonic `version` column, incremented by the recommendation model on every Eloquent update and returned with every recommendation resource. The action request accepts an optional `expected_version` for compatibility with existing clients; the Decision Center sends it, and the locked service compares it only after rechecking viewer access. A typed `dss_recommendation_state_conflict` returns HTTP 409. The actor continues to reconcile successful API responses directly; a stale-action 409 invalidates the central DSS query family. The API regression passes (one test, five assertions, with the known container warning), and the focused Decision Center component file passes 11/11. PHP syntax/Pint, targeted ESLint, migration status, and live site/Vite/API health pass. The development database migration was applied. No browser-context pair, socket delivery, latency sample, or matrix promotion occurred; API clients that omit `expected_version` retain their prior server-state validation behavior.

The conflict handler awaits central DSS invalidation while the mutation remains pending; the existing `act.isPending` guards keep recommendation actions disabled until the refetch finishes. The focused UI regression verifies that disabled interval and the conflict refresh request.

### 410. Return line Split action was inferred by the SPA instead of the API (reproduced; fixed in Delta 923)

A focused red-first component regression supplied a return whose parent response allowed Split while the line response said `available_actions.can_split=false`. Before the fix, the detail page still rendered “Split quantity” because it rebuilt eligibility from the parent action, line disposition, and received quantity. The API resource exposed only the parent `can_split` flag, so the line-specific button had no server action contract. The button and open modal now use this server flag; submitted quantities remain local form validation, and `ReturnRequestService::splitItem()` retains all existing locked receipt, allocation, Quality, and state checks. The reproduction was a component harness; the API test exercises the real authorized endpoint. No two-browser delivery, socket trace, latency sample, or matrix promotion was added.

### 411. Mapped option queries were misclassified as static by the SPA freshness policy (reproduced; fixed in Delta 924)

The SPA QueryClient sets `refetchOnMount: 'always'` only when `isRealtimeManagedQueryKey()` matches a central entity, signal, or cache-group prefix. Before Delta 924, the helper returned false first for any key containing an `options` segment. A focused regression showed that Return Case source-option data was present in `queryKeysForRealtimeList()` but `isRealtimeManagedQueryKey()` still returned false. This is a concrete source-level mismatch; it is not a captured end-user stale display. The same classification order affected mapped option queries with positive TTLs, including Purchase Order option reads.

Delta 924 checks realtime prefixes first, so mapped live queries revalidate on mount regardless of key shape; unmapped option/reference queries retain their explicit TTL. The focused regression and freshness source audit pass. No browser-remount reproduction, peer socket delivery, request-rate impact, cache hit rate, API p95, or matrix result was measured. No matrix cell is promoted.

### 412. Return Case source intake could keep stale eligible quantities and status (source-level gap reproduced; refresh wiring added in Delta 926)

The Return Case create screen queried source search and source-option data but did not mount a Return Case realtime list subscription. Its options include Delivery/PO availability and receipt-derived quantities. The source API revalidates on submit, so this finding is stale UI risk; no invalid business write was demonstrated. Before the fix, focused regressions showed that the Return Case map omitted source search/options on customer RMA, supplier PO and receipt signals, the shared source hook was absent, and `ChainStepAdvanced::broadcastOn()` lacked the Return Case source entity channels for Delivery/PO changes. Delta 926 adds both scoped source families to central invalidation, subscribes the create form through shared internal and portal hooks, and adds content-free Delivery refreshes to customer and all-return channels and PO refreshes only to the all-return channel. A red-first regression caught that PO identifiers must not fan out to customer-only viewers, whose source API rejects supplier sources; the final event targets only users who can read the source through Return Management APIs. API refetch still determines eligibility and submission still revalidates. The focused map tests pass (5 selected), hook and form regressions pass (1 each), the backend channel test passes (1 test / 6 assertions), and the existing scoped-channel authorization test passes (12 assertions with a PHPUnit warning). Targeted ESLint and PHP syntax/Pint pass. No two-browser peer observation, Reverb frame, latency/cache metric, or matrix promotion occurred.


### 413. PR bulk approval response was not reconciled to the actor's list cache (confirmed client root cause)

`spa/src/pages/purchasing/purchase-requests/index.tsx` invalidated PR lists after bulk approval but did not apply the mutation response. With the subsequent list request delayed or failing, the actor retained the previous status and status-derived list controls. The focused component regression reproduced this by returning `approved` from the bulk API mock and holding the second GET open; it failed while the row still showed `Pending`. Delta 927 applies successful per-ID statuses to cached PR list queries before invalidation refetch completion, removes those IDs from pending/approval-inbox filtered lists, and clears the actor's selected toolbar after bulk approval. These focused component regressions pass. This confirms the actor cache gap and one stale-selection case; it does not prove backend delivery, peer refresh, or related-record/dashboard propagation.


### 414. Purchase Request list actions were inferred from client status (projections added in Delta 929)

The paginated resource previously omitted `available_actions`, while the list page inferred Convert to PO from fetched status/source and the SPA permission store. The Credit Note detail rendered Finalize and Apply from local permission, status, and balance rather than server-projected actions. ### 416. HR profile-update review controls were inferred from status and local permissions (reproduced; action projection and conflict recovery added in Delta 931)

The HR/Finance review resource omitted current-viewer permission and action flags. `profile-update-requests/index.tsx` inferred whether to show Finance Approve/Reject from `status` plus the locally cached permission store and stored a copied row in the confirmation dialog. ### 417. Maintenance completion stale confirmation did not recover from a committed transition (fixed in Delta 932)

The Maintenance Work Order service locked the persisted row before completion, but stale completed state was passed to the generic transition validator and rendered as HTTP 422. ### 418. Bill payment stale decisions returned generic 422 (typed 409 added in Delta 936)

`BillService` row-locked and rechecked payment status for approve, reject, and void, but status conflicts used `BusinessRuleException`; `BillController` rendered those failures as HTTP 422. A focused red-first Accounting test approved a pending payment, repeated the approval, and observed the generic exception had no conflict code. Delta 936 adds `BillPaymentStateConflictException` and maps the stale approval/rejection/void branches to a stable `bill_payment_state_conflict` HTTP 409 response. ### 419. MRB double release was safely blocked but stale status returned 422 (typed 409 and dialog refetch added in Delta 937)

The existing MRB race test held two model snapshots, released once, then invoked the second release. The row lock and status re-read already prevented duplicate quarantine stock movement, but the stale release raised an untyped `BusinessRuleException` and the controller rendered HTTP 422. The SPA release modal routed that failure through field validation and did not refetch the current MRB. Delta 937 adds `MaterialReviewRecordStateConflictException`, returns HTTP 409 `material_review_record_state_conflict`, and makes the modal invalidate authorized MRB/related query families while keeping typed release values intact. A red-first focused regression failed on the missing code, then passed with 5 assertions including the HTTP retry. ### 420. CAPA verdict visibility trusted client state and stale terminal verdicts returned 422 (server action projection and 409 added in Delta 938)

`NcrActionResource` did not provide `available_actions`; the CAPA detail page inferred verdict visibility from local permission and action status. Its React key included status, so a realtime refetch that changed status remounted the panel and could discard unsaved notes. The service correctly row-locked and re-read both action and NCR, but terminal-state failures flowed through the generic 422 state-machine exception. Delta 938 adds viewer-scoped `permissions.verify_effectiveness` and `available_actions.can_verify`, loads the parent NCR status for the projection, keeps the component keyed by action ID, and returns a stable `ncr_action_state_conflict` 409 for final verdict/NCR state races; the UI invalidates authorized NCR queries on that conflict. The focused API test passes 6 assertions after red-first failures on the missing projection and 422 path. This proves the server response contract and endpoint behavior only; peer delivery, rendered other-role buttons, and latency remain unverified. The transition matrix remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 421. The realtime observer contract checked registration but not lifecycle signaling (guard strengthened in Delta 939)

`RealtimeMutationCoverageContractTest` found all `*RealtimeObserver` classes registered in an application service provider, but an observer with no model hook or no signal/invalidation call would still pass. Delta 939 adds both source-shape assertions for each discovered observer; the focused contract test passes 2 tests / 4,020 assertions. This closes an empty/disconnected-observer gap in the guard. It does not prove that each route or direct writer invokes the producer, nor does it establish after-commit delivery or peer rendering; the 566 unverified transition rows remain unchanged.


### 422. NCR terminal close/cancel/disposition races returned generic 422 and did not refresh the actor view (typed 409 added in Delta 940)

The row-locked NCR service already prevented a second Close, Cancel, or Set Disposition after the record became terminal. These terminal guards raised generic `BusinessRuleException`, so the API returned 422; the SPA handlers only showed a toast and left stale confirmations enabled. Delta 940 adds `NcrStateConflictException` and a stable `ncr_state_conflict` 409 response for all three routes. The detail invalidates its authorized NCR query on the conflict, disables the actions while refreshing and after server state revokes them, and keeps cancellation reason text in the open dialog. A red-first endpoint test observed 422; the focused regression now passes 6 assertions across all three actions. No browser pair or peer event was observed, so the matrix rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 423. Shared broadcaster forwarded arbitrary event fields past the refresh-envelope boundary (allowlist added in Delta 945)

`StandardizedPusherBroadcaster` normalized legacy events into a common refresh envelope but passed every original payload key through to Pusher/Reverb. A red-first broadcaster test proved that a fake event containing `customer_email`, `amount`, and `status` reached the transport mock alongside the refresh metadata. This was a confirmed transport-boundary exposure risk, not evidence that a real user previously received those fields. A source audit found all 128 explicit `ShouldBroadcast` classes implement `broadcastWith()` and their literal payload keys are within the accepted metadata set; email/notification snapshots in `OfficialReceiptIssued`, `RfqLifecycleEvent`, and `ReturnRequestUpdated` are not broadcast events.

Delta 945 filters normalized payloads through one shared allowlist: refresh/action/version/event metadata, opaque record/actor/related IDs (including legacy aliases), notification ID/type, cache group, restore flag and the Pusher socket exclusion token. The authorized API remains the source of record fields. The red-first test now passes, verifies sensitive extra keys are absent, retains supported notification metadata, and confirms the socket token still reaches Pusher as `socket_id`. The 512-character allowed ID still trips the configured message-size guard. The focused file passes 7 tests / 17 assertions in 3.49 seconds with 7 PHPUnit warnings. No real sensitive broadcast, browser delivery, matrix promotion, or cache change is claimed.


### 424. Reconnect/focus catch-up invalidated unrelated active reference queries (scoped to mapped freshness families in Delta 946)

`catchUpRealtimeQueries()` invalidated every active TanStack Query when the socket reconnected, the tab regained focus, or delivery entered degraded mode. This included active, unmapped lookups with explicit multi-minute reference-data TTLs, so a routine catch-up forced extra authorized API requests even though no event mapped to those keys. The existing classifier and central invalidation map already define which entity, signal, and cache-group prefixes require freshness recovery.

Delta 946 centralizes the managed-prefix check in the invalidation map and uses it for catch-up invalidation, retrying an overlapping active read, and the trailing retry of still-invalidated queries. Dynamic related-detail query families are included through ID-free prefixes derived from their registered key factories. The freshness classifier uses the same check. A focused regression proves mapped Inspection and sales-order fulfillment queries refresh while the unmapped Production downtime-category lookup remains cached; three existing catch-up/refetch-race cases and the new case pass (4 selected). ESLint passes on the three changed SPA files. No API request-rate benchmark or browser-pair delivery measurement was taken; route matrix outcomes remain unchanged.


### 425. Dev Nginx catch-all immutable caching was suspected and ruled out (Delta 960)

The public Vite development site was checked before changing Nginx. The active `docker/nginx/default.conf` SPA location has no `public, immutable` header; the production template's immutable asset policy is in a separate file. Live requests to `/` and `/@vite/client` return `Cache-Control: no-cache`, while `/sw.js` returns `no-store, no-cache, must-revalidate, max-age=0` with Cloudflare `BYPASS`. The service-worker source separately excludes API/Sanctum paths. Therefore the suspected dev catch-all header is not a confirmed cause and no Nginx change was made. This is a point-in-time public-header check, not proof for authenticated API responses on every route or every browser cache.


### 426. Mutation inventory producer references do not yet prove writer reachability (Delta 961)

The existing contract checks that each direct-status-write row names a real event, observer, service, or helper, but class existence does not establish that the source writer reaches it. A PHP AST scan limited to one source file at a time avoided the API container's 128 MiB memory limit. The 192 inventory rows contain 175 distinct `Class::method` references; 154 rows have one, 19 have two, seven have three, one has seven, and 11 describe non-method sources. Among 105 `service:` rows, three grouped/atypical writer references did not resolve to one simple `Class::method` entry; for 102 parsed service rows the named producer appeared directly or through a same-class helper in 73. Five of the 20 `event:` rows were visible from the first parsed writer method. A stricter scan limited to single-signature service/event/helper rows resolved 105 methods and found a direct or same-class-helper symbol reference in 66. The other rows' descriptions include observer-based NCR, stock-count, item-cost-review, attendance, and stock-movement refreshes, and parent-chain fan-out. These are scanner matches, not coverage percentages: rows can group multiple methods, observers can emit through Eloquent lifecycle callbacks, and some references name the terminal service rather than the immediate caller. The scan therefore cannot distinguish a real missing event from an indirect path and is not a valid failing assertion. No code or test changed; normalizing each row to a direct call, observed model/observer, or documented indirect path is still required before an invocation guard can be enforced. The 566 unverified transition rows and all current matrix counts remain unchanged.


### 427. A crashed outbox worker could leave delivery reported healthy (reproduced; degraded health now detects stale leases)

`RealtimeQueueHealth` checked queued jobs, overdue pending rows, and failed rows, but ignored `processing` outbox rows. A worker can die after claiming a row; once a retry is consumed while the existing lease is still active, that queue job can finish without publishing and leave no queued job while the durable row stays in `processing`. `OutboxDispatcher` only considers that lease reclaimable after ten minutes. During that interval the SPA could treat delivery as healthy and omit its degraded-mode catch-up polling. The focused `RealtimeQueueHealthTest` inserted a processing row locked five seconds in the past with both Redis lanes empty and reproduced the false `healthy` result (one test failed, 4 assertions).

Delta 974 marks delivery degraded when a processing row has no lease or a lease at least two seconds old, aligned with the existing pending-row freshness threshold. The health cache namespace advanced from `v2` to `v3` so a prior one-second healthy snapshot cannot mask the new check after deploy. The focused regression now passes (1 selected test / 4 assertions; PHPUnit reports its existing container configuration warning). No user-facing record matrix cell is promoted: this fixes degraded-mode detection, not delivery or browser rendering. The ten-minute durable outbox recovery remains unchanged.


### 428. The local outbox-dispatch lane had a high queue-wait tail under burst load (measured; local warm capacity increased)

A metadata-only aggregation of the running dev `realtime-2026-11-06.log` found 3,071 `DispatchOutboxMessage` queue starts: high-lane wait p50 70.9 ms, p95 1,697.4 ms, max 4,604.5 ms. In the same log, 3,078 `BroadcastEvent` starts on the separate `broadcasts` lane had wait p50 3.0 ms, p95 18.6 ms, max 293.4 ms. This identifies the outbox-dispatch lane as the queue-wait bottleneck in that sample; it does not isolate one business action or establish commit-to-UI latency. Runtime inspection showed the local Horizon supervisor had only one active high worker, despite allowing a second process after autoscaling.

Delta 975 sets the local high-lane minimum and maximum to two workers, while production configuration and the single-worker broadcast lane remain unchanged. The queue-lane config regression failed at the old min=1 and passes at min=2 (1 test / 3 assertions, existing environment warning). Horizon was restarted and runtime inspection confirms two active high workers. Fresh worker RSS is about 72 MiB each. Delta 976 parsed the next 15 minutes of local logs in Manila time: 54 outbox jobs, wait p50 225.3 ms, p95 717.6 ms, max 765.4 ms. The observed p95 is 57.7% below the earlier 1,697.4 ms sample, but the windows have different, uncontrolled traffic mixes; this supports a lower queue-wait tail, not causal attribution or the commit-to-UI SLO. No after-change browser measurement was made.


### 429. Horizon did not monitor the active outbox queue's wait threshold (reproduced; threshold aligned)

Horizon 5's `MonitorWaitTimes` looks up thresholds using `<connection>:<queue>`. The configured outbox supervisor and `DispatchOutboxMessage` use `redis_broadcasts:high`, but `config/horizon.php` only declared `redis:high`; therefore waits for the actual realtime dispatch lane fell back to Horizon's default 60 seconds rather than the intended 15 seconds. A focused config regression failed with `null` for the active queue key, then passed after adding `redis_broadcasts:high => 15`. The guard now checks that all default supervisors have a positive threshold for every served connection/queue pair (15 assertions; existing PHPUnit warning that `/var/www/.env` is absent). Horizon was restarted and reports running. This restores the intended operational alert threshold; no missed alert or user-visible stale-screen incident was separately observed, and no matrix outcome changes.


### 430. Badge refresh outbox creation was deferred beyond the source transaction (reproduced; made atomic)

`BadgeInvalidationObserver` implemented `ShouldHandleEventsAfterCommit`. That delayed the entire observer, including `OutboxService::record()`, until after the business transaction committed. `OutboxService` already writes its durable outbox row in the current transaction and schedules queue publication with `DB::afterCommit()`. A process exit after the record commit but before the observer callback could therefore leave a committed badge-affecting change with no durable `BadgesChanged` signal. A red-first `BadgeRealtimeTest::test_badge_refresh_outbox_is_staged_inside_the_source_transaction` created a badge-backed `ProfileUpdateRequest` inside a transaction and observed zero outbox rows before commit (expected one).

Delta 980 removes the after-commit observer contract. The observer now records the refresh row in the source transaction; the existing `OutboxService` callback still queues delivery only after commit, and the badge/dashboard cache-generation rotations remain deferred by their own after-commit logic. The focused new regression and existing badge-source regression pass (2 selected tests / 4 assertions; known container `.env` warning). This closes the identified durability window for badge refresh signals. It does not establish peer WebSocket delivery, rendered updates, or matrix coverage; no user-visible incident was separately reproduced.


### 431. Persisted form drafts survived access-scope changes across a full reload (reproduced; keys now scope to authorization)

`useFormDraftAutosave` keyed localStorage by `userId:formKey`, so the same account reused a draft after its role, linked employee/department, permissions, delegations, or features changed. The auth store only cleared drafts for an access change when an earlier in-memory user existed; after a full reload it intentionally had no previous user to compare. Its startup sweeper also retained valid, unexpired autosave envelopes in the legacy key format. A red-first hook regression saved a draft, simulated reload with the same user ID and a different role, and observed that the old persisted payload remained (expected deletion); a second red-first case showed an unexpired legacy-format draft was not swept.

Delta 981 moves ordinary drafts to `ogami:formdraft:v2:{userId}:scope-{fingerprint}:{formKey}`. The fingerprint includes role, employee/department, active state, permissions, delegated approvals, and feature access. The hook now persists only when its explicit user ID matches the current auth-store identity. When `/me` resolves, `applyUser()` removes that user's old scopes and legacy keys even on first bootstrap. The startup/daily sweep removes recognized legacy autosave envelopes but preserves pending-operation journals with a different shape. The six focused hook tests pass; both regressions were observed red before their corresponding fixes, and targeted ESLint passes. The existing ChecklistDecisionPanel draft fixture now uses the scoped key helper, and its focused restore case passes (1 selected, 24 skipped). No two-browser role-switch, installed tablet, full frontend suite, transition-matrix outcome, or latency result is claimed.

### 432. Cold bootstrap could not detect persisted retry data from an earlier access scope (reproduced; marker and cleanup added)

Even after ordinary form drafts were access-scoped, other app-owned local/session retry payloads had no prior in-memory auth identity to compare after a full reload. The first bootstrap also had no durable marker on pre-upgrade browser profiles. Red-first `authStore` regressions reproduced that an old-scope delivery retry remained after `/me` resolved a changed permission set, and that unmarked legacy retry state remained on first bootstrap.

Delta 982 adds `ogami:auth-cache-scope:v1`, a small localStorage cleanup marker with user ID, opaque authorization fingerprint and timestamp. On auth resolution, changed, malformed, expired, or missing-with-persisted-state markers clear app-owned draft/retry storage before writing the current scope. A 30-day marker age bounds legacy retry retention; same-scope retry state is preserved across cold bootstrap, and logout clears the marker. A related form-draft test now expects retry removal on scope change; the legacy envelope sweeper still preserves exact-operation journal shapes when there is no auth-scope transition. Auth-store tests pass 19/19; form-draft hook tests pass 6/6; targeted ESLint passes. This is unit evidence only: no browser/device auth-scope switch, API replay, peer delivery, or matrix outcome is claimed. Storage-blocked browsers remain fail-open for persistent cleanup, while each active session still clears in-memory query data on detected access changes.

### 433. Completed Payroll runs left an unreachable progress snapshot in Redis (reproduced; terminal entries now retired)

`PayrollPeriodResource` exposes the progress snapshot only while the period is `Processing`, but `ProcessPayrollJob` used the normal progress writer for its terminal completion signal. The terminal `put()` therefore re-created a period-scoped Redis entry that no resource would read, with a one-hour TTL. A red-first focused `PayrollComputeClaimFencingTest` completed an empty run and observed the 0/0 terminal snapshot still readable after the period had returned to Draft.

Delta 983 makes `PayrollProgressTracker::put()` cache only Processing snapshots and adds an after-commit `forget()` to the shared `PayrollPeriodService::releaseClaim()` path. Thus job completion/failure, administrator force-unlock and stale reaping retire active progress after the database state commits. `ProcessPayrollJob` still sends its existing content-free terminal refresh event so subscribed clients refetch the terminal state. The focused regression passes (1 warning / 4 assertions, 21.55 seconds); PHP syntax and Pint checks pass. The first post-fix attempt collided with a previously running 28-minute full API suite on the shared test DB and deadlocked during schema teardown; that exact broad-suite process group was stopped under the user's earlier instruction and the single focused rerun passed with the test DB idle. No runtime Redis payload audit, cache-hit measurement, UI render, two-user delivery or latency result is claimed.
